Full analysis record, methods detail, robustness checks and correction history

Status: Supplementary material accompanying the main article



This edition supersedes the 9 October 2026 version. It incorporates the corrections arising from an adversarial audit (61 confirmed findings) and a complete re-run of stages 4–9 on a corrected pipeline. Quantitative claims were re-derived from the final run's output files wherever those outputs survive; the few that are carried over from the corrections document, computed on superseded runs, or of unrecoverable provenance are labelled as such. A record of the defects found and fixed is given in the appendices.



What this document is. This document is the complete analysis record behind the main article. It carries the full methods detail, every robustness check that was run, the limitations register, and the record of defects found and corrected during the analysis. It is supplied so that any number in the article can be traced to the output that produced it, and so that the corrections made along the way are on the record rather than invisible.

Numbering. All display items have been renumbered sequentially in the order they appear here: the 22 figures are Figure S1 to Figure S22 and the 44 tables are Table S1 to Table S44. The original section numbering is unchanged, so cross-references of the form “Section 6.9” still resolve within this document. Two figures and two tables were originally placed out of numeric sequence to avoid disturbing earlier numbering; the sequential S-numbering removes that artefact.

References. Section 11 is the reference list. Every entry in it was checked against a primary source — the publisher’s deposit, the journal’s record, the archive record or the issuing organisation’s page — rather than against a citation aggregator.

Where to look. Sections 3 and 5 give the methods and the analytical decisions in full; Section 6 the complete results; Section 8 the robustness checks, including the six-scenario threshold sensitivity analysis; Section 9 the limitations register; and Section 12 the appendices, which include the software environment, the output inventory, the reproduction instructions and the audit and correction record.

Contents

If the entries are blank, press Ctrl+A then F9 (⌘+A, fn+F9 on macOS) to build the field.



Abstract

Objective. To quantify spatial population structure, gene flow and candidate signatures of local selection in Plasmodium falciparum across Ghana's agro-ecological zones using the MalariaGEN Pf8 release; to measure the zone-level frequencies of the canonical antimalarial resistance codons, and the haplotypes they form within isolates; and to establish whether zone-level differentiation is detectable at all before any locus-specific signal is interpreted.

Design limits, stated first. Zone is not an isolable variable in this dataset. Three of the four represented zones are each filled by exactly one administrative region (Guinea savanna / Upper East, coastal savanna / Greater Accra, transitional zone / Brong Ahafo); collection year differs systematically between zones; and four of the six zone pairs share no Pf8 sequencing study at all, including coastal savanna versus Guinea savanna, the pair that carries every headline number. Ecology, administrative region, collection period and sequencing batch are collinear by construction. Every zone-level result below is a statement about that compound variable. It is arranged broadly from south to north, but not monotonically: the transitional zone is the second-most-northern of the four and is represented by only 25 analysed isolates, and it breaks the south-to-north ordering in all but one of the variables examined here - the single exception is pfaat1 S258L, which declines monotonically from coastal savanna through deciduous forest and the transitional zone to Guinea savanna - so no claim of a latitudinal cline is made from the four zone means alone. No adjustment within these data can decompose the compound variable.

Data. Pf8 contains 33,325 samples and 12,493,205 variant sites. Ghana contributes 6,653 samples, of which 4,905 pass the release's own quality control. Isolates were collected between 2009 and 2021 and are geolocated only to administrative level 1, giving seven distinct coordinates across seven regions. A spatial join to a Ghana agro-ecological zone layer placed these 4,905 in four zones: Guinea savanna (n = 3,605), deciduous forest (n = 815), coastal savanna (n = 435) and transitional zone (n = 50); the smaller zone counts quoted under Methods are for the 1,900 isolates that survive this analysis's own sample QC. The Sudan savanna and the wet-evergreen and moist-evergreen forest zones are unrepresented. The zone layer itself has no citable published source: it is an unattributed re-host of an anonymous ArcGIS Online item, identified byte-for-byte by checksum, and is used here as accessed rather than as authoritative.

Methods. Genotypes were obtained from the Pf8 Zarr store over HTTPS, restricted to Ghanaian samples, sites passing the release filter, biallelic single-nucleotide polymorphisms only (the ingest mask is FILTER_PASS & is_snp & numalt == 1), and the GT, AD, DP and GQ fields. Sample quality control applied a within-host fixation index (Fws) threshold of 0.95, a call-rate floor of 0.80, a heterozygosity outlier rule and clonal pruning; mixed calls were resolved to the majority allele by read depth and allele counts taken as one allele per isolate. Variant missingness is now recomputed after the sample cut rather than before it, which yields 3,916 SNPs across 1,900 isolates (mean missingness 2.95%): Guinea savanna 1,334, deciduous forest 337, coastal savanna 204, transitional zone 25. Analyses comprised three ordinations of the published Pf8 genotype-distance matrix (originally computed on the 4,905 release-QC-pass isolates and now re-run on the 1,900-isolate analysis set, by re-slicing the distance submatrix rather than reusing the cached full-set one), pairwise Hudson FST with a block jackknife, ADMIXTURE at K = 1-8 with cross-validation, Mantel tests of isolation by distance at zone and region level with a leave-one-population-out jackknife, TreeMix with 0-5 migration edges and jackknife edge standard errors, and a BayPass XtX scan calibrated against 100,000 pseudo-observed neutral SNPs with Benjamini-Hochberg q-values. Two further checks were added at final revision and both are reported below: a six-scenario threshold sensitivity analysis that rebuilds the analysis matrix under varied Fws and MAF cuts and recomputes pairwise FST and the region-level Mantel test in each, and a call-quality audit of the 19 XtX outliers against the other 3,897 scanned SNPs on the Pf8 annotation fields (MQ, VQSLOD, QD, FS, SOR, ReadPosRankSum, ExcessHet, InbreedingCoeff, culprit, RegionType). The resistance panel is separate and has been rebuilt from the codon up: 57 codons in nine genes were reconstructed from the CDS and the Pf3D7 reference genome - each codon mapped to its three genomic positions, every variant record at those positions collected regardless of numalt or is_snp, each isolate's alleles applied to the reference codon, and the codon translated - so that what is reported is the full residue spectrum including the reference residue, with Wilson confidence intervals and a four-zone chi-square test per residue. Mixed calls make a codon uncalled rather than being assigned a residue. Within-isolate haplotypes were counted on complete cases only; calls at the 25 of the 57 panel codons for which MalariaGEN publishes a per-sample genotype were compared one-for-one against it, on isolates both pipelines called; and the pfk13 list was taken from the WHO compendium of validated and candidate markers, v1.0 (December 2025), rather than written by hand.

Principal findings: population structure. Zone differentiation is very low, and after the sensitivity analysis this is the best-supported finding in the report. The largest pairwise FST is 0.00232 (SE 0.00053, 95% CI 0.00128-0.00336) between coastal and Guinea savanna, and one of six pairs - Guinea savanna versus the transitional zone (n = 25) - has a confidence interval spanning zero; the other five have strictly positive lower bounds. Varying the two thresholds that define the dataset leaves the maximum pairwise FST within 0.00204-0.00255 across all six scenarios, over sample sizes from 1,900 to 4,403 isolates and SNP counts from 3,275 to 10,876. Two directions within that range are worth recording: relaxing the Fws cut slightly raises FST (0.00232 to 0.00255 with no Fws filter at all), so polyclonal isolates were not masking differentiation, and tripling the SNP count at MAF >= 0.01 lowers it (0.00204), as expected if rarer variants are less differentiated between zones. Isolation by distance, by contrast, is not a robust result. At administrative-region level the test is significant at the published thresholds (Mantel r = 0.427, p = 0.025 with negative FST pairs floored to zero; r = 0.414, p = 0.041 without flooring the three negative pairs) and it is absent at zone level (r = 0.471, p = 0.122, significant in 0 of 4 leave-one-zone-out replicates, and with four populations admitting only 24 distinct label permutations). But across the six threshold scenarios the region-level test is significant in only two: the published thresholds and MAF >= 0.02 (r = 0.434, p = 0.047 censored, p = 0.050 uncensored, so that scenario is marginal in both directions). It fails at every relaxation of the Fws monoclonality cut (Fws >= 0.90, r = 0.318, p = 0.087; Fws >= 0.80, r = 0.256, p = 0.169; no Fws filter, r = 0.245, p = 0.179) and at MAF >= 0.01 (r = 0.432, p = 0.062). It is also significant in only four of seven leave-one-region-out replicates, collapsing when Upper East is dropped. The finding holds at the published thresholds and almost nowhere else, and is reported here as not robust - not as a supported result, and not as a weak but real distance effect. ADMIXTURE cross-validation error is minimised at K = 2 (0.83925) over K = 1 (0.84384), an improvement of 0.00459 on a near-flat curve. The K = 2 minor component is not uniform across zones (means 0.092 deciduous forest, 0.085 coastal savanna, 0.053 Guinea savanna, 0.042 transitional zone; Kruskal-Wallis H = 105.4, p = 1.1 x 10 to the -22). That is a real contrast - coastal and forest high, Guinea savanna and transitional low - concordant with the FST ranking but not a monotone latitudinal cline, and of negligible magnitude. TreeMix, now run with edge standard errors, selects a descriptive graph with two migration edges — a model choice, not a count of historical migration events (weights 0.152, p = 7.5 x 10 to the -4, and 0.805, p = 3.3 x 10 to the -16; variance explained 0.9969 at m = 2 against 0.8970 at m = 0); a third edge is indistinguishable from zero (p = 0.33). The edge tests are conditional on a topology, root and edge count all fixed beforehand, and the variance-explained criterion across the full m = 0 to 5 sweep still prefers m = 3, so the two criteria disagree and are reconciled here on identifiability grounds.

Principal findings: the selection scan. The BayPass scan returns 19 SNPs above a calibrated XtX threshold of 10.84, of which 13 also pass a 10% false-discovery threshold; 3.9 false positives are expected at the quantile threshold, so expected null exceedances are 20.6% of the 19 calls — a ratio expected under the assumed null, not a measured false-discovery rate. Seventeen of the 19 fall in 13 annotated genes; two are intergenic, including one (Pf3D7_11_v3:1,054,447, XtX 20.79, q = 0.005) that ranks fifth in the whole scan and survives FDR correction, the other being Pf3D7_12_v3:715,788 (XtX 15.09, q = 0.017). The 13 genes are headed by PTP7 (max XtX 26.44), the amino-acid transporter AAT1 (PF3D7_0629500, max XtX 24.20), FIKK4.2, ACS8 and NT2. A call-quality audit of all 19 outliers against the other 3,897 scanned SNPs clears the set as a whole: every significant difference runs in the outliers' favour (higher VQSLOD, p = 0.008; QD, p = 0.016; QUAL, p = 0.007), none has MQ < 50, strand bias or read-position bias, all 19 are RegionType Core, and the eight SNPs in exported-protein genes - the subset most open to a repetitive-family artefact worry - are in fact the cleanest in the panel (FS = 0.00 for all eight, MQ median 60.00, VQSLOD significantly higher than the rest), so that concern is not supported. The audit does, however, downgrade two adjacent chromosome-11 sites to lower confidence: Pf3D7_11_v3:1,054,447 itself and Pf3D7_11_v3:1,054,078 (SNRPF, XtX 13.98, q = 0.031), 369 bp apart, the two lowest-InbreedingCoeff sites in the panel (0.193 and 0.147), with 1,054,078 carrying ExcessHet = 69.59, the only nonzero value among all 3,916 SNPs and roughly 400x the genome-wide maximum. In a near-haploid organism that points to collapsed paralogy or copy-number variation rather than to mismapping, whose MQ and FS signatures are both clean here; the mechanism is unverified and a read-level depth check would settle it. The consequence is that the fifth-ranked outlier in the whole scan is one of the two least secure entries in it. The AAT1 outlier is pfaat1 Ser258Leu, the second-highest XtX in the scan and the only outlier variant with a published gene-editing phenotype - though pfaat1 appears nowhere in the WHO marker compendium at any tier and is not a WHO-classified marker. Several other outlier genes are functionally characterised in the literature at gene level (PTP7, an EMP1 trafficking protein; NT2; ACS8), so the claim is variant-level and not a claim of unique annotation. Amambua-Ngwa et al. (2023, Nature Microbiology) edited S258L into parasites and measured a 1.5-fold rise in chloroquine IC50, in a single chloroquine-resistant Southeast Asian line (NHP4026), with epistasis between S258L and F313S and an explicit pfcrt-background caveat; it is therefore a variant that potentiates chloroquine resistance modestly and conditionally, not a stand-alone resistance determinant, and the longitudinal 0% to 97% rise that paper reports is Gambian (1984-2014) and says nothing about Ghana. S258L survives FDR correction (q = 0.005) and falls from 0.824 in coastal savanna to 0.544 in Guinea savanna (four-zone chi-square p = 1.9 x 10 to the -18), with deciduous forest at 0.735 and the transitional zone at 0.640 - so for this one variable the four zones do fall in strict south-to-north order, the sole variable reported here in which the transitional zone does not break the ordering. The second AAT1 outlier is a synonymous site 883 bp away, so this is one signal with a linked silent site, not two independent lines of evidence.

[Calculated] Principal findings: drug-resistance markers. The previous version's resistance findings were affected by a defect in how markers were identified - the panel matched SnpEff protein-change strings, which can see neither a resistance allele that is itself the reference residue nor a residue that requires two nucleotide changes in one codon - and the panel has been rebuilt by codon reconstruction, which corrects it. Two findings follow directly from the rebuild. pfdhps A437G, wholly absent from the previous panel, stands at 0.897 for G437 (n = 1,892; deciduous forest 0.964, coastal savanna 0.941, Guinea savanna 0.875, transitional zone 0.800; chi-square p = 1.2 x 10 to the -6); the official Pf8 methodology states that the 3D7 sequence translates as a G at 437 and that this is the mutant allele, so the resistance residue is the reference residue and no ALT annotation for it can exist. And the former "naming discrepancy" at pfcrt 75 was never a naming matter: N75E requires two SNPs in one codon (403621 A>G, annotated Asn75Asp; 403623 T>A, annotated Asn75Lys), and neither annotation names it. Haplotypes are now counted within isolates on complete cases. The pfdhfr IRN triple mutant is 0.771 (n = 1,876; 95% CI 0.751-0.789) - not the marginal range 0.786-0.908 previously quoted, and, as it must be, below its lowest component on the same complete-case set (51I = 0.788); NRN sits at 0.116 and the fully wild-type NCS at 0.091, so it is not a near-fixed single haplotype, and the quadruple IRNL is 0.000. pfcrt CVIET is 0.071 and is demonstrably a single unit: only CVMNK (0.929) and CVIET occur at codons 72-76, with nothing intermediate. pfdhps GE (437G + 540E) is 0.010. The pfdhfr marginals remain high and lowest in Guinea savanna (S108N 0.908 overall / 0.885 Guinea savanna; C59R 0.886 / 0.864; N51I 0.786 / 0.764; chi-square p = 9.4 x 10 to the -7, 4.8 x 10 to the -5 and 2.4 x 10 to the -4), with the transitional zone (n = 25) at or near the top of all three. The pfk13 panel was rebuilt from the WHO compendium v1.0 (December 2025) and covers all 14 validated markers (F446I, N458Y, C469Y, M476I, Y493H, G533S, R539T, I543T, P553L, R561H, P574L, C580Y, R622I, A675V) and all 6 candidates (E252Q, P441L, C469F, N537I, G538V, V568G); the old list was written from memory, misclassified P441L and C469F as validated when WHO classes them as candidate, and omitted P574L, G533S and R622I. All 23 pfk13 codons carry no screened resistance allele: twenty-two are fixed at the reference residue, and codon 458 is at 0.9995 on a single Asn458Asp call, which is not the validated N458Y, on 1,845 to 1,876 isolates called per codon of 1,900, across a 2009-2021 sampling period - a tested historical negative, not a statement about the present. Validation is now per sample rather than per marginal frequency: 47,012 of 47,012 calls agree with MalariaGEN's published genotypes, 100.0000% across 25 markers, codon 75 included.

[Observed] Correction to the previous version of this report. Two defects, not one, and the second was in a correction. First, the earlier statement that no validated antimalarial resistance locus appears among the outliers was a filter artefact, not a biological result. The ingest filter required numalt == 1, and in Pf8 most canonical resistance codons are multiallelic or carry a spanning-deletion allele, so most of the panel was removed before any analysis ran. The cascade into the scan matrix, stated once and used in the same form throughout this report, is: 44 canonical codon-allele records genotyped by the superseded panel; 21 polymorphic in this sample; 6 of the 21 passed the ingest mask; 3 then cleared the MAF >= 0.05 cut and actually reached the scan matrix (pfdhfr C59R, pfdhfr N51I and pfaat1 S258L). pfcrt I356T, pfmdr1 D1246Y and pfcrt N326S passed the mask but were cut by MAF at the frequencies the superseded panel and the filter saw, 0.016, 0.011 and 0.008. Two of those three reproduce in the rebuilt panel (I356T 0.0158, D1246Y 0.0113); the third does not. The codon-reconstruction panel puts pfcrt N326S at 0.0005 - 1 of 1,874 called, with MalariaGEN agreeing on all 1,874 - because roughly 15 of the 16 carriers the string-matching panel counted were depth-resolved mixed calls that the new panel leaves uncalled, so the 0.008 is a fifteen-fold overstatement and is quoted here only as the value the MAF cut acted on. Second, the panel that was re-fetched to repair that artefact was itself wrong, because it identified codons by matching SnpEff ANN_HGVS_p strings: it missed pfdhps A437G entirely, reported the wrong residue at pfcrt 75, and used a pfk13 list written from memory. Its validation claim - that marginal frequencies agreed with MalariaGEN at 21 of 23 markers to within 0.03, the pfcrt 75 difference being a residue-naming matter - is withdrawn: marginal agreement does not establish per-sample agreement, and the codon-75 difference was a real error in our calls, not a naming convention. The codon-reconstruction panel reported above supersedes both and agrees with MalariaGEN call-by-call at 47,012 of 47,012 calls.

[Interpreted] Conclusion. At the resolution these data permit, Ghanaian P. falciparum shows very low but statistically detectable differentiation at the retained common variants. Five of the six pairwise estimates have 95% confidence intervals excluding zero; only Guinea savanna against the transitional zone, where n = 25, spans it. Very low is not the same as undetectable, and neither is a demonstration of random mating, for which these data contain no direct evidence. What the sensitivity analysis adds is stability of the magnitude rather than absence of the effect: the maximum pairwise FST stays within 0.00204-0.00255 across all six threshold scenarios, over 1,900 to 4,403 isolates and 3,275 to 10,876 SNPs, so the headline result is insensitive to both of the cuts that define the dataset. The companion claim does not survive the same treatment. The region-level isolation-by-distance result is significant at the published thresholds and at MAF >= 0.02, but in only 2 of 6 threshold scenarios and 4 of 7 leave-one-region-out replicates, and this report therefore no longer asserts a distance effect, weak or otherwise. What remains of the spatial signal is a single south-to-north contrast, visible in the FST ranking and in the K = 2 minor component, with no robust evidence that genetic distance scales with geographic distance. That very low differentiation is a statement about neutral common-variant structure only: neutral common-variant differentiation between zones is negligible, so a spatially sparse design is adequate for characterising neutral structure, but marker-frequency representativeness is a separate question, and the resistance panel in this analysis shows significant between-zone variation at p < 0.001 at pfdhfr, pfdhps and pfaat1. That threshold is stated because it is a selection: on the four-zone chi-square as reported, several further loci are nominally significant at p < 0.05, among them pfcrt I356T (p = 1.6 x 10 to the -3), pfmdr2 I492V (p = 0.020) and pfmdr1 N86Y (p = 0.029). Low neutral FST therefore does not license an assumption that marker frequencies are spatially representative. The pfk13 result is a genuine tested negative - no screened resistance allele occurs at any of the 23 codons, which cover the complete WHO-validated set of 14 and all 6 candidates: twenty-two are fixed at the reference residue and codon 458 is at 0.9995 on a single Asn458Asp call, which is not the validated N458Y, on 1,845 to 1,876 isolates called per codon - rather than an untested absence; it is also a statement about 2009-2021 and not about current parasite populations. The XtX set remains a ranked candidate list, of which 13 SNPs survive FDR 10% and two adjacent chromosome-11 sites - one of them the fifth-ranked SNP overall - are flagged lower confidence on ExcessHet and InbreedingCoeff grounds. All zone-level results describe a compound variable in which ecology, administrative region, collection period and sequencing study cannot be separated; that is a property of the Pf8 metadata, not of the analysis.

[Interpreted] Robustness of these conclusions to the corrected re-runs and the final checks. The population-structure readings held on a 16.6% larger SNP panel (3,359 to 3,916 SNPs) with corrected filter ordering, a bitwise-reproducible single-threaded BayPass, seeded permutation tests and full false-discovery accounting: near-panmixia, maximum FST of approximately 0.0023, and a near-flat ADMIXTURE cross-validation curve. Three quantities moved and should not be described as unchanged: the XtX threshold (the manuscript's 8-thread run gave 11.232, the final single-threaded run gives 10.840), the outlier count (15 to 19) and the outlier gene list (11 to 13 genes). One conclusion has since been lost, and it was the report's only positive spatial finding. The threshold sensitivity analysis shows the region-level Mantel result significant in 2 of 6 scenarios, and it also withdraws the earlier claim that the result no longer depends on the censoring of negative FST pairs: of the six scenarios only the baseline clears p < 0.05 both censored and uncensored, the second significant scenario (MAF >= 0.02) giving p = 0.047 censored against p = 0.050 uncensored. Isolation by distance is retained in the report and reported with the scenarios in which it fails, but it is graded not robust, and the previous version's listing of it among the strengthened results is withdrawn. Two strengthenings stand. Near-panmixia is now supported by FST stability across 0.00204-0.00255 over all six scenarios, which is better evidence than any single run could give; and TreeMix now carries jackknife edge standard errors, so the m = 2 graph is reported as a selected descriptive model rather than as a settled edge count. Two further checks changed nothing material. Re-running the three ordinations on the 1,900-isolate analysis set rather than the 4,905 pre-QC isolates leaves the reading intact - silhouette by zone -0.020 against -0.016 on the full set, so removing polyclonal and clonally redundant isolates does not sharpen the structure; trustworthiness 0.499 (UMAP), 0.526 (t-SNE), 0.510 (PCoA), PCoA distance correlation 0.287 against 0.322 - and the call-quality audit clears the outlier set while downgrading two chromosome-11 SNPs. The resistance panel is a different matter and was not confirmed but replaced: an external critique upheld in full showed that the marker identification method was unsound, and the panel was rebuilt from the codon up, changing the pfdhfr triple-mutant figure, adding a locus that had been missing altogether, correcting a residue call, correcting the pfk13 list, and replacing the validation argument. The population analyses are therefore a partial confirmation with two strengthened results, one lost result, two downgraded outlier SNPs and one materially revised candidate list; the resistance results are new.

1. Introduction and background

1.1 The problem

Malaria transmission in Ghana is spatially heterogeneous, and the heterogeneity tracks ecology. The country spans a south-to-north gradient of agro-ecological zones that differ in rainfall, vector species composition, seasonality and consequently in entomological inoculation rate. If parasite populations in these zones are demographically separate to any meaningful degree, then drug-resistance alleles, antigenic variants and locally adaptive alleles can differ in frequency between them. If instead the zones exchange parasites freely, an allele arising anywhere will spread everywhere, and the surveillance question becomes one of timing rather than of geography.

The two scenarios demand different control responses and are distinguishable from genomic data. The present analysis addresses the prior question that both depend on: how much spatial structure is there in Ghanaian P. falciparum, and is it enough to support zone-level inference at all?

[Interpreted] A refinement of the framing. An earlier version of this report ran the argument directly from the amount of neutral structure to a conclusion about surveillance design - that if the parasites form one population, spatially sparse sentinel sampling is representative. That step does not hold, and the project's own literature review argues against it: selection builds connectivity and locus-specific allele trajectories that neutral demography does not predict, so a resistance allele can have a site-specific frequency even under neutral panmixia. Neutral differentiation and marker-frequency representativeness are two distinct quantities and have to be measured separately. This analysis therefore does both - genome-wide differentiation at common variants, and direct resistance-marker frequencies by zone - and reports them as two findings rather than deriving the second from the first.

Scope. The thesis proposal and literature review (supplied separately as "genomic analysis of Consistent signatures of selection.docx", 145 paragraphs, approximately 11,400 words) set out the biological rationale, the resistance-marker panel and the published context. This report does not restate that material. It documents what was computed, on what data, with which decisions, and what the output actually shows, following a full re-run of stages 4-9 in which seven defects were fixed: six identified by adversarial audit, and a seventh found by the re-run itself. The seventh is the more instructive one and is claimed here rather than buried. The "skip if output exists" guards in step05b, step07b and step08b did not check whether their inputs had changed, so when the SNP set went from 3,359 to 3,916 both ADMIXTURE and TreeMix silently re-reported the previous day's results as "stage finished". Only step08c caught it, because it asserts that its position table and the BayPass output have matching row counts and so failed loudly with the right diagnosis; ADMIXTURE and TreeMix have no such assertion. The guards now require each output to be newer than its input. The audit did not raise this defect.

[Interpreted] A later round, and a fourth consecutive correction containing its own defect. After that re-run, an external critique raised five points. All five were upheld and four were defects in the analysis code rather than in the prose. They were confined to the resistance panel, which has since been replaced in its entirety by scripts/step12_codon_panel.py; the population-structure results in Sections 6 and 7 are unaffected. The pattern across all four rounds is worth stating plainly, because it bears on how much weight these numbers can carry: each layer was checked for internal consistency against our own outputs, and what escaped was external correctness - whether the marker list matched an authoritative source, whether the annotation direction matched the reference genome, whether a co-occurrence claim had co-occurrence data behind it. The verification architecture was built to catch stale numbers and it did. It was not built to ask whether the list was the right list.

[Interpreted] A fifth round, and the loss of a result. Four outstanding robustness checks were run at final revision, and they are the reason this version grades one finding lower than the last. Three of the four left the conclusions standing or strengthened them: the maximum pairwise FST varies only between 0.00204 and 0.00255 across six threshold scenarios spanning 1,900 to 4,403 isolates and 3,275 to 10,876 SNPs, so near-panmixia does not depend on the Fws or MAF cuts; the 19 XtX outliers are better-supported calls than the rest of the scanned panel rather than artefacts, although two adjacent chromosome-11 sites are downgraded within that set; and re-running the ordinations on the 1,900-isolate analysis set, so that they describe the same isolates as everything else, changes nothing material. The fourth check overturned a conclusion. The region-level isolation-by-distance result - the only positive spatial finding in this report - is significant in 2 of those 6 threshold scenarios and in 4 of 7 leave-one-region-out replicates, collapsing when Upper East is dropped, and is therefore graded not robust wherever it appears below. It is not deleted: it holds at the published thresholds, and it is reported together with the scenarios in which it fails. But it can no longer be described as supported, moderately supported, or a weak but real distance effect, and the preceding version's listing of it among the strengthened results is withdrawn. The remaining check, the provenance of the agro-ecological zone layer, cannot be closed analytically at all and stays open.

[Observed] Two incidents during the final runs, recorded because the verification argument depends on them. First, results/baypass/xtx_all_snps.csv was found at 0 bytes, truncated at 08:29:53 - hours after the 01:42 run that produced it, so almost certainly a stray shell redirect where a read was intended during a verification pass. It was the only zero-byte file under results/ or interim/. It was regenerated from the intact BayPass outputs, and the regeneration is consistent with the surviving outputs rather than proven row-for-row: outlier_snps.bed, xtx_calibration.json and xtx_threshold.txt are byte-identical by md5 before and after, and the restored file carries the same 3,916 rows and the same 19 outliers. Second, the --suffix patch to step03_umap.py suffixed the file writes but not the print statements, so the run reported writing to the published dimred_comparison.csv when it had in fact correctly written dimred_comparison_qcset.csv. No data were at risk - the published file's mtime and contents were both checked - but a log that misreports its own output path is exactly what produces a later misattribution, and it was caught by inspecting the output directory rather than by reading the log. Both are fixed.

1.2 Objectives addressed by the present analysis

The thesis defines three specific objectives. The work documented here engages all three. Objectives 1 and 3 are now answered within the limits the design permits; objective 2 is answerable only in a weakened form, because a barrier test requires resolution this design does not have, and the one distance-related signal it did produce has not survived sensitivity analysis. Their status is as follows.

Table S1. Status of the three thesis objectives after the corrected re-run, the rebuild of the resistance panel and the final four robustness checks. Objective 2's grade is lowered here: the region-level distance effect reported in earlier versions is significant in only 2 of 6 threshold scenarios and 4 of 7 jackknife replicates and is now graded not robust. Objective 1's grade is raised, because the threshold sensitivity analysis shows the low-differentiation result to be insensitive to both cuts that define the dataset. Objective 3 was previously recorded as preliminary with a null result, then answered on a panel that was itself defective; both episodes are set out below.

[Observed] Why objective 3's status changed, the first time. The first version of this report recorded objective 3 as preliminary, with no validated resistance locus recovered, and interpreted that absence biologically. The absence was an artefact of data ingest. The genome-wide callset was built under FILTER_PASS & is_snp & numalt == 1, and in Pf8 most canonical resistance codons are multiallelic or carry a spanning-deletion ALT allele, so the mask discarded most of the panel before any analysis ran: across the nine panel genes the filter keeps 3,330 of 13,367 PASS records, and of the 21 canonical codons that were polymorphic in this sample only 6 passed the mask, of which only 3 then cleared the MAF >= 0.05 cut and reached the scan matrix. Among the codons that never reached it are the whole polymorphic CVIET set in pfcrt (74, 75, 76, 220, 271, 371), pfmdr1 N86Y and Y184F, pfdhfr S108N, and pfdhps S436A, K540E and A581G. Those markers were never tested, so the null was not a result.

[Observed] Why it changed again, and this is the more serious error. The panel that replaced the masked callset found codons by matching SnpEff's ANN_HGVS_p protein-change strings against a hand-written list. That method has two failure modes and both occurred in this dataset. (i) A resistance allele that is the reference residue can never appear as an ALT change: at pfdhps 437 the 3D7 sequence translates as G, which the Pf8 methodology states is the mutant allele, so the only annotation present is the revertant Gly437Ala, a search for "Ala437Gly" matched nothing, and A437G was missing from the panel entirely at a true frequency of 0.897. (ii) A residue reachable only by two changes within one codon is never annotated: pfcrt 75 carries 403621 (A>G, Asn75Asp) and 403623 (T>A, Asn75Lys), Asn to Glu needs both, and so no annotation names N75E - the residue the CVIET haplotype actually carries. That, and not a "naming discrepancy", is the entire explanation of the apparent disagreement with MalariaGEN reported in the previous version, and the error was ours. The panel is now built by reconstructing each codon from the CDS and the reference genome and reporting the full residue spectrum, reference residue included, so that neither failure mode is possible.

[Observed] What else the rebuild changed. The pfdhfr "triple mutant" was previously reported as the range of its three marginal frequencies, 0.786-0.908, which is not a haplotype frequency at all; counted on isolates called at all three codons it is 0.771 (n = 1,876), below the lowest of its components on the same complete-case set (51I = 0.788) as a conjunction must be, with NRN at 0.116 and wild-type NCS at 0.091. The complete-case 0.788 and the panel-wide marginal 0.786 (n = 1,891) are two different quantities for 51I, and the lower bound of the superseded range is the latter. The pfk13 list was written from memory: it contained 13 distinct protein changes inflated to an apparent 14 by a duplicated M476I record, included P441L and C469F as validated when WHO classes them as candidate, and omitted P574L, G533S and R622I - R622I having been requested but never retrieved, so that it became a silent non-result rather than a reported one. And the validation argument was too weak for the claim it supported: agreement of marginal frequencies to within 0.03 does not establish that the two pipelines call the same isolates the same way. Per-sample comparison now does, at 47,012 of 47,012 calls across 25 markers.

[Observed] A corroboration that must be stated more weakly. MalariaGEN's own published kelch13 status is consistent with the pfk13 negative, but it cannot carry the weight previously placed on it. Pf8 assigns that status over the whole 349-726 domain and classifies any missing call in the domain as undetermined, so only 36 isolates have a complete status. That is weak corroboration for per-codon calls on roughly 1,860 isolates, and the two should not be presented as equivalent evidence. Within those 36, the commonest change is Ala578Ser, which is explicitly not a resistance marker.

[Observed] A further consequence, which Section 5.6 takes up, is that the original candidate panel was itself too narrow. pfaat1 is absent from config.KNOWN_RESISTANCE_LOCI, the eight-locus list the original analysis flagged outliers against, which is why the one of the three surviving resistance variants that reached the outlier table surfaced there as a transporter hit rather than as a known resistance locus. The outlier table does name it - PF3D7_0629500, AAT1, amino acid transporter AAT1, 2, 24.198 - so the failure was one of classification, not of annotation; it simply was not on the list of loci being watched. That eight-locus list is a different eight from the original panel's eight genes in which canonical codons were observed, and the two should not be confused; the rebuilt panel spans nine genes.

[Observed] The rebuilt panel converts objective 3 from a null into a measurement with three positive findings and one informative negative: significant between-zone heterogeneity at pfdhfr, at pfdhps (437G, p = 1.2 x 10 to the -6) and at pfaat1 S258L, and a tested negative covering all 14 WHO-validated plus all 6 candidate pfk13 markers. On S258L, what Amambua-Ngwa et al. (2023) demonstrated experimentally is narrower than this report previously implied: a 1.5-fold rise in chloroquine IC50 in a single chloroquine-resistant Southeast Asian line (NHP4026), with epistasis between S258L and F313S and an explicit caveat that the effect depends on pfcrt background. The variant therefore potentiates chloroquine resistance, modestly and conditionally. The 0% to 97% longitudinal rise reported in that paper is Gambian (1984-2014); the paper says nothing about Ghana. Describing S258L as "the functionally validated chloroquine-resistance variant" overstated all three of magnitude, generality and geography. It should also not be called a WHO-classified marker: pfaat1 appears nowhere in the WHO compendium at any tier, as is also true of pfap2mu and pfmdr2, while pfcoronin appears only at the potential tier. The compendium classifies variants rather than genes, and it contains no validated or candidate lumefantrine marker at all.

[Hypothesized] Which drug maintains the pfaat1 cline is a separate inferential step, and the previous version took it wrongly. It asserted that the functional experiment did not test amodiaquine and then framed the Ghanaian cline as amodiaquine-maintained. Amodiaquine was tested - IC50 values for chloroquine, amodiaquine, lumefantrine, mefloquine and quinine were all measured - and the shifts were reported as well below the threshold associated with clinical resistance; the authors explicitly reject the hypothesis that other quinoline partner drugs drive these frequencies, and conclude that chloroquine was the primary selective force. Three candidate explanations for a persistent intermediate cline in Ghana therefore stand together and none is tested here: historical chloroquine pressure persisting after the 2005 switch to artesunate-amodiaquine, the documented fitness cost of S258L holding the allele short of fixation, and - as an untested possibility only - contemporary 4-aminoquinoline pressure from amodiaquine. These data cannot discriminate between them: drug-use intensity by zone is not available in the Pf8 metadata, and the cline is confounded with administrative region, collection period and sequencing study in the same way as every other zone-level result here.

1.2.1 What the analysis can and cannot separate

[Interpreted] Four features of the Pf8 Ghana metadata constrain what any zone-level number can mean. They are properties of the sampling frame, not analytical shortcomings that better methods would overcome, and they are stated here rather than deferred to the limitations section because they condition every result in Sections 6 and 7. The region, year and study tabulations in this subsection are computed on the 4,905 release-QC-pass isolates, not on the 1,900 that enter the population analyses, because the sampling frame is a property of the release rather than of this analysis's sample cut.

Administrative region. Three of the four zones are each filled by exactly one region - Guinea savanna by Upper East (3,605 of 3,605 release-QC-pass isolates), coastal savanna by Greater Accra (435 of 435), transitional zone by Brong Ahafo (50 of 50); only deciduous forest draws on more than one (Ashanti, Central, Eastern, Volta). For those three, "zone" and "region" are the same variable, and any difference attributed to ecology could equally be attributed to the region's health system, drug supply, sampling protocol or collection year.

Collection period. Zone-level year distributions differ systematically: Guinea savanna spans 2009-2019 with a 2018 peak, coastal savanna is concentrated in 2016-2018, deciduous forest in 2013-2021, and the transitional zone falls entirely in 2011-2013. Over 2009-2021 Ghana changed first-line therapy and scaled up chemoprevention, so drug-related allele frequencies would be expected to move with time independently of geography. The confounding is not removable by reweighting, because for the transitional zone and the coastal savanna the year ranges do not overlap at all. The same fact makes every resistance frequency in this report a 2009-2021 average rather than a current value, including the pfk13 zeros.

Sequencing study (new in this version). Pf8 isolates arrive through named partner studies, and in Ghana those studies are almost perfectly nested within zones. Four of the six zone pairs share no study whatsoever - coastal savanna versus Guinea savanna, coastal savanna versus transitional, deciduous forest versus Guinea savanna, and deciduous forest versus transitional - and the first of these is the pair behind the maximum FST and the polyclonality contrast. Each zone is dominated by one study: the transitional zone is 100% one study (1083-PF-GH-CONWAY, 50 of 50), coastal savanna 89% (387 of 435, 1241-PF-GH-ANINAGYEI), Guinea savanna 74% (2,678 of 3,605, 1168-PF-GH-AMENGA-ETEGO-SM) and deciduous forest 48% (391 of 815, 1200-PF-GH-MAIGA-SM). Study-associated heterogeneity in this dataset is demonstrable, not hypothetical: holding zone constant, so that study varies together with collection year, finer geography and recruitment, call rate differs between the six studies within Guinea savanna at p = 2.8 x 10 to the -197. Deciduous forest draws on five studies in all; across the four of them large enough to test (811 of the zone's 815 isolates, the fifth contributing only 4) call rate differs at p = 7.7 x 10 to the -88, and the polyclonal fraction ranges from 0.430 to 0.578 (Kruskal-Wallis p = 0.0019).

Spatial resolution. Isolates are geolocated only to administrative level 1, so 1,900 isolates carry seven distinct coordinates between them. No sub-regional analysis is possible, and isolation by distance rests on 21 pairs derived from 7 points - a test fragile enough that, as the threshold sensitivity analysis shows, it changes verdict when either of the two dataset-defining cuts is moved.



Figure S1. Sample locations on the ghanaAEZ.geojson agro-ecological zone layer. Every isolate resolves to one of seven administrative-level-1 coordinates (black points), which is the entire spatial resolution available to this analysis. Four of the layer's zones receive isolates; Sudan savanna, wet evergreen and moist evergreen receive none, and the layer also carries a Volta lake waterbody polygon that is not a zone. Note that the transitional zone (grey) lies between the forest zones and Guinea savanna, making it the second-most-northern of the four sampled zones - the reason a simple south-to-north ordering of zone means is not available. The legend reproduces the layer's own spelling, "Decidous forest". Source: pf8_proof/ghana_samples_aez_map.png.

[Interpreted] Partial mitigation, reported in fairness. Both zone pairs that do share a study reproduce the direction of the overall polyclonality difference; within 1083-PF-GH-CONWAY, Guinea savanna is more polyclonal than the transitional zone (0.532 versus 0.500 - that is 25 of 47 against 25 of 50, computed on the 4,905 release-QC-pass isolates, since on the 1,900-isolate analysis set the polyclonal fraction is identically zero by construction), matching the overall ordering. That margin is three isolates wide in the denominator, the numerator being 25 in both arms, and should not be read as more than a direction. The zone ordering of polyclonality is therefore not purely a batch artefact. It is simply not separable from batch for the pairs that carry the largest differences.

Caveat on the zone layer itself. The agro-ecological zone assignment depends on data/ghanaAEZ.geojson, whose provenance could not be established. It is byte-for-byte identical (md5 96b1a7f4da62d6f0153826f41af96152) to an ArcGIS Online item uploaded by an anonymous personal account with null licence and null source information, so that item is a re-host and not an origin. The layer is a hybrid of two classification schemes - MoFA/FAO savanna, transition and coastal names combined with a Hall and Swaine style wet-evergreen / moist-evergreen forest split - and no published Ghana zonation matches it. It must not be attributed to any named institution. On spelling, one rule is used throughout this report: the layer's string "Decidous forest" is retained wherever it is a join key or appears inside a quoted output, file path or figure legend, and running prose and table labels use the correct spelling "deciduous forest". The spatial join keys on the layer value, so the data are never silently rewritten. This is a publication blocker, documented in Section 9.3. It remains open at final revision and cannot be closed analytically: the ArcInfo-coverage fingerprint dates the original to a pre-2005 workflow and CERSGIS (University of Ghana) is the most likely custodian, so closing it requires correspondence with cersgis@ug.edu.gh. The interim citation and the md5 stand in the meantime.

1.3 Research questions as operationalised

The thesis questions were translated into the following testable forms. The translation is itself an analytical decision and is revisited in Section 5.

Is between-zone differentiation distinguishable from zero? Operationalised as pairwise Hudson FST with block-jackknife confidence intervals, and - new in this version - recomputed under six scenarios that vary the Fws and MAF thresholds, so that the magnitude is reported with its sensitivity to the cuts that define the dataset rather than at one setting only.

Does genetic distance increase with geographic distance? Operationalised as a Mantel test of FST/(1 - FST) against log geographic distance, run at both zone and region level, with the permutation test seeded, the censoring of negative FST pairs disclosed and reported both ways, a leave-one-population-out jackknife to test whether any single population carries the result, and - new in this version - recomputation of the region-level test under all six threshold scenarios, since a permutation test on 21 pairs from 7 points can depend heavily on the matrix it is given. This question is the one whose answer changed at final revision.

Do individual isolates assign to zone-associated ancestry components? Operationalised as ADMIXTURE at K = 1-8 with 10-fold cross-validation, visual inspection of three ordinations - now computed on the 1,900-isolate analysis set as well as on the 4,905 pre-QC isolates the published figures used, by re-slicing the distance submatrix - and an explicit Kruskal-Wallis test of the minor-component proportion against zone, so that the magnitude and the significance of any zone effect are reported separately.

Is there evidence of gene flow beyond a simple bifurcating population tree? Operationalised as TreeMix with 0-5 migration edges, run with jackknife standard errors so that each edge carries a p-value and the number of edges is informed by a test rather than only by the shape of the likelihood curve.

Which loci are more differentiated than neutral demography predicts? Operationalised as a BayPass XtX scan with a threshold calibrated on pseudo-observed data simulated under the estimated population covariance matrix, reported alongside the expected number of false positives at that threshold, the implied expected-null-exceedance ratio, per-SNP Benjamini-Hochberg q-values, and - new in this version - a per-SNP call-quality audit of every outlier against the rest of the scanned panel, so that a high XtX value is reported with the quality evidence for or against the call behind it.

New, and now rebuilt. What are the zone-level frequencies of the canonical antimalarial resistance residues, and of the haplotypes they form within isolates? Operationalised not by annotation matching but by codon reconstruction: each of 57 codons in nine genes is mapped to its three genomic positions through the CDS, every variant record at those positions is collected regardless of numalt or is_snp, each isolate's alleles are applied to the reference codon and the codon is translated, so that the full residue spectrum including the reference residue is reported with Wilson confidence intervals and a four-zone chi-square test. A codon with a mixed call is uncalled rather than assigned a residue. Multi-codon haplotypes (pfdhfr 51/59/108 and 164, pfcrt 72-76, pfdhps 437/540) are counted on complete cases only, so that a conjunction can never exceed its weakest component. The pfk13 marker list is taken from WHO compendium v1.0 (December 2025) rather than written by hand, covering in full all 14 validated and all 6 candidate markers with the two tiers kept distinct; those 20 markers occupy 19 distinct codons, because C469 carries both the validated C469Y and the candidate C469F, and the panel genotypes 23 codons in all - the four additional ones (449, 481, 515 and 527) being codons the compendium places in neither tier, so no WHO classification is claimed for them. Validation is call-by-call against MalariaGEN's published genotypes rather than by comparison of marginal frequencies. This question was present in the thesis objectives but, as Section 1.2 explains, was first not tested at all and then tested with a defective panel.

[Interpreted] Questions 1, 3 and 4 are answerable at the resolution these data permit and are answered in Section 6. Question 1 is answered robustly: the low-differentiation reading survives both threshold relaxations, with max FST between 0.00204 and 0.00255 across the six scenarios. Question 2 is answered negatively. The distance effect it tests is significant at the published thresholds but in only 2 of 6 threshold scenarios and 4 of 7 leave-one-region-out replicates, so the honest answer is that these data do not establish isolation by distance; whether that is a statement about Ghanaian parasite demography or about the fragility of a Mantel test on 21 pairs from 7 coordinates cannot be settled here, and both readings are consistent with everything else in the report. Question 5 is answerable only in the weak sense of producing a ranked candidate list: with four populations and 3,916 SNPs the scan has limited power, 3.9 of the 19 SNPs above the quantile threshold are expected to be false positives, only 13 survive FDR 10%, and two - including the fifth-ranked SNP overall - carry ExcessHet and InbreedingCoeff signatures that put them at lower confidence. Question 6 is answered directly, by genotyping rather than by scan, which is why a reference-residue frequency at or near 1.000 at a pfk13 codon is now informative where a non-significant XtX value would not have been - subject to two limits that must travel with the number. It is fixation among called genotypes at twenty-two of the 23 codons and 0.9995 at codon 458, on 1,845 to 1,876 isolates per codon rather than all 1,900, and it describes isolates collected between 2009 and 2021. The corroborating MalariaGEN kelch13 status exists for only 36 of those isolates, because Pf8 treats any missing call across amino acids 349-726 as undetermined, so it is supporting rather than independent evidence.



2. Data and materials

2.1 Source and provenance

[Observed] All genotype and metadata inputs derive from the MalariaGEN Plasmodium falciparum Community Project release 8 (Pf8), served publicly over HTTPS from https://pf8-release.cog.sanger.ac.uk. The release comprises 33,325 samples and 12,493,205 variant sites called against the 3D7 reference assembly (contigs Pf3D7_01_v3 … Pf3D7_14_v3, plus apicoplast and mitochondrial contigs, which this analysis excludes).

Four Pf8 artefacts were used:

Sample manifest — data/Pf8_samples.txt, 33,325 rows and 17 columns, giving country, administrative level 1, latitude and longitude at that level, collection year, the release's own QC-pass flag, and the named partner study through which each isolate entered the release (see §2.6).

Variant call data — the Pf8 Zarr store at /zarr, holding GT, AD, DP and GQ. The per-site annotations actually retrieved into the local store are CHROM, POS, REF, ALT, numalt, is_snp and FILTER_PASS.

Mean genotype distance matrix — data/Pf8_mean_genotype_distance.npy, a 33,325 × 33,325 single-precision matrix published with the release (4.44 GB).

Reference annotation — data/PlasmoDB-71_Pfalciparum3D7.gff, used for Step 9. Release 68 is also present in data/ but config.py points at release 71.

Correction to the original field list. An earlier draft of this section listed RegionType among the retrieved annotations, and §3.5 appealed to a direct tabulation of it. RegionType was never fetched: it is absent from step03a_fetch_callset_zarr.py's VARIANT_FIELDS and from the local store, and no log records such a tabulation. The core-genome restriction is nonetheless secure, but it holds by construction rather than by observation — Pf8's FILTER vocabulary itself contains the region categories, so a non-core site cannot carry FILTER_PASS. §3.5 now makes that structural argument instead.

[Interpreted] The numalt field deserves advance notice here, because its use in the ingest filter turned out to be the single most consequential data-handling decision in the analysis. Restricting the callset to numalt == 1 excluded most canonical antimalarial-resistance codons — 28 of the 44 codon-allele records genotyped, among them pfcrt K76T, pfmdr1 N86Y, pfdhps K540E and 8 of the 14 validated pfk13 codons; of the 21 records that are polymorphic in this sample, only 6 survived the filter before any analysis ran; the recovery of those sites is reported in §3.10 and §6.9. Readers of this section should not assume that the 3,916-SNP analysis matrix described in §4 is a representative sample of functionally interesting variation — it was, by construction, a sample of biallelic variation only.

2.1.1 The agro-ecological zone layer

[Observed] A Ghana agro-ecological zone polygon layer (data/ghanaAEZ.geojson) was supplied with the project. It carries eight polygons — Coastal savanna, Wet evergreen, Moist evergreen, "Decidous forest", Transitional zone, Guinea savanna, Sudan savanna, and a polygon named "Waterbody - Volta lake" — and exactly four attribute fields: id, Name, AREA and PERIMETER. It contains no author, source, date, licence or lineage field of any kind.

[Observed] The file was tracked to its acquisition point. It is 285,669 bytes, md5 96b1a7f4da62d6f0153826f41af96152, and is byte-for-byte identical to ArcGIS Online item 0776dae5c5314102bd03fdaab8df0356, "Agro Ecological Zones Ghana" — the md5 matches on both sides, so the identification is certain rather than inferred. Queried through the ArcGIS REST API, that item's licenseInfo and accessInformation fields are both null, and its owner is an anonymous personal account with an empty display name, no stated organisation, and a creation date two days before the upload.

md5 data/ghanaAEZ.geojson  # 96b1a7f4da62d6f0153826f41af96152

[Interpreted] The internal evidence points to a pre-GeoJSON lineage, and the ArcGIS item is an unattributed re-host rather than an origin. The file was written by GDAL/OGR (legacy crs member declaring urn:ogc:def:crs:OGC:1.3:CRS84, clockwise rings, 15-digit coordinates, layer name ghanaAEZ), and AREA/PERIMETER as an uppercase pair are the auto-maintained attributes of an ESRI ArcInfo coverage. The implied chain is coverage → shapefile → GeoJSON. The geometry is a clean topological partition of Ghana: AREA totals 23,871,848 ha against Ghana's official total area of 23,853,300 ha (238,533 km², land plus inland water), a +0.08% discrepancy. The comparison is total-to-total rather than land-to-land, because the layer's sum includes the 587,310 ha Volta Lake polygon. The layer is, in other words, a competently made national dataset whose authorship has been stripped somewhere along that chain.

[Observed] On the classification itself, what the layer contains can be stated plainly. Ghana's canonical MoFA/FAO agro-ecological classification is normally given as five or six zones. This layer carries seven terrestrial polygons plus a water body. It uses the MoFA savanna, transition and coastal names; it splits the high forest into "Wet evergreen" and "Moist evergreen", which are forest vegetation types from the Hall & Swaine lineage; it labels the semi-deciduous zone "Decidous forest", a label the FAO/MoA vegetation table does not use; and its eighth polygon is "Waterbody - Volta lake".

[Interpreted] Read against those sources, the scheme is best understood as a hybrid of two classifications rather than a reproduction of either — MoFA/FAO zone names for the savanna, transition and coastal belts, Hall & Swaine forest vegetation types for the evergreen split — with a water body included that is not an agro-ecological zone at all. Nine candidate sources were searched directly and none holds a matching scheme or a matching layer: HDX, data.gov.gh, GADM, Harvard Dataverse, Zenodo, figshare, FAO GAEZ, FAO Africover and ICPAC. "No published scheme matches it" is therefore an inference from a finite negative search, not an observation; a source not on that list could still hold the original.

Provenance gap. The original author, vintage and licence of ghanaAEZ.geojson could not be established, and on present evidence cannot be. [Unknown] The layer must not be attributed to Wageningen University or to Climate Adaptation Services: those names attach to other items in the same ArcGIS Online account and to a storymap theme, not to this file, and naming either as its author would be a fabricated citation. Two leads remain open. [Proposed] First, whoever supplied the original Colab notebook should be asked for it; its !wget cells are the most likely surviving record of a fetch URL. Second, the ArcInfo-coverage fingerprint is consistent with a pre-2005 national coverage, which makes the Centre for Remote Sensing and GIS at the University of Ghana (cersgis@ug.edu.gh) the most plausible custodian; that enquiry has not yet been made.

[Proposed] Pending that, the layer is cited as accessed rather than as authoritative, and not as a published dataset: Agro ecological zones Ghana [Data set]. (2025). ArcGIS Online. https://www.arcgis.com/home/item.html?id=0776dae5c5314102bd03fdaab8df0356 — flagged as an unattributed re-host and identified by md5 96b1a7f4da62d6f0153826f41af96152. The classification is cited separately and generically: MoFA/FAO for the savanna, transitional and coastal names, and the Hall & Swaine/FAO forest vegetation literature for the evergreen split, with a note that the scheme as implemented is a hybrid of the two.

Spelling of the zone names. One rule applies throughout this work. The layer's own string, including the "Decidous forest" misspelling, is retained wherever it is a join key, a stored data value, or part of a quoted field value, output path or file name — so interim/analysis_samples.csv, results/fst_pairwise.csv, results/batch_study_by_zone.csv and the fact sheet all read "Decidous forest". The spatial join keys on the layer value, so it is never silently rewritten in the data. Running prose and table row and column labels use the correct spelling, "Deciduous forest". Where a block enumerates the layer's Name values as data — the area list in §2.4 — the layer spelling appears in quotation marks. No table mixes the two spellings within itself.

[Proposed] The provenance gap is a publication blocker for the layer as a cited dataset, but it is a weaker threat to the results than it first appears, and it is convertible into a sensitivity result. Because isolates join to zones on administrative-level-1 centroids — seven points in total (§2.3) — the join is coarse enough that it is unlikely to be sensitive to polygon detail. Repeating it against an authoritative six-zone Ghana layer and checking whether the seven centroids receive the same zone labels would settle the question. That check has not yet been run.

2.2 Units of analysis and observation period

[Observed] The unit of analysis is the parasite isolate (one blood sample from one infected individual). Ghana contributes 6,653 isolates to Pf8; 4,905 carry the release QC-pass flag, and 1,900 survive the pipeline's own filtering to enter the analysis set. Collection years span 2009 to 2021.

[Observed] Table S2 gives the regional composition of the Ghanaian subset at three stages. The pronounced imbalance — Upper East supplies 73.5% of the QC-passing isolates and 70.2% of the final analysis set — is a property of the Pf8 sampling frame, not of any filter applied here.

Table S2. Ghanaian isolates by administrative region at three stages of the pipeline. Counts are from the Pf8 manifest, interim/ghana_samples.csv and interim/analysis_samples.csv respectively. The final-set column is from the corrected re-run of 2026-10-10; the isolate set is unchanged from the original run — only the SNP count changed (3,359 to 3,916).

2.3 Spatial resolution: the binding constraint

[Observed] Pf8 geolocates isolates to administrative level 1. Every isolate from a given region therefore carries the identical coordinate pair, and the 4,905 QC-passing Ghanaian isolates occupy exactly seven distinct points. Step 2 prints this explicitly: "4,905 samples occupy 7 distinct coordinate pairs."

[Interpreted] This is the single most consequential property of the dataset for the thesis questions. Individual-level geographic distance does not exist in these data, so no analysis can resolve structure finer than the region. More seriously, as Table S3 shows, three of the four zones are each filled by exactly one region, which makes zone and region statistically indistinguishable for those three. A zone effect and a region effect cannot be separated.

Table S3. Zone composition of the final analysis set (n = 1,900). Only the deciduous forest zone aggregates more than one region; the other three are single-region zones, so for them "zone" and "region" are the same variable under two names.

2.4 Zones represented and zones absent

[Observed] The thesis frames Ghana as five ecological zones. The Pf8 Ghanaian sample, joined to the supplied layer, occupies four: Guinea savanna, deciduous forest, coastal savanna and transitional zone. No isolate falls in the Sudan savanna, and none in the moist-evergreen or wet-evergreen forest zones present in the layer. Step 2 reported zero unmatched samples and zero duplicate matches, so the absence is one of sampling, not of joining.

[Observed] The layer's own AREA field gives the size of each polygon. Of the 23,871,848 ha the layer assigns to Ghana, the four sampled zones account for 90.0%, and the four unsampled polygons are all small. Names below are the layer's Name strings and areas its AREA values:

"Guinea savanna" 10,835,471 ha (45.4%) — sampled

"Decidous forest" 6,675,558 ha (28.0%) — sampled

"Transitional zone" 2,936,745 ha (12.3%) — sampled

"Coastal savanna" 1,033,380 ha (4.3%) — sampled

"Moist evergreen" 769,514 ha (3.2%) — not sampled

"Wet evergreen" 740,806 ha (3.1%) — not sampled

"Waterbody - Volta lake" 587,310 ha (2.5%) — not a zone

"Sudan savanna" 293,063 ha (1.2%) — not sampled

[Interpreted] On these figures the AREA field explains most of the absence better than an appeal to sampling priorities does. The Sudan savanna is the smallest terrestrial polygon in the layer: 293,063 ha, 1.2% of the total, a sliver in the far north-east. An earlier draft of this section treated its absence as a substantive gap in the ecological gradient, as though the sampling frame had overlooked it. The better explanation is that it is a very small zone which Pf8's administrative-level-1 sampling frame was never likely to capture: with one centroid per region and no region lying wholly inside it, there is no coordinate at which a Sudan savanna isolate could have been recorded. The same reasoning applies to the two evergreen forest polygons, which are jointly 6.3% of the layer.

[Interpreted] The consequence for the thesis framing is real but narrower than previously stated. What is missing is not a sampled-but-overlooked zone; it is the far-northern extreme of the transmission gradient, and it is missing because the geolocation resolution cannot represent zones smaller than a region. The Upper East centroid lies close to the Sudan savanna boundary (Figure S2), so the Guinea savanna sample may well include parasites from populations straddling that transition — which would attenuate rather than inflate any north–south contrast. This cannot be checked at administrative-level-1 resolution.

Status of this re-reading. The reversal of the original §2.4 reading is not one of the audit's confirmed defects; it follows from a bonus finding in the corrections document (§F) and is argued here on the layer's own area figures. It should be defended on those figures in the response to reviewers rather than cited to a defect number.



Figure S2. Sampling locations on the Ghana agro-ecological zone layer. Each black point is one administrative-level-1 centroid, not an individual isolate; the seven points carry 4,905 QC-passing isolates between them. The Sudan savanna polygon at the north-eastern tip is 1.2% of the layer's total area. Generated by scripts/step02_assign_aez.py as figures/ghana_samples_aez_map.png.

2.5 Temporal composition

[Observed] Collection year is strongly unbalanced across zones (Table S4). Median collection year ranges from 2012 (transitional zone) to 2018 (coastal savanna). The transitional zone contributes no isolate after 2013; the coastal savanna contributes none before 2016.

Table S4. Collection year by zone in the final analysis set (n = 1,900). Computed from interim/analysis_samples.csv. Column totals match the zone counts in Table S3.

[Interpreted] Any between-zone difference observed in this dataset is also a between-period difference. Ghana changed first-line therapy and scaled up chemoprevention over this window, so allele frequencies at drug-related loci would be expected to move with time independently of geography. This confounding is not removable by reweighting, because for the transitional zone and the coastal savanna the year ranges do not overlap at all. It bears directly on the marker results in §6.9: the pfaat1 S258L frequencies and the pfdhfr triple-mutant frequencies both differ significantly between zones whose sampling windows differ by three to six years, and neither difference can be attributed to ecology rather than to date on these data alone. The pfdhfr pattern is in any case not a simple south-to-north gradient. Ordered south to north, the four sampled zones run coastal savanna, deciduous forest, transitional zone, Guinea savanna, so the transitional zone is the second-most-northern of the four; it carries the highest frequency of all four zones at two of the three triple-mutant codons, and it is also the earliest-sampled (median 2012) and by far the smallest (n = 25). Its position in any ordering is as much a statement about date and sample size as about ecology. pfaat1 S258L, by contrast, does fall monotonically along that same south-to-north ordering (§6.9).

2.6 Sequencing study as a fourth design confounder

[Observed] The design limitations listed in §9.2 of the original draft named three confounders of the zone comparison: zone–region collinearity, administrative-level-1 spatial resolution, and collection year. There is a fourth, which that draft did not mention at all. Pf8 isolates arrive through named partner sequencing studies, and in Ghana those studies are almost perfectly nested within zones.

[Interpreted] Study is therefore a third name for the same variable that "zone" and "region" already share, and unlike the other two it carries its own technical variance with it.

Which isolates these counts describe. The study-composition table and the within-zone batch tests in this section are computed on the 4,905 QC-passing isolates — the set on which study membership and Fws are defined — and not on the 1,900-isolate analysis set used for the population analyses in §§5–8. The two denominators must not be read against each other: "1083-PF-GH-CONWAY is 100% of the transitional zone, 50 of 50" is a statement about the 4,905 set, in which the transitional zone holds 50 isolates, not about the n = 25 transitional zone of Table S3. Study nesting within zone is a property of the Pf8 sampling frame and is not created by the later cuts, but the counts in this section are not the analysis-set counts. §4.2.2, §6.2, §7.2, §7.3 and §10.1 cite these figures and refer back here for the denominator.

[Calculated] The composition was also recomputed on the 1,900-isolate analysis set, to check that the later cuts do not change the picture. They do not. The same eleven studies are present; the same three studies span two zones each; the same four of the six zone pairs share no study; and dominance barely moves — transitional zone 100% (25 of 25), coastal savanna 86.8% (177 of 204), Guinea savanna 73.8% (984 of 1,334), deciduous forest 46.6% (157 of 337). The deciduous forest again draws on five studies, the smallest of them holding three isolates. Computed from interim/analysis_samples.csv. The within-zone Kruskal–Wallis tests were not re-run on the 1,900 set, so every test statistic in Table S7 remains a 4,905-set figure.

[Observed] Eleven studies contribute the 4,905 QC-passing Ghanaian isolates. Table S5 gives the full cross-tabulation. Three of the eleven span more than one zone — 1241-PF-GH-ANINAGYEI and 1183-PF-GH-AWANDARE-SM across the coastal savanna and the deciduous forest, and 1083-PF-GH-CONWAY across the Guinea savanna and the transitional zone — and the remaining eight are confined to a single zone each. Every zone is dominated by one study: the transitional zone is 100% one study (1083-PF-GH-CONWAY, 50 of 50), the coastal savanna 89.0% (1241-PF-GH-ANINAGYEI, 387 of 435), the Guinea savanna 74.3% (1168-PF-GH-AMENGA-ETEGO-SM, 2,678 of 3,605), and the deciduous forest — the only zone with no majority study, and the only one drawing on five contributing studies — 48.0% (1200-PF-GH-MAIGA-SM, 391 of 815).

Table S5. Sequencing study by agro-ecological zone, QC-passing Ghanaian isolates (n = 4,905). From results/batch_study_by_zone.csv, written by scripts/step11_batch_confounding.py. Three of the eleven studies span more than one zone; the deciduous forest draws on five studies, the Guinea savanna on six, the coastal savanna on two and the transitional zone on one.

[Observed] The consequence is set out in Table S6: four of the six zone pairs share no sequencing study whatsoever, including the coastal savanna–Guinea savanna pair that carries the largest pairwise FST in the study (0.00232) and the headline polyclonality contrast.

[Interpreted] For those four pairs, zone, region, collection period and sequencing batch all change together, and no arrangement of these data separates them: at zero overlap a study term and a zone term are not jointly identifiable.

Table S6. Sequencing studies shared between each pair of zones, QC-passing isolates (n = 4,905). From results/batch_zone_pair_overlap.csv. Recomputing the same six pairs on the 1,900-isolate analysis set returns identical counts and the same named studies.

[Observed] Study-associated heterogeneity in these data is demonstrable rather than hypothetical. Holding zone constant — which leaves study varying alongside collection year, finer geography, recruitment and biology — per-isolate call rate differs sharply between studies within the same zone (Table S7): within the Guinea savanna, across its six studies, Kruskal–Wallis H = 923.0, p = 2.8 × 10⁻¹⁹⁷, n = 3,605; within the deciduous forest, across the four of its five studies large enough to test, H = 406.7, p = 7.7 × 10⁻⁸⁸, n = 811. Within the deciduous forest those same four studies also differ in Fws (Kruskal–Wallis H = 14.9, p = 1.9 × 10⁻³, n = 811), and their polyclonal fractions span 0.430 to 0.578 — a spread comparable in size to the between-zone polyclonality differences reported in §6.2. The Guinea savanna shows no significant within-zone difference in Fws (H = 9.17, p = 0.103), so its p = 2.8 × 10⁻¹⁹⁷ is a statement about call rate alone and not about polyclonality. The coastal savanna, with two studies, shows no significant within-zone difference on either statistic; the transitional zone cannot be tested at all, because it is one study.

Table S7. Within-zone differences between sequencing studies, QC-passing isolates. Kruskal–Wallis tests on per-isolate call rate and on Fws, computed within each zone so that zone is held constant while study varies together with collection year, finer geography and recruitment; the tests therefore measure study-associated heterogeneity rather than isolating a technical batch cause. From results/batch_within_zone_tests.csv. The deciduous forest test excludes a four-isolate study (1114-PF-PDN-DBS-GH-GHANSAH), hence 4 of 5 studies and n = 811 against 815 in Table S5. The two p-value columns are tests on the call-rate and Fws distributions; no test of the polyclonal fraction itself exists in the outputs, so the last column is a range only and carries no p-value.

[Observed] One partial mitigation is available, and is reported here in fairness to the result. Both zone pairs that do share a study reproduce the overall direction of the polyclonality difference: within 1083-PF-GH-CONWAY alone, Guinea savanna isolates are more polyclonal than transitional-zone isolates (0.532 against 0.500), which matches the ordering seen across the full sample.

[Interpreted] The transmission-intensity gradient is therefore not purely a batch artefact. It is simply not separable from batch for the two pairs that carry the largest differences.

[Interpreted] The practical reading is as follows. Study confounding does not threaten the central finding that the observed differentiation is very low, but neither does that finding bound the true differentiation: batch effects can move an observed between-group contrast in either direction — through cohort composition, calling differences and differential missingness — so a batch-confounded design that still returns FST ≈ 0.002 is reporting a low observed value whose relation to the true value is not established, not an upper bound on it. It does threaten any positive between-zone claim — the maximum-FST pair, the polyclonality gradient, the ADMIXTURE minor-component ordering, and the marker-frequency differences in §6.9 — because for the pairs concerned there is no study-level control to appeal to. Each of those claims should be read as "differs between zones, which in these data means between zone, region, period and sequencing batch jointly".

What would resolve it. Nothing available within Pf8. Separating study from zone requires either isolates from the same study in both the coastal savanna and the Guinea savanna, or a replication cohort sequenced under one protocol across the gradient. Statistical adjustment is not an option: with four of six pairs at zero overlap, a study term and a zone term are not jointly identifiable. This is listed as design limitation 7 in §9.2 and is the one limitation on that list that new sequencing, rather than reanalysis, would have to fix.

2.7 Variable summary

Table S8. Variables used. "Derived" variables are written by the pipeline into interim/ and results/. RegionType, listed in the original draft, was never retrieved and has been removed; see §2.1.



3. Methodology

The analysis is driven by scripts/run_all.sh. It comprises the nine numbered stages of the supplied pipeline, plus Stage 3a, written during this session to replace a failed VCF download, and Stage 3b, the supplied per-isolate quality-control script that run_all.sh labels as part of Stage 3 but that is given its own row in Table S9, and four stages added after review: Stage 10, a multiallelic-aware resistance marker panel; Stage 11, a diagnostic for the confounding of agro-ecological zone with sequencing study; Stage 12, which replaces Stage 10's marker panel entirely after an external critique showed that Stage 10's method of identifying markers — matching SnpEff protein-change strings against a hand-written list — could not see a resistance allele that is the reference residue and could not see a residue requiring two changes in one codon (both failures occurred); and Stage 13, which rebuilds the analysis matrix under six settings of the two thresholds that define the dataset and recomputes pairwise FST and the region-level Mantel test under each. Stage 13 is the stage whose result matters most, and it does not go the manuscript's way: it leaves near-panmixia stronger than before and it removes the support for isolation by distance, which is significant at the published thresholds and at almost none of the others. That is a loss of a result, not a refinement of one, and Section 3.14 states it as such.
