Stage 10 is retained in the repository and in Table S9 for the record, but no number in this manuscript now comes from it; Section 3.10 sets out the replacement method and Section 3.10.1 the reason it was needed. Stages run in order; each writes its outputs to disk so that a failure costs one stage rather than the whole run. That caching is stated here with its qualification rather than as a design virtue, because it was also the pipeline's most dangerous defect: the guards skipped a stage whenever its output file already existed, without checking whether the input had changed, so when the SNP panel moved from 3,359 to 3,916 SNPs, ADMIXTURE and TreeMix silently reported the previous day's results as "stage finished". Only Stage 8 carried an assertion that caught it. The guards now require the output to be newer than its input; Section 3.13 sets out what happened and what was changed. Stages 10 to 13 are read-only with respect to everything upstream: they create new output files and modify none, so no estimate in Sections 6.1 to 6.7 depends on them. Two further checks changed no stage's method but changed what the existing stages describe: Stage 3's three ordinations were re-run on the 1,900-isolate analysis set rather than the 4,905 pre-QC isolates (Section 3.4.1), and the 19 XtX outliers were audited against the Pf8 per-variant quality annotations (Section 3.11.1). This section describes each stage: what it does, why, what it assumes, and how it constrains what follows. Where a method changed in the corrected re-run, or in the rebuild that followed the external critique, or in these final checks, the change and its motivation are stated in place rather than relegated to a revision log.

3.1 Pipeline overview

Table S9. Pipeline stages. Stages 3a and 3b are given their own rows although run_all.sh labels both as "stage 3": 3a was written during this session to replace a failed VCF download, 3b was supplied. Stages 10 and 11 were added after the internal audit; Stage 12 was added after the external critique and replaces Stage 10; Stage 13 was added last, as the threshold robustness check the audit had asked for. Stage 8 is now run with one thread (Section 3.9). Two of the final checks carry no stage number: the re-run of Stage 3 on the analysis set is a change to step03_umap.py and not a new stage (Section 3.4.1), and the quality audit of the 19 XtX outliers writes results/outlier_quality_check.csv from a script the run record does not name (Section 3.11.1). All other scripts were supplied with the project and were modified only as recorded in Section 5 and in this section. Section numbers in this table follow the numbering used here: Section 3.9 covers Stages 5 to 9, Section 3.10 Stage 12 (with Stage 10's failure diagnosed in Section 3.10.1), Section 3.11 the false-discovery accounting applied to Stage 8, Section 3.12 Stage 11 and Section 3.14 Stage 13.

3.2 Stage 1 — manifest filtering

What and why. The manifest is restricted to Ghana and then to isolates flagged QC-pass by the release. Relying on the release's flag rather than re-deriving sample quality from raw data is a deliberate deference to MalariaGEN's own curation, which has access to sequencing-run level information this analysis does not.

Implementation note. The script captures each retained isolate's row index in the full 33,325-sample distance matrix as the column pf8_row. This is the only safe way to slice the Ghanaian block later: the pandas index would shift under any subsequent filter, whereas pf8_row is fixed at the moment of extraction. Stage 3 asserts that the maximum pf8_row does not exceed the matrix dimension, which would catch a Pf7/Pf8 mismatch.

[Observed] 6,653 Ghanaian rows, reduced to 4,905 by the release QC filter (1,748 dropped). pf8_row range 2,301 to 29,498.

3.3 Stage 2 — agro-ecological zone assignment

What and why. Isolate coordinates are joined to the zone polygon layer with GeoPandas, giving each isolate a zone label. Because Pf8 resolves Ghanaian collection locations only to administrative level 1, the coordinates are region centroids, and the join is effectively a lookup table from seven points to four zones. Every number in this manuscript that is stratified by zone inherits that resolution.

[Observed] All 4,905 isolates matched exactly one polygon: zero unmatched, zero rows implicated in duplicate matches. The 4,905 isolates occupy seven distinct coordinate pairs.

[Interpreted] Assumption. That a region's centroid is a reasonable proxy for where its isolates were collected. For compact regions this is tolerable; for regions that straddle a zone boundary it is not. The script prints a region-by-zone cross-tabulation specifically so that boundary-straddling regions can be checked by eye, and flags Brong Ahafo (forest/transition) and Volta (coastal/Guinea savanna) as the two warranting manual review.

[Observed] The Brong Ahafo and Volta assignments have not been manually reviewed against the map.

3.3.1 Provenance of the zone layer

[Observed] The polygon layer used for the join, ghanaAEZ.geojson, has no citable source. This was investigated rather than assumed, and the conclusion is negative: the file carries only the fields id, Name, AREA and PERIMETER, with no attribution, author, date or licence. The uppercase AREA/PERIMETER pair is the auto-maintained attribute signature of an ESRI ArcInfo coverage, so the lineage is coverage to shapefile to GeoJSON, and the file itself was written by GDAL/OGR. The file as used here is byte-for-byte identical to ArcGIS Online item 0776dae5c5314102bd03fdaab8df0356, "Agro Ecological Zones Ghana" — identical md5, 96b1a7f4da62d6f0153826f41af96152 — whose licenseInfo and accessInformation fields are both null and whose owner is an anonymous personal account. That item is therefore an unattributed re-host, not an origin.

[Observed] The classification the layer encodes is a hybrid. It keeps the MoFA/FAO savanna, transitional and coastal names but splits the high forest into "Wet evergreen" and "Moist evergreen", which are vegetation types from the Hall and Swaine lineage, and collapses the semi-deciduous zones into a single "Decidous forest", a label the FAO/MoA vegetation table does not use. It also contains a water body, which is not an agro-ecological zone. HDX, data.gov.gh, GADM, Harvard Dataverse, Zenodo, figshare, FAO GAEZ, FAO Africover and ICPAC were checked directly and none hosts a matching layer. That sweep rules out a matching layer; it does not establish that the label appears in no published scheme anywhere, and no such claim is made.

[Unknown] The original provenance and licence of the layer.

Handling. The layer is cited as accessed rather than as authoritative, identified by its md5 checksum, with the classification cited separately (MoFA/FAO for the savanna and transitional zones, Hall and Swaine/FAO for the evergreen split) and the hybrid character stated. Zone names are reproduced verbatim in tables, figures, output files and every grouping key, including the "Decidous forest" misspelling, because the spatial join, and every downstream grouping, keys on the strings as written; running prose uses the conventional spelling "Deciduous forest" for the same zone. The provenance gap is carried as a limitation in Section 7, and the obvious robustness check — repeating the admin-1 centroid join against an authoritative six-zone layer and testing whether zone assignments are stable — has not been done.

[Proposed] Write to CERSGIS at the University of Ghana (cersgis@ug.edu.gh) to ask whether the layer originates with them and under what licence. The ArcInfo-coverage fingerprint dates the original to a pre-2005 workflow, and CERSGIS is the most likely custodian of a Ghanaian coverage of that vintage. This is the one outstanding check of the four that cannot be closed analytically: no file inspection, checksum or repository sweep can supply an attribution that the file does not carry, so it remains open and is carried as a limitation rather than resolved.



Figure S3. The spatial join. Isolate collection points, resolved to administrative-level-1 centroids, over the agro-ecological zone layer. Seven distinct coordinate pairs carry all 4,905 isolates, which is the resolution limit of every zone-stratified result in this manuscript. The layer's provenance could not be established (Section 3.3.1).

3.4 Stage 3 — three ordinations of the genotype distance matrix

What and why. Pf8 publishes a precomputed mean genotype distance matrix over all 33,325 samples. The Ghanaian block, 4,905 by 4,905, is sliced out by memory-mapping the 4.44 GB file and taking the submatrix, which keeps peak memory in the low hundreds of megabytes. Three dimensionality-reduction methods are then applied to the same matrix so that they can be compared on equal terms.

UMAP (n_neighbors = 30, min_dist = 0.1, metric = precomputed) — preserves local neighbourhood structure with partial global layout. These are the parameter values specified in the thesis workflow.

t-SNE (perplexity = 30, metric = precomputed, init = random) — preserves local neighbourhoods only. Initialisation must be random rather than PCA-based because the input is a distance matrix rather than feature vectors.

PCoA (classical metric multidimensional scaling) — Gower double-centring followed by an eigendecomposition. Unlike the other two it is linear, so axis scaling is interpretable and each axis carries a proportion of variation explained.

Comparison metrics. Because the three methods optimise different objectives, a visual comparison alone would be uninformative. Three quantitative measures are computed on the same embeddings: trustworthiness (do points that are close in two dimensions correspond to genuine near neighbours in the full matrix?), the Spearman correlation between pairwise distances in the embedding and in the original matrix on a 2,000-sample subset (does the embedding preserve global structure?), and the silhouette coefficient by zone (do the zone labels separate?). The silhouette is also computed on the original distance matrix, which gives the ceiling any embedding could achieve.

Deviation from the thesis workflow. The workflow document specifies UMAP for this step. t-SNE and PCoA were added at the user's request during this session so that the three could be compared. This is an addition, not a substitution: the UMAP configuration is unchanged.



Figure S4. The same Ghanaian distance submatrix under UMAP, t-SNE and PCoA, coloured by agro-ecological zone, on the 4,905 pre-QC isolates. Computed before the corrected re-run and unaffected by it, because this stage reads the published distance matrix rather than the genotype matrix rebuilt at Stage 4a. This is the published version and it is retained unchanged; Figure S5 is its counterpart on the 1,900-isolate analysis set (Section 3.4.1).

3.4.1 Re-run on the analysis set

The mismatch. As published, this stage described the 4,905 release-QC-passing isolates while every other result in the manuscript described the 1,900 that survive Stage 3b and Stage 4a. The figures and the comparison metrics were therefore computed on a different population from the FST, ADMIXTURE, Mantel, TreeMix, BayPass and marker-panel results they sit beside, and in particular on a set that still contained the polyclonal and clonally redundant isolates those later stages remove. Nothing was wrong with the computation; what was wrong was that the sample set was not the one the reader would assume.

The change. step03_umap.py now takes --samples, a file of isolate identifiers, and --suffix, a string appended to every output filename. Given --samples it re-slices the distance submatrix from the memory-mapped full matrix for exactly those isolates, rather than reusing the cached 4,905-isolate submatrix written by the first run. That distinction is the substance of the patch: the cached submatrix is the wrong object for a sample subset, and re-using it and subsetting the labels afterwards would have reported the full-set geometry under the smaller set's name. The pf8_row indices captured at Stage 1 make the re-slice safe, since they are fixed at extraction and do not shift under the intervening filters (Section 3.2). Outputs carry the _qcset suffix so that the published figures and CSVs are left untouched and the two sample sets can be compared side by side rather than one overwriting the other.

Table S10. The three ordinations and their comparison metrics on the published pre-QC sample set and on the analysis set. Analysis-set values from results/dimred_comparison_qcset.csv. The table is numbered after Table S12 rather than in document order so that the existing table numbers, which other sections cite, do not shift.

[Observed] On the 1,900-isolate analysis set the silhouette by zone computed on the raw distances is −0.020, against −0.016 on the 4,905. Trustworthiness rises slightly for all three methods (UMAP 0.486 to 0.499, t-SNE 0.507 to 0.526, PCoA 0.495 to 0.510) and the PCoA distance correlation falls from 0.322 to 0.287. The embedding-level silhouettes on the analysis set are −0.277 for UMAP, −0.063 for t-SNE and −0.079 for PCoA.

[Interpreted] Removing the polyclonal and clonally redundant isolates does not sharpen the structure. The silhouette becomes slightly more negative, which is the opposite of what the obvious objection to the published figures would predict — that mixed infections and repeat-sampled parasites were blurring a zone signal that would emerge once they were removed. They were not, and it does not. The small rise in trustworthiness is expected on a smaller point set and is not evidence of better structure; the fall in the PCoA distance correlation runs the other way. The conclusion of this stage is therefore unchanged, and the figures can now be reported on the same sample set as the rest of the analysis.



Figure S5. The same three ordinations on the 1,900-isolate analysis set, coloured by agro-ecological zone, with the distance submatrix re-sliced for those isolates rather than taken from the cached full-set matrix. Compare Figure S4, which is the published 4,905-isolate version. The figure is numbered after Figure S6 rather than in document order so that the existing figure numbers do not shift.

A log defect in this patch. The --suffix patch suffixed the file writes but not the print statements, so the run reported writing the published results/dimred_comparison.csv when it had in fact correctly written results/dimred_comparison_qcset.csv. No data were at risk and none was overwritten — verified from the published file's modification time and contents — but the log misreported its own output path, which is a reproducibility defect in its own right. It is fixed, and Section 3.13.1 records it alongside the other incident from these runs.

3.5 Stage 3a — genotype retrieval, and the consequence of the numalt filter

What and why. This stage did not exist in the supplied pipeline and was written during this session to replace a failed VCF download (Section 5.1). It fetches genotypes directly from the Pf8 Zarr store over HTTPS, chunk by chunk, retaining only what the downstream steps read.

The Zarr store is chunked 131,072 variants by 100 samples per field. Because the 6,653 Ghanaian samples fall in 77 of the 334 sample-chunk columns, and the sites of interest in 95 of the 96 variant-chunk rows, only a subset of the grid must be transferred. Each chunk is downloaded, decompressed in memory, sliced to the Ghanaian columns and the retained rows, then written to a local store and discarded, so peak memory stays at a few gigabytes and the transferred volume is never written to disk in full.

Sites are retained where FILTER_PASS is true, is_snp is true and numalt equals 1. Fields retained are GT, AD (reference and first alternate only), DP and GQ.

[Observed] 2,190,026 of 12,493,205 Pf8 sites are PASS biallelic SNPs. The transfer moved 299.8 GB in 3.20 hours at approximately 26 MB/s with zero failed requests, producing a 20.57 GB local store.

3.5.1 What the numalt == 1 restriction excludes

[Observed] The original version of this section presented the three-part site filter as a neutral convenience. It is not. Requiring numalt == 1 and is_snp restricts the store to sites with exactly one alternate allele and no indel or spanning-deletion ALT. In Pf8 that restriction bites hard at several of the sites a drug-resistance question depends on — not because of any general property of resistance codons, but because of what the release happens to record at these particular positions: pfcrt K76T is numalt = 2; pfcrt M74I is numalt = 3; pfmdr1 N86Y is numalt = 3; pfdhps K540E is numalt = 2; pfdhps A581G is numalt = 2. Each therefore fails numalt == 1 and is dropped. The five numalt values are read from results/resistance_panel_canonical.csv. The per-codon is_snp flags are deliberately not asserted: no saved output carries an is_snp column at codon level and no log prints one, the local store cannot settle it because by construction it holds only numalt == 1 sites, and the only is_snp figure on the record is the aggregate at step10_resistance_panel.log:16 (numalt == 1 & is_snp, 3,330). An earlier draft attached "with is_snp false" to four of the five codons; that is withdrawn as unevidenced, and the paragraph rests on the numalt values, which are fully evidenced. The earlier draft also justified the exclusion by asserting that the canonical resistance codons are "among the most-sampled, most-variable positions in the genome" and therefore the most likely to be multiallelic. Nothing in this project measures that, and the claim is withdrawn; what is on the record is the per-codon observation above.

[Observed] Quantified over the nine genes of the resistance panel (Section 3.10): the Pf8 Zarr holds 18,341 variant records inside those gene spans, of which 13,367 are FILTER_PASS. Of those 13,367, the filter keeps 3,330 and discards 10,037 as multiallelic or indel. At the canonical codons specifically, it discards 28 of the 44 canonical records and keeps 16 — but what it keeps is largely uninformative: 10 of the 16 are monomorphic in these isolates, including six pfk13 codons, pfmdr1 S1034C and N1042D, pfcrt C72S and pfmdr2 T484I. Of the 21 canonical codons that are actually polymorphic here, 15 are discarded, among them the pfcrt CVIET-lineage set at codons 74, 75, 76, 220, 271 and 371 — whose Stage 10 records are M74I, Asn75Asp, K76T, A220S, Q271E and R371I — together with pfmdr1 N86Y and Y184F, pfdhfr S108N, and pfdhps S436A, K540E, A581G and A613S. Two qualifications travel with that pfcrt set, because an earlier draft called it "the whole CVIET-defining set" and that is wrong in both directions. First, these are Stage 10's annotation records, and Stage 10's record at codon 75 is Asn75Asp, not N75E: resistance_panel_canonical.csv carries pfcrt,Asn75Asp,numalt=2,in_genomewide_scan=False,freq=0.0795, and step10_resistance_panel.log:22 lists the pfcrt canonical set as Ala220Ser, Arg371Ile, Asn326Ser, Asn75Asp, Cys72Ser, Gln271Glu, Ile356Thr, Lys76Thr, Met74Ile. N75E was never in Stage 10's panel at all — that is precisely the failure mode of Section 3.10.1 — so Stage 10 cannot have discarded it. Second, CVIET is the codon 72–76 motif (results/codon_panel_haplotypes.csv labels it pfcrt_72_76, "CVIET core motif"), so 220, 271 and 371 are additional pfcrt haplotype positions rather than CVIET-defining, while two codons that are CVIET-defining are absent from the discarded list: codon 72, where C72S passed the mask (in_genomewide_scan = True) and is monomorphic here, and codon 73, which Stage 10's panel did not contain. The earlier formulation — that the discarded set "includes every canonical resistance codon", or that the numalt clause dropped every canonical codon in pfcrt, pfmdr1 and pfk13 — is contradicted by the project's own output and is corrected to the counts given here.

[Observed] The cascade, defined once. Stage 10's panel held 44 canonical codon-allele records, covering 41 distinct protein changes, in eight of the nine panel genes (pfcoronin contributed none); 21 were polymorphic in these 1,900 isolates; 6 of the 21 survived the ingest mask — the in_genomewide_scan field, defined at step10_resistance_panel.py:349 as numalt == 1, i.e. "would have survived the ingest filter"; and 3 reached the MAF-filtered scan matrix: pfdhfr C59R, pfdhfr N51I and pfaat1 S258L. The other three that passed the mask — pfcrt I356T, pfmdr1 D1246Y and pfcrt N326S — were then cut by MAF ≥ 0.05 at frequencies of 0.016, 0.011 and 0.008. These four numbers (44, 21, 6, 3) carry exactly this meaning wherever they appear in this manuscript, and the Abstract, Table S1, Section 6.8.3, Table S27 and Section 10.1 use this wording. In particular, the genome-wide scan did test three of the 21 polymorphic canonical codons, and no pfcrt, pfmdr1, pfdhps or pfk13 codon reached the MAF-filtered scan matrix — pfdhps because nothing survived the ingest mask, the others because what survived was either too rare (pfcrt I356T, pfcrt N326S and pfmdr1 D1246Y all passed the mask, reached the genome-wide store and were then cut by MAF at 0.016, 0.011 and 0.008) or monomorphic here (six pfk13 codons — F446I, P441L, Y493H, P553L, R561H, A675V — passed the mask and were fetched and found invariant). "Could not test at all" would be true of the final MAF-filtered scan matrix but false of ascertainment, which is the distinction this paragraph exists to draw, so the stronger wording is not used.

These counts describe the scan's ascertainment, not the marker panel. 44, 21, 6 and 3 are properties of Stage 10's annotation-matched record set and of what the genome-wide ingest filter did to it. They are not the denominators of the marker panel as it now stands. The panel is defined by codon, not by annotation record: Stage 12 reconstructs 57 codons across the same nine genes, of which 24 are polymorphic here and 33 fixed at the reference residue (Section 3.10). The two framings answer different questions and are not interchangeable, and because Stage 10's list was itself incomplete — it had no pfdhps 437 at all, and no N75E — the 44 should be read as "the records Stage 10 found", never as "the canonical codons that exist".

[Interpreted] Consequence. The genome-wide store built by this stage cannot carry most of the canonical resistance codons, so the absence of pfcrt, pfmdr1, pfdhps and pfk13 from the Stage 8 outlier list is uninformative about resistance at those loci: it is an ascertainment artefact, not a result. The two pfdhfr codons and pfaat1 S258L that did reach the scan are a different matter — they were tested, and Section 6.8 reports them as tested results rather than as silence. This is not a limitation that can be repaired by reinterpretation; it required a separate retrieval, which is Stage 12. The filter is retained unchanged for the genome-wide analyses, where biallelic SNPs are the appropriate unit and the exclusion is not differential with respect to zone. It is lifted only for the panel codons, in a parallel store that is never merged with this one.

How this was missed. The filter is one line of a retrieval script, applied before any analysis ran: a three-term conjunction, mask = (FILTER_PASS & is_snp & (numalt == 1)), at scripts/step03a_fetch_callset_zarr.py:334-336, with the same test on the VCF-validation path at :236 and again at :370. (An earlier version of this note cited "a single clause on line 13"; line 13 of that file is a docstring sentence about PASS and RegionType, which is the separate claim Section 3.5.2 withdraws, and conflating the two defects obscured both.) It fails silently: the excluded sites never appear in any count, log or diagnostic downstream. Nothing in the pipeline's output distinguishes "this codon is monomorphic" from "this codon was never fetched". Section 7 treats this as a general lesson about ingest filters rather than a one-off error.

3.5.2 The core-genome filter

The supplied pipeline expects an optional BED file of core-genome intervals (data/pf_core_regions.bed), absent here, and warns at Stage 4a that without it subtelomeric var/rifin regions will contaminate the outlier scan. The earlier version of this section dismissed that warning as "a false alarm", citing a direct tabulation of RegionType over the PASS biallelic site set. No such tabulation exists: RegionType is not among the fields step03a retrieves, the local store holds no such array, and no log records the counts. That claim is withdrawn.

[Interpreted] The argument that replaces it is structural rather than empirical, and is checkable from the release metadata without any fetch: Pf8's FILTER vocabulary itself contains the region categories (FILTER_SubtelomericHypervariable, FILTER_SubtelomericRepeat, FILTER_InternalHypervariable, FILTER_Centromere, FILTER_Apicoplast, FILTER_Mitochondrion), so a non-core site cannot carry FILTER_PASS by construction. The PASS filter therefore performs the regional exclusion the BED would have performed, as a matter of definition.

Two qualifications follow. First, because the argument is definitional, it is labelled [Interpreted] and not [Observed]; the manuscript's own evidence key reserves [Observed] for values printed by a pipeline script and preserved in a log or results file. Second, the exclusion is regional, not family-based: members of antigenic gene families that lie inside core regions do survive into the analysis matrix. Their absence from the outlier list is therefore a tested result, weakly powered, rather than a filtering tautology, and Section 6.8 states it that way. The structural argument has since acquired direct support of a kind it did not have: the quality audit of Section 3.11.1 reads RegionType for the 19 outliers and finds all 19 are Core.

3.5.3 Validation

[Observed] The store was verified against the one completely downloaded VCF (chromosome 10) and against two partially downloaded ones (chromosomes 1 and 13), in three independent 35 to 40 kb windows. In each window the site list matched exactly and all four genotype fields agreed value for value: 3,957, 3,470 and 4,378 PASS biallelic SNPs respectively, with zero differing values across 471,231,990 compared values in total. The verification is in logs/step03a_fetch_callset.log.

3.6 Stage 3b — isolate quality control

Four per-isolate checks are applied, computed from a panel of 50,000 biallelic PASS SNPs spread proportionally across all 14 contigs.

Within-host fixation index (Fws). Fws measures how far an isolate's read-level allele frequencies depart from the population's. A clonal infection has read frequencies near 0 or 1 at every site and Fws near 1; a mixed infection carries intermediate frequencies and a lower value. It is computed as Fws = 1 − (Σ Hw,i / Σ Hs,i), where Hw = 2q(1 − q) from the isolate's own read counts and Hs = 2p(1 − p) from pooled read counts across qualifying isolates. Sites contribute only where total depth is at least 5. The conventional monoclonality threshold of 0.95 is applied (config.py MIN_FWS = 0.95). That threshold is a convention and not a property of these data, it discards the majority of the isolates, and Section 3.14 reports what the results do when it is relaxed.

Call rate. The proportion of panel SNPs with a genotype call. The pipeline applies an absolute floor and, optionally, a median-absolute-deviation outlier rule; the choice between them is discussed in Section 5.4.

Heterozygosity outliers. Blood-stage P. falciparum is haploid, so a heterozygous call indicates a mixed infection or a genotyping error rather than true heterozygosity. Isolates more than three median absolute deviations above the median heterozygous call rate are flagged. This check is largely redundant with Fws and is retained as an independent cross-check.

Clonal pruning. Near-identical isolates (outbreak-linked or repeat-sampled parasites) inflate within-zone homogeneity and therefore inflate between-zone FST. The cached Ghanaian distance submatrix from Stage 3 is thresholded at CLONAL_THRESHOLD = 0.00005, connected components are extracted with a single-linkage rule, and one representative is retained per component — chosen by best call rate, then best Fws, then identifier, so the choice is deterministic.

[Observed] Median heterozygous call rate 0.0004 (range 0.0000 to 0.0030). Median Fws 0.895 (range 0.310 to 1.000); 2,095 of 4,905 isolates monoclonal at Fws ≥ 0.95 (42.7 per cent), 2,810 below. 2,017 of 4,905 isolates pass all four checks (41.1 per cent). 77 isolates were not retained as the representative of their clonal component.

On the failure counts. The script prints the five failure reasons as independent, non-exclusive flags (step03b_sample_qc.py:397 and 402-406), and they overlap: 20 + 0 + 126 + 2,810 + 77 = 3,033 against 4,905 − 2,017 = 2,888 actual failures. The 77 is therefore the total count flagged fail_clonal, not a count of isolates failing on clonal pruning alone, and an earlier version of this paragraph read it the second way.



Figure S6. Per-isolate quality-control distributions on the 50,000-SNP panel: call rate, heterozygous call rate and Fws, with the applied thresholds marked. Unchanged by the corrected re-run, which altered only Stage 4a and later.

3.7 Stage 4a — building the genotype matrix

This stage converts the Zarr store into the single filtered genotype matrix that every subsequent stage reads. The order of operations matters and is deliberate:

Site-level filters on the small one-dimensional arrays (FILTER_PASS, is_snp, numalt == 1), screening the whole genome before any genotype is fetched. Section 3.5.1 states what this excludes and why that matters.

Genotype-level masking: calls below a read-depth floor of 5 are set to missing. A low-confidence call thus becomes missing data rather than a confident wrong answer, which matters because the missingness filter later in this stage then sees it for what it is. The GQ component of the pipeline default was removed on the evidence set out in Section 4.2.5.

Sample alignment: genotype columns are reordered to match the metadata table, so that sample order is fixed once and never re-derived.

Mixed-call resolution: heterozygous calls are replaced by the allele with more reads; ties become missing (Section 5.3). Stage 12 deliberately does not follow this rule; Section 3.10.2 says why.

Two-pass missingness, then MAF — described below.

3.7.1 The corrected filter order, and why it matters

In the original run, variant missingness was computed once, over all 2,017 isolates that entered this stage, and the sample-missingness cut was applied afterwards. 117 isolates then failed that cut and were dropped. Every SNP had therefore been charged for missing calls in 117 isolates that did not survive into any analysis, so SNPs were discarded for failing a criterion measured partly on data that was itself discarded. The bias is not random: it penalises exactly the SNPs that are hardest to call in the worst-covered isolates.

The stage is now explicitly two-pass. A provisional variant set is formed under the same rule as before, so that the same isolates fail the sample cut and the analysis sample set is unchanged. The sample cut is applied. Variant missingness is then recomputed on the isolates that remain, and the MAF filter applied last so that a SNP is not retained on the strength of a handful of confident calls. The per-sample call counts over the provisional set are accumulated a block at a time, so the provisional genotype subset is never materialised in memory.

[Calculated] Thresholds and the sample set are identical before and after. The corrected order yields 3,916 SNPs where the original yielded 3,359, an increase of 16.6 per cent on the same 1,900 isolates — additional statistical power obtained purely by measuring the existing criteria in the right order.

[Observed] Applied to the final run: 3,709,090,355 calls fell below DP 5; of 12,540,727,880 called genotypes, 10,845,661,093 survived the mask (13.52 per cent masked); 194,167 mixed calls were resolved by read depth, 129,665 to REF, 55,582 to ALT and 8,920 to missing on a tie; the provisional variant pass used for the sample cut held 1,634,808 sites; 117 high-missingness samples were dropped, leaving 1,900; variant missingness ≤ 0.1 recomputed on those 1,900 retained 1,807,331 sites; and MAF ≥ 0.05 gave the final 3,916.

Thresholds. MAF of at least 5 per cent and missingness of at most 10 per cent are specified in the thesis workflow document for Step 4 and were not altered. The workflow document writes these as MAF > 5 per cent and missingness < 10 per cent; the implementation uses the non-strict forms — keep_maf = maf >= args.min_maf at step04a_prepare_callset.py:310 and keep_var = var_missing <= args.max_missing at :300, with the same non-strict test at :280 for the provisional pass. The difference changes no reported count, and the thresholds are quoted here in the form the code applies so that the two statements cannot be read as contradictory. The depth floor and the sample-missingness bound are pipeline defaults. The depth and quality settings were revised during this session, and that revision is documented in Section 4.2.5 — not, as the previous version of this paragraph stated, in Section 5.5, which concerns the TreeMix jackknife block size. The MAF floor is the second of the two thresholds Stage 13 varies, and it is the more consequential of the two for the SNP count: lowering it to 0.01 nearly triples the panel (Section 3.14).

3.8 Stage 4b — pairwise differentiation

Estimator. Hudson's FST is computed per locus and summed as a ratio of averages — numerator and denominator accumulated across loci before dividing — rather than as an average of per-locus ratios, which is upwardly biased. Hudson's estimator is preferred over Weir and Cockerham's here because it is insensitive to unequal sample sizes between populations, which is pertinent when one zone has 1,334 isolates and another has 25.

[Calculated] Uncertainty. Standard errors come from a block jackknife over contiguous blocks of SNPs, which accounts for linkage between nearby sites. The block length in force is blen = min(2000, max(10, n/20)), so with 3,916 SNPs it is 195 SNPs, giving approximately 20 jackknife blocks.

Caveat. Twenty blocks is few, and the manuscript should not claim otherwise. Section 5.5 rejects the supplied TreeMix block size — 500 SNPs, which on the final 3,916-SNP panel would give about 8 blocks and gave 6 or 7 on the superseded 3,359-SNP panel — on precisely this ground, that too few blocks make the standard errors and hence the migration-edge tests unreliable, and raised the TreeMix block count to roughly 78 (3,916 SNPs in 50-SNP blocks; logs/stage7.log records SNPs 3,916 at block size 50, and config.py:164 sets TREEMIX_BLOCK_SIZE = 50). The FST standard errors and confidence intervals reported in Section 6.4 rest on about a quarter of that number, under an automatic rule rather than a considered choice. The block count was not stated in the original manuscript; it is stated here because the headline differentiation intervals depend on it.

Ploidy handling. Allele counts are taken as one allele per isolate (Section 5.3). This matters: counting both copies of a diploid-coded haploid genotype would double every sample size entering the FST correction, the TreeMix drift standard errors and the BayPass XtX statistic, making all three anticonservative.

[Observed] Sensitivity. This estimator was recomputed under six threshold scenarios at Stage 13. The maximum pairwise value stays within 0.00204 to 0.00255 across all six, over isolate counts from 1,900 to 4,403 and SNP counts from 3,275 to 10,876 (results/sensitivity_summary.csv; Section 3.14). The point estimates reported in Section 6.4 are therefore not an artefact of either threshold, which is a stronger statement about them than the jackknife standard errors alone can make.

3.9 Stages 5 to 9

ADMIXTURE (Stage 5). Genotypes are exported to PLINK transposed text format, converted to binary, LD-pruned (50-SNP window, 10-SNP step, r-squared threshold 0.2) and run through ADMIXTURE for K = 1 to 8 with 10-fold cross-validation. Pruning is not optional: linked SNPs carry redundant information and inflate apparent structure. Pruning the 3,916-SNP matrix leaves 3,385 SNPs. K = 1 is run explicitly to provide the no-structure reference point that the supplied sweep omits, without which a CV minimum at K = 2 cannot be read as evidence of structure at all. All eight K values, K = 1 included, were run on that same 3,385-SNP pruned panel, so the cross-validation curve is internally comparable.

[Observed] A test that was previously absent. The earlier manuscript asserted that no ancestry component aligned with geography. That assertion was never tested. The stage now tests it: the K = 2 minor component is compared across zones by Kruskal–Wallis, which gives H = 105.4 at p = 1.05 × 10⁻²², so the null of no zone difference is rejected decisively. The effect is nonetheless negligible in magnitude: zone-mean minor-component values run from 0.0419 to 0.0921. The substantive reading survives; the claim of no association does not, and is replaced throughout by the quantified version.

[Calculated] The ordering of those means is reported but not read as a cline, because it is not one. The two southern zones are highest (Deciduous forest 0.0921, Coastal savanna 0.0851) and the two northern ones lowest (Guinea savanna 0.0534, Transitional zone 0.0419) — but the Transitional zone is the second-most-northern of the four and carries the lowest mean of all, so a monotone south-to-north ordering fails there. Zone accounts for 1.9 per cent of the variance in the minor component (between-group sum of squares over total, recomputed for this manuscript from results/admixture/ghana_pruned.2.Q against the zone labels). The correspondence with pairwise FST is partial rather than exact: the pair with the smallest FST (Coastal savanna against Deciduous forest, 0.00045) does have the closest zone means, but the pair with the largest (Coastal savanna against Guinea savanna, 0.00232) is only the fourth-largest difference in means, so the earlier claim that the component is "ordered as pairwise FST is" is withdrawn.

Mantel test (Stage 6). Isolation by distance is tested by regressing linearised differentiation, FST / (1 − FST), on the logarithm of great-circle distance computed on the WGS84 ellipsoid, at two levels: four zones and seven administrative regions. Three changes were made after the audit, and a fourth check, run later and reported in Section 3.14, bears on how the result may be graded at all.

The permutation test is seeded. skbio draws its permutations from NumPy's unseeded global generator, so the previous p-values were irreproducible — at 999 permutations the Monte Carlo standard error on p was itself about 0.005, comparable to the margin by which the result cleared 0.05. Permutations are now generated in-script from a seeded generator (seed 42) and the correlation recomputed, with 9,999 permutations, one-sided, since isolation by distance predicts a positive association. The finest attainable p-value is reported alongside the test, because at four populations the permutation distribution is coarse (six pairs) and a null result there is uninformative.

The censoring of negative FST pairs is disclosed and quantified. Hudson's estimator legitimately returns small negative values when between-population differentiation is below the within-population expectation, and skbio's DistanceMatrix rejects negatives, so such pairs must be floored to zero before the test. The original code did this silently at step06_mantel.py:148. The script now names every floored pair and its value in the log and in the JSON output, and runs the test twice — on the censored matrix and on the uncensored one — so that the reader can see what the flooring does. At region level 3 of 21 pairs are floored (Ashanti–Volta −0.001037, Eastern–Greater Accra −0.000203, Greater Accra–Volta −0.000142); at zone level none are.

A leave-one-out jackknife is run. The test is repeated with each population dropped in turn, which is the relevant robustness question when a correlation computed over 21 pairwise points from seven populations may rest on a single extreme population. Seven is the number of populations dropped in turn, not the number of points the correlation rests on (results/mantel_result_region.json records npops = 7, pairs = 21). Section 3.14 reports what this jackknife and the threshold sensitivity analysis together imply, and the short answer is that this result may not be graded as supported.

TreeMix (Stage 7). A maximum-likelihood population graph is fitted with 0 to 5 migration edges, rooted on Guinea savanna, using a jackknife block size of 50 SNPs, which on the final 3,916-SNP panel gives roughly 78 blocks. Model fit is summarised as the proportion of variance in between-population covariance explained, computed over off-diagonal entries only, matching TreeMix's own definition. The sweep is now run with -se, which was omitted previously; without it every migration line in the .treeout file reads "weight NA NA NA" and no edge can be tested, so model choice had to rest on variance explained alone — a rule of thumb with no null distribution behind it. That is the objection, and it is not that variance explained is monotone in m: on this project's own output it is not. results/treemix/variance_explained.csv gives 0.896997, 0.940513, 0.996907, 0.999664, 0.999579, 0.999586 for m = 0 to 5, so it falls between m = 3 and m = 4. Nor is it true that variance explained cannot identify a stopping point: results/treemix/model_selection.json records a rule that does — the smallest m reaching 0.998 (step07c_plot_treemix.py:45), which gives m = 3 over 0 to 5, with a plateau fallback within 0.001 of the best (:46) giving m = 2 over 0 to 2, and that pair is where the superseded manuscript's "m = 3 (full), m = 2 (identifiable)" came from. The rule picks a number; what it cannot do is attach an error rate to it. With -se, TreeMix computes a jackknife mean and standard error for each migration weight and a p-value against zero, so each edge weight becomes testable against zero, though that test is conditional on the graph fitted at that m and on the root fixed in advance, and it does not by itself settle the edge count: the variance-explained rule over the full m = 0 to 5 sweep still picks m = 3, and reporting m = 2 remains a modelling choice made on identifiability grounds. This is also what Section 5.5's change to the block size was for: the stated rationale, that too few blocks make the migration-edge tests unreliable, presupposes that such tests exist, and until -se was passed they did not.

BayPass (Stage 8). The core model estimates the population covariance matrix Ω from genome-wide allele counts, then reports XtX per SNP — a differentiation statistic standardised by that covariance structure. A SNP is called an outlier when its XtX exceeds a threshold taken from pseudo-observed data simulated under the estimated Ω. The logic is that a threshold drawn from the empirical XtX distribution assumes a fixed proportion of the genome is under selection, whereas a threshold from simulated neutral data assumes only that the demographic model is adequate. Section 3.11 describes the false-discovery accounting now applied on top of that threshold, and Section 3.11.1 the quality audit of the SNPs it selects.

Determinism. BayPass is run single-threaded. Despite a fixed -seed, the multithreaded core model is not reproducible: the comment block at scripts/step08b_baypass.sh:32-36 records that two multithreaded runs of the same input differed by up to 1.1 in XtX and gave 15 against 16 outliers, while two single-threaded runs were bitwise identical, because the order in which threads accumulate contributions is not fixed. (The range cited must include line 32, which is the line that names the pair as 8-thread.) NTHREADS now defaults to 1 at step08b_baypass.sh:37 with that reason in the script, and run_all.sh exports NTHREADS=1 for Stage 8 even in parallel mode. Three BayPass runs survive on the server and are not interchangeable: (i) the multithreaded run behind the original manuscript, on the superseded 3,359-SNP panel — threshold 11.232, 15 outliers, archived under results/baypass_8thread_archive/; (ii) a single-threaded run on that same 3,359-SNP panel, whose core model took 23 s (logs/step08b_1thread.log:1,25); and (iii) the final single-threaded run on the 3,916-SNP panel, core model 37 s and POD simulation 13 min 5 s, giving threshold 10.840 and 19 outliers (logs/stage8.log:25,49,89,104,106; results/baypass/ghana_core_baypass.log). No cost of determinism is quoted here, because the record cannot support one. Runs (ii) and (iii) are both single-threaded and differ in panel size, not in thread count, so the 23 s to 37 s gap measures a 16.6 per cent larger SNP panel rather than the price of single-threading; an earlier version of this note read that 14 s as the cost of determinism, which was wrong. No multithreaded core-model runtime survives anywhere: grep "threads:" logs/*.log returns exactly three lines (3,916 SNPs at 1 thread, 3,359 at 12, 3,359 at 1), the 8-thread artefacts in results/baypass_8thread_archive/ are outputs with no log, and the only multithreaded log, logs/stage8_pod_rerun.log, says at line 4 "core model output already present — skipping", so it never timed a core model. The "~26 s" in the script comment is that comment's own figure for the 3,359-SNP panel and is not this run's runtime. What the record does support is that determinism is cheap relative to the POD simulation, which dominates the wall time at 13 min 5 s.

[Unknown] The script comment describes the non-deterministic pair as two 8-thread runs, but the only retained multithreaded log (logs/stage8_pod_rerun.log:1) records 12 threads, and no log of an 8-thread pair survives. The thread count of the runs that established the non-determinism therefore cannot be confirmed from the record; the non-determinism itself, and the single-threaded remedy, are what the archived outputs and the script support.

Annotation (Stage 9). Outlier positions are intersected with protein-coding gene features from PlasmoDB-71 using bedtools, and cross-referenced against a panel of antimalarial resistance loci and against antigenic and invasion gene families. The cross-reference list is config.KNOWN_RESISTANCE_LOCI, an eight-gene-ID dict hard-coded at config.py:173-182 and read at step09_annotate.py:178 — not the Stage 10 or Stage 12 marker panel, so no number in this manuscript depends circularly on either. The panel was described in the original manuscript as "eight validated antimalarial resistance loci". That description was wrong, and the corrected version offered in the previous revision — that pfcoronin, pfap2mu and pfmdr2 are "candidate rather than WHO-validated markers" — was also wrong. Checked against WHO's compendium of molecular markers v1.0 (December 2025): pfap2mu and pfmdr2 appear in it at no tier at all, and pfcoronin appears at the potential tier only, which is below candidate. None of the three is a candidate marker in WHO's sense. The list also omitted PF3D7_0629500 (pfaat1), which the thesis proposal names as a panel member and which the compendium likewise does not contain. That omission is why the scan's second-ranked gene surfaced as an anonymous transporter rather than as a hit on a pre-specified candidate.

[Observed] That omission has not been repaired in code, and the manuscript should not claim it has. scripts/config.py on the server still defines KNOWN_RESISTANCE_LOCI as the original eight entries — pfcrt, pfmdr1, pfdhfr, pfdhps, pfk13, pfmdr2, pfcoronin, pfap2mu — with no PF3D7_0629500, and its modification time precedes the final re-run. The final Stage 9 cross-reference accordingly printed "None recovered" and listed all eight as absent from the outlier set (logs/rerun_5to9.log:44-51), and AAT1 appears in results/outlier_genes.csv with an empty family field. An earlier version of this paragraph stated that pfaat1 "has been added to config.KNOWN_RESISTANCE_LOCI and the cross-reference re-run"; that is not what the reported run did.

[Proposed] Add PF3D7_0629500 to config.KNOWN_RESISTANCE_LOCI and re-run step09_annotate.py, so that the AAT1 hit is flagged by the pipeline rather than identified by hand in Section 6.8. It is a one-line change and it affects no estimate, only the annotation label.

Three counts that must not be conflated. The Stage 12 retrieval panel is nine genes (Table S11), covering 57 codons. Of those 57, 24 are polymorphic in these isolates, in eight of the nine genes — pfcoronin's three codons are all fixed at the reference residue. And config.KNOWN_RESISTANCE_LOCI, the Stage 9 cross-reference list, is a different eight — pfcrt, pfmdr1, pfdhfr, pfdhps, pfk13, pfmdr2, pfcoronin, pfap2mu — which excludes pfaat1. That exclusion is the mechanism by which a real positive was missed, so the counts are named explicitly wherever they appear rather than left to the reader to disentangle.

Caveat. The cross-reference intersects gene bodies with no flanking window (--flank defaults to 0 at step09_annotate.py:61 and the final run applied none), so an outlier in near-complete linkage with a panel locus but outside its annotated span is not flagged. This is a live limitation, not a defect of the superseded run that has since gone away. The pfap2mu-adjacent SNP is still an outlier in the final run and is still unflagged: results/baypass/outlier_snps.bed lists Pf3D7_12_v3:715,788 at XtX 15.086, ninth of the 19, it is absent from results/outliers_annotated.txt, and PF3D7_1218300 (pfap2mu) still starts at 716,700 — the gap is still exactly 912 bp. It is also one of the two outliers falling outside any annotated gene: the 19 outlier SNPs give 17 SNP-gene overlaps across 13 genes (logs/rerun_5to9.log), and the other of the two is the fifth-ranked outlier overall, XtX 20.79 at Pf3D7_11_v3:1,054,447, q = 0.005 — which is also one of the two SNPs the quality audit downgrades (Section 3.11.1). Naming the live pfap2mu near-miss is stronger evidence for this caveat than the gene-count arithmetic alone. Outlier counts and gene counts are therefore not interchangeable, and the finding is not "19 outliers in 13 genes". Neither this stage nor Stage 8 applies LD pruning or clumping, so the per-gene outlier SNP counts in Section 6.8 count linked sites separately; Section 3.11 and Section 6.8 state where that matters.

3.10 Stage 12 — the resistance marker panel, by reconstructed codon

What and why. Section 3.5.1 establishes that the genome-wide store cannot carry most of the canonical resistance codons. This stage answers the marker question directly instead, on the same 1,900 isolates and the same zone labels, in a parallel retrieval that leaves every existing output untouched. It is not a re-analysis of the genome-wide matrix and its results are not merged into it. It is the second attempt at this question. The first, Stage 10, identified markers by matching SnpEff's ANN_HGVS_p protein-change strings against a hand-written list, and an external critique showed that this could not work; Section 3.10.1 sets out why, and Section 3.10.2 the method that replaces it. Every marker number in this manuscript now comes from step12_codon_panel.py.

[Observed] The codon panel bypasses the release filter as well as the numalt filter, and that was not previously disclosed. Stage 12 reconstructs codons from every variant record at a panel position, iterating over all ALT alleles without consulting FILTER_PASS. Bypassing numalt == 1 was the deliberate fix that recovered the panel; bypassing FILTER_PASS came with it and was never stated. An audit of all 171 panel positions resolves what it costs. Of the variant records at those positions, 29 fail the release filter, at 29 distinct positions. Among the 1,900 analysis isolates, six non-reference calls are sourced from those 29 records, at three positions, and all three are in pfk13 at WHO-validated marker codons: 1,724,975 (codon 675, A675V) with four carriers, 1,725,315 (codon 561, R561H) with one, and 1,725,339 (codon 553, P553L) with one. None of the six changes a reported residue, for two separable reasons. At codons 561 and 553 every alternative allele at the failing position is synonymous — the reference codons are CGT and CCG, and the ALTs give CGG, CGC, CGA and CCA, CCT, which still encode Arg and Pro — so the one homozygous call among the six, isolate SPT19969 at codon 561, correctly counts as Arg561 and the reported 1,864 of 1,864 is right. At codon 675 the alternative is non-synonymous but encodes Pro, not the validated Val, and all four carriers are heterozygous, so the mixed-call rule made the codon uncalled for them. No screened WHO resistance residue is produced by any filter-failing record, and the tested negative therefore stands. One consequence should still travel with the number: those four codon-675 carriers have well-supported minor alleles — 22% to 28% of 173 to 435 reads at GQ 99 — so codon 675's denominator of 1,845 is the lowest of the pfk13 codons (the panel minimum is 1,834, at pfmdr1 1034), and its exclusions are not random with respect to genotype.

[Observed] The single Asn458Asp call was checked against the release’s own depth and quality fields and is consistent with a real variant rather than an artefact. The retrieval script and its captured output are archived at provenance_final/audit_scripts/check_n458d.py and provenance_final/audit_logs/n458d.log, so the query can be re-run against the public store; it is a check of MalariaGEN’s called fields, not an independent examination of the underlying read alignments. Because one call in 1,866 at a validated marker codon could as easily be an annotation or base-calling artefact as a real variant, the underlying data were retrieved. Codon 458 draws on Pf3D7_13_v3 positions 1,725,626, 1,725,625 and 1,725,624. The middle position carries no variant record. The last fails the release filter and has no non-reference carrier among the 1,900. The first, 1,725,626, passes the filter and is triallelic (REF T, ALT A and C, numalt = 2), which is why the numalt == 1 ingest mask excluded it from the genome-wide matrix. Isolate PF0106-C is homozygous for the second ALT, C, with allele depths of 0, 0 and 40 — forty of forty reads supporting that allele — at DP 40 and GQ 99, the maximum. On the minus strand C complements to G, turning the reference codon AAT into GAT, which is Asp. The call is unambiguous in the release’s depth and quality fields, and the reconstruction is arithmetically correct. It is also not the validated allele: WHO validates N458Y, which requires AAT to TAT, a different substitution at the same base. So codon 458 is 0.9995 reference rather than fixed, the one alternative residue is Asp and not Tyr, and no WHO-screened artemisinin marker is observed.

[Observed] The reconstruction has a bounded input domain, and the bound was not previously stated. A variant record whose ALT differs in length from its REF is skipped by a continue, but the isolate carrying it is not marked uncalled, so in principle it would be reported with the reference-derived residue. Three further limits travel with that one. The spanning-deletion allele is written “*” and has length one, so where REF is a single base it passes the length test rather than failing it, and is substituted into the codon as though it were a nucleotide, yielding the sentinel residue X rather than a silent reference call. An equal-length multi-base record contributes only its first base. And records are retrieved by anchor position, so a record anchored outside the panel whose REF span covers a codon position is never fetched at all. Absence of a variant record is likewise read as the reference base.

[Observed] Those conditions occur in this panel, and on the genotypes retrieved for them they change nothing in it; the retrieval scripts and their captured output are archived under provenance_final/audit_scripts and provenance_final/audit_logs. Of the 125 variant records sitting exactly at the 171 panel positions, 16 carry at least one length-changing ALT and 2 have a multi-base REF; a further 19 records anchored outside the panel have a REF span covering a panel position. Genotypes were retrieved for all 32 affected records across all 1,900 isolates. Three have any homozygous non-reference carrier: pfcrt codon 76 with 133, pfcrt codon 271 with 138, and pfdhps codon 431 with 26. In every case the carriers sit on a supported, equal-length allele — codon 76 on ALT1 C, with none on the 55-base insertion; codon 271 on ALT1 GAACGTAAGA, with none on ALT2, none on the spanning deletion and none on the single-base C; codon 431 on ALT1 G, with none on the spanning deletion and none on the 20-base insertion. No isolate anywhere in the panel carries a length-changing allele as a homozygous call, and the 62 mixed calls at these records are removed by the mixed-call rule regardless. The counts reconcile exactly with the reported table: Lys76Thr 133, Gln271Glu 138, Ile431Val 26, and homozygous-reference counts of 1,741 and 1,735 against reported reference-residue counts of 1,741 and 1,735. No residue anywhere in the panel is X, which is consistent with no spanning-deletion allele having been substituted — though that is the panel’s own output checked against itself, not an independent confirmation. The two multi-base REF records differ from the reference at their first base only, so applying one base loses nothing here. And none of the 57 codons is record-free. That is weaker than it sounds, and the weaker form is the accurate one: 46 of the 171 panel positions carry no variant record at all, so at those positions the reference base is read for every isolate whether or not that isolate was covered there. Per codon, 21 have a record at all three positions, 26 at two and 10 at only one, which means the called mask for those 10 rests on a single position’s genotype. Reading absence of a record as the reference allele is therefore an assumption about callability, not a measurement of it; no per-isolate depth field is consulted anywhere in the reconstruction. The counts come from provenance_final/audit_scripts/check_coverage.py. The limitation is real and bounds what the implementation may be applied to; on these inputs it moves no published number. What still rests on an assumption is callability: a codon is called when its constituent genotypes are present and unmixed, which is a statement about the genotype array and not an independent measurement of per-isolate coverage.

3.10.1 Why matching annotation strings cannot find the markers

Failure mode (i): a resistance allele that is the reference residue can never appear as an ALT protein change. SnpEff annotates changes away from the reference, so if 3D7 already carries the resistance-associated residue, the only annotation at that codon names the reversion. pfdhps 437 is exactly this case, and the official Pf8 methodology says so outright — the sentence was verified in the source document: "3D7 sequence translates as a G at 437, this is thought to be the mutant allele, i.e. 3D7 does not have the wild type allele." The only annotation at that codon is Gly437Ala, the revertant. Stage 10 searched for "Ala437Gly", matched nothing, and A437G was absent from the panel entirely — not reported as rare, not reported as monomorphic, simply not there. Its true frequency is 0.897 over 1,892 called isolates, the second-highest resistance-residue frequency in the panel after pfdhfr S108N at 0.908. A locus of that frequency was missing from a manuscript about antimalarial resistance, and no internal consistency check could have found it, because nothing was inconsistent.

[Observed] Failure mode (ii): a residue reachable only by two changes within one codon is never annotated. pfcrt codon 75 carries two variant records, 403621 (A>G, annotated Asn75Asp) and 403623 (T>A, annotated Asn75Lys). Asn→Glu requires both. No single annotation names N75E, which is the residue the CVIET haplotype actually carries and the residue MalariaGEN reports; N75E consequently never appeared in Stage 10's panel at all. The previous version of this manuscript described the resulting disagreement with MalariaGEN as a "naming discrepancy" in which "Pf8's SnpEff annotation labels the ALT as Asp while MalariaGEN calls it Glu", and resolved it by adopting MalariaGEN's nomenclature. That diagnosis was wrong. It was not a naming difference: Stage 10 was reporting the frequency of a residue that does not exist in these isolates, under a method structurally incapable of producing the one that does. The claim of a naming discrepancy at pfcrt 75 is withdrawn.

3.10.2 The method: reconstruct the codon, report every residue

The script works at the codon rather than at the annotation string. Each requested codon is mapped to its three genomic positions through the gene's CDS, read from the same PlasmoDB-71 GFF the rest of the pipeline uses: CDS segments are concatenated in coordinate order, reversed for minus-strand genes so that the order is translation order, the total length is asserted to be a multiple of three, and codon i is positions 3i−2 to 3i of that sequence. The reference bases at those three positions are read from the 3D7 reference genome FASTA, reverse-complemented on the minus strand, and translated, so the reference residue is read from the genome rather than assumed from the marker's name. Every variant record in the Pf8 Zarr at any of the three positions is then collected — regardless of numalt and regardless of is_snp — each isolate's called alleles are substituted into the reference codon, and the result is translated. The output is the full residue spectrum at each codon, the reference residue included and labelled as such, so a resistance allele cannot hide in the reference and a residue requiring two substitutions cannot be missed.

[Observed] Mixed calls make the codon uncalled. A heterozygous call at any constituent position makes the codon ambiguous — the two reads imply two different residues — so the isolate is recorded as uncalled at that codon rather than assigned one of them (step12_codon_panel.py:363-368). This is a deliberate departure from the Stage 4a and Stage 10 rule, which resolves a mixed call to the allele with more reads (Section 3.7, Section 5.3). The reason for the departure is that a codon is a three-position object: resolving each position independently by read depth can manufacture a residue that no single parasite in the infection carries. The cost is a per-codon denominator slightly below 1,900, and the benefit is that no reported residue is a construction artefact. The mixed-call tallies are small and concentrated, but they are position-level counts, not isolate counts, and the two must not be conflated: the script accumulates n_mixed over all three positions of a codon, so an isolate mixed at two positions is counted twice. logs/step12_codon_panel.log records 52 mixed calls at pfcrt 75, 27 at pfcrt 74, 26 at 76, 25 each at 220, 271 and 371, 24 at 326, 3 at pfaat1 258, 2 at pfap2mu 160 and 1 at pfcrt 73. The isolate-level loss at pfcrt 75 is 26, not 52: the codon is called in 1,874 of 1,900, and 75 is the two-SNP codon (403621 and 403623), so 26 × 2 = 52 — 52 isolates could not have been lost from a denominator of 1,874. The two quantities diverge the other way where plain missingness dominates: 25 mixed calls but 31 isolates uncalled at codon 220, 24 against 26 at 326. The denominator cost at a codon is therefore read from its n_called, not from its mixed-call tally.

[Observed] Scale and denominators. 57 codons across the nine panel genes, spanning 171 genomic positions, of which 125 carry at least one Pf8 variant record (125 records in total); a position with no record contributes its reference base, which is the correct behaviour and not an omission. Metadata transfer 211 MB, genotype transfer 2.53 GB. No codon was left without a variant record anywhere in its three positions. Per-codon denominators run from 1,834 to 1,900 of the 1,900 isolates, so every frequency in Section 6 is reported against its own denominator and the denominators are not interchangeable between codons.

[Calculated] A built-in check on the CDS mapping. The reference residue the script derives from the genome matches the published wild-type (or, at pfdhps 437, the published reference) residue at all 57 codons — C72, V73, M74, N75, K76, A220, Q271, N326, I356, R371 in pfcrt; N86, Y184, S1034, N1042, D1246 in pfmdr1; A16, N51, C59, S108, I164 in pfdhfr; I431, S436, G437, K540, A581, A613 in pfdhps; F446, N458, C469, M476, Y493, G533, R539, I543, P553, R561, P574, C580, R622, A675 and the remaining nine pfk13 codons; T484, I492; G50, R100, E107; S160; S258, F313. Agreement at all 57, across three minus-strand genes (pfk13, pfmdr2, pfaat1) and six plus-strand ones (pfcrt, pfmdr1, pfdhfr, pfdhps, pfcoronin, pfap2mu) — the strand column of results/codon_panel_residues.csv — is a direct test of the CDS concatenation, the strand handling and the codon-boundary arithmetic, and it passes. An earlier version of this sentence said "four minus-strand genes" and "five plus-strand ones", contradicting its own parenthetical list of three; the total of nine was right, which is probably why it survived, but a wrong strand census in the one sentence offered as proof that strand handling was tested is exactly the sentence that must be right.

Two honest qualifications about the retrieval. First, the reference FASTA fetched is the PlasmoDB-54 3D7 genome while the CDS coordinates come from the PlasmoDB-71 GFF. The 3D7 assembly sequence is stable across those releases and the 57-codon reference-residue check above would have failed loudly on any coordinate drift, but the version pair is a mismatch on its face and is stated rather than smoothed over. Second, the script retrieves FILTER_PASS and is_snp but applies neither: unlike Stage 10, which kept FILTER_PASS records only, Stage 12 uses every record at the three positions. For reconstructing a codon that is the defensible choice — a non-PASS record still tells you which base an isolate carries, and discarding it would silently leave the reference base in place — but it is not what the previous section said, and the fraction of the 125 records that are non-PASS was not tabulated.

[Unknown] How many of the 125 variant records used are non-PASS, and whether any ALT allele at a panel position was an indel. Both are now answered in Section 3.10 and this entry is retained only to record that they once were not: 29 records fail the release filter, 16 carry a length-changing ALT, and no isolate carries a length-changing allele as a homozygous call. The script skips an ALT whose length differs from REF and writes a note when it does; the log reports 24 notes but prints only the first ten, all of which are mixed-call notes, so the remaining fourteen are not on the record. Re-running with the note cap lifted would settle both questions and change no frequency unless an indel ALT is present.

python step12_codon_panel.py

3.10.3 The marker list and where it comes from

Provenance of the codon list. The pfk13 codons are taken from WHO's compendium of molecular markers of antimalarial drug resistance, version 1.0, December 2025, and cover all 14 validated and all 6 candidate artemisinin-partial-resistance variants — 20 variants at 19 distinct codons, because C469Y (validated) and C469F (candidate) share codon 469 — plus four further propeller-region codons (449, 481, 515, 527), which brings the reconstructed pfk13 total to 23. Those four are described in the retrieval script's own comment as "the potential-tier codons that fall in the same region" (step12_codon_panel.py:85); that is the script's annotation and not an extraction from the compendium, so their tier is reported as unextracted rather than asserted here. The non-pfk13 codons are the 25 markers MalariaGEN itself genotypes and publishes for Pf8 — which is also what makes the per-sample concordance check in Section 3.10.6 possible — plus pfcrt 73 (needed to read the 72–76 motif as a motif), pfdhps 431, pfmdr2 492, pfcoronin 50/100/107, pfap2mu 160 and pfaat1 258/313. The previous pfk13 list was written from memory. It held 13 distinct protein changes rather than 14 (a duplicated M476I record inflated the apparent count), classified P441L and C469F as validated when the compendium classifies both as candidate, and omitted P574L, G533S and R622I altogether. R622I — the variant of greatest current concern in the Horn of Africa — was requested but never retrieved, so it became a silent non-result. That is a defect in the marker list, not in the prose, and it is why the panel was rebuilt from the compendium rather than patched.

WHO status is a property of a variant, not of a gene. The previous version of Table S11 assigned tiers to genes, which is a category error, and in one cell it compounded the error: pfmdr1 was labelled "WHO-validated (4-aminoquinolines, lumefantrine)" when the compendium contains no validated or candidate lumefantrine marker of any kind — verbatim: "The review did not identify any validated or candidate molecular markers associated with lumefantrine resistance." No lumefantrine claim rests on pfmdr1 anywhere in this manuscript. Three further genes are in the panel that the compendium does not contain at any tier — pfaat1, pfap2mu and pfmdr2 — and a fourth, pfcoronin, is in it at the potential tier only. They stay in the panel because they are scientifically interesting and cheap to genotype once the machinery exists; they must never be described as WHO-classified markers.

Table S11. The nine panel genes, the codons Stage 12 reconstructs in each, and the evidential status of their resistance determinants. The previous version of this table assigned WHO tiers to whole genes; the compendium classifies variants, so those labels were a category error and are replaced. Three of the nine genes appear in the compendium at no tier at all (pfmdr2, pfap2mu, pfaat1) and a fourth only at the potential tier (pfcoronin), so four of the nine carry no WHO classification that could support a resistance claim. Where the compendium's per-variant tiers for pfcrt, pfmdr1, pfdhfr and pfdhps were not extracted in this project, no tier is asserted rather than guessed; on that stricter reading — genes for which this manuscript asserts no tier at all — the figure is eight of the nine, and the two readings are stated separately rather than added together.

[Unknown] The compendium's per-variant tiers for the pfcrt, pfmdr1, pfdhfr and pfdhps codons in this panel, and the tier of the four extra pfk13 propeller codons (449, 481, 515, 527). Only the artemisinin section's validated and candidate lists, the lumefantrine statement and the presence or absence of the four non-classical genes were extracted. Nothing in Section 6 rests on a WHO tier for these four genes or for those four codons.

3.10.4 Frequencies, intervals and tests

Point estimates. The frequency of a residue is computed per codon, as the count of isolates carrying it divided by the count of isolates with a non-missing reconstructed codon there, within the 1,900-isolate analysis set. Denominators therefore differ between codons and between zones, and are reported per cell. Because the spectrum is complete, the frequencies at a codon sum to one; the reference residue's frequency is reported on the same footing as any other, which is what makes pfdhps G437 at 0.897 visible at all.

Wilson score intervals. Confidence intervals are Wilson score intervals rather than Wald intervals. This is not a cosmetic choice. Many codons sit at or very near 1.000 — 33 of the 57 are fixed at the reference residue in every called isolate — and the Transitional zone contributes 25 isolates. A Wald interval at p-hat = 0 or 1 has zero width, and at small n it can extend outside [0, 1]; the Wilson interval is well behaved in both cases and has better coverage at the extremes, which is where this panel's most consequential readings sit. It is what lets a zero be reported as a bounded negative: pfdhfr IRNL at 0.000 carries the interval 0.000 to 0.002 over 1,874 complete cases, and the Transitional zone's own zero carries 0.000 to 0.133 over 25.

Heterogeneity test. Between-zone variation at each codon is tested by a chi-squared test on the four-zone count table for that residue. The test is of heterogeneity, not of a north-south trend: a significant result says the four zones differ, and the direction of any cline must be read from the frequencies themselves — the more so because the Transitional zone is the second-most-northern of the four, so a frequency ordering is not a geographic ordering. The test family is residues rather than codons, as the first sentence implies: results/codon_panel_residues.csv carries 27 residue-level chi-squared p-values across the 24 polymorphic codons, because pfdhps 436 contributes four tests of its own (Ser 4.5 × 10⁻⁵, Ala 1.6 × 10⁻³, Tyr 2.8 × 10⁻², Phe 8.1 × 10⁻²) rather than one. No multiplicity correction is applied across those 27 tests, which Section 6 states when quoting individual p-values; the findings that carry interpretive weight — pfdhfr 51/59/108 at p = 2.4 × 10⁻⁴, 4.8 × 10⁻⁵ and 9.4 × 10⁻⁷, pfdhps 437 at p = 1.2 × 10⁻⁶, and pfaat1 S258L at p = 1.9 × 10⁻¹⁸ — would survive any conventional correction over 27 tests, the largest of them sitting well under 0.05/27 = 1.9 × 10⁻³. Codons fixed at the reference residue have no test and the chi-squared column is empty for them, which is reported as absence of a test rather than as a non-significant result.

[Observed] Coverage of the panel. 57 codons reconstructed across nine genes; 24 polymorphic in these 1,900 isolates and 33 fixed at the reference residue in every called isolate. The 24 polymorphic codons carry 26 non-reference residues between them, because pfdhps 436 alone shows three (Ala, Phe, Tyr) alongside the reference Ser. The polymorphic codons fall in eight of the nine genes; pfcoronin contributes none.

The pfk13 negative, stated with its two limits. All 23 pfk13 codons carry no screened resistance allele in any isolate with a call, twenty-two of them at the reference residue in every called isolate and codon 458 at 0.9995, and the twenty-third, codon 458, carries a single isolate with Asn458Asp — which is not the validated variant at that codon, N458Y — giving the reference residue a frequency of 0.9995 over 1,866 called isolates. No validated or candidate artemisinin-resistance residue was observed at any codon. Two limits must travel with that statement. First, the denominator is per codon, 1,845 to 1,876 of 1,900 across the pfk13 codons (1,834 to 1,900 across the panel as a whole), not "all 1,900 isolates at every codon"; the earlier formulation "all 14 validated pfk13 codons at 0.000 in all 1,900 isolates" is wrong on the codon count, on the denominator and on the panel it was computed from, and is withdrawn. Second, the sampling period is 2009 to 2021, so this is a historical negative and says nothing about the frequency today. What is now defensible, and was not before, is that the negative covers the complete validated set — 14 validated and 6 candidate variants, 20 in all at 19 distinct codons — rather than an incomplete list written from memory, with the tiers kept distinct.

3.10.5 Within-isolate haplotypes, on complete cases only

Why this was needed. The previous version reported the pfdhfr "triple mutant" as the range of its three marginal frequencies, 0.786 to 0.908. That is not a haplotype frequency and cannot be one: marginal frequencies say how common each residue is, not whether the three occur in the same parasite. The range also sat above the value a haplotype frequency must respect, since a haplotype cannot be commoner than its rarest component. The quoted range is withdrawn.

Method. For each named haplotype, an isolate is counted only if it has a reconstructed residue at every constituent codon; isolates uncalled at any one of them are excluded from both numerator and denominator rather than being assumed wild type. The component frequencies are then recomputed on that same complete-case set, so that the arithmetic constraint — haplotype frequency ≤ minimum component frequency — is checkable rather than approximate, and the script prints the minimum component beside the haplotype so the check is visible in the log. The full distribution of observed haplotypes is printed, not only the target, because a target frequency on its own cannot distinguish "one dominant haplotype" from "a target plus several near-competitors". Four haplotypes are reconstructed: pfdhfr 51/59/108 (IRN), pfdhfr 51/59/108/164 (IRNL), pfcrt 72–76 (CVIET) and pfdhps 437/540 (GE).

[Observed] Complete-case coverage is high: 1,876 of 1,900 isolates (98.7 per cent) are called at all three pfdhfr codons, 1,874 at all four, 1,873 (98.6 per cent) at all five pfcrt codons, and 1,888 (99.4 per cent) at both pfdhps codons. On those sets the pfdhfr triple mutant IRN is carried by 1,446 isolates, 0.771 (95 per cent CI 0.751 to 0.789) — below the minimum component frequency on the same set (51I = 0.788), as it must be, and well below the superseded 0.786 to 0.908 range. At roughly three-quarters it is common but not close to fixed, and it is not a single dominant haplotype: NRN sits at 0.116, the fully wild-type NCS at 0.091, ICN at 0.018 and NCN at 0.004. The quadruple IRNL is absent, 0 of 1,874, 0.000 (0.000 to 0.002). pfcrt CVIET is carried by 133 of 1,873, 0.071 (0.060 to 0.084), and is genuinely a unit: the only two 72–76 haplotypes observed anywhere in the dataset are CVMNK at 0.929 and CVIET at 0.071, with nothing intermediate — demonstrated here rather than inferred, as it previously was, from six similar marginals. pfdhps GE (437G with 540E) is 18 of 1,888, 0.010 (0.006 to 0.015), against GK at 0.887 and AK at 0.103.

3.10.6 Per-sample concordance against MalariaGEN's published calls

Why the previous check was not enough. The previous version validated this stage by comparing marginal frequencies against MalariaGEN's published marker genotypes and reporting that "21 of 23 markers agree within 0.03". Two things are wrong with that. Agreement of marginal frequencies does not establish that two pipelines called the same isolates the same way — two pipelines could disagree on every isolate and still produce identical frequencies — and the two markers that did not agree were pfcrt 75, where the disagreement was not a tolerance failure but the structural failure of Section 3.10.1. The "21 of 23 within 0.03" claim is withdrawn and replaced by a call-by-call comparison.

Method. For each of the 25 codons MalariaGEN publishes for these isolates in Pf8_drug_resistance_marker_genotypes.tsv, derived from a different pipeline, the reconstructed residue is compared with MalariaGEN's call isolate by isolate, restricted to isolates where both pipelines made a call. The comparison is on residues, not on genotype codes, so it is a comparison of the biological statement each pipeline makes.

[Observed] 47,012 of 47,012 calls agree — 100.0000 per cent, across all 25 markers, with zero disagreements at any marker. pfcrt 75 now agrees exactly, 1,874 of 1,874, because codon reconstruction yields Glu where string matching yielded Asp. Per-marker denominators run from 1,834 (pfmdr1 1034) to 1,900 (pfcrt 72 and 356). Full table in results/codon_panel_concordance.csv.
