In logs/rerun_corrected.log the line "Heterozygous call rate: 0.0000" is printed under the mixed-call header and before the per-SNP resolution loop, so it is the pre-resolution rate — 194,167 of 10,845,661,093 called genotypes, or 1.8 × 10⁻⁵, which rounds to 0.0000. After resolution the rate is exactly zero by construction, every mixed call having been assigned an allele or set to missing, and isolate_allele_counts warns if one survives. The honest statement is therefore that mixed calls were rare to begin with, at under two in every hundred thousand called genotypes, which is consistent with the Fws filter having already removed most mixed infections.

[Interpreted] The asymmetry — 2.33 reference calls for every alternate call — is expected, since at a site where most isolates carry the reference allele, a mixed infection is more likely to be reference-dominant. It is not evidence of a reference bias in the resolution rule itself, though that possibility has not been formally excluded.

A different rule in the codon panel. The majority-allele rule chosen here is applied in the genome-wide callset and in step10_resistance_panel.py. The codon panel that supersedes it, step12_codon_panel.py (Section 5.7.3), does not use it: a codon with any mixed call among its three positions is reported as uncalled rather than resolved to a residue. That is the stricter choice and it is deliberate — a majority-allele call at each of three positions independently can compose a codon that no parasite in the isolate carries — but it means the two panels do not use the same ploidy convention, and the codon panel's denominators (1,834 to 1,900 of 1,900 per codon) are never larger than they would be under the Step 4a rule and for most codons are smaller — as low as 1,834 at pfmdr1 1034, while five codons are still called in every isolate.

5.4 Sample QC thresholds

Three thresholds were put to the user after the first QC run, each accompanied by the counts each option would produce. The call-rate and clonality decisions are discussed in Sections 4.2.3 and 4.2.4. The Fws threshold was retained at the conventional 0.95 (options at 0.90 and 0.80 were offered and declined).

[Interpreted] Presenting the sample counts alongside each option made the trade-off explicit, but it also means these thresholds were selected with knowledge of their effect on sample size. That is standard exploratory practice and is reported here rather than concealed.

[Unknown] Whether the 117 isolates dropped for excess missingness are a tail of the retained distribution or a distinct bad-data population cannot be established from what the pipeline recorded. The threshold is MAX_SAMPLE_MISSING = 0.20 at config.py:146, and no output preserves the per-sample missingness values it was applied to: results/sample_qc_full.csv holds the Stage-2 call rate, a different metric computed on a different variant set, and the run log names only 8 of the 117 isolates. An earlier draft asserted the tail reading; it had no source and is withdrawn. The mechanism of the filter-ordering defect of Section 5.7 does not depend on it either way — those isolates were charged against every SNP's missingness before they were removed, which cost 557 SNPs — about 14 per cent of the final panel, the measured 3,359 → 3,916 difference — whatever the shape of their distribution.

5.5 TreeMix jackknife block size

[Observed] Problem. The supplied configuration used 500 SNPs per jackknife block.

[Calculated] On the 3,359-SNP matrix current when the choice was made, that gives 6 or 7 blocks; on the final 3,916-SNP matrix it gives 7 or 8. Either way it is far too few for stable standard errors, which in turn makes the migration-edge tests unreliable.

[Calculated] Decision. Reduced to 50 SNPs, giving approximately 67 blocks on the matrix of the day and 78 on the final one. With retained SNPs averaging roughly 6 kb apart across the ~23.3 Mb genome, a 50-SNP block spans about 300 kb, which comfortably exceeds the scale over which linkage disequilibrium persists in P. falciparum. The block therefore remains large enough to absorb linkage while being small enough to give a usable number of replicates. A 100-SNP alternative was offered and declined; the figure actually put to the user was about 34 blocks, computed on the 3,359-SNP matrix, which on the final matrix would be 39.

[Observed] The decision could not be judged until the re-run. The sweep was originally executed without TreeMix's -se flag, so no standard errors were produced at any block size and the block-size choice had no observable consequence (Section 5.7, Table S19, Pass 2 — the defect was raised by the audit, not by the pre-run review). With -se now set, the two edges at m = 2 carry standard errors of 0.048 and 0.100 on weights of 0.152 and 0.805, giving p = 7.5 × 10⁻⁴ and 3.3 × 10⁻¹⁶, while the third edge added at m = 3 has weight 0.065 against a standard error of 0.147 (p = 0.328). Roughly 78 blocks is therefore enough to separate a supported edge from an unsupported one, which is the property the reduction was intended to buy.

5.6 BayPass calibration

Three runs, three thresholds. Three BayPass runs are referred to across this manuscript, and they are named consistently from here on. The manuscript run is the 8-thread run on the 3,359-SNP matrix whose numbers the original text reported: threshold 11.232, 15 SNPs above it (0.45 per cent of that scan), 11 annotated genes; it is archived under results/baypass_8thread_archive/. An 8-thread re-run of the same input with the same -seed 42 is the reproducibility check described below, and it gave 16. The interim single-threaded run, still on the 3,359-SNP matrix, gave 11.047 and 17 outliers and is recorded in corrected/xtx_calibration.json. The final run is single-threaded on the 3,916-SNP matrix: 10.840 and 19 outliers. Naming them separately matters because the corrections document records the threshold moving 11.232 → 11.047 while the addendum records 11.232 → 10.840; both are correct and they describe different steps, and "the interim 3,359-SNP matrix" must not be used as a label for the manuscript run.

Problems found. Three in the supplied step08b_baypass.sh, and — as the audit later established — one in the corrections applied to it.

The pseudo-observed data (POD) simulation passed the .geno matrix directly as sample.size. That file interleaves reference and alternate counts, giving 2 × npop columns, whereas simulate.baypass requires npop columns of total haploid sample size per SNP. The call would have failed, and the script would have fallen through to an empirical quantile.

Only 1,000 pseudo-observed SNPs were simulated. The threshold is the 0.999 quantile of their XtX, so it would have rested on a single simulated value.

A stale G.pod from a previous run was not removed before each run, and an R failure aborted the stage under set -e instead of falling through to the documented empirical fallback.

Corrections. Reference and alternate columns are now summed to total haploid counts; the simulation uses 100,000 pseudo-observed SNPs, which places of order 100 values above the 0.999 quantile rather than one; a stale G.pod is deleted before each run; and an R failure now falls through to the empirical fallback.

A fourth correction was made for the wrong reason, and is withdrawn. The earlier version of this section stated that the simulation applied no minor-allele-frequency filter while the observed SNPs had been filtered at MAF ≥ 0.05, and that setting pi.maf = 0.05 corrected the mismatch by matching the two spectra. It does not. In baypass_utils.R, pi.maf truncates the simulated ancestral frequency π to [pi.maf, 1 − pi.maf]; per-population frequencies are then drawn around π through the covariance matrix, scaled by √(π(1 − π)) and clipped to [0, 1]; and only then are genotype counts drawn binomially from those per-population frequencies. It is the population-frequency step, not the binomial draw alone, that pushes realised minor-allele frequencies well below 0.05. 0.05 is also simulate.baypass's own default, so the change restored a default rather than imposing a filter. The pseudo-observed set is not MAF-matched, and the claim that it was — made in this section and twice elsewhere in the manuscript — was false.

[Observed] Measured during the audit on the superseded 3,359-SNP run: 9,396 of the 100,000 pseudo-observed SNPs (9.4 per cent) have realised MAF below 0.05, against none of the observed SNPs, and the pseudo-observed spectrum is shifted upward overall (median MAF 0.212 against 0.136 observed). The two spectra differ throughout their range, not only at the boundary.

[Observed] The stated reason for the threshold moving was also wrong. The first calibration, on 1,000 unfiltered pseudo-observed SNPs, gave a threshold of 8.375 and 40 outliers (1.19 per cent of the then-current scan); recalibrating on 100,000 gave 11.232 and 15 (0.45 per cent), the figures the manuscript run reported. The earlier text attributed that move to MAF matching, reasoning that rare simulated sites have low XtX and so depress the upper quantile, and concluded that the 25 SNPs between the two thresholds had "failed calibration". Neither the mechanism nor the conclusion survives measurement. Across 200 random 1,000-SNP subsamples of the same 100,000-SNP pseudo-observed set — with no MAF difference between them at all — the 0.999 quantile ranges from 8.988 to 13.248 (median 10.59) and the implied outlier count from 7 to 41. Both originally reported values fall inside that range. The correlation between pseudo-observed MAF and pseudo-observed XtX is −0.029, so the asserted mechanism is essentially absent; and MAF matching done properly moves the threshold in the opposite direction, to 10.53 when the pseudo-observed set is restricted to realised MAF ≥ 0.05 and to 10.59 ± 0.21 when it is importance-resampled to the observed MAF histogram.

Correction. The 1,000-SNP calibration was not wrong because it was unfiltered. It was wrong because a 0.999 quantile estimated from 1,000 values is unstable by roughly ±2 XtX units. The claim that the 25 intermediate SNPs "failed calibration" is withdrawn: they fell between two estimates of the same quantity, one of which was too noisy to rank them either way. The correction to 100,000 SNPs remains an improvement; the explanation offered for it did not.

[Observed] Reproducibility. BayPass above one thread is not reproducible. With -seed 42 fixed, the manuscript run and an 8-thread re-run of the same input differed by up to 1.2 in XtX, two fresh 8-thread runs of that input differ by up to 1.1 and give 15 against 16 SNPs above the then-reported threshold, and two single-threaded runs are bitwise identical. NTHREADS now defaults to 1 in step08b_baypass.sh, and run_all.sh exports NTHREADS=1 in parallel mode; the core model takes seconds at this scale, so determinism is effectively free. Re-running the same 3,359-SNP input single-threaded — the interim run — gave 11.047 and 17 outliers against the manuscript run's 11.232 and 15, which is the size of the non-determinism in the numbers that were originally reported.

[Observed] Final calibration. On the final 3,916-SNP matrix, the single-threaded run gives a 0.999 pseudo-observed quantile of 10.840 (10.839767826730082 as written). Observed XtX spans 2.48 to 26.44 with a median of 3.45. Nineteen SNPs exceed the threshold, 0.49 per cent of the scan, against 3.916 expected by construction — an expected-null-exceedance ratio of 20.6 per cent. Thirteen of the nineteen also pass Benjamini–Hochberg at q ≤ 0.10; the counts at the neighbouring thresholds are 8 at q ≤ 0.01, 10 at q ≤ 0.05 and 17 at q ≤ 0.20. Of the nineteen, 17 fall in 13 annotated genes and two are intergenic, one of them the fifth-strongest SNP in the whole scan (XtX 20.79, q = 0.005) — so the gene list of Section 6.8 is 13 genes carrying 17 outlier SNPs, and the scan is not "19 outliers in 13 genes".

[Interpreted] The nineteen are a ranking and the thirteen are the finding, and both are reported. The quantile list defines the set on which the downstream gene annotation and interpretation were built, so suppressing it would misrepresent how the analysis was done; the q ≤ 0.10 subset is what survives multiplicity correction and is what any claim should rest on. The two intergenic outliers are part of the nineteen and are not represented in the gene list at all, which is a further reason to quote both counts rather than either alone.



Figure S8. Observed XtX along the genome against the pseudo-observed null, with the 0.999 quantile threshold at 10.840. Nineteen SNPs exceed the threshold; thirteen of them also pass Benjamini–Hochberg at q ≤ 0.10, and seventeen fall in annotated genes. Source: results/baypass/ (final single-threaded run, 3,916 SNPs).

Caveat. Two calibration weaknesses remain and are not corrected in the reported numbers. First, the pseudo-observed set is still not MAF-matched; because proper matching lowers the threshold, the reported threshold is the conservative one and matching would admit more outliers, not fewer. Second, the pseudo-observed and observed XtX distributions do not agree well through the body of the range — on the superseded run the audit measured a Kolmogorov–Smirnov D of 0.158 below XtX 8, with the pseudo-observed distribution stochastically larger through the body and markedly shorter in the extreme tail. Distributional overlap is the only model-adequacy check this analysis performs, and it is weaker than §8.1 previously claimed.

5.7 Defects found and corrected

A systematic review of the unexecuted stages was carried out before they ran, and ten defects were corrected on that basis. Three further rounds followed: an adversarial audit of the completed analysis, which raised 110 findings and confirmed 61 under three-lens verification; the corrected re-run itself, which found defects both in the pipeline and in the corrections written to address the audit; and an external expert critique of the marker panel, all five of whose points were upheld and four of which were defects in code. Table S19 lists all twenty-seven defects now known to have been found and corrected, grouped by the pass that found them. Twenty-five are defects in code or analysis; the last two are errors in reading a source document, recorded here because they changed what the manuscript asserted. Each correction was verified either against the code path in question or by execution.

Table S19. All twenty-seven defects now known to have been found and corrected, grouped by the pass that found them. Twenty-five are defects in code or analysis; the last two are errors in reading a source document, included because they changed what the manuscript asserted. Note for readers coming from the corrections document: four of the six items its §7 still lists as outstanding — the filter order, the TreeMix sweep without -se, the study/batch confounding and the absence of FDR accounting — appear here as corrected, because that list was written before the addendum runs and is superseded here. Confirmed defects that have not been corrected are listed separately in Section 5.7.4. The original scripts remain unmodified in pf8_ghana_pipeline.zip in the project root.

5.7.1 The ingest filter on PASS biallelic SNPs: the first defect that changed a conclusion

Of the twenty-seven defects in Table S19, most changed a number, a standard error, a reproducibility property or a resource bound. Three changed a conclusion, and each is separated from the rest: the ingest filter described here, the annotation-string matching that forced a complete rebuild of the marker panel (Section 5.7.3), and the misreading of the pfaat1 functional work (Table S19, Pass 4, and the note at the end of this subsection).

[Observed] step03a_fetch_callset_zarr.py builds the genome-wide callset under FILTER_PASS & is_snp & numalt == 1. Read as English, that is "PASS, SNP, biallelic" — a filter so conventional that it was proposed, accepted and implemented without comment (Section 5.1). In Pf8, however, most canonical antimalarial-resistance codons are either multiallelic or carry a spanning-deletion ALT allele, so the mask excluded them, and the two tests do not contribute equally: pfcrt K76T and M74I, pfmdr1 N86Y and pfdhps A581G all fail is_snp outright, while pfdhps K540E passes is_snp and is excluded by numalt == 2. Across the nine panel genes, 13,367 PASS records enter the filter and 3,330 leave it: 10,037 are dropped, including 28 of the 44 canonical codon-allele records on the list then in use. That list has since been replaced twice (Section 5.7.3); the filter's effect on the genes' records is a property of the filter and does not depend on it.

[Observed] The analysis then searched its outlier list for exactly those loci, found none, and read the absence as biology. The abstract and §6.8 both reported that no validated antimalarial resistance locus appears among the outliers, and Objective 3 — zone-specific variants relevant to drug response — was written up as a null result. The loci had been removed from the search space before the search began.

[Observed] A new script, step10_resistance_panel.py, re-fetched the nine panel genes with the numalt and is_snp restrictions lifted; it has itself been superseded by step12_codon_panel.py, which identifies codons by genomic position rather than by annotation string (Section 5.7.3). The final panel genotypes 57 codons across the nine genes, of which 24 are polymorphic. The recovered panel does not merely fill a gap; it reverses the finding. pfaat1 S258L — a variant with a demonstrated but modest, epistasis- and background-dependent effect that potentiates chloroquine resistance, and which is named twice in the project's own thesis proposal — runs from 0.824 in coastal savanna to 0.544 in Guinea savanna (0.735 deciduous forest, 0.640 transitional zone; 0.609 overall, n = 1,896), with a χ² heterogeneity p of 1.9 × 10⁻¹⁸ across zones, and carries q = 0.0049 in the XtX scan. pfdhps A437G, which was absent from the panel altogether until the fourth round, stands at G437 = 0.897 (n = 1,892) with χ² p = 1.2 × 10⁻⁶. The pfdhfr triple mutant IRN is common but not near-fixed, at 0.771 overall (n = 1,876) and 0.867, 0.788, 0.750 and 0.880 in coastal savanna, deciduous forest, Guinea savanna and the transitional zone respectively. Meanwhile all 23 pfk13 codons genotyped, covering every WHO validated and candidate variant, carry no screened resistance allele — twenty-two fixed at the reference residue, and codon 458 at 0.9995 on a single Asn458Asp call, which is not the validated N458Y — which converts an untested absence into a genuine tested negative, on 1,845 to 1,876 isolates called per codon and for the 2009–2021 sampling period.

Not a cline. The zone frequencies above should not be read as a south-to-north gradient. The transitional zone is the second-most-northern of the four zones, not the most southern, and the orderings contradict one another: it carries the highest pfdhfr Ser108Asn and Cys59Arg frequencies in the panel at 1.000 (n = 25) and the highest triple-mutant frequency at 0.880, but the lowest pfdhps G437 at 0.800, while its pfaat1 258L at 0.640 sits between coastal savanna's 0.824 and Guinea savanna's 0.544. Any single ordering claim breaks at the transitional zone, and it rests on 25 isolates from one sequencing study (Section 5.8). The correct statement is heterogeneity between zones, demonstrated by the χ² tests, not a monotone cline.

[Interpreted] An absence is evidence only if the thing absent was inside the search space, and nothing in the pipeline checked that it was. The resistance-locus panel lived in config.KNOWN_RESISTANCE_LOCI, three stages downstream of the filter that removed it, and no stage asserted that the panel's target positions were present in the analysis matrix. A single such assertion — do the codons we intend to look for survive our own filters? — would have failed at Stage 4 and prevented the interpretation entirely. This is the generalisable lesson of the defect, and it is a design lesson rather than a coding one: a pipeline that reports on the absence of named targets must verify that those targets reached the stage that reports on them. Section 5.7.3 records that satisfying this lesson was not sufficient, because it says nothing about whether the list of targets was the right list.

[Observed] Two consequences for the rest of the manuscript. The abstract's resistance sentence must be withdrawn and replaced, not softened. And the "two outlier SNPs" at AAT1 are one signal, not two: the second is Pf3D7_06_v3:1214350, a synonymous variant 883 bp from S258L, so it is a linked site rather than an independent line of evidence.

Correction: the amodiaquine framing was wrong. An earlier version of this note said that Amambua-Ngwa et al. (2023, Nature Microbiology) demonstrated chloroquine resistance for S258L, and that extending it to amodiaquine was an inference the paper could not support because amodiaquine had not been tested. Amodiaquine was tested: it is one of five drugs whose IC50 values the methods report measuring, the authors describe the observed shifts as well below the threshold associated with clinical resistance, and they explicitly reject the hypothesis that use of other quinoline partner drugs drives these frequencies, concluding instead that chloroquine was the primary selective force. The demonstrated effect is a 1.5-fold rise in chloroquine IC50 in a single chloroquine-resistant Southeast Asian line (NHP4026), with epistasis between S258L and F313S and an explicit caveat about pfcrt background; the 0 per cent to 97 per cent longitudinal rise is Gambian, 1984–2014, and the paper says nothing about Ghana. Wherever this manuscript discusses contemporary selection on pfaat1, amodiaquine may appear only as an untested possibility, set alongside historical persistence of chloroquine pressure and the documented fitness cost. The previous framing inverted the paper's own conclusion, and that is a worse error than the overreach it was written to avoid.

5.7.2 Stale output caches: the most dangerous class of defect

One defect of the audit round was found by the corrected re-run itself, and it is recorded here because its failure mode is the worst available: it produces plausible results, from the right code, and reports success.

[Observed] step05b_admixture.sh, step07b_treemix.sh and step08b_baypass.sh each guarded their expensive work with a "skip if the output already exists" test. None of them tested whether the input had changed. When the filter-order fix raised the panel from 3,359 to 3,916 SNPs, every stage dutifully regenerated its own inputs — and then ADMIXTURE and TreeMix found their previous output files on disk, skipped the computation, and printed "stage finished". Had the matter rested there, the previous day's ancestry proportions and migration edges would have been reported as results of the new matrix.

[Observed] Only Stage 8 caught it. step08c_calibrate_xtx.py asserts that its SNP position table and the BayPass output have matching row counts, because they must correspond row for row; the assertion failed and named the cause. The confirming evidence was in the timestamps: ghana_pruned.bim rebuilt at 01:27, ghana_pruned.2.Q still dated 03:57 the previous day. ADMIXTURE and TreeMix carry no comparable assertion and failed silently. All three guards now require the output to be newer than its input.

[Interpreted] The asymmetry is the point. One stage happened to assert a structural relationship between its output and its current input, and that single assertion is the entire reason a wrong set of results was not published. The two stages without such an assertion were caught by inspecting file modification times by hand — that is, by luck and suspicion, not by anything in the pipeline. Existence-based caching turns any re-run into a silent partial re-run, and because the skipped stages log success, there is no signal to investigate. Every stage should assert at least one property linking its output to the input it was actually computed from; a row count, a checksum or a timestamp comparison is enough.

5.7.3 Identifying markers by annotation string: the most consequential defect found after the audit

The panel built to repair the ingest-filter defect of Section 5.7.1 found its markers by matching SnpEff's ANN_HGVS_p protein-change strings against a hand-written list of expected changes. That scheme has two failure modes, and both occurred in this dataset. It is recorded here at length, as well as in Table S19, because of the three defects that changed a conclusion it is the one with the largest reach: it removed an entire locus from the manuscript, it produced the wrong explanation for a disagreement with an external dataset, and it was invisible to every check the pipeline performed on itself.

[Observed] A resistance allele that is itself the reference residue can never appear as an ALT change. At pfdhps 437 the 3D7 reference codon translates as glycine, which is the resistant residue; the official Pf8 methodology states the position directly, noting that "3D7 does not have the wild type allele" there. The only annotation at codon 437 is therefore Gly437Ala, the revertant. The panel searched for "Ala437Gly", matched nothing, and reported nothing — with no error and no empty-result warning, because the absence of a matching string is indistinguishable from the absence of the variant. pfdhps A437G, the most frequent pfdhps marker in the dataset at G437 = 0.897 (n = 1,892) and the second most frequent antifolate marker behind pfdhfr S108N at 0.908, was missing from the manuscript entirely rather than under-reported.

[Observed] A residue reachable only by two changes in one codon is never annotated. pfcrt codon 75 carries two variant records, 403621 (A>G, annotated Asn75Asp) and 403623 (T>A, annotated Asn75Lys). Asparagine to glutamate requires both changes together, so neither annotation names N75E, which is the residue the CVIET haplotype actually carries. The previous version of this manuscript reported the resulting disagreement with MalariaGEN as a naming discrepancy and left it at that. It was not a naming discrepancy. The call was wrong, this is the whole explanation of it, and the explanation offered instead was reassuring in a way the evidence did not license.

The fix is positional rather than textual. step12_codon_panel.py maps each requested codon to its three genomic positions through the CDS, collects every variant record at those positions regardless of numalt or is_snp, applies each isolate's alleles to the reference codon, and translates. The reported quantity is the full residue spectrum at the codon, the reference residue included, so a resistance allele cannot hide in the reference and a two-SNP residue cannot be missed. A codon with any mixed call among its three positions is reported as uncalled rather than resolved to a residue. The panel covers 57 codons across the nine genes, of which 24 are polymorphic, with Wilson intervals per zone in codon_panel_residues.csv.

[Observed] The marker list itself was the other half of the defect, and it was written from memory. The pfk13 list was not transcribed from an authoritative source. It contained 13 distinct protein changes, with a duplicated M476I record inflating the apparent count to 14; it omitted P574L and G533S; and it described P441L and C469F as validated when WHO classifies both as candidate. R622I was on the list but was never retrieved, which under string matching produced silence rather than an error, so its absence became a silent non-result rather than an omission. The list is now taken variant by variant from the WHO compendium v1.0 (December 2025), covering all 14 validated variants (F446I, N458Y, C469Y, M476I, Y493H, G533S, R539T, I543T, P553L, R561H, P574L, C580Y, R622I, A675V) and all 6 candidates (E252Q, P441L, C469F, N537I, G538V, V568G), with each variant's tier recorded against it, plus the four potential-tier codons that fall in the same propeller region (449, 481, 515 and 527). The 20 validated and candidate variants span 19 distinct codons, because C469Y and C469F share codon 469; with the four potential-tier codons the panel reconstructs 23.

[Observed] What the rebuilt negative actually says. Every one of the 23 pfk13 codons is fixed at the reference residue, with a single N458D call giving codon 458 a reference frequency of 0.999. Two qualifications the previous version did not make. The denominator is per codon and runs from 1,845 to 1,876 of the 1,900 isolates, not 1,900 at every codon, so "all 1,900 isolates" was never the right phrase. And the isolates were collected between 2009 and 2021, which makes this a historical negative: it is evidence about the sampling period and carries no information about the present. The negative is now stronger in substance — it covers the complete validated set, including the three validated markers the old results never reported, and it reports the two candidates that had been misclassified as validated — and weaker in the form it may be stated in.

[Observed] Validation replaced, not merely strengthened. The previous panel was validated by comparing marginal allele frequencies against MalariaGEN's published genotypes, reporting agreement within 0.03 for 21 of 23 markers. That check cannot detect a per-isolate disagreement, and it did not detect the codon-75 error: two pipelines can agree on a marginal while disagreeing about which isolates carry the allele. Concordance is now computed call by call. On isolates where both pipelines call, 47,012 of 47,012 calls agree — 100.0000 per cent — across 25 markers in pfcrt, pfmdr1, pfdhfr, pfdhps and pfmdr2, with codon 75 among them, listed marker by marker in codon_panel_concordance.csv.

Caveat on the kelch13 cross-check. Pf8's own kelch13 resistance status is assigned over the whole 349–726 domain, and any missing call in that range makes the status undetermined. Only 36 of the 1,900 isolates therefore carry a complete Pf8 status. That is weak corroboration for per-codon calls made on roughly 1,860 isolates, and the two must not be presented as equivalent evidence. Note also what the 100 per cent figure above does and does not cover: MalariaGEN publishes individual genotypes for the 25 markers in the five genes named, and no pfk13 codon is among them. The pfk13 negative therefore has no per-codon external check at all, and nothing beyond the weak domain-level corroboration above.

[Calculated] Haplotypes, and a number that could not have been right. The pfdhfr triple mutant was reported as the range of its three marginal frequencies, 0.786 to 0.908. That is not a haplotype frequency, and the range could not have contained one: a conjunction of three alleles cannot be more frequent than the rarest of them, so the range's own lower bound was its ceiling, attainable only if the three mutant alleles nested perfectly. Counted within isolates called at all three codons, IRN is 0.771 (n = 1,876; 95% CI 0.751–0.789), below the complete-case 51I marginal of 0.788 as it must be. It is also not a near-fixed single haplotype: NRN sits at 0.116 and the fully wild-type NCS at 0.091. pfdhfr IRNL, the quadruple, is 0.000 (n = 1,874). pfcrt CVIET, by contrast, is genuinely a unit at 0.071 (n = 1,873) — only CVMNK at 0.929 and CVIET are observed, with nothing intermediate — but that is now demonstrated from reconstructed haplotypes rather than inferred from six similar marginals. pfdhps GE (437G with 540E) is 0.010 (n = 1,888).

[Interpreted] The lesson is Section 5.7.1's, one level up. Section 5.7.1 concluded that a pipeline reporting on the absence of named targets must verify that those targets reached the stage that reports on them. The panel built to satisfy that lesson did satisfy it: every codon on its list was retrieved, genotyped and reported, and the retrieval was asserted. It was still wrong, because the list was not the right list and the matching rule could not express two of the things on it. Checking that you found what you looked for does not check that you looked for the right things, or that your way of looking was capable of finding them. The assertions that would have caught this are external, not internal: compare the panel's membership against a published compendium, and compare its calls against an independently published genotype, isolate by isolate rather than in aggregate. Both are now in place, and neither existed before the fourth round.

5.7.4 Scope of the review, and what the discovery pattern implies

[Interpreted] Scope. The first review covered only the stages that had not yet run, and found its defects by reading code. It was not an exhaustive audit, and it missed, structurally, two whole classes of problem: anything already executed, and anything that can only be seen by comparing a result against its own inputs or against an outside source. The two first-round defects that would have produced silently wrong rather than obviously broken output — the doubled allele counts and the TreeMix selection rule — were found by reading, not by any test.

[Observed] Four passes, four methods, four new classes of defect. The audit raised 110 findings and confirmed 61, including one that invalidated a conclusion (Section 5.7.1), four that changed reported numbers, and three that concerned reproducibility or undisclosed data handling. The third pass — executing the corrections and cross-checking the new marker panel against MalariaGEN's published calls — found two more, one of them inside the corrections: the SnpEff annotation-index bug that attached correct frequencies to wrong amino-acid names, which no amount of internal consistency checking would have exposed because the frequencies were right. The fourth pass was an external expert critique of the marker panel. All five of its points were upheld and four were defects in code; between them they restored a locus that had been missing altogether, replaced the pfk13 marker list, replaced a marginal range that had been presented as a haplotype frequency, replaced the validation procedure, and inverted a published interpretation (Section 5.7.3, Table S19).

[Interpreted] The fourth successive round in which a correction contained its own defect. That is a pattern, not an incident, and it has one common thread. Pass 2 found that the ingest filter had removed the very loci the analysis reported absent. Pass 3 found that the panel built to recover them indexed SnpEff annotations positionally, attaching right numbers to wrong names. Pass 4 found that the panel built after that identified its markers by annotation string, which cannot see a resistance allele sitting in the reference or a residue needing two changes, and that its pfk13 list had been written from memory rather than from a source. In every round the correction was checked for internal consistency — against our own outputs, our own intermediate files, our own expected counts — and in every round the checks passed. What escaped each time was external correctness: whether the marker list matched an authoritative source, whether the annotation direction matched the reference genome, whether a claimed haplotype frequency had co-occurrence data behind it, whether agreement of marginals meant agreement of calls. The verification architecture was built to catch stale and mutually inconsistent numbers, and it caught them reliably. It was never built to ask whether the right question was being put to the right list.

[Interpreted] What that implies about remaining risk. The honest reading is that the rate of discovery has not decayed across four passes. Each used a method the previous one did not have — code reading, adversarial re-derivation of the results, cross-validation against an independent external dataset, and external expert critique of the domain content — and each found defects the earlier methods were structurally incapable of seeing. There is no basis in this history for assuming the fourth pass exhausted the set, and the fact that three successive corrections each contained a defect of their own is direct evidence against assuming it. The residual risk is concentrated wherever no external cross-check exists. The marker panel now has two: its membership against WHO compendium v1.0 and its calls against MalariaGEN's published genotypes, call by call on 47,012 calls. The TreeMix topology, the BayPass covariance matrix and the ADMIXTURE ancestry proportions have no independently published counterpart for these isolates, so nothing analogous is available for them, and they should be read as internally consistent rather than externally corroborated. The pfk13 negative sits between the two cases: the per-codon calls are ours alone, and Pf8's own kelch13 status corroborates them on 36 isolates (Section 5.7.3).

Confirmed defects not corrected. These are known, verified and still present in the reported results.

step09 does not URL-decode GFF attributes, so %2C leaks into the published outlier gene file, and no nearest-gene distance is reported. This matters for the two intergenic SNPs among the nineteen: one of them is 59 bp from the end of SNRPF, which the published file does not say.

The BayPass pseudo-observed set is still not MAF-matched; pi.maf truncates only the simulated ancestral frequency (Section 5.6). The reported threshold is the conservative one, so the direction of the error is known, but the methods claim had to be withdrawn rather than reproduced.

The pseudo-observed and observed XtX distributions do not agree well through the body of the range, and distributional overlap is the only model-adequacy check the scan performs.

ghanaAEZ.geojson has no citable source. It is an unattributed ArcGIS Online re-host, verified byte-for-byte (md5 96b1a7f4da62d6f0153826f41af96152), with licenseInfo and accessInformation both null and an anonymous personal account as owner. The zone join has not been repeated against an authoritative six-zone Ghana layer, which would convert the provenance gap into a sensitivity result.

Zone is confounded with sequencing study, and no analysis can remove it: it is a property of the Pf8 sampling frame, not of the statistics. It is now quantified on the 4,905 QC-passing isolates and reported as a fourth design limit rather than fixed.

The codon panel is externally validated on only the 25 markers for which MalariaGEN publishes individual genotypes. The other 32 of its 57 codons — every pfk13 codon and pfaat1 S258L among them — have no per-codon external check; for the pfk13 codons the only external counterpart is Pf8's own domain-level kelch13 status, which is complete on 36 isolates (Section 5.7.3). The pfaat1 XtX signal is not one, being drawn from the same genotypes.

The codon panel and the genome-wide callset use different ploidy conventions for mixed calls — uncalled against majority-allele (Section 5.3) — so their denominators are not directly comparable. The choice is deliberate and is documented, but it has not been varied to see whether any panel frequency depends on it.

[Observed] The reassurance that is actually earned. Against all of the above, one robustness result stands and should be stated plainly rather than assumed. The corrected re-run enlarged the SNP panel by 16.6 per cent, replaced an uncorrected quantile with an FDR-controlled list, gave TreeMix inferential support for the first time, and corrected the ploidy treatment that inflates FST by up to 31×. The conclusions did not move. Maximum pairwise FST is 0.00232 (SE 0.00053) against 0.0024 before; the ADMIXTURE cross-validation curve is still near-flat, with K = 2 at 0.83925 against K = 1 at 0.84384; and near-panmixia across Ghana's agro-ecological zones still holds. A headline that survives a materially larger panel and a stricter multiplicity correction is better supported than one that was merely never tested. Two limits on that reassurance should be stated with it. It applies to the population-structure results, which the marker-panel rebuilds do not touch; and it is an internal-consistency argument of exactly the kind that the pattern above shows to be insufficient on its own.

5.8 Decisions that may warrant reconsideration

Two of the items previously listed here have been resolved and are recorded as such, and a third — the TreeMix model choice — is now recorded as a selected descriptive model rather than resolved; the rest stand, with six additions.

Stands. The TreeMix model choice is narrowed but not closed. The earlier text retained m = 3 as a candidate on the grounds that the identifiability argument (four populations support about two edges) was theoretical, and declined to choose. With -se in place the third edge has weight 0.065 against a standard error of 0.147, p = 0.328, so m = 2 is the graph the edge tests select; but those tests are conditional on a topology, root and edge count all fixed beforehand, and the variance-explained criterion across the full m = 0-5 sweep still prefers m = 3. Report m = 2 as a selected descriptive model, with the disagreement between the two criteria stated rather than removed.

Resolved. Variant missingness is no longer measured over isolates that are subsequently discarded, which was costing 557 SNPs at no benefit — about a seventh of the corrected panel, reported elsewhere here as the 16.6 per cent uplift the fix produced (Table S19).

Resolved. The Mantel permutation tests are seeded, the negative-FST censoring is disclosed and reported both ways, and a leave-one-out jackknife is included.

Stands. Disabling the call-rate MAD rule (Section 4.2.3) was chosen on sample-count grounds after seeing the effect. A stricter absolute floor of 0.90 was offered and not taken; the data to compare it properly were never generated.

Stands. The MAF ≥ 5 per cent filter is faithful to the thesis specification but still discards 99.8 per cent of the sites that survive missingness filtering — 1,807,331 variants in, 3,916 out. The filter-order fix recovered 16.6 per cent more SNPs at an unchanged threshold, but the threshold itself has never been varied, and the BayPass scan is the analysis most limited by SNP density.

Stands. Zone assignment for Brong Ahafo and Volta was never manually reviewed against the map, as Step 2 recommended — and the map in question has no established provenance (Section 5.7.4).

New. Single-threaded BayPass buys bitwise determinism at a wall-time cost that is negligible on 3,916 SNPs. If the callset ever grows enough for that to matter, the trade-off must be re-made explicitly and stated in the methods, not reverted silently in a configuration default.

New. The transitional zone contributes 25 isolates, all from a single sequencing study, and it is the second-most-northern of the four zones, so it breaks any simple geographic ordering of the frequency and FST results as well as being the least precisely estimated. All three of the FST comparisons that previously had confidence intervals spanning zero — three of the six comparisons — involve it, and on the final matrix the one remaining such interval is Guinea savanna against the transitional zone. Its pfdhps G437 interval is 0.609–0.911 and its pfdhfr 59R and 108N intervals run to 1.000 on 25 isolates, so every panel frequency it carries is wide. Whether it should be analysed as a fourth population at all, rather than reported as too small to support a population-level estimate, is a decision that has been taken by default rather than deliberately.

New. Marker lists must be transcribed from an authoritative source and cited to it, never written from memory. The pfk13 list now comes from WHO compendium v1.0 (December 2025), variant by variant and with each variant's tier recorded; it was written from memory before that, which is how a duplicated record inflated the count to 14, how P574L and G533S came to be omitted, and how P441L and C469F came to be presented to readers as validated; R622I was on the list and was never retrieved, so its absence from the results was a silent non-result rather than an omission. The same discipline has not been applied to the non-pfk13 members of the panel, which remain a curated list with no single citable source.

New. Markers are now identified by codon position rather than by annotation string, and that is a change of method, not only of code. Any future addition to the panel must specify a gene, a codon and an expected residue, never a protein-change string, because a string cannot express a resistance allele that is the reference residue or a residue requiring two changes in one codon (Section 5.7.3). The same applies to anything else in this pipeline that selects records by matching free text.

New. Whether the panel should contain these 57 codons at all is a decision taken by accretion rather than by criterion. It began as a hand-written list, was repaired twice, and now mixes WHO-tiered pfk13 variants with genes that appear nowhere in the compendium at any tier — pfaat1, pfap2mu and pfmdr2. They are retained because they are scientifically interesting and because pfaat1 carries the strongest zone signal in the dataset, which is a defensible reason, but the panel still has no stated inclusion criterion and ought to have one before it is published.

New. Validation should be per-sample wherever an external per-sample source exists. Marginal agreement was accepted as cross-validation for two rounds and it concealed a wrong call at pfcrt 75 through both of them. Where no per-sample source exists at all — pfaat1 S258L and the three population-structure results — and where one exists but is weak, as for the pfk13 codons, where only 36 isolates carry a complete Pf8 kelch13 domain status, the methods should say so rather than leave a reader to infer it from which numbers carry a concordance figure.



6. Results

All results in this section are from the corrected re-run completed on 10 October 2026, after the seven defects listed in CORRECTIONS_AND_NEW_RESULTS.md were fixed. Unless stated otherwise the analysis set is 3,916 SNPs across 1,900 isolates, and every figure and table is traceable to a named output file. The re-run touched stages 4-9, which Sections 6.1 and 6.4 to 6.7 report. Sections 6.2 and 6.3 report stages 1-3, which the fixes did not affect and which were not re-run at that point, so their published outputs and figures carry 8-9 October timestamps (results/sample_qc_full.csv, results/dimred_comparison.csv, pf8_proof/sample_qc.png, pf8_proof/dimred_comparison_zone.png). Stage 3 has since been re-run on the 1,900-isolate analysis set as a robustness check, writing to separate _qcset files (corrected/dimred_comparison_qcset.csv, corrected/dimred_comparison_zone_qcset.png) that leave the published stage-3 outputs untouched; both runs are reported in Section 6.3. Two further checks postdate the corrected re-run and are reported where they bear: a threshold-sensitivity analysis of the dataset definition itself (Section 6.5), which overturns one conclusion, and a variant-quality audit of the selection outliers (Section 6.8). No value computed on the superseded interim 3,359-SNP matrix is quoted here as current; where an earlier figure appears it is labelled as the value it replaces.

Robustness. The filter-order fix enlarged the SNP panel by 557 SNPs, 16.6 per cent (3,359 to 3,916), on the identical 1,900 isolates, and ADMIXTURE, TreeMix and BayPass were re-run from scratch after the stale-output cache guards were fixed (Addendum G). The substantive readings all survived that change: near-panmixia, maximum pairwise FST of the same order (0.0024 to 0.0023), a near-flat cross-validation curve with its minimum still at K = 2, and isolation by distance still weak and leverage-dependent. Four specific results did move, and are reported as moved rather than as confirmations. (i) The region-level Mantel conclusion changed: significant leave-one-out replicates went from 2 of 7 to 4 of 7, the uncensored test crossed from p = 0.0509 to p = 0.0413, and the undisclosed censoring of negative FST values no longer determines significance (Section 6.5). That improvement has since been overtaken. A threshold-sensitivity analysis run after the re-run finds the region-level Mantel significant in only 2 of 6 threshold scenarios, and isolation by distance is no longer reported anywhere in this work as a supported result (Section 6.5). (ii) TreeMix model choice is now reported as a selected descriptive model at m = 2, on the third edge's own p-value and on parameter counting, while the variance-explained criterion across the full sweep still prefers m = 3; the two criteria disagree and the choice is settled on identifiability grounds, so the earlier hedge between m = 3 across the full sweep and m = 2 within the identifiable range is replaced by a stated model choice rather than by a resolution (Section 6.7). (iii) The number of FST confidence intervals excluding zero rose from three to five of six (Section 6.4). (iv) The final single-threaded BayPass run gives 19 SNPs above a threshold of 10.840, of which 13 pass FDR 10 per cent, against 15 above 11.232 in the manuscript's 8-thread run; 17 of the 19 fall in 13 annotated genes and two are intergenic (Section 6.8). An intermediate 8-thread re-run with the same seed gave a different count again, which is why BayPass is now run single-threaded; the three runs are kept distinct in Section 6.8. That a 16.6 per cent larger panel left every headline reading in place while moving these particulars is reported as a result rather than left implicit — but surviving a change of panel is not the same as surviving a change of threshold, and on isolation by distance the two checks disagree.

6.1 The analysis dataset

[Observed] The final genotype matrix is 3,916 SNPs x 1,900 isolates with 2.95 per cent missing calls. SNP counts per chromosome range from 119 (chromosome 1) to 525 (chromosome 14).

[Calculated] Mean called isolates per SNP is 1,844 of 1,900; the least-called retained SNP is genotyped in 1,710 isolates, and the worst-covered retained isolate is missing 29.7 per cent of its calls. Per-chromosome SNP counts scale with chromosome length (Pearson r = 0.79, p = 7.4 x 10-4, n = 14), but not tightly: density ranges from 125 SNPs/Mb on chromosome 3 to 328 SNPs/Mb on chromosome 4. Computed directly from interim/ghana_core_snps.npz on the analysis server.

Table S20. SNPs per chromosome in the final analysis matrix (interim/ghana_core_snps.npz). Chromosome lengths are 3D7 reference lengths; densities are calculated.

[Observed] The filtering cascade that produces the matrix is reproduced below from logs/rerun_corrected.log. The in-memory genotype array is 2,190,026 variants x 6,653 samples x 2. Depth masking at DP < 5 touches 3,709,090,355 genotype cells, and the called-genotype total falls from 12,540,727,880 to 10,845,661,093 — 13.52 per cent of called genotypes masked. 194,167 mixed calls were then resolved by read depth: 129,665 to REF, 55,582 to ALT, and 8,920 discarded as tied. A provisional variant pass of 1,634,808 sites was used to decide the sample cut, which dropped 117 high-missingness isolates; variant missingness was then recomputed on the 1,900 retained isolates, leaving 1,807,331 sites, of which 3,916 pass MAF >= 0.05.

[Calculated] The two depth-mask figures are two separate facts and not a subtraction pair, and an earlier draft of this section joined them as one: 12,540,727,880 - 3,709,090,355 = 8,831,637,525, which is not the reported post-mask total. The reconciliation is that the array holds 2,190,026 x 6,653 x 2 = 29,140,485,956 cells, of which 12,540,727,880 — 43.0 per cent — carried a call at all. 3,709,090,355 is the count of cells failing DP < 5, most of which carried no call to begin with. The number of called genotypes actually removed is the difference between the two called totals, 1,695,066,787, and that is the quantity the logged 13.52 per cent refers to.

[Calculated] Variant missingness had been measured across 2,017 isolates, 117 of which were then discarded, so every SNP was charged for isolates that did not survive. Recomputing missingness after the sample cut, at identical thresholds and on an identical isolate set, raises the panel from 3,359 to 3,916 SNPs: 557 SNPs, or 16.6 per cent (557/3,359, a percentage not printed by any log).

[Interpreted] Because the thresholds and the isolate set are the same in both runs, the gain is attributable to the order of the two filters rather than to any change of threshold, and no other difference between the runs is known that would produce it (CORRECTIONS_AND_NEW_RESULTS.md, Addenda B and H).

Caveat on the 13.52 per cent mask rate. The rate is computed over the full fetched callset — 6,653 samples, before the sample-alignment step that reduces the matrix to 2,017 — and not over the 1,900 isolates analysed, so it is not comparable with the per-isolate call rates in Table S14, which are computed after the mask and on a different sample again. Section 4 is where this scope question is discussed in full; it is flagged here so the two numbers are not read against each other. An earlier draft went further and asserted that the retained isolates have systematically higher call rates, making 13.52 per cent an upper bound on the masking applied to the analysis matrix. That claim is withdrawn as unsupported. The median call rate is 0.9962 across all 4,905 zone-assigned isolates entering QC, 0.9968 for the 2,017 that pass and 0.9956 for the 2,888 that do not — a separation of about 0.001, which carries no quantitative bound — and call rate is in any case computed on a 50,000-SNP panel with no depth mask applied, so it does not measure what the DP >= 5 floor removes. Whether the mask falls more or less heavily on the retained isolates is not established by any measurement in this work.

Table S21. Composition of the final analysis set (interim/analysis_samples.csv). Zone labels in this table and in the prose use the corrected spelling. The source layer ghanaAEZ.geojson and every downstream output file carry the misspelt string "Decidous forest", which is the value the spatial join keys on and which is never rewritten in the data; quoted file contents therefore show the misspelling (Section 2.1.1).

[Observed] Per administrative region the counts are Upper East 1,334, Greater Accra 204, Ashanti 157, Central 125, Eastern 34, Brong Ahafo 25, Volta 21 (interim/analysis_samples.csv).

[Interpreted] The design is severely unbalanced in four coupled ways, and every result in this section inherits all four. Zone is confounded with region: three of the four zones are a single administrative region, so no analysis can separate zone from region. Zone is confounded with year: median collection year runs from 2012 in the transitional zone to 2018 on the coast, a six-year spread over a period of declining national transmission. Zone is confounded with sample size: 70.2 per cent of isolates come from one region. Zone is confounded with sequencing study: four of the six zone pairs share no partner study at all, including the Coastal-Guinea savanna pair that carries every headline number (results/batch_zone_pair_overlap.csv). The fourth of these was absent from the original manuscript and is quantified in Section 6.2, where the denominator on which it is computed is also stated.

6.2 Sample quality control and study composition

[Observed] The quality-control figures below are a stage-3 product, unaffected by the corrections and not re-run; they are reproduced from results/sample_qc_full.csv on the analysis server. The study-composition and batch tests later in this subsection are new in this revision (scripts/step11_batch_confounding.py, corrected/batch_study_by_zone.csv, corrected/batch_within_zone_tests.csv, corrected/batch_zone_pair_overlap.csv) and had no counterpart in the original manuscript.

[Observed] Of 4,905 isolates entering QC, 2,017 passed (41.1 per cent). Median call rate was 0.9962 (minimum 0.7561); median heterozygous call rate 0.00036; median Fws 0.8953 (range 0.3101-1.0000), with 2,095 isolates (42.7 per cent) monoclonal at Fws >= 0.95.

Table S22. Quality-control outcomes. Categories overlap: an isolate may fail more than one check, so the flagged counts sum to more than 4,905 - 2,017. Source: results/sample_qc_full.csv.



Figure S9. Sample quality control (n = 4,905). Left to right: genotype call rate with the 0.80 floor marked; heterozygous call rate against call rate, coloured by Fws; the Fws distribution with the 0.95 threshold marked; and the lower quartile of pairwise genotype distances with the clonal threshold at 0.00005 (config.CLONAL_THRESHOLD, line 133). The panel's own printed legend reads "clonal <= 0.0001" because that label is formatted to four decimal places at scripts/step03b_sample_qc.py:460; the legend and this caption differ by rounding, not by threshold. The Fws panel shows a clear mode above 0.95 with a long left shoulder, which is what makes 0.95 a defensible cut. The clonality panel shows no comparable break, which is why that threshold required a sensitivity analysis. Generated by scripts/step03b_sample_qc.py; unchanged by the corrected re-run.

Denominator for the composition and batch figures. Every study-composition, polyclonality and within-zone batch figure in this subsection is computed on the 4,905 zone-assigned isolates that enter sample QC, not on the 1,900-isolate analysis set used in Sections 6.1 and 6.4 to 6.7. The zone counts on that larger set are coastal savanna 435, deciduous forest 815, Guinea savanna 3,605, transitional zone 50, against 204 / 337 / 1,334 / 25 in the analysis set, so figures such as "1083-PF-GH-CONWAY is 100 per cent of the transitional zone, 50 of 50" sit beside an analysis-set n of 25 for that zone and are not in conflict with it. The larger set is the right denominator because study membership and Fws are defined there, and because the Fws >= 0.95 filter is one of the cuts that produces the analysis set. Study nesting within zone is a property of the Pf8 sampling frame and is not created by the later cuts, but the counts in this subsection are not analysis-set counts and should not be read as such. Sections 4.2.2, 7.2, 7.3 and 10.1 quote these same figures and refer back to this note.

[Calculated] Two checks establish how far this denominator matters. First, the polyclonal fractions cannot be computed on the analysis set at all: all 1,900 isolates have Fws between 0.9502 and 1.0000 by construction, so the polyclonal fraction there is identically zero. Second, the zone-pair study overlap was recomputed on the 1,900 isolates and is unchanged: the same four of six pairs share no study, with the same two studies (1183-PF-GH-AWANDARE-SM, 1241-PF-GH-ANINAGYEI) shared between coastal savanna and deciduous forest and the same single study (1083-PF-GH-CONWAY) shared between Guinea savanna and the transitional zone. Deciduous forest still draws on five studies in the analysis set (157, 100, 56, 21 and 3 isolates). Computed from interim/analysis_samples.csv on the analysis server.

[Observed] The polyclonal fraction by zone, over the 4,905 isolates entering QC and in north-to-south geographic order, is: Guinea savanna 0.597 (2,153 of 3,605), transitional zone 0.500 (25 of 50), deciduous forest 0.539 (439 of 815), coastal savanna 0.444 (193 of 435).

[Interpreted] The ordering is not monotone along the north-south axis. The transitional zone is the second-most-northern of the four, lying north of the deciduous forest, and it has the lower polyclonal fraction of the two, so the gradient holds for three of the four zones and breaks at the fourth. The transitional value rests on 25 of 50 isolates and carries essentially no information. The earlier framing of this as the variable that "orders cleanly along the north-south gradient" was obtained by quoting only the two endpoints, 0.597 and 0.444, and is withdrawn. What survives is a three-zone trend in the direction transmission intensity predicts, which is weaker evidence than a clean monotone gradient but is still the largest ecological contrast in the analysis.

[Observed] The contrast is also confounded with sequencing study, and the study-associated heterogeneity is demonstrable rather than hypothetical. Holding zone constant, so that study varies together with collection year, finer geography and recruitment: within deciduous forest, which draws on five studies, the four large enough to test (the fifth, 1114-PF-PDN-DBS-GH-GHANSAH, contributes 4 isolates and is dropped at the n >= min_n guard in scripts/step11_batch_confounding.py:109) differ in Fws — Kruskal-Wallis H = 14.9, p = 1.9 x 10-3 on n = 811 — and their polyclonal fractions span 0.430 to 0.578; they also differ in call rate at p = 7.7 x 10-88. Within Guinea savanna, the six contributing studies differ sharply in call rate, p = 2.8 x 10-197, with polyclonal fractions spanning 0.532 to 0.682, but their Fws distributions are not distinguishable (H = 9.17, p = 0.103). Within coastal savanna neither test is significant (2 studies, n = 435, Fws p = 0.378). The Kruskal-Wallis p-values are tests on the Fws and call-rate distributions; no test on the polyclonal fraction itself was run, and the quoted p-values should not be attached to the fractions (results/batch_within_zone_tests.csv).

[Interpreted] Because no study contributes isolates to both Guinea savanna and coastal savanna, the between-study variation above is of the same order as the between-zone contrast it would have to be separated from. One partial mitigation is reportable: within the single study that spans Guinea savanna and the transitional zone (1083-PF-GH-CONWAY), Guinea savanna is still the more polyclonal of the two, 25 of 47 against 25 of 50, matching the overall ordering. That margin is three isolates wide in the denominator and should not be read as more than a direction. The gradient is therefore not purely an artefact of batch, but for the pairs that carry the largest differences it is not separable from it.

6.3 Ordination of the genotype distance matrix

[Observed] Scope. Stage 3 reads the published Pf8 mean genotype distance matrix rather than the 3,916-SNP genotype matrix built here, and it is now reported on two samples. The published run uses the full 4,905 x 4,905 Ghanaian block, that is, the pre-QC set of 4,905 isolates, before the Fws and clonality filters and before the variant filters that define the 3,916-SNP matrix; the 1,900 isolates analysed in Sections 6.1 and 6.4 to 6.7 are a 38.7 per cent subset of those 4,905. Stage 3 has since been re-run on that 1,900-isolate analysis set, by re-slicing the distance submatrix to the analysis samples rather than reusing the cached full-set one (scripts/step03_umap.py, which now takes --samples and --suffix). Both runs are reported below; the published outputs are unchanged and the re-run writes to separate _qcset files. Like the quality-control figures in Section 6.2, neither is affected by the corrections to stages 4-9.

[Calculated] Pairwise genotype distances are tightly concentrated: median 0.00112, with the 1st and 99th percentiles at 0.00090 and 0.00129, a coefficient of variation of 0.085, and no missing entries. Mean within-zone distance barely differs from mean between-zone distance for any zone (Guinea savanna 0.00111 against 0.00112; deciduous forest 0.00114 against 0.00112; coastal savanna 0.00108 against 0.00110; transitional zone 0.00125 against 0.00117). Computed from interim/ghana_dist_submatrix.npy; these summaries are not printed by the pipeline and are tagged accordingly.



Figure S10. UMAP, t-SNE and PCoA of the same 4,905 x 4,905 Ghanaian distance matrix, coloured by agro-ecological zone. Point counts per zone are in the legend. PCoA axes carry their variance proportions; UMAP and t-SNE axes are in arbitrary units and inter-cluster distances in those two panels carry no quantitative meaning. Note that this is the pre-QC sample of 4,905 isolates; Figure S10a is the same three methods on the 1,900 analysed elsewhere. Generated by scripts/step03_umap.py.

Table S23. Quantitative comparison of the three ordinations on the 4,905 pre-QC isolates (results/dimred_comparison.csv). Trustworthiness is computed with k = 30 neighbours; the Spearman correlation uses a 2,000-isolate random subset; silhouette is computed against zone labels, where values near zero indicate overlapping groups and negative values indicate that isolates sit closer to other zones than to their own. Table S23a gives the same statistics on the 1,900-isolate analysis set.

[Observed] PCoA's first two axes explain 0.71 and 0.59 per cent of positive-eigenvalue variation respectively, 1.29 per cent together. The eigendecomposition returned 1,347 negative eigenvalues summing to -0.000587 against a positive sum of 0.00365, indicating the distance is not perfectly Euclidean; proportions are reported relative to the positive eigenvalues, the usual convention (logs/step03_dimred.log).

[Interpreted] Three readings follow. First, the silhouette on the original matrix is -0.016, essentially zero: zone labels do not describe the genetic variation at this level, and no embedding could make them do so. Second, PCoA preserves global structure best, with a Spearman correlation of 0.322 between embedded and original distances against 0.115 for t-SNE and 0.029 for UMAP, which is expected of a linear method and makes it the one to read for overall structure here. That is a claim about global distance preservation only: on the local trustworthiness statistic in Table S23 the three methods lie within 0.02 of each other and t-SNE scores highest, so neither metric makes any one method better in general. Third, trustworthiness near 0.5 for all three is not a failure of the methods: when distances are as uniform as these, neighbourhoods are nearly arbitrary and no two-dimensional projection can preserve them. The tight clumps visible in the UMAP panel are not explained by anything computed here. They are not zone clusters — the UMAP silhouette of -0.306 establishes that much — but their composition was not checked, and the clonality filter flags only 77 isolates in the whole set, too few to account for the clumping on their own.

[Observed] The same ordinations on the analysis set. Re-run on the 1,900 isolates, the silhouette by zone on the raw distances is -0.020, against -0.016 on the 4,905. Trustworthiness is slightly higher for all three embeddings (UMAP 0.499, t-SNE 0.526, PCoA 0.510, against 0.486, 0.507 and 0.495), and the PCoA distance correlation falls from 0.322 to 0.287. The UMAP distance correlation changes sign, from 0.029 to -0.066. The per-method silhouettes move in both directions and all remain negative (corrected/dimred_comparison_qcset.csv).

Table S23a. The three ordinations recomputed on the 1,900-isolate analysis set, beside the published 4,905-isolate run (results/dimred_comparison.csv, corrected/dimred_comparison_qcset.csv). Both columns read the same published Pf8 distance matrix; the right-hand column re-slices it to the analysis samples. Settings are identical (trustworthiness at k = 30; silhouette against zone labels). This table is new in this revision and is lettered so that the existing table numbering is unchanged.



Figure S10a. UMAP, t-SNE and PCoA of the 1,900 x 1,900 submatrix of the same Pf8 distance matrix — the analysis set used in Sections 6.1 and 6.4 to 6.7 — coloured by agro-ecological zone, with point counts per zone in the legend. Compare Figure S10, which is the same three methods on the 4,905 pre-QC isolates. PCoA axes carry 1.1 and 0.9 per cent of positive-eigenvalue variation; UMAP and t-SNE axes are in arbitrary units and inter-cluster distances in those two panels carry no quantitative meaning. The legend retains the misspelt source-layer label "Decidous forest" (Section 2.1.1). Generated by scripts/step03_umap.py with --samples interim/analysis_samples.csv and --suffix _qcset.

[Interpreted] Removing the polyclonal and clonally redundant isolates does not sharpen the structure. The silhouette by zone becomes slightly more negative, -0.020 against -0.016, which is the opposite of what would be seen if zone signal had been diluted by mixed infections. The small rises in trustworthiness are not evidence of better-resolved structure either: trustworthiness is computed on a sample 61 per cent smaller, where fewer isolates compete for each neighbour rank, and all six values still sit near 0.5. The one movement with a plain reading is the fall in the PCoA distance correlation, 0.322 to 0.287: the best-performing embedding reconstructs the distances slightly less well on the filtered set. A tight detached clump also remains in both the UMAP and the t-SNE panels of Figure S10a after the filters, so the 77 clonally redundant isolates and the polyclonal infections do not account for the clumping noted above; what does is still not established. The substantive gain here is one of reporting rather than of result: the ordinations can now be read on the same 1,900 isolates as the rest of Section 6, and on that sample they say what they said before.

Not an independent confirmation. The ordinations read a different input from Sections 6.4 to 6.7 — the published Pf8 mean genotype distance matrix, not the 3,916-SNP panel built here — so their agreement with those sections remains weak corroboration rather than a replication, and that is unchanged by the re-run. The sample objection, however, is now answered. The published run is computed over isolates that include the polyclonal and clonally redundant infections the rest of the analysis excludes, and polyclonality itself varies by zone (Section 6.2); restricting to the 1,900 analysis-set isolates removes that difference, and the near-zero silhouette survives it (Table S23a).

Reproducibility incident in this re-run. The --suffix patch to scripts/step03_umap.py suffixed the file writes but not the print statements, so the run reported writing to the published results/dimred_comparison.csv when it had in fact correctly written dimred_comparison_qcset.csv. No data were at risk and nothing in Table S23 is affected: the published file's modification time and contents were checked and are unchanged. A log that misreports its own output path is nonetheless exactly the defect that later causes one run's numbers to be attributed to another, which is why it is recorded rather than quietly fixed. The print statements now carry the suffix.

6.4 Pairwise differentiation between zones



Figure S11. Pairwise Hudson FST between agro-ecological zones (3,916 SNPs, 1,900 isolates). Note the colour-bar scale: the maximum value is 0.0023. Generated by scripts/step04b_fst.py.

Table S24. Pairwise Hudson FST, ordered by magnitude (results/fst_pairwise.csv). Standard errors are from a block jackknife. Great-circle distances between zone centroids are from results/geographic_distance_km_zone.csv. One of the six confidence intervals spans zero: Guinea savanna against the transitional zone. The small-sample caveat for the transitional zone rests on its n = 25 and its wide standard errors, not on its confidence intervals, two of which now exclude zero.

[Observed] All six values lie below 0.0024. Five of the six confidence intervals exclude zero; one, Guinea savanna against the transitional zone, spans it. Two of the five that exclude zero do so narrowly: Coastal-Transitional has a lower bound of 0.00006 and Deciduous forest-Transitional of 0.00017.

[Observed] This corrects two separate errors in the original manuscript. The abstract stated that two of six intervals span zero and Section 6.4 stated three; on the 3,359-SNP matrix the correct figure was three, so the abstract was wrong and the section was right. On the corrected 3,916-SNP matrix the figure is one, because the larger panel tightened the two transitional-zone intervals enough to exclude zero. Neither of the earlier counts, and no statement that all three transitional-zone pairs span zero, should be quoted anywhere in this work.

[Interpreted] The two geographically extreme zones (coastal and Guinea savanna, 557 km apart) are the most differentiated and the two adjacent southern zones (coastal and deciduous forest, 145 km apart) the least, but the full ordering is not monotone in distance: deciduous forest against Guinea savanna, 493 km apart, is less differentiated than coastal against the transitional zone at 282 km. The earlier claim that the ranking is "consistent with distance" overstates a four-point pattern, and the formal test at zone level is not significant (Section 6.5).

[Interpreted] An FST of 0.0023 means roughly 0.23 per cent of genetic variation is attributable to zone membership. Published within-country FST for P. falciparum routinely falls below 0.05, and continental-scale African subpopulation structure is itself modest (Amambua-Ngwa et al., 2019). The Ghanaian values sit an order of magnitude below even that within-country expectation. The practical reading is that these zones are not demographically separate populations; they are one population sampled in four places.

[Interpreted] Statistical against practical significance. Five pairs are statistically distinguishable from zero, which with 1,900 isolates and 3,916 SNPs is a low bar, and the number rose from three to five purely by adding 557 SNPs. None is practically significant in the sense of implying restricted gene flow. A resistance allele arising in Accra would be expected to reach Bolgatanga; the question these data cannot answer is how quickly.

Two confounds specific to this table. Time. The transitional zone has a median collection year of 2012 against 2018 on the coast, a six-year offset, so the two transitional-zone comparisons that newly exclude zero are precisely those most exposed to temporal rather than spatial differentiation. The headline Coastal-Guinea pair is one median year apart and is correspondingly the most defensible as a spatial contrast. Batch. The Coastal-Guinea pair shares no sequencing study at all, and the two pairs that do share studies (Coastal-Forest, 2 studies; Guinea-Transitional, 1 study) are the two lowest values in the table. The study overlap is reported in results/batch_zone_pair_overlap.csv on the 4,905-isolate set; see the denominator note in Section 6.2, and note that the same four of six pairs share no study when the overlap is recomputed on the 1,900 isolates used here. Neither confound is corrected for anywhere in this analysis, and neither can be with this design.

6.5 Isolation by distance

[Observed] At region level (7 populations, 21 pairs, distances 87-578 km) the Mantel correlation is r = 0.427 with p = 0.0252 against 9,999 permutations, one-sided, seeded at 42. At zone level (4 populations, 6 pairs, distances 145-557 km) it is r = 0.471 with p = 0.1218. These are the values at the published thresholds; the threshold-sensitivity analysis later in this subsection is what decides how much weight they will bear, and the answer is: very little.

[Observed] Disclosure of censoring. The region-level test is run on FST/(1 - FST), and three of the 21 region pairs have negative point estimates of FST, which step06_mantel.py floors to zero before the linearisation. In the original manuscript this truncation was not disclosed anywhere, in the methods, the results or the figure caption, and the affected pairs were described only as sitting "at or very near zero differentiation". They do not sit near zero; they are negative and were censored. The three pairs and their values are now printed by the pipeline:

3 of 21 pairs have negative FST and are floored to zero:
    Ashanti - Volta:          -0.001037
    Eastern - Greater Accra:  -0.000203
    Greater Accra - Volta:    -0.000142

Table S25. The region-level Mantel test with and without the flooring of three negative pairwise FST values (results/mantel_result_region.json). At zone level no pair is negative, so the two versions coincide at r = 0.4709, p = 0.1218.

[Observed] On the final matrix the censoring no longer determines the result: both versions clear p < 0.05, and the difference between them is 0.013 in r and 0.016 in p. This was not true of the interim 3,359-SNP matrix, where censoring moved the test across the 0.05 line (p = 0.051 uncensored against 0.030 censored) and the undisclosed truncation was therefore result-determining. The disclosure is required either way, and both versions are reported as a standing practice rather than because the conclusion depends on it.



Figure S12. Isolation by distance at administrative-region level: linearised differentiation FST/(1 - FST) against log great-circle distance, 21 region pairs, with the least-squares line. Mantel r = 0.427, p = 0.025 (9,999 permutations, one-sided, seed 42). The three points lying exactly on zero (Ashanti-Volta, Eastern-Greater Accra, Greater Accra-Volta) are the censored negative FST pairs listed above, plotted at their floored value. This is the baseline scenario only; Table S26a shows that the fitted relationship is not significant under four of the six threshold choices. Generated by scripts/step06_mantel.py.

[Observed] A Mantel correlation driven by one population is a single contrast, not a distance gradient. The leave-one-population-out jackknife below refits the region-level test seven times, dropping one region each time.

Table S26. Leave-one-region-out Mantel jackknife (results/mantel_jackknife_region.csv). The result is significant in 4 of the 7 replicates. At zone level the equivalent jackknife is significant in 0 of 4 replicates. This jackknife varies which populations enter the test; Table S26a varies the thresholds that decide which isolates and which sites exist at all.

[Interpreted] The signal is leverage-dependent in a specific and interpretable way. Significance is lost in exactly three of the seven replicates: dropping either of the two southern regions that define the short-distance end (Greater Accra, p = 0.232; Eastern, p = 0.136) or the single northern region that defines the long-distance end (Upper East, p = 0.218). Of the remaining four, dropping Brong Ahafo, Ashanti or Volta raises the correlation (r = 0.609, 0.568 and 0.546 against 0.427 on the full data), while dropping Central lowers it slightly and leaves it only just significant (r = 0.396, p = 0.043). The pattern is that of a north-south contrast between Upper East and the southern regions, with a weak and noisy distance relationship among the southern regions themselves, rather than a smooth gradient operating at all scales. On the interim 3,359-SNP matrix only 4 of 7 replicates were significant, so the corrected panel did improve this jackknife; the threshold analysis immediately below shows that the improvement does not generalise beyond the published thresholds.

[Observed] Sensitivity to the two thresholds that define the dataset. The jackknife varies the populations. It does not vary the two filters that decide which isolates and which sites exist at all: the Fws >= 0.95 monoclonality cut, which discards 57.3 per cent of isolates and does so unevenly by zone (Section 6.2), and the MAF >= 0.05 floor, which discards 99.8 per cent of candidate sites. scripts/step13_sensitivity.py loads the 2.19 M x 4,682 genotype matrix once, rebuilds the analysis matrix under six threshold scenarios, and recomputes the full pairwise FST table and the region-level Mantel test for each.

Table S26a. Threshold sensitivity of the region-level Mantel test and of maximum pairwise FST (results/sensitivity_summary.csv, scripts/step13_sensitivity.py).
