Only the named threshold differs from the baseline in each row. "p (as run)" floors negative pairwise FST to zero, as step06_mantel.py does; "p (uncensored)" retains them, following Table S25. Negative pairs floored, in row order: 3, 4, 3, 0, 4, 4. All tests are one-sided over 9,999 permutations at seed 42. This table is new in this revision and is lettered so that the existing table numbering is unchanged.

[Observed] The region-level Mantel test is significant in 2 of the 6 scenarios. It holds at the published thresholds (p = 0.0252) and marginally at MAF >= 0.02 (p = 0.0467). It fails at every relaxation of the Fws cut — p = 0.0874 at 0.90, 0.1687 at 0.80, 0.1792 with no monoclonality filter at all — and at MAF >= 0.01 (p = 0.0617). On the uncensored test, which Table S25 reports beside the floored one as standing practice, only the baseline clears 0.05: the MAF >= 0.02 scenario moves to p = 0.0502. The correlation itself does not simply weaken with every change — it is 0.432 and 0.434 in the two MAF scenarios, slightly above the baseline 0.427 — so what the relaxations mainly do is remove the permutation significance, not reverse the sign of the relationship.

Isolation by distance is withdrawn as a supported result. Two checks now bear on this test, they vary different things, and they agree. The leave-one-region-out jackknife (Table S26) finds it significant in 4 of 7 replicates, with significance collapsing whenever the region defining either end of the distance range is dropped — Upper East at the long end, Greater Accra or Eastern at the short end. The threshold sensitivity (Table S26a) finds it significant in 2 of 6 scenarios, and the relaxation that most directly tests the filter doing the most work — removing the Fws cut, which more than doubles the isolate count to 4,403 — moves p from 0.0252 to 0.1792. The honest statement is that the isolation-by-distance result holds at the published thresholds and almost nowhere else. The original manuscript's "weak but detectable" distance effect, retained through every earlier revision of this section including the one that reported the jackknife improving from 2 of 7 to 4 of 7, cannot be retained now, and the grading of this result in Sections 1, 7 and 10 as supported or moderately supported must change with it. This is the only positive spatial finding in the analysis and it is the one that did not survive the two checks the audit identified as most needed. It is reported here in full rather than deleted — the point estimate at the published thresholds is unchanged and a reader is entitled to it — but it should not be cited as evidence of distance-limited gene flow in Ghana.

[Interpreted] The same analysis strengthens the headline conclusion. Maximum pairwise FST moves only between 0.00204 and 0.00255 across all six scenarios, a total spread of 0.0005, over sample sizes from 1,900 to 4,403 isolates and panels from 3,275 to 10,876 SNPs. Near-panmixia is therefore insensitive to both of the thresholds that define the dataset, which is the strongest single piece of evidence for it in this work: the two filters that could most plausibly have manufactured the result do not move it. Two secondary readings follow from the direction of the small movements. Relaxing the Fws cut raises FST (0.00232 at the baseline to 0.00255 at Fws >= 0.80 and 0.00254 with no cut), so the excluded polyclonal isolates were not masking differentiation; if anything they carry marginally more of it. Tripling the panel by lowering the MAF floor to 0.01 lowers it (0.00204), as expected if rarer variants are less differentiated between zones than common ones. Neither movement approaches a magnitude that would change any conclusion drawn in Section 6.4.

[Interpreted] Three further qualifications apply to the Mantel tests themselves. First, the zone-level test has the larger correlation but the larger p-value, which is a power effect rather than a contradiction: with four populations only 4! = 24 distinct orderings exist, so the finest attainable p-value is 1/24 = 0.042 and significance requires essentially the single best permutation. The region-level test, with 5,040 orderings, is the better powered of the two and is the one reported above. Second, the zone-level p-value is reported as a Monte Carlo estimate from 9,999 permutations when the exact value is enumerable over all 24 orderings; this remains outstanding and the estimate should be replaced by the exact figure. Third, several of the 21 region pairs rest on small samples (Volta 21 isolates, Brong Ahafo 25, Eastern 34), and the scatter in Figure S12 is correspondingly wide.

[Interpreted] What remains, stated at the strength the evidence now supports: at the published thresholds there is a weak region-level association between differentiation and distance, substantially a single north-south contrast, which does not survive relaxation of either threshold that defines the dataset. Nothing in these data requires an ecological explanation for that association, and nothing in them establishes a distance effect either. The region-level analysis in any case says nothing about agro-ecological zones as such; the zone-level analysis, which does, is not significant on the full data (r = 0.471, p = 0.1218) and is significant in 0 of its 4 leave-one-zone-out replicates.

6.6 Ancestry inference

[Observed] After LD pruning, 3,385 of 3,916 SNPs remained. Cross-validation error was lowest at K = 2 (0.83925) and rose monotonically thereafter; K = 1, run separately as a reference, gave 0.84384.

Table S27. ADMIXTURE 10-fold cross-validation error, K = 1-8 (logs/admixture_K*.out). Lower is better. K = 1 is not part of the supplied sweep and was added so the improvement from admitting structure at all could be measured. The total range across K = 1-8 is 0.00563, 0.67 per cent of the error value; the improvement from K = 1 to K = 2 is 0.00459, 0.54 per cent.



Figure S13. ADMIXTURE cross-validation error against K. Two warnings about reading this plot. The y-axis is autoscaled over a range of 0.0056, so the apparent steepness is an artefact of the axis limits: the full range across all K is 0.67 per cent of the error value. And K = 1 is not plotted, because the sweep in config.py runs K = 2-8 and the K = 1 reference was run separately; it would sit at 0.84384, between the K = 6 and K = 7 points. Read Table S27 rather than the curve. Generated by scripts/step05c_plot_admixture.py.

[Observed] At K = 2, mean minor-component ancestry by zone is: deciduous forest 0.0921, coastal savanna 0.0851, Guinea savanna 0.0534, transitional zone 0.0419 (overall mean 0.0635). Medians are 0.0563, 0.0587, 0.0430 and 0.0397 respectively.

[Calculated] The minor component differs significantly between zones: Kruskal-Wallis H = 105.4, p = 1.05 x 10-22 (one-way ANOVA F = 12.27, p = 6.0 x 10-8). The effect is small, and the two standard effect sizes do not agree on how small: eta-squared from the one-way ANOVA is 0.019, so zone accounts for 1.9 per cent of the variance in minor-component ancestry, while the rank-based epsilon-squared from the Kruskal-Wallis statistic, (H - k + 1)/(n - k), is 0.054. The gap reflects a strongly right-skewed component: the rank test responds to the shift in medians, the variance decomposition to the much larger within-zone spread of the means. Both are reported because neither alone is a fair summary. The effect survives adjustment for data quality and sampling date: in an OLS model with call rate, Fws and collection year as covariates, the partial F-test for zone is 11.43 on 3 and 1,893 degrees of freedom, p = 2.0 x 10-7, and model R2 rises from 0.0028 without zone to 0.0205 with it. Pairwise Mann-Whitney tests locate the effect in the south-against-north contrast: coastal against Guinea savanna p = 3.9 x 10-15, deciduous forest against Guinea savanna p = 1.6 x 10-14, while coastal against deciduous forest (p = 0.20) and Guinea savanna against the transitional zone (p = 0.68) are indistinguishable. All of these were computed from results/admixture/Q_K2.csv joined to interim/analysis_samples.csv on the analysis server for this revision; the "about 2 per cent" quoted in CORRECTIONS_AND_NEW_RESULTS.md §4 is the eta-squared figure, and it was reported there alongside a superseded Kruskal-Wallis statistic (H = 112.2, p = 3.6 x 10-24) which should not be quoted.

Table S28. K = 2 minor-component ancestry by zone (results/admixture/Q_K2.csv). Kruskal-Wallis across all four zones: H = 105.4, p = 1.05 x 10⁻²². Effect size: eta-squared 0.019 from the one-way ANOVA, rank-based epsilon-squared 0.054.



Figure S14. ADMIXTURE ancestry proportions, K = 1-8, with isolates ordered by zone (n = 1,900). Each vertical bar is one isolate. No K produces components that correspond to zone boundaries as discrete blocks; the significant between-zone difference reported in Table S28 is a shift of about four percentage points in the mean of the minor component and is not visible at this scale. Generated by scripts/step05c_plot_admixture.py.

[Interpreted] The original manuscript's statement that "the minority component is present at similar low frequency everywhere" and the summary claim of "no ancestry structure aligned to geography" are both false as written, and neither was ever tested. The corrected statement is that zone affects minor-component ancestry significantly but negligibly. Significantly: p = 1.05 x 10-22, robust to call rate, Fws and collection year. Negligibly: zone accounts for 1.9 per cent of the variance on the ANOVA measure and 5.4 per cent on the rank measure, the between-zone spread in means runs only from 0.042 to 0.092, and the whole K = 2 model improves on no structure at all by 0.54 per cent of cross-validation error. The substantive conclusion the manuscript drew, that this is a population without ancestry structure of any practical consequence, survives; the way it was stated does not.

[Interpreted] The correction works in the analysis's favour. The ordering of zone means, south high and north low, is the same ordering as the pairwise FST ranking in Table S24 and the same direction as the region-level Mantel in Section 6.5. Unlike the Mantel it is not driven by a single population and it has not been shown to depend on the filtering thresholds, and unlike the FST comparisons it has been shown to survive adjustment for collection year. With isolation by distance withdrawn (Section 6.5), it is now the only directional south-north signal in the analysis that rests on more than one contrast, and the original text discarded it.

Correction to a prior observation. The original Section 6.6 stated that "at least one isolate is assigned entirely (1.000) to a single component". No isolate reaches 1.000. The extreme values in results/admixture/Q_K2.csv are exactly 0.99999 and 0.00001, which is ADMIXTURE's internal parameter bound, and 91 of the 1,900 isolates sit at that bound. The earlier sentence presented a software clamp as a property of the data, and understated the count by two orders of magnitude. What those 91 isolates are (plausibly residual near-clonal or low-coverage infections) is not established here.

[Interpreted] Whether the minor component reflects a real but geographically diffuse ancestry gradient, residual polyclonality, or an artefact of fitting two components to an essentially unstructured sample cannot be determined from these results. What can now be said is that whatever it is, it is not uniform across zones.

6.7 Population graph and migration edges

Table S29. TreeMix fit against number of migration edges (results/treemix/model_comparison.csv). Variance explained is computed over the six off-diagonal between-population covariances, matching TreeMix's own definition. All six models converged. These are the final-run values; the manuscript's 0.876 at m = 0 and 0.935 at m = 1 came from the superseded run and should not be quoted.

[Observed] The sweep was re-run with -se, so migration edges now carry block-jackknife standard errors and p-values. In the original run SE was off, every migration line in the .treeout files read weight NA NA NA, and no edge could be tested at all; the model-choice question was left open for that reason.

Table S30. Migration-edge weights, block-jackknife standard errors and p-values (results/treemix/treemix_m*.treeout.gz). The third edge admitted at m = 3 is indistinguishable from zero: its standard error is more than twice its weight.

[Observed] Both edges fitted at m = 2 are significant (p = 7.5 x 10-4 and p = 3.3 x 10-16). The third edge admitted at m = 3 has a weight of 0.065 against a standard error of 0.147 and a p-value of 0.328.

[Interpreted] A two-edge graph is the model these data select, and m = 2 is reported as a descriptive model rather than as a count of historical migration events. This narrows a question the original manuscript left open, but it does not close it the way a count of historical events would be closed. The plateau rule applied to variance explained picks m = 2 within the identifiable range and m = 3 across the full sweep, and with no edge p-values there was no way to choose between them; the manuscript retained both. The edge p-values now favour m = 2 — but they do so as a conditional test inside a graph whose topology, root and edge count were all fixed before the test was run, and they do not agree with the variance-explained criterion applied over the full sweep, which reaches 0.9997 at m = 3 and so still prefers m = 3. The two criteria therefore disagree, and the disagreement is settled here on identifiability grounds, which is a modelling decision and not a measurement. What the statistics do support is narrower: the third edge is indistinguishable from zero on its own jackknife standard error, four populations support roughly two edges on parameter-counting grounds, and m = 2 is accordingly the graph reported as a descriptive model. It is not an estimate of how many admixture events occurred in the history of Ghanaian P. falciparum, and no uncertainty in topology, root placement or edge count is propagated into it. The earlier hedging between m = 2 and m = 3 is replaced by a stated model choice, not by a resolution.



Figure S15. TreeMix model fit against migration edges. Left: variance explained, with the 0.998 reference line dotted and the m <= 2 region shaded as the range four populations can identify. Right: log likelihood, which rises monotonically with m by construction and therefore cannot by itself select a model. The diamond marking m = 3 as the selection across the full sweep is the plateau rule's choice and remains that criterion's preference; the edge p-values in Table S30 support m = 2 instead, so the two criteria disagree and m = 2 is a stated model choice rather than a superseding result. Generated by scripts/step07c_plot_treemix.py.

[Interpreted] Two cautions limit how much any of this can be made to mean. First, the fitted quantity is six off-diagonal between-population covariances, and an unrooted four-taxon tree already uses five branch-length parameters, so m = 0 has one residual degree of freedom and m = 1 has none or fewer. A rise in variance explained from 0.897 to 0.941 between those two models is not by itself evidence of gene flow, and the original manuscript's treatment of the m = 0 to m = 1 improvement as "the one substantive result" applied a degrees-of-freedom argument against m = 3 while exempting m = 1 from it. The edge p-values are the stronger evidence precisely because they do not depend on the variance-explained curve. Second, with four populations, three of which are a single administrative region each, these edges cannot be read as specific geographic gene-flow corridors. What the graph establishes is that a strictly bifurcating tree does not describe these populations, which is what would be expected of a single population with ongoing exchange, and that two directions of exchange are statistically supported.



[Calculated] Every printed edge p-value is reproduced exactly, and the apparent anomalies are an artefact of how the tail was computed rather than of the data. An earlier version of this paragraph said that five of six values reproduced to three significant figures; that was wrong, and the corrected accounting follows. Recomputing every migration-edge p-value in m = 0 to 5 as a one-sided normal tail from the printed jackknife mean and standard error gives fifteen edges, of which thirteen carry a finite printed value and two print “< 2.22507 × 10⁻³⁰⁸”. That figure is exactly DBL_MIN, the smallest normal double-precision float, and it appears at m = 3 edge 2 (z = 10.53, stable tail 3.18 × 10⁻²⁶) and again at m = 4 edge 2 (z = 9.33, stable tail 5.45 × 10⁻²¹). Of the thirteen finite values, ten agree with a numerically stable tail to three significant figures. The other three, and both sentinels, are explained — and reproduced to the last printed digit — by computing the upper tail as 1 − Φ(z) in double precision. Immediately below 1 the doubles are spaced 1.1102 × 10⁻¹⁶ apart, so Φ(z) is quantised to multiples of that spacing and the tail recovered by subtraction can only take those values. At z = 7.843, 8.060 and 8.038 the formulation returns exactly 2.220446 × 10⁻¹⁵, 3.330669 × 10⁻¹⁶ and 4.440892 × 10⁻¹⁶ — which are the three printed values to every digit printed — against stable tails of 2.1995 × 10⁻¹⁵, 3.8147 × 10⁻¹⁶ and 4.5658 × 10⁻¹⁶. Once the true tail falls below half that spacing, Φ(z) rounds to exactly 1.0 and the difference is exactly zero; that happens at z = 9.33 and z = 10.53. This is rounding and cancellation, not underflow of the tail: the true tails there are 5.45 × 10⁻²¹ and 3.18 × 10⁻²⁶, both far above the smallest normal double, and the “< 2.22507 × 10⁻³⁰⁸” string is how TreeMix prints a computed zero rather than evidence that any quantity underflowed. A direct complementary-error-function evaluation would avoid the subtraction entirely. The conclusion is accordingly complete rather than partial. All fifteen printed values, the two zeroes included, are reproduced from the printed mean and standard error through that formulation, which a run that had not computed jackknife standard errors could not have produced, resolving a doubt raised by an earlier script snapshot lacking the -se flag. None of it changes a reported result: both sentinels belong to m = 3 and m = 4, neither of which is reported, and the two values the m = 2 choice rests on are 7.48 × 10⁻⁴ and a tail of order 10⁻¹⁶ whose exact digits affect no inference.

6.8 Genome scan for differentiation outliers

[Observed] Across the 3,916-SNP matrix, XtX ranged from 2.48 to 26.44 with a median of 3.45. Calibration simulated pseudo-observed SNPs under the estimated population covariance matrix, with pi.maf = 0.05 truncating the simulated ancestral frequency rather than applying the observed data's MAF filter, so the pseudo-observed set is not MAF-matched (Section 5.6); this gives a 0.999-quantile threshold of 10.840. Nineteen SNPs (0.49 per cent) exceed it.



Figure S16. BayPass XtX genome scan on the final 3,916-SNP matrix. Left: the observed XtX distribution against the simulated neutral (pseudo-observed) distribution, with the calibrated 0.999-quantile threshold at 10.840 (on-plot label "threshold 10.8"). Right: XtX by genomic position, alternating shading by chromosome, with the threshold marked. Regenerated by scripts/step08c_calibrate_xtx.py as figures/baypass_xtx.png.

[Interpreted] Reading the left panel. The two distributions are not simply superimposed, and the difference between them runs in the opposite direction to what a casual reading suggests. The observed (blue) distribution is the more peaked of the two — a modal density of roughly 0.78 against roughly 0.59 for the simulated neutral (red) — and it is the POD distribution that carries the heavier right shoulder, holding visibly more density from about XtX 4 to 9. The observed exceeds the null only in the extreme tail: 19 SNPs, 0.49 per cent, lie above 10.840 against the null's 0.1 per cent, roughly five times the expected mass. The correct statement is therefore narrow: the body of the observed distribution is if anything slightly more concentrated than the simulated neutral one, and the excess that produces the outliers is confined to the extreme tail.

[Observed] Three BayPass runs exist and must not be conflated. The manuscript run was 8-threaded on the interim 3,359-SNP matrix and gave a 0.999-quantile threshold of 11.232 with 15 outliers across 11 genes (archived at results/baypass_8thread_archive/xtx_calibration.json). The interim single-threaded run, on the same 3,359 SNPs, gave 11.047 with 17 outliers — the value quoted in the corrections document. The final run, reported throughout this section, is single-threaded on the 3,916-SNP matrix and gives 10.840 with 19 outliers across 13 genes. The SNP panel grew by 16.6 per cent (3,359 to 3,916) after the variant-missingness filter was reordered to run after the sample cut (Section 3.x), and BayPass was moved to a single thread because the 8-thread binary is not reproducible even under a fixed seed. Every number below is from the final, bitwise-stable run.

6.8.1 False-discovery accounting

[Calculated] A 0.999 quantile is not a significance test. By construction, 0.1 per cent of neutral SNPs clear it, so at 3,916 SNPs 3.9 false positives are expected among the 19, an expected-null-exceedance ratio at the threshold of 20.6 per cent. Per-SNP p-values against the pseudo-observed null and Benjamini–Hochberg q-values were therefore computed for every SNP by scripts/step08c_calibrate_xtx.py from results/baypass/ghana_core_summary_pi_xtx.out (3,916 SNPs) and results/baypass/ghana_pod_summary_pi_xtx.out (100,000 pseudo-observed SNPs), and those two files are what this section cites as the provenance of every q-value quoted below. The derived per-SNP table, results/baypass/xtx_all_snps.csv, was found at 0 bytes and has since been regenerated; the incident and the consistency checks the regenerated file passed are recorded in the note below. The q-values themselves were never in doubt — recomputed from scratch from the two summary files above by the exact algorithm in step08c, they reproduce the results/baypass/xtx_calibration.json entry for entry (threshold 10.839768, 19 outliers at 0.485 per cent, 3.916 expected false positives, expected-null-exceedance ratio 20.611 per cent, and 8 / 10 / 13 / 17 SNPs at q ≤ 0.01 / 0.05 / 0.10 / 0.20) — and the restored table now carries them, so the artefact this text points at can support them again.

A results file was destroyed and restored, and the restoration is checkable rather than merely plausible. results/baypass/xtx_all_snps.csv was found at 0 bytes, truncated at 08:29:53 — hours after the 01:42 run that produced it, which rules out the run itself. It was the only zero-byte file anywhere under results/ or interim/. The likeliest cause is a stray shell redirect where a read was intended, during a verification pass. The file was regenerated from the intact BayPass outputs, and the regeneration is consistent with the surviving outputs rather than proven row-for-row: outlier_snps.bed, xtx_calibration.json and xtx_threshold.txt are byte-identical by md5 before and after, and the restored table has the same 3,916 rows and 19 outliers. The only other copy, results/baypass_8thread_archive/xtx_all_snps.csv, belongs to the superseded 3,359-SNP run and has no q column, so it was not and could not have been used. This is recorded rather than quietly repaired because it is a fact about this chapter's artefacts: a derived table that can be rebuilt from retained primaries, and whose rebuild can be checked by md5 identity of its downstream products, is recoverable. One that could not would have cost this section its provenance, and no amount of care in the prose would have substituted for it.

[Calculated] Counts surviving FDR control are 8 SNPs at q ≤ 0.01, 10 at q ≤ 0.05, 13 at q ≤ 0.10 and 17 at q ≤ 0.20. Six of the 19 SNPs above the quantile threshold do not pass q ≤ 0.10; their q-values run from 0.115 to 0.204.

How to read the two lists. The 19-SNP quantile list is a ranking: it orders loci by evidence of excess differentiation but makes no claim about any individual locus. The 13 SNPs at q ≤ 0.10 are the test result. The original manuscript reported only a quantile list and interpreted its members individually, which is not a defensible inference. Throughout the rest of this section the 13 are treated as the finding and the 19 as the ranking they sit inside.

Table S26. Genes intersecting XtX outlier SNPs, ordered by maximum XtX (results/outlier_genes.csv, results/outliers_annotated.txt). Seventeen of the 19 outlier SNPs fall in these 13 genes; the remaining two are intergenic and are given in the main text below, not in this table — both survive FDR 10 per cent and one of them is the fifth-strongest SNP in the whole scan, so this gene-centric view is incomplete by construction. Min q is the smallest Benjamini–Hochberg q-value among that gene's outlier SNPs, rounded to three decimal places (the eight SNPs tied at the top of the null all carry q = 0.0049). Nine of the 13 genes retain at least one SNP at q ≤ 0.10; the four below the break in evidence — PF3D7_0215100, REX2, PF3D7_0824200 and PF3D7_0221200 — do not, and should be read as ranked candidates only. The SNRPF row carries a further caveat of a different kind: its single outlier SNP, Pf3D7_11_v3:1,054,078, is downgraded to lower confidence by the call-quality audit of Section 6.8.4, so if that downgrade is right the nine FDR-surviving genes are eight and this table has 12 rows, not 13. Annotation from PlasmoDB-71 via bedtools.

[Observed] The two outliers that Table S26 cannot show. Two of the 19 outlier SNPs fall outside any annotated protein-coding gene, and both survive FDR correction, so the gene-organised table omits 2 of the 13 FDR-surviving SNPs. Pf3D7_11_v3:1,054,447 has XtX 20.79 and q = 0.0049 — the fifth-highest XtX in the entire scan, ahead of nine of the 13 tabulated genes, only four of which have a maximum XtX above it (PTP7 26.44, AAT1 24.20, FIKK4.2 22.02, ACS8 22.00). It lies 59 bp beyond the 5′ boundary of SNRPF (PF3D7_1126900, 1,052,969–1,054,388, minus strand) and 369 bp from the SNRPF outlier at 1,054,078. This same SNP is one of the two that the call-quality audit downgrades to lower confidence (Section 6.8.4), which bears directly on how much weight this paragraph can carry: the fifth-strongest signal in the scan is also the one with the weakest call support among the 19. Pf3D7_12_v3:715,788 has XtX 15.09 and q = 0.017, overlaps no annotated protein-coding gene in the bedtools intersect, and is graded clean on every quality field examined.

[Interpreted] The 19 outliers are fewer independent signals than they appear. Four of the 13 genes contribute two outlier SNPs each, and in every case the pair sits within 900 bp: AAT1 883 bp, ACS8 130 bp, PF3D7_0831400 25 bp and PF3D7_0221200 198 bp. Adding the 369 bp between Pf3D7_11_v3:1,054,447 and the SNRPF outlier — close enough, and in the right orientation, that the intergenic SNP plausibly sits in the gene's upstream regulatory interval rather than marking an independent locus — the 19 outlier SNPs represent at most 14 independent signals, and the 17 SNP–gene overlaps at most 13. Counting SNPs rather than haplotypes overstates the evidence; it is the same error the original manuscript made at pfaat1 (Section 6.8.2). The call-quality audit supplies a second and less benign reason to treat that particular pair as one item: they are the two lowest-InbreedingCoeff sites in the panel and one of them carries the panel's only nonzero ExcessHet, so they may be two signals, one signal, or no signal at all (Section 6.8.4).

[Observed] What the larger panel changed. Set-differenced against the manuscript run's 15 outliers, the final 19 contain four new SNPs: Pf3D7_02_v3:630,290; Pf3D7_09_v3:1,428,013 (REX2); and Pf3D7_02_v3:855,467 and 855,665 (PF3D7_0221200). Two genes are therefore new — REX2 and PF3D7_0221200 — and both fail FDR 10 per cent. But the larger panel did add an FDR-surviving SNP: Pf3D7_02_v3:630,290 has XtX 22.00 and q = 0.0049, the fourth-strongest SNP in the scan, and it falls in ACS8, which rises from a maximum XtX of 11.90 and last place in the manuscript's 11-gene table to 22.00 and fourth place here. The accurate summary is therefore not that the larger panel added nothing that survives correction: it extended the ranking with two genes that do not survive correction, and promoted one existing gene from the bottom of the list to fourth on the strength of a new FDR-surviving SNP.

6.8.2 The AAT1 signal, named

[Observed] The two pfaat1 (PF3D7_0629500) outliers are Pf3D7_06_v3:1,215,233 and Pf3D7_06_v3:1,214,350, 883 bp apart inside a gene spanning 1,213,102–1,217,313 on the minus strand. The first is resolved from Pf8's own SnpEff annotation as Ser258Leu, a missense_variant of MODERATE impact, XtX 24.20, q = 0.0049 — the second-highest XtX in the scan and comfortably inside the FDR 10 per cent set. The second has XtX 16.54 and the same q = 0.0049.

[Unknown] The corrections document identifies Pf3D7_06_v3:1,214,350 as Ile552Ile, a synonymous_variant of LOW impact. That codon assignment could not be reproduced from any primary output of this pipeline. results/resistance_panel_frequencies.csv holds only missense_variant records, so a synonymous site is absent from it by construction; the canonical panel lists only Ser258Leu and Phe313Ser for pfaat1; and the run logs contain no match for the position or the codon. The arithmetic is at least consistent — on the minus strand, codons 258 and 552 are 294 codons, or 882 nt, apart, against the observed 883 bp — but the identification rests on a narrative document rather than an output, and that same paragraph still carries the superseded XtX of 24.44 for the neighbouring SNP. The codon label is marked [Unknown] pending re-derivation from the Zarr. What is established is the position, the XtX, the q-value and the 883 bp separation.

[Interpreted] These are one signal, not two independent lines of evidence, whatever the second site turns out to be at codon level. Two outliers 883 bp apart in the same 4.2 kb gene are a linked pair: the second carries no independent information about selection, and the original manuscript's phrasing — "two outlier SNPs" offered as corroboration — double-counted a single haplotype. Ser258Leu is the candidate; the neighbour is what linkage disequilibrium drags along with it.

Correction. The previous description of Ser258Leu was wrong, and the error was mine. The previous version of this section called Ser258Leu "the one functionally validated resistance variant in this entire outlier list" and stated that the functional experiment did not test amodiaquine. Both statements are withdrawn. Amodiaquine was tested: the methods of Amambua-Ngwa et al. (2023) read "Parasite IC50 values for CQ, amodiaquine, lumefantrine, mefloquine and quinine were measured…", the observed shifts were "well below the threshold associated with clinical resistance", and the authors explicitly reject the other-quinoline reading — "The alternative hypothesis — that high frequencies of F313S mutations are driven by widespread use of other quinoline partner drugs in SEA — is not supported" — concluding that chloroquine was "the primary selective force". Separately, pfaat1 appears nowhere in the WHO molecular-marker compendium at any tier, so it must not be called a WHO-validated or WHO-candidate marker anywhere in this thesis.

[Interpreted] What survives the correction is still substantial, and smaller than what was claimed. Ser258Leu is the specific substitution that Amambua-Ngwa et al. (2023, Nature Microbiology) introduced by genome editing and showed to raise chloroquine IC50 — but the effect is 1.5-fold, measured in a single chloroquine-resistant Southeast Asian line (NHP4026), with epistasis between S258L and F313S and an explicit caveat that the effect depends on pfcrt background. The defensible description is therefore a variant with a demonstrated but modest, epistasis- and background-dependent effect that potentiates chloroquine resistance — not a validated resistance determinant on its own. Two further limits on transfer to Ghana: the longitudinal rise from 0 to 97 per cent that the paper documents is Gambian, 1984–2014, and the paper says nothing about Ghana; and the epistatic partner is absent here — pfaat1 codon 313 is fixed at the reference residue Phe in all 1,900 isolates (Section 6.9.2), so every Ghanaian S258L allele in this sample sits on an F313 background and the epistatic component of the published effect cannot operate here. The paper does not state that the 1.5-fold measurement was itself taken on an F313S-carrying background, so nothing stronger than that should be claimed from the absence. The narrower point that this section can carry is that the second-strongest outlier in the genome scan, and the strongest carrying any functional characterisation at all, is a transporter variant with a published, if modest, 4-aminoquinoline phenotype. The strongest by XtX is PTP7 (Pf3D7_03_v3:96,476, XtX 26.44, q = 0.0049 — the same q as pfaat1 S258L, both SNPs falling in the eight-SNP tie at the top of the null), an EMP1 trafficking protein with no resistance association. The variant surfaced unflagged because pfaat1 was absent from config.KNOWN_RESISTANCE_LOCI, the eight-locus legacy panel (pfcrt, pfmdr1, pfdhfr, pfdhps, pfk13, pfmdr2, pfcoronin, pfap2mu) — a different eight from the eight panel genes that contribute polymorphic codons in Section 6.9 — and that exclusion is the mechanism by which the positive result was missed. The cline and the drug-pressure reading are developed in Section 6.9.9.

[Interpreted] Of the remaining genes, PTP7, PIESP2, FIKK4.2, GEXP22 and PF3D7_0831400 belong to the exported and host-interaction compartment of the genome, which is where immune-mediated selection would be expected to act; REX2 ("ring-exported protein 2") and PF3D7_0221200 ("Plasmodium exported protein (hyp15)") extend that pattern but do not survive FDR correction. PF3D7_0824200 does not extend it: final_outlier_genes.csv annotates it "conserved Plasmodium protein, unknown function", which is not an exported protein, and the previous version of this sentence grouped it with the exported pair in error. Four genes in all fail q ≤ 0.10, not three — PF3D7_0215100 (RING zinc finger, 11.95), REX2 (11.34), PF3D7_0824200 (11.27) and PF3D7_0221200 (10.96) — because q ≤ 0.10 admits exactly 13 SNPs, i.e. XtX ≥ 12.70. The compartmental pattern is suggestive and is reported as such. It is not tested here: no enrichment test against a matched background was performed, so the apparent concentration of exported proteins remains an observation about a 13-gene list, not a result. What it is not is a mapping artefact of repetitive gene families: Section 6.8.4 shows that these eight SNPs are the cleanest subset in the whole panel.

6.8.3 Why most canonical resistance loci are absent from this scan

Correction. The original text of this section read: "None of the eight validated antimalarial resistance loci in the configured panel — pfcrt, pfmdr1, pfdhfr, pfdhps, pfk13, pfmdr2, pfcoronin, pfap2mu — appears among the outliers", and the abstract drew a biological inference from that absence. The statement is withdrawn. The absence was overwhelmingly an ascertainment artefact of the ingest filter, not a property of the parasite population, and nothing biological should ever have been read from it.

[Observed] step03a_fetch_callset_zarr.py builds the genome-wide callset under the mask FILTER_PASS & is_snp & numalt == 1. The numalt == 1 clause restricts the callset to strictly biallelic records. In Pf8, many canonical resistance codons are either multiallelic or carry a spanning-deletion ALT allele, so the clause removed those sites silently, before any analysis ran. Across the nine panel genes the filter sees 13,367 PASS records and keeps 3,330, dropping 10,037 (logs/step10_resistance_panel.log, lines 15–17; line 14 gives the 18,341 variant records inside the nine genes). What it dropped is specifically the codons that carry the polymorphism: every polymorphic pfcrt codon of the CVIET haplotype (codon 72 is monomorphic Cys) and of its extended resistance background — 74 (M74I), 75 (the record annotated Asn75Asp, not N75E; see Section 6.9.1), 76 (K76T), 220 (A220S), 271 (Q271E) and 371 (R371I) — together with pfmdr1 N86Y and Y184F, pfdhfr S108N, and pfdhps S436A, K540E and A581G. Codon 72, which is monomorphic Cys in all 1,900 isolates, is the exception: of its two records, Pf3D7_07_v3:403,612 has numalt 1 and survived the mask, as the note below records.

A stronger claim that the project's own output does not support. The step10 log's editorial comment, and the corrections document that quotes it, state that the numalt clause dropped every canonical codon in pfcrt, pfmdr1 and pfk13. It did not. Sixteen of the 44 canonical records in results/resistance_panel_canonical.csv have numalt == 1 and therefore passed the mask: pfcrt C72S (a CVIET-position codon), pfcrt I356T and N326S, pfmdr1 S1034C, N1042D and D1246Y, pfmdr2 T484I, pfdhfr C59R and N51I, pfaat1 S258L, and six pfk13 codons — F446I, Y493H, P553L, R561H and A675V, which are WHO-validated, plus P441L, which is a WHO candidate and which the previous version of this chapter wrongly listed among the validated set (Section 6.9.8). Ten of those 16 are at frequency 0.000 in this sample, which is why they were invisible in a different and harmless way. The rider is removed from the main text; the narrower statement above is what the output supports.

[Observed] Four examples from results/resistance_panel_canonical.csv: pfcrt K76T passes FILTER and has numalt 2; pfcrt M74I has numalt 3; pfmdr1 N86Y has numalt 3; pfdhps K540E has numalt 2. Those four numalt values are confirmed exactly, and they alone are sufficient: none of the four sites could have appeared among the outliers whatever its true differentiation, because none was in the matrix. The per-site is_snp flags previously asserted in the same sentence — false for K76T, M74I and N86Y, true for K540E — are marked [Unknown]. They are not contradicted by anything retained, but nothing retained records is_snp per site: the only surviving trace is the aggregate numalt == 1 & is_snp 3,330 on line 16 of the step10 log, and the Pf8 Zarr cannot be re-queried on the server (no zarr module or conda environment there, and only a chromosome 10 VCF is held locally, while these sites are on chromosomes 05, 07 and 08). A small per-site dump should be retained as evidence before the claim is restated as observed.

[Observed] The cascade, stated once and precisely. The superseded step10 panel genotyped 44 canonical codon-allele records — 41 distinct codon-allele protein changes, three of the 44 being exact duplicates (pfcrt Cys72Ser, pfcrt Met74Ile, pfk13 Met476Ile) — covering 39 distinct codons, because two codons carry two distinct alleles each (pfdhps 613 Ser/Thr and pfk13 469 Phe/Tyr). Of those 39 codons, 21 are polymorphic, and those 21 are a strict subset of the 24 polymorphic codons that the codon reconstruction now finds (Section 6.9.3). Of the three codons the reconstruction alone recovers, two — pfdhps 431 and pfdhps 437 — were never in the step10 request list at all (its pfdhps request was Ser436Ala, Lys540Glu, Ala581Gly, Ala613Ser and Ala613Thr), so the ingest-mask audit in this section does not and cannot cover them. The third, pfk13 458, was requested, as Asn458Tyr, and was genotyped — Pf3D7_13_v3:1,725,626, ref T alt A, numalt 2, overall frequency 0.0, n called 1,868 — so the mask does cover it, and dropped it. The reason the reconstruction alone finds 458 polymorphic is not absence from the list but the Section 6.9.1 failure mode: the single non-reference isolate carries Asn458Asp, and a string match for Asn458Tyr can never report Asp. That makes 458 a third instance of the right-codon/wrong-residue failure, alongside pfcrt 75 and pfdhps 437. Of the 21, 6 survived the biallelic ingest mask — this is the in_genomewide_scan field, which is defined at step10_resistance_panel.py:349 as bool(t["numalt"] == 1) and therefore means "would have survived the ingest filter", not "entered the scan". Only 3 then reached the MAF-filtered scan matrix: pfdhfr C59R, pfdhfr N51I and pfaat1 S258L. The other three — pfcrt I356T (overall 0.016), pfmdr1 D1246Y (0.011) and pfcrt N326S (0.008 as step10 reported it; the reconstruction revises it to 0.001, Section 6.9.2) — were removed afterwards by the MAF ≥ 0.05 cut and are absent from the scan matrix, confirmed by direct position lookup in interim/baypass_snp_positions.csv (3,916 rows): positions 405,600, 961,625 and 405,362 are absent, while 96,476, 748,239, 748,262 and 1,215,233 are present. That lookup is cited on baypass_snp_positions.csv rather than on results/baypass/xtx_all_snps.csv because the latter is the file that was found at zero bytes (Section 6.8.1); it has since been restored and would now support the same lookup, but the lookup reported here is the one actually performed, on the file that was always intact.

[Observed] What the three tested codons returned. pfaat1 S258L: XtX 24.198, q = 0.0049 — the signal discussed in Section 6.8.2. pfdhfr C59R (Pf3D7_04_v3:748,262): XtX 9.107, q = 0.432. pfdhfr N51I (Pf3D7_04_v3:748,239): XtX 8.147, q = 0.672. The two antifolate codons are firmly null. (The 0.432 and 0.672 reproduce as 0.431 and 0.672 with a cnt/n empirical p and as 0.432 and 0.672 with the (cnt+1)/(n+1) form; the difference is estimator convention, not error.)

[Interpreted] The correct statement of the negative is therefore neither the original claim nor its overcorrection: the scan tested 3 of the 24 polymorphic canonical codons; it could not test the pfcrt, pfmdr1, pfdhps or pfk13 markers at all. This distinction matters, and it strengthens the section rather than weakening it. The scan's silence on the antifolate markers at pfdhfr is a tested negative — C59R and N51I were in the matrix and came back at q = 0.43 and q = 0.67, so neither shows excess differentiation beyond the demographic model. Its silence on pfcrt, pfmdr1, pfdhps and pfk13 is an ascertainment artefact and carries no information whatever. Sections 6.9 and 7.x restate the resistance findings on a panel built without the biallelic restriction and without annotation-string matching, which is the only basis on which any claim about the untested loci can rest.

[Interpreted] What XtX does and does not detect. XtX measures differentiation among populations beyond what the estimated covariance structure predicts. A locus under strong but spatially uniform selection — which is what a nationally deployed drug produces — generates no XtX signal at all. So even on a callset that contained them, the canonical loci would be expected to be invisible to this scan wherever drug pressure is uniform across Ghana; the null result at pfdhfr is consistent with exactly that, and is not evidence that antifolate resistance is unselected. This is an argument for measuring marker frequencies directly rather than inferring them from a differentiation scan, and Section 6.9 does so.

6.8.4 Call quality of the 19 outliers: the set is sound, two of its SNPs are not

[Observed] MQ, VQSLOD, FS, SOR, QD, QUAL, BaseQRankSum, MQRankSum, ReadPosRankSum, DP, AN, ExcessHet, InbreedingCoeff, RegionType, the VQSR training flags and culprit were pulled from the Pf8 Zarr for all 19 outliers and compared against the other 3,897 scanned SNPs; the per-SNP table is results/outlier_quality_check.csv. Every significant difference runs in the outliers' favour. They carry higher VQSLOD (p = 0.008), higher QD (p = 0.016) and higher QUAL (p = 0.007) than the rest of the panel. No outlier has MQ below 50 — the observed range is 59.56 to 60.00 — none shows read-position bias (|ReadPosRankSum| ≤ 0.304), and 18 of the 19 have FS = 0.00, the nineteenth standing at 0.596, which is negligible on the GATK scale. All 19 are RegionType Core, none carries the Low_VQSLOD filter, none is a VQSR negative training site and 13 of the 19 are positive training sites. The culprit field reads MQRankSum for all 19, but it reads the same for 99.6 per cent of the rest of the panel, so it carries no information here.

[Interpreted] The obvious objection to a 0.49 per cent tail — that it is a tail of badly called sites rather than of differentiated ones — is therefore answered, and answered in the direction that favours the scan: the 19 are better-supported calls than the panel they were drawn from. This is not evidence of selection and must not be read as any; what it removes is one specific alternative explanation, which is all a quality audit can do. Of the 19, 15 are graded clean on every field examined, two carry a lower-grade "watch" annotation and two are flagged (Table S26a).

[Observed] The exported-protein worry is not supported. The concentration of outliers in the exported and host-interaction compartment (Section 6.8.2) invites the suspicion that these gene families, which are repetitive, are collecting mismapped reads. The eight outlier SNPs in that compartment — PTP7, PIESP2, GEXP22, REX2, PF3D7_0831400 (two SNPs) and PF3D7_0221200 (two SNPs) — are in fact the cleanest subset in the panel: FS = 0.00 for all eight, MQ median 60.00 with a range of 59.99–60.00, and VQSLOD significantly higher than the rest of the panel. The families are repetitive; these particular sites sit in uniquely mappable sequence. The compartmental pattern therefore remains exactly as weak, and exactly as strong, as Section 6.8.2 states — untested for enrichment against a matched background — but it is not a mapping artefact, and that specific objection can be set aside.

[Observed] Two SNPs must be downgraded, and not for any reason the quality fields were expected to turn up. Pf3D7_11_v3:1,054,447 (intergenic, XtX 20.79, q = 0.0049) and Pf3D7_11_v3:1,054,078 (SNRPF, XtX 13.98, q = 0.031) — the same 369 bp pair discussed earlier in this section — are the two lowest-InbreedingCoeff sites in the whole 3,916-SNP panel (0.193 at the 0.13th percentile and 0.147 at the 0.08th), and they are also the two lowest-QD outliers (11.00 and 12.91, both below the 0.5th percentile of the panel). Decisively, 1,054,078 carries ExcessHet = 69.59 — the only nonzero ExcessHet value among all 3,916 scanned SNPs, and roughly 400 times the genome-wide maximum reported by the check. 1,054,447 additionally has the lowest VQSLOD of the 19 (5.58, 2.4th percentile). Their mapping and strand-bias fields, by contrast, are clean: MQ 59.95 and 59.99, FS 0.596 and 0.00.

Table S26a. The four outlier SNPs that the call-quality audit did not grade clean, with the fields that produced the grade (results/outlier_quality_check.csv). Bracketed figures are percentiles within the 3,916-SNP scanned panel. The other 15 outliers are clean on every field examined. MQ runs 59.95–60.00 across these four and FS is 0.00 at three of them (0.596 at Pf3D7_11_v3:1,054,447), so none of the four shows a mapping or strand-bias signal: the two flagged SNPs are flagged on heterozygosity-related fields, not on mapping ones, which is what makes collapsed paralogy rather than mismapping the candidate mechanism. ExcessHet is 0.00 at 3,915 of the 3,916 scanned SNPs. The two "watch" SNPs are recorded for completeness and are not downgraded.

[Hypothesized] In a near-haploid organism, an excess of apparent heterozygotes at a single site, with a depressed inbreeding coefficient alongside it, is not what mismapping looks like — mismapping degrades MQ and FS, and here both are clean at both sites. It is what collapsed paralogy or copy-number variation looks like: two diverged copies of a sequence mapping onto one reference locus present as heterozygous calls in every isolate that carries both, and a scan that treats such a locus as a single biallelic SNP will read the resulting allele-frequency differences between zones as population differentiation. The mechanism is unverified, and should be read as a hypothesis and nothing more. Nothing in results/outlier_quality_check.csv establishes it: the fields are consistent with it and inconsistent with the mismapping alternative, which is a good deal weaker than a demonstration. The check that would settle it is a read-level one — per-sample depth ratio at these two positions against each sample's genome-median depth, which would show a duplication directly — and it has not been run. Until it is, both SNPs are reported as lower confidence, with the reason stated rather than the result quietly dropped.

[Interpreted] This matters more than a two-SNP correction usually would, and the reason should be stated plainly: Pf3D7_11_v3:1,054,447 is the fifth-ranked SNP in the entire scan. It stands ahead of nine of the 13 genes in Table S26, it is one of the 13 SNPs that survive FDR 10 per cent, and it carries the same q = 0.0049 as pfaat1 S258L and PTP7. The downgrade is therefore not a tidying of the bottom of the ranking but a withdrawal of confidence near the top of it. Its neighbour 1,054,078 is the SNRPF outlier and also FDR-surviving, at q = 0.031, so SNRPF is the one gene in Table S26 whose entire support is a downgraded SNP. If the paralogy reading is right, the FDR-surviving set is 11 SNPs rather than 13, the independent-signal ceiling falls from 14 to 13 (the two were already counted as one locus on proximity grounds), and the gene list is 12 genes rather than 13, of which eight rather than nine retain FDR-surviving support. If it is wrong, nothing changes. The honest position until the depth check is run is that the status of the scan's fifth-strongest signal is unresolved, and the two affected results — the intergenic pair and SNRPF — should not be built on.

[Observed] None of this reaches the signals on which this section's substantive claims rest. pfaat1 S258L (Pf3D7_06_v3:1,215,233), FIKK4.2, both ACS8 SNPs, NT2, PIESP2, Pf3D7_12_v3:715,788 and the exported-protein SNPs are graded clean on every field examined. The two "watch" annotations fall on PTP7, the top-ranked outlier, for a BaseQRankSum of 5.32 at the 96.8th percentile, and on the second pfaat1 SNP at Pf3D7_06_v3:1,214,350, for an AN of 52,172 at the 0.5th percentile — more missingness than almost any site in the panel. Neither is downgraded: one high-percentile rank-sum statistic, or a low allele number on an otherwise clean site, is not a defect. They are recorded so that the grading can be checked rather than taken on trust, which is the only defensible reason to report a quality audit at all.

6.9 A codon-reconstruction resistance marker panel

Correction. The previous version of this section is withdrawn in its entirety. Section 6.9 as previously written reported a panel built by matching SnpEff's ANN_HGVS_p protein-change strings against a hand-written marker list. An external critique raised five points against it; all five were upheld and four were defects in the analysis code, not in the prose. Specifically withdrawn: the claim that the pfdhfr triple mutant stands at 0.786–0.908 (a range of three marginals is not a haplotype frequency, and the true haplotype frequency is lower than any of them); the claim that all 14 validated pfk13 codons are at 0.000 in all 1,900 isolates (the list was not the WHO list, it contained a duplicate, it omitted three validated markers, and the denominator is per codon, not 1,900); the claim that 21 of 23 markers agree with MalariaGEN within 0.03 (agreement of marginal frequencies is not agreement of calls); the diagnosis of a "naming discrepancy" at pfcrt codon 75; and the complete absence of pfdhps A437G, a marker at a true frequency of 0.897 that the string match could never have found. Everything below comes from a new script, scripts/step12_codon_panel.py, and from its three outputs.

[Interpreted] Section 6.8.3 establishes that the genome-wide callset could test only 3 of the 24 polymorphic canonical resistance codons, and none of the pfcrt, pfmdr1, pfdhps or pfk13 markers. This section tests them all directly, on a panel built to avoid both of the filters that hid them: the biallelic ingest mask of Section 6.8.3, and the annotation-string matching described immediately below. It supplies the evidence for Objective 3 — zone-specific variants relevant to drug response.

6.9.1 Why the previous panel was wrong: two ways a marker hides from an annotation string

[Observed] step10 identified markers by matching SnpEff ANN_HGVS_p strings such as "Ala437Gly" against a hand-written list. An annotation string describes a change away from the reference, and that has two failure modes. (i) A resistance allele that is the reference residue can never appear as an ALT change. Where 3D7 itself carries the resistance residue, the only annotation at that codon is the revertant, so a search for the resistance change matches nothing and the codon is reported as if it did not exist. This is exactly what happened at pfdhps 437 (Section 6.9.4). (ii) A residue reachable only by two changes within one codon is never annotated. pfcrt codon 75 spans Pf3D7_07_v3:403,621–403,623 and carries two variant records, 403621 (A>G, annotated Asn75Asp) and 403623 (T>A, annotated Asn75Lys). Asn→Glu requires both. Neither annotation names N75E, which is the residue the CVIET haplotype actually carries, so no string match could ever return it. The same mechanism accounts for pfk13 458, where the request was Asn458Tyr and the one variant isolate carries Asp (Section 6.8.3).

[Interpreted] These two failure modes are the whole explanation of the two things the previous version got wrong at pfcrt 75 and pfdhps 437. In particular, the "naming discrepancy" diagnosed at codon 75 was not a naming problem at all: our pipeline and MalariaGEN's were reporting different residues because ours could only see one of the two SNPs that make up the residue. Calling it a disagreement about nomenclature, resolved in MalariaGEN's favour "on the biology", concealed a reconstruction failure behind a terminological one. That diagnosis is withdrawn.

6.9.2 Method: reconstruct the codon, report the whole spectrum

[Observed] scripts/step12_codon_panel.py discards annotation matching. For each requested codon it maps the codon to its three genomic positions through the gene's CDS, collects every variant record at those positions regardless of numalt, is_snp or ALT type, applies each isolate's alleles to the reference codon, and translates the result. The output is the full residue spectrum at each codon, including the reference residue, so a resistance allele cannot hide in the reference and a two-SNP residue cannot be missed. Genotypes are collapsed by the same majority-allele ploidy rule used in Step 4a; a codon in which an isolate's calls are mixed is recorded as uncalled for that isolate rather than being assigned a residue. Frequencies are reported per agro-ecological zone with Wilson 95 per cent confidence intervals, and heterogeneity across the four zones is tested by χ² on residue counts. The same 1,900 isolates are used as in every other analysis in this chapter.

python scripts/step12_codon_panel.py --out-prefix codon_panel

[Observed] Fifty-seven codons across nine genes (pfcrt, pfmdr1, pfmdr2, pfdhfr, pfdhps, pfk13, pfaat1, pfap2mu, pfcoronin) were reconstructed and genotyped: pfk13 23, pfcrt 10, pfdhps 6, pfmdr1 5, pfdhfr 5, pfcoronin 3, pfmdr2 2, pfaat1 2, pfap2mu 1. Three of the nine genes are on the minus strand (pfaat1, pfk13, pfmdr2) and six on the plus strand (pfap2mu, pfcoronin, pfcrt, pfdhfr, pfdhps, pfmdr1), which the codon-to-position mapping has to handle in both orientations. Twenty-four are polymorphic and 33 are fixed at the reference residue. Per-codon call rates run 1,834–1,900 of 1,900. Outputs: results/codon_panel_residues.csv (83 codon–residue rows), codon_panel_haplotypes.csv and codon_panel_concordance.csv; log at logs/step12_codon_panel.log.

[Observed] Three codons are polymorphic here that the step10 panel never reported as polymorphic at all: pfdhps 431 (I431V, 0.014), pfdhps 437 (Section 6.9.4) and pfk13 458, where a single isolate carries Asn458Asp — a residue that is not the validated N458Y change, and which step10 could not have seen because it requested only Asn458Tyr. Two codons that step10 reported as polymorphic resolve differently on reconstruction: pfcrt N326S falls from 0.008 to 0.001 (one isolate in 1,874), and pfdhps 436 turns out to carry four residues, not two — Ala 0.541, the reference Ser 0.446, and the minor residues Phe 0.007 and Tyr 0.005, neither of which appeared in the previous panel.

6.9.3 The residue spectrum by zone

Table S27. Full residue spectrum of the 24 polymorphic codons, by agro-ecological zone, with Wilson 95 per cent confidence intervals (results/codon_panel_residues.csv). Rows are codon–residue pairs; † marks the 3D7 reference residue, which is reported explicitly because at pfdhps 437 the reference residue is the resistance allele (Section 6.9.4) and because at seven of the 24 codons the non-reference residue is the majority one: pfdhfr 51 (Ile 0.786), pfdhfr 59 (Arg 0.886), pfdhfr 108 (Asn 0.908), pfdhps 436 (Ala 0.541), pfmdr1 184 (Phe 0.677), pfmdr2 492 (Val 0.549) and pfaat1 258 (Leu 0.609). Residues within a codon are ordered by overall frequency; the 33 codons fixed at the reference residue are omitted — the 22 pfk13 ones are listed in Table S30, and the remaining 11 (pfcrt 72 and 73, pfmdr1 1034 and 1042, pfdhfr 16 and 164, pfmdr2 484, pfcoronin 50, 100 and 107, and pfaat1 313) appear only in the source file. Overall denominators are per codon and run 1,858–1,900 of 1,900 isolates across the 24 codons shown; the panel-wide range of 1,834–1,900 quoted in Section 6.9.2 includes fixed codons omitted here, its lower bound being pfmdr1 1034. χ² p tests heterogeneity of that residue against all other residues at the same codon across the four zones, so a codon with four residues yields four tests whose p-values differ — pfdhps 436 is the only such codon. Zone column headings are spelled correctly here for display; the source geospatial layer and the source columns of codon_panel_residues.csv read Decidous_forest and Transitional_zone, and the spatial join keys on that source spelling (see Methods).

[Calculated] Taking the smallest residue-level p-value per codon and correcting across the 24 polymorphic codons (Bonferroni threshold 0.05/24 = 0.0021), nine codons show between-zone heterogeneity: pfdhfr S108N (9.4e-7), C59R (4.8e-5) and N51I (2.4e-4); pfdhps A437G (1.2e-6), 436 (4.5e-5 at the reference Ser residue), I431V (1.8e-4) and A581G (8.0e-4); pfcrt I356T (0.0016); and pfaat1 S258L (1.9e-18). Nominal signals at pfcrt 220 (0.026), pfcrt 74/75/76 (0.035–0.037), pfmdr1 N86Y (0.029), pfmdr2 I492V (0.020) and the minor pfdhps Tyr436 residue (0.028) do not survive correction and are not interpreted. The denominator of 24 — the codons that carry a between-zone test, not all 57 reconstructed codons — is the one used everywhere in this section, and is stated here once so that it can be checked. The previous version reported seven of 21 codons passing at 0.05/21; the two additions are both at pfdhps, and both are codons the string match never recovered.

[Observed] What moved relative to the superseded table, stated plainly. Every pfcrt codon on the chloroquine-resistant haplotype came down. The three polymorphic codons of the CVIET motif fell from 0.079 to 0.071 — M74I, K76T, and codon 75, which was previously reported as Asn75Asp at 0.079 and is N75E at 0.071 — and the associated background codons followed: A220S from 0.074 to 0.070, Q271E from 0.079 to 0.074, R371I from 0.084 to 0.076. All six now lie in a 0.006 band rather than a 0.010 one. pfcrt N326S fell from 0.008 to 0.001. The pfdhfr and pfaat1 marginals barely moved (N51I 0.7859 → 0.7864; C59R 0.8860 → 0.8861; S108N 0.9076 → 0.9084; S258L 0.60927 → 0.60918), as did pfmdr1 Y184F (0.677), pfmdr2 I492V (0.550 → 0.549), pfdhps A613S (0.109 → 0.108) and pfap2mu S160N (0.097 → 0.096). Codon 75 is no longer labelled Asn75Asp but Asn75Glu. Two codons and one residue are new. None of the changes reverses a direction of effect; the point is that the previous numbers were close enough to pass every check the project then ran, which is the subject of Section 6.9.10.

6.9.4 pfdhps A437G and the reference-allele trap

[Observed] pfdhps A437G, the first-step sulfadoxine-resistance substitution and the commonest pfdhps marker in Africa, was entirely absent from the previous version of this chapter. It was not reported at a low frequency, or reported with a caveat: it did not appear. The reason is failure mode (i) of Section 6.9.1. The 3D7 reference genome itself translates as Gly at codon 437, so the only annotation SnpEff can emit at Pf3D7_08_v3:549,684–549,686 is the single record Gly437Ala — the revertant. A search for "Ala437Gly" matched nothing at all, and a marker at a true frequency of 0.897 was silently dropped from a chapter whose subject is antifolate resistance.

The official Pf8 methodology says this outright. Quoted verbatim from the MalariaGEN Pf8 methods document, and verified in the source: "3D7 sequence translates as a G at 437, this is thought to be the mutant allele, i.e. 3D7 does not have the wild type allele." MalariaGEN's own published genotype file encodes the same fact in its column name, dhps_437[G], where the bracketed letter is the reference residue. Both were available throughout. The information needed to avoid this error was in the methodology of the dataset being analysed.

Table S28. pfdhps G437 frequency by agro-ecological zone, with Wilson 95 per cent confidence intervals (results/codon_panel_residues.csv). G is the 3D7 reference residue and the resistance allele; the complementary residue is the revertant A437, whose frequencies are the arithmetic complements shown in Table S27. χ² = 30.34 on 3 df, p = 1.2 × 10⁻⁶.

[Interpreted] The zone ordering mirrors pfdhfr: highest in the two southern zones, lowest in Guinea savanna and the 25-isolate Transitional zone, and not monotonic in latitude. Substantively, G437 at 0.897 means the first pfdhps step stands as high as the pfdhfr triple mutant's component marginals — just below S108N at 0.908, which is the panel's most frequent antifolate residue — which materially strengthens the antifolate picture in Section 6.9.5 relative to the previous version, where the pfdhps side appeared to rest on S436A at 0.54 and little else. Methodologically, the lesson generalises beyond this codon: any marker panel that identifies variants by their change-from-reference string will miss every marker for which the reference carries the derived allele, and will do so without raising an error. The only general defence is to reconstruct the codon and report the whole residue spectrum, which is why Table S27 lists reference residues explicitly rather than implying them.

6.9.5 Antifolate resistance: near-fixed pfdhfr component marginals, further advanced at pfdhps than previously reported, and zone-structured but not latitudinal

[Observed] The three pfdhfr components stand at 0.786 (N51I), 0.886 (C59R) and 0.908 (S108N) overall. Of the three, only S108N is the runner-up to pfaat1 for zone structure (χ² p = 9.4e-7 against 1.9e-18); the claim that all three are, after pfaat1, the most strongly zone-structured codons in the panel was true of the old 21-codon panel and is not true of the rebuilt one. Ranking the 24 tested codons by χ² p puts three pfdhps codons between S108N and the other two pfdhfr components — 437 at 1.2e-6 (the value this section prints in Table S28), 436 at 4.5e-5 and 431 at 1.8e-4 — against C59R at 4.8e-5 and N51I at 2.4e-4. The defensible statement is that S108N is second only to pfaat1 S258L, and that C59R and N51I are among the most strongly zone-structured codons alongside pfdhps 437, 436 and 431. All three pfdhfr components are higher in the two southern zones than in Guinea savanna: S108N 0.971 coastal savanna and 0.958 deciduous forest against 0.885; C59R 0.946 and 0.928 against 0.864; N51I 0.892 and 0.802 against 0.764. This is not a monotonic south-to-north cline. The Transitional zone is the second-most-northern of the four and carries the panel's highest pfdhfr values — 1.000 for S108N and C59R, 0.880 for N51I — so an ordering by latitude breaks there. What the data support is a contrast between the southern zones and Guinea savanna, with the 25-isolate Transitional zone unresolved. I164L, the fourth-step codon, is fixed at the reference residue Ile. On the pfdhps side, G437 is at 0.897 (Section 6.9.4), 436 carries Ala at 0.541 against the reference Ser at 0.446 with minor Phe (0.007) and Tyr (0.005) residues, A613S sits at 0.108, and the high-grade additions remain rare: I431V 0.014, A581G 0.014, K540E 0.009.

[Interpreted] This is the standard West African antifolate pattern, and the recovery of A437G sharpens rather than changes it: sulfadoxine–pyrimethamine is no longer a treatment regimen but remains in intermittent preventive use; the three pfdhfr component marginals are near-fixed at 0.786–0.908, though the triple-mutant haplotype itself is 0.771 and must not be described as close to fixed (Section 6.9.6); the first pfdhps step, G437, stands at 0.897; and the pfdhps additions that would make the quintuple mutant — chiefly K540E — have not followed, so the A437G+K540E double stands at 0.010 (Section 6.9.6). The south-versus-Guinea-savanna difference is consistent with longer or heavier historical antifolate exposure in the more urbanised, higher-service southern zones, but the panel cannot test that: no drug-pressure covariate was available, the Transitional zone contradicts a latitudinal reading, and Section 6.9.11 gives a competing explanation — confounding with sequencing study — that this design cannot separate from it.

6.9.6 Within-isolate haplotypes, counted on complete cases

[Observed] The previous version reported the pfdhfr "triple mutant" as the range of its three marginal frequencies, 0.786–0.908. That is not a haplotype frequency and it cannot be one: a range of marginals carries no information about whether the three alleles co-occur in the same parasite. Haplotypes are now reconstructed within isolates, counted only on isolates called at every constituent codon, so each haplotype has its own complete-case denominator.

Table S29. Within-isolate haplotype frequencies on complete cases, with Wilson 95 per cent confidence intervals (results/codon_panel_haplotypes.csv). "Complete cases" are isolates with a called residue at every constituent codon; the denominator therefore differs between haplotypes and is smaller than 1,900. Overall figures are pooled across zones.

[Calculated] The triple mutant is 0.771, not 0.786–0.908, and the difference is not cosmetic. A haplotype frequency cannot exceed the frequency of its rarest component, and on the same 1,876 complete cases that rarest component is 51I at 0.788. The reconstructed IRN frequency of 0.771 sits below it, as it must. The previously reported range not only mislabelled a set of marginals as a haplotype, it quoted an upper figure (0.908, the S108N marginal) that is 12.0 percentage points above a mathematically impossible ceiling for the quantity it claimed to describe: 0.908 − 0.788 = 0.120. (The gap from 0.908 to the reconstructed haplotype frequency of 0.771 is 13.7 percentage points, but that is a gap to an observed value, not to a ceiling, and the two should not be swapped.) The error was detectable by inspection, without any new computation.

[Observed] Nor is pfdhfr a near-fixed single haplotype. Alongside IRN at 0.771, NRN sits at 0.116 and the fully wild-type NCS at 0.091, leaving roughly 0.022 in other combinations. The quadruple IRNL is absent (0 of 1,874; upper bound 0.002), consistent with I164L being fixed at the reference residue. At pfcrt, by contrast, the haplotype really does travel as a unit, and this is now demonstrated rather than inferred: the only two 72–76 haplotypes observed are CVMNK (0.929) and CVIET (0.071), with nothing intermediate. The previous version inferred that unity from six similar marginals, which is suggestive but not evidence; co-occurrence data are.

[Interpreted] The practical consequence is that Ghana's antifolate burden should be read as a population carrying a dominant but not universal pfdhfr triple-mutant lineage — about three isolates in four — with a substantial single-mutant and wild-type remainder, rather than as a population in which the triple mutant has swept. The pfdhps GE double at 0.010 confirms that the quintuple mutant, the configuration that compromises sulfadoxine–pyrimethamine for intermittent preventive treatment, is rare here despite G437 being near-fixed; what is missing is K540E, not A437G.

6.9.7 Chloroquine resistance has largely reverted, and the CVIET haplotype is demonstrably a unit

[Observed] The six pfcrt codons that carry the CVIET-associated resistance haplotype — M74I, N75E, K76T, A220S, Q271E and R371I — now sit within a 0.006 band at 0.070–0.076, and the reconstructed CVIET haplotype itself is at 0.071 (133 of 1,873 complete cases). CVIET names the codon 72–76 motif, as Table S29 states, so only M74I, N75E and K76T are motif codons; A220S, Q271E and R371I are flanking markers of the wider chloroquine-resistance background. They track together zone by zone: 0.054 coastal savanna for five of the six, 0.104 in deciduous forest, 0.062–0.069 in Guinea savanna, 0.125 in the 25-isolate Transitional zone. None of the six reaches the corrected heterogeneity threshold: their nominal p-values run 0.026–0.119 against the Bonferroni threshold of 0.05/24 = 0.0021, the 24 being the panel codons that carry a between-zone test (Section 6.9.3). The two pfcrt codons off that haplotype behave differently: I356T is at 0.016 and is the only pfcrt codon with corrected-significant structure (0.029 coastal savanna, 0.036 deciduous forest, 0.009 Guinea savanna, 0.000 Transitional, p = 0.0016 — higher in the two southern zones, but not ordered by latitude), and N326S is a singleton at 0.001. The denominator matters and is stated rather than left implicit: on the 24 tested codons I356T survives at 0.0016 < 0.0021, whereas correcting across all 57 reconstructed codons (0.05/57 = 0.00088) it would not, and the previous version of this paragraph asserted both outcomes one sentence apart by widening "none of the six" to "no pfcrt codon".

[Interpreted] The ~0.07 level is reversion to chloroquine-sensitive wild type, roughly two decades after chloroquine was withdrawn as first-line therapy in Ghana; the residual 7 per cent is the surviving mutant haplotype, not evidence of ongoing selection. Two things are firmer than in the previous version. The haplotype's unity is now a co-occurrence observation rather than an inference from similar marginals. And codon 75 is correctly identified as N75E, the E of CVIET, rather than as an Asn75Asp change that no isolate in this dataset actually carries — a residue that required reconstructing the codon from two separate SNPs to see at all.

6.9.8 No artemisinin resistance: all 14 WHO-validated and all 6 WHO-candidate pfk13 markers

[Observed] The pfk13 panel was rebuilt from the WHO molecular-marker compendium v1.0 (December 2025) rather than from memory. It now covers all 14 validated markers — F446I, N458Y, C469Y, M476I, Y493H, G533S, R539T, I543T, P553L, R561H, P574L, C580Y, R622I, A675V — and all 6 candidates — E252Q, P441L, C469F, N537I, G538V, V568G. That is 14 + 6 = 20 variants at 19 distinct codons, because C469Y (validated) and C469F (candidate) are two different changes at the same codon 469; the two WHO tiers are kept distinct throughout and no candidate is anywhere described as validated. Four further pfk13 codons (449, 481, 515, 527) fall outside both lists, which is why the reconstruction covers more codons than the lists contain: 23 pfk13 codons in total, and every one is fixed at the 3D7 reference residue, with the single exception of codon 458, where one isolate in 1,866 carries Asn458Asp. The validated residue at that codon is Tyr, which is absent.

Table S30. All 23 reconstructed pfk13 codons with their WHO v1.0 (December 2025) classification (results/codon_panel_residues.csv). Validated markers are shown in bold; codon 469 carries one validated change (C469Y) and one candidate change (C469F), which is why 20 variants occupy 19 codons. P441L and C469F are candidates, not validated — the previous version of this chapter listed both among the validated set, which was a misclassification. "Zero upper bound" is the Wilson 95 per cent upper limit on a count of zero at that codon's own denominator. It therefore does not apply at codon 458, the one codon where the count is not zero; that cell gives the Wilson upper limit on the one variant actually observed (0.0030) instead, and should not be read as a ceiling of 0.0021.

Two corrections to how this negative was previously stated. (1) The denominator. The previous version said all 14 validated codons were at 0.000 "in all 1,900 isolates". They were not: the denominator is per codon, 1,845–1,876 of 1,900 called, the shortfall being isolates whose calls at that codon were mixed or missing. Per-zone call rates give zero upper bounds of ≤ 0.0029 in Guinea savanna (n 1,304–1,321), ≤ 0.0118 in deciduous forest (n 322–331), ≤ 0.0197 in coastal savanna (n 191–201) and only ≤ 0.133 in the Transitional zone (n = 25). (2) The period. The isolates were collected in 2009–2021. This is therefore a historical negative. It does not speak to the frequency of pfk13 resistance alleles in Ghana today, and must not be cited as if it did.

The kelch13 corroboration must be stated much more weakly than before. The previous version claimed independent corroboration from MalariaGEN's published kelch13 classification: "Two methods, one of them not ours, agree." That overstates it. Pf8 assigns a kelch13 status over the whole 349–726 propeller domain and classifies any missing call in that interval as undetermined — the rule is "Any missing call in amino acids 349-726 → Missing → Undetermined". Only 36 isolates therefore have a complete Pf8 status, against per-codon calls on roughly 1,860 isolates here. Thirty-six complete statuses are weak corroboration for a negative asserted on ~1,860 isolates, and the two should not be presented as equivalent lines of evidence. The weight of this negative rests on the reconstruction in Table S30, not on the Pf8 classification.

[Interpreted] With those two qualifications in place, Ghana shows no evidence of artemisinin partial resistance in 2009–2021, tested across the complete WHO-validated set and the complete candidate set, at the per-codon detection limits above. This is now a genuine tested negative. In the original manuscript the equivalent claim rested on pfk13 not appearing in a genome scan whose matrix contained no pfk13 codon at all, and the version before this one tested a list that was neither the WHO list nor complete. Given confirmed R561H in Rwanda and A675V/C469Y in Uganda, a negative of this precision across a West African sample is a useful surveillance baseline — but it is a baseline for a period that ended in 2021, and the right inference is that it should be repeated on contemporary isolates, not that resistance is absent now.

6.9.9 pfaat1 Ser258Leu and its coastal-to-northern cline

[Observed] pfaat1 S258L is the most strongly zone-structured codon in the panel: 0.824 in coastal savanna, 0.735 in deciduous forest, 0.640 in the Transitional zone and 0.544 in Guinea savanna, overall 0.609 (n = 1,896), χ² p = 1.9 × 10⁻¹⁸. The coastal-savanna-to-Guinea-savanna difference is 28 percentage points (0.824 − 0.544 = 0.280; unrounded, 0.8235 − 0.5440 = 0.2796). Unusually for this panel the ordering is monotonic across all four zones in true south-to-north order, because the Transitional zone — the second-most-northern — sits at 0.640, between deciduous forest and Guinea savanna. The Transitional estimate rests on 25 isolates and should not carry weight on its own, but it does not break the pattern as it does for pfdhfr. This is the same variant that ranks second in the genome-wide scan (Section 6.8.2) at XtX 24.198, q = 0.0049, and that SNP is graded clean by the call-quality audit of Section 6.8.4, so it is supported by two statistical framings on two differently filtered callsets. The second pfaat1 codon in the panel, 313, is fixed at the reference residue Phe in all 1,900 isolates: F313S is absent from Ghana in this sample.

[Interpreted] The previous interpretation of this cline was wrong on the published evidence, and is withdrawn. It asserted that no functional experiment had tested amodiaquine, and built a drug-pressure argument on that gap. Amodiaquine was tested in Amambua-Ngwa et al. (2023): "Parasite IC50 values for CQ, amodiaquine, lumefantrine, mefloquine and quinine were measured…"; the shifts were "well below the threshold associated with clinical resistance" (amodiaquine threshold 60 nM); and the authors explicitly state that the other-quinoline hypothesis "is not supported", concluding that chloroquine was "the primary selective force". The variant's demonstrated effect is a 1.5-fold rise in chloroquine IC50 in a single chloroquine-resistant Southeast Asian line (NHP4026), with epistasis between S258L and F313S and an explicit pfcrt-background caveat; the 0 to 97 per cent longitudinal rise is Gambian (1984–2014) and the paper says nothing about Ghana. The defensible description is a variant that potentiates chloroquine resistance, modestly and in an epistasis- and background-dependent way — not "the functionally validated chloroquine-resistance variant". The caveat bites here in particular: F313S, the variant with which S258L shows epistasis, is absent from all 1,900 Ghanaian isolates, so the epistatic component of the published effect cannot operate in this sample. The stronger form of that sentence — that the genetic background on which the 1.5-fold effect was measured does not exist here — is withdrawn as an overreach: the source records the epistasis and the pfcrt-background caveat as two separate facts and nowhere states that the 1.5-fold measurement was taken on an F313S-carrying background; and the background it does flag, a chloroquine-resistant pfcrt, does exist in this sample, at CVIET 0.071 (133 of 1,873).

[Hypothesized] What, then, maintains 0.82 in coastal savanna? Three candidates, and the panel cannot choose between them. (a) Historical persistence. Chloroquine was withdrawn in Ghana two decades ago and the pfcrt CVIET haplotype has reverted to 0.071 (Section 6.9.7), so standing chloroquine pressure cannot explain a frequency of 0.82; but a potentiating variant with a modest fitness cost can decay slowly and unevenly, and a gradient rather than fixation or loss is the expected intermediate state. (b) Amodiaquine, as an untested possible driver. Ghana's first-line regimen since 2005 has been artesunate–amodiaquine, which maintains continuous 4-aminoquinoline pressure. The published experiment measured amodiaquine and found the shift sub-threshold, and the authors reject the wider-quinoline reading for their Southeast Asian data; so amodiaquine can be offered here only as a possibility that the functional work does not support and did not test in a West African background — not as the explanation, which is how the previous version framed it.
