(c) The documented fitness cost reported by Amambua-Ngwa et al., which is the condition under which a post-withdrawal equilibrium is a gradient rather than fixation: the allele is retained where exposure is highest and purged where it is not. If (b) or (c) carries the gradient, it is a drug-exposure or treatment-coverage gradient — higher treatment-seeking and more complete AS-AQ coverage in the south — rather than anything ecological about agro-ecological zones as such. That is testable with drug-pressure covariates joined at the same spatial resolution, and is not tested here.

[Observed] Two points of context, both narrower than the previous version's. pfaat1 appears nowhere in the WHO compendium at any tier, so it is not "the most common validated resistance variant", which is what the previous version called it and which was a category error (Section 6.9.11). The replacement superlative has to be narrower still, and its exclusion rule stated: at 0.609, S258L is the most common variant in this panel with a published allele-replacement experiment behind it, outside the antifolates. It is not the most common resistance-associated non-antifolate variant, because pfmdr1 Y184F is higher at 0.677 (n = 1,858) and pfmdr2 I492V is close behind at 0.549; Y184F carries no WHO label either, so the tiers cannot be doing the excluding — what distinguishes S258L is comparable functional-replacement data, which Y184F does not have. And pfaat1 is absent from the eight-locus config.KNOWN_RESISTANCE_LOCI panel inherited by this pipeline, which is why it reached second place in the outlier table of the original manuscript — behind PTP7 at XtX 26.44, and labelled only as "amino acid transporter AAT1" — while the abstract of the same document announced that no validated resistance locus had been found. It did not head that table, and the earlier phrasing that said it did is corrected here.

6.9.10 Per-sample validation against MalariaGEN's published calls

[Observed] The panel was validated against Pf8_drug_resistance_marker_genotypes.tsv, MalariaGEN's independently produced genotype calls for the same 1,900 isolates, call by call rather than frequency by frequency. On isolates where both pipelines called, 47,012 of 47,012 calls agree — 100.0000 per cent across 25 markers in five genes, including all nine pfcrt codons and codon 437 of pfdhps. Codon 75 now agrees exactly, because reconstruction yields Glu where string matching yielded Asp.

Table S31. Per-sample concordance with MalariaGEN's published genotypes (results/codon_panel_concordance.csv). "Calls compared" counts isolates called by both pipelines at that marker. The MalariaGEN column name carries the reference residue in brackets, which is how dhps_437[G] encodes the fact described in Section 6.9.4. No marker recorded a single disagreement.

What this replaces, and why the replacement matters. The previous version's validation was: "Twenty-one of 23 comparable markers agree within 0.03." Agreement of marginal frequencies does not establish that two pipelines assign the same residue to the same isolate — two pipelines can reach the same marginal from different sets of samples — and the test's tolerance was wide enough to pass real errors. Two concrete cases from this chapter make the point. pfcrt N326S was reported at 0.008 by the old panel and is 0.00053 here (one isolate of 1,874; 0.001 to three decimal places); the discrepancy is about fifteenfold in relative terms — not eightfold, which is what the rounded 0.001 suggests and which understates the point — it is invisible at a ±0.03 tolerance, and the per-sample test now shows 1,874 of 1,874 calls agreeing with MalariaGEN at that codon. And pfcrt 75 accounted for both of the markers the old test flagged as outside tolerance — both flagged markers were the same codon — and the discrepancy was then explained away as a "naming disagreement, not a data disagreement" in which MalariaGEN was "correct on the biology". That explanation was wrong: it was a reconstruction failure (Section 6.9.1), and the ±0.03 framing turned a code defect into a terminology footnote. The 0.03 claim, and the naming-discrepancy note that accompanied it, are both withdrawn. Per-sample concordance is the only version of this check that can detect the failure modes this chapter has actually suffered.

6.9.11 What the panel may and may not claim about WHO classification

[Observed] The WHO compendium classifies variants, not genes, and the previous version repeatedly attached gene-level WHO labels. Three specific corrections follow. (1) There is no validated or candidate lumefantrine marker at all — verbatim: "The review did not identify any validated or candidate molecular markers associated with lumefantrine resistance." So a label of the form "pfmdr1, WHO-validated, lumefantrine" is a category error twice over: it ascribes a tier to a gene, and it ascribes it for a drug that has no classified markers. (2) pfaat1, pfap2mu and pfmdr2 appear nowhere in the compendium at any tier. They stay in the panel — pfaat1 for the functional work of Section 6.9.9, pfap2mu and pfmdr2 as loci of standing interest — but none of them may be described as a WHO-classified marker anywhere in this thesis. (3) pfcoronin is in the compendium at the potential tier only, which is below candidate; all three of its reconstructed codons (G50E, R100K, E107V) are fixed at the reference residue here. Within pfk13, the tiers are per variant and are given in Table S30; P441L and C469F are candidates, not validated, which the previous version got wrong.

6.9.12 Limitations of the panel

[Observed] The zone contrasts in Table S27 inherit every design limitation of the FST analysis, including the one the original manuscript did not list. Zone is confounded with sequencing study. The confounding analysis is computed on the 4,905 QC-passing isolates, not on the 1,900-isolate analysis set, because study membership is a property of the full QC-passing cohort: those 4,905 come from 11 sequencing studies, of which Guinea savanna draws on six (n = 3,605), deciduous forest on five (n = 815, of which four, n = 811, are large enough to enter the within-zone test — the fifth is a 4-isolate study), coastal savanna on two (n = 435) and the Transitional zone on a single study (1083-PF-GH-CONWAY, n = 50); 435 + 815 + 3,605 + 50 = 4,905. Four of the six zone pairs share no partner study at all, among them coastal savanna against Guinea savanna, which is the pair carrying both the maximum FST and every marker contrast reported above. Study-associated heterogeneity within these data is demonstrable rather than hypothetical — holding zone constant, call rate differs between studies within Guinea savanna at p = 2.8e-197 (Kruskal–Wallis, 6 studies, n = 3,605) and within deciduous forest at p = 7.7e-88 (4 studies, n = 811); because collection year, finer geography and recruitment also differ between studies inside a zone, this is heterogeneity associated with study rather than a demonstrated technical cause. A zone difference in marker frequency and a study difference in marker frequency are not separable here for the pairs that carry the largest effects.

[Observed] Three further design constraints. The Transitional zone contributes 25 isolates from one sequencing study, so its column in Table S27 is indicative only and its 1.000 and 0.000 entries should not be read as fixation or absence; the χ² tests retain it because excluding it would discard a zone, but no finding above depends on it. Monoclonal-fraction differences between zones mean the majority-allele ploidy rule resolves mixed infections at different rates in different zones, which could bias frequency estimates in the same direction as a real cline. And the agro-ecological zone layer itself has no citable published source (Methods), so "zone" is a reproducible label on this specific GeoJSON rather than an authoritative classification.

[Observed] Three constraints specific to the reconstruction. Mixed calls become missing data, not residues. A codon at which an isolate's calls are mixed is recorded uncalled, which is why per-codon denominators run 1,834–1,900 rather than 1,900, and which systematically removes polyclonal infections — the isolates most likely to carry a minority resistance allele. Every frequency here is therefore a frequency among isolates resolvable to a single residue at that codon, and the direction of any resulting bias is unknown. The residue-level χ² tests within a codon are not independent. For a two-residue codon the two tests are the same test, and at pfdhps 436 the four tests are nested; the Bonferroni correction in Section 6.9.3 is applied across the 24 codons using each codon's smallest residue-level p, which is the least unreasonable choice available but is not an exact multiplicity control. Haplotypes are within-isolate residue combinations on complete cases, not phased haplotypes. For a monoclonal infection the two coincide; for the polyclonal fraction that survives the ploidy rule they need not, and no phasing was attempted.

[Interpreted] Taken together, these mean the gradients in Table S27 are better supported than their causal attribution to agro-ecology. The frequencies themselves are now as solid as this dataset allows — every one of 47,012 per-sample calls agrees with an independently produced genotype set, across the 25 panel codons MalariaGEN publishes per sample (Section 6.9.10), which is a far stronger statement than the marginal agreement previously cited. What the gradient is a gradient in — drug exposure, sampling study, treatment-seeking, or agro-ecological zone — this design cannot resolve, and Section 7.x does not claim it can.

6.9.13 What the corrected run did not change, and what it did

[Observed] The genome-wide conclusions survived a 16.6 per cent larger SNP panel (3,359 to 3,916), a corrected FDR treatment, a reproducible single-threaded BayPass, a seeded Mantel test and a TreeMix sweep with standard errors. Maximum pairwise FST moved from 0.0024 to 0.0023, the ADMIXTURE cross-validation curve remained near-flat with a negligible K = 2 minimum (0.83925 against 0.84384 at K = 1, both on the same 3,385-SNP pruned panel, a 0.54 per cent improvement), and near-panmixia across Ghana still holds. Nine of the 13 outlier genes retain FDR-surviving support — eight if the SNRPF downgrade of Section 6.8.4 is right — and the leading outlier is the same gene as before: PTP7, at XtX 26.48 on the manuscript run and 26.44 on the final one. The marker panel's qualitative findings also survived its own rebuild: antifolate markers near-fixed and zone-structured, chloroquine markers reverted to under a tenth, pfk13 clean, pfaat1 S258L strongly clined.

[Interpreted] The robustness is real, and so is the pattern of error, which should be stated rather than smoothed over. This is the fourth consecutive round in which a correction contained its own defect. Each layer was checked for internal consistency against the project's own outputs, and that checking worked. What escaped it was external correctness: whether the marker list matched an authoritative source (pfk13), whether the annotation direction matched the reference genome (pfdhps 437), whether a co-occurrence claim had co-occurrence data behind it (the triple mutant), whether a per-sample claim had per-sample data behind it (the MalariaGEN check), and whether a cited paper said what it was cited as saying (pfaat1). The verification architecture was built to catch stale numbers, and it did. It was not built to ask "is this the right list, in the right direction, against the right source?" — and all five of the defects in this addendum are instances of that one question going unasked. (Four of the five were defects in the analysis code and the fifth, the pfaat1 citation, was a defect in the prose; that is a different cut of the same five, and the earlier "four of five" figure belonged to it, not to this one.) The call-quality audit of Section 6.8.4 is the first check in this project that asked an external question of the data — not "do the numbers agree with each other?" but "are these calls what they appear to be?" — and it returned one reassurance and one unresolved problem at the fifth-ranked outlier. The conclusions in Section 7.x are stated with that in mind.



7. Interpretation and discussion

7.1 What the analysis establishes

[Interpreted] The evidence for negligible differentiation is strong, but it is neither five independent lines nor a test of random mating. FST, ADMIXTURE and TreeMix are three summaries of one genotype matrix: all three are computed from the same per-zone allele counts, and at this level of differentiation a four-population covariance matrix and the pairwise FST matrix are near-deterministic functions of each other. Only the ordinations use a different input — MalariaGEN's published Pf8 genotype-distance matrix — and they are now computed on the same 1,900 isolates as everything else, because stage 3 has been re-run on the analysis set (silhouette by zone −0.020; Table S36 row 21, Figure S20). The agreement between rows 1, 3 and 4 of Table S32 is therefore substantially arithmetic, not corroborative. What this revision adds is robustness rather than independence: the FST summary has been recomputed under six combinations of the two thresholds that define the dataset, and the maximum pairwise value moves only between 0.00204 and 0.00255 (row 5 of Table S32; Table S35). Those six scenarios are not independent lines either — they are the same pipeline on overlapping data — but they establish that the headline does not depend on the analyst's threshold choices, which is a different and more useful thing. The region-level Mantel test does not share that robustness, and it has to be given up. It is a fourth summary of the same matrix, computed from region-level FST, so it was never independent of the others; and it is significant in only 2 of 6 threshold scenarios and 4 of 7 leave-one-region-out replicates, so it cannot be cited as a converging line at all (Table S33, row 1). It was the one result here carrying a positive structure claim, and losing it is a real loss rather than a tidying-up. Described accurately, what remains is: three summaries of one 3,916-SNP genotype matrix (ADMIXTURE on 3,385 LD-pruned sites of it), plus three ordinations of an independently published distance matrix on the same 1,900 isolates, all pointing the same way, and insensitive to both thresholds that define the dataset. The negative half of the result is better supported than it was. The positive half is gone.

Table S32. Convergent evidence for low differentiation between zones. Rows 1, 3 and 4 are three summaries of the same per-population allele frequencies and are not statistically independent of one another; only row 2 uses a different input (MalariaGEN's published genotype-distance matrix), and it is now computed on the same 1,900 isolates rather than on a larger sample. Row 5 is not an independent line but a sensitivity analysis on row 1. The region-level Mantel test, a fourth summary of the same matrix, was discussed alongside these rows in the previous draft; it is significant in only 2 of 6 threshold scenarios and is no longer counted as converging evidence (Table S33, row 1). See the text.

[Interpreted] Two further results point the other way — toward weak but real structure — and a third, the region-level Mantel test, pointed that way at the published thresholds and does not survive varying them. The previous draft either listed these inside the convergence table, where they do not belong, or reported them as negatives when they are not. The Mantel row is now a different kind of problem: it is retained in Table S33 with its sensitivity attached, because the result should be reported honestly rather than deleted, but it is graded not robust and nothing in this document may lean on it.

Table S33. Candidate evidence of weak structure. These results are not convergent with Table S32 and should not be counted as though they were; row 1 is itself computed from the same genotype matrix as Table S32, and after the threshold-sensitivity analysis it is reported as not robust rather than as weak-but-real. Rows 3 and 4 are the only two entries here that still carry a positive structure claim. Row 4 is computed from the rebuilt codon-reconstruction panel (§7.5) and replaces the superseded annotation-string panel entirely.



Figure S17. Isolation by distance at region level on the corrected 3,916-SNP matrix: pairwise FST against great-circle distance for 21 pairs from 7 administrative regions. Censored fit r = 0.427, p = 0.025 (9,999 seeded permutations); uncensored r = 0.414, p = 0.041. Three pairs with negative FST were floored to zero and are named in the output JSON. This figure shows the relationship at the published thresholds only. Under four of the six threshold scenarios in Table S35 the same test is not significant, so the figure must not be read as a robust distance effect.

[Interpreted] Ghanaian P. falciparum behaves as a single, largely panmictic population for common neutral variation, with weak departures detectable in minor-component ancestry and in resistance-marker frequencies. A region-level distance effect is present at the published thresholds but disappears under almost any variation of them, so it is not among the departures this analysis can claim. Those departures that remain are not a clean latitudinal ordering — the transitional zone breaks the monotone pattern in most variables examined (§7.2), pfaat1 S258L excepted — but they are not nothing either. This is a substantive answer to the thesis's first objective, and a negative one for the strong form of its hypothesis: the ecological zones are not acting as demographic barriers at a scale these data can detect. It is not, however, a negative answer for the thesis's third objective; see §7.4 and §7.5.

[Calculated] The earlier draft recorded the ADMIXTURE result as "no ancestry structure aligned to geography" and graded it a well-supported negative. That statement is false as written, and it was never tested. The minor component differs between zones at Kruskal–Wallis H = 105.4, p = 1.05 × 10⁻²², and the contrast — coastal 0.085 and forest 0.092 high, Guinea savanna 0.053 and transitional 0.042 low — is concordant with the FST ranking, though it is not monotone with latitude. The substantive point survives intact and is simply a different point: the magnitude is negligible. Mean minor ancestry spans roughly 0.04 to 0.09 on a component whose admission to the model improves cross-validation error by 0.54 per cent. A difference can be highly significant at n = 1,900 and still be too small to matter for any operational purpose. That is what is being claimed here.

Caveat. A proportion-of-variance figure for the zone effect on minor-component ancestry (of order 2 per cent) was computed during the audit, but on the superseded 3,359-SNP ADMIXTURE output and with no named statistic attached. It has not been recomputed on the final 3,916-SNP run and is therefore not quoted here as a result. [Unknown] — the only effect size the final run supports is whatever is derived from H = 105.4 on 1,900 isolates, and no such statistic was computed and recorded. The Kruskal–Wallis statistic and the per-zone means in Table S33 are from the final run.



Figure S18. Pairwise Hudson FST between the four ecological zones on the corrected 3,916-SNP matrix. Every value is at or below 0.00232; the Guinea savanna–transitional pair (0.00047) is the one whose confidence interval spans zero.

[Interpreted] That the headline is a negative is not surprising, and that is a point in its favour. Ghana is approximately 670 km north to south, human mobility along the north–south corridor is substantial, P. falciparum undergoes obligate sexual recombination in the mosquito, and transmission across most of the country is high. All four favour genetic homogenisation. The finding is consistent with the continental picture, in which West African parasite populations form a single major subpopulation (Amambua-Ngwa et al., 2019), and with the observation that differentiation within West African countries is weak relative to the differentiation between endemicity strata that Mobegi et al. (2014) reported between The Gambia and Guinea.

7.2 What the analysis cannot establish, and why

[Observed] Zone effects cannot be separated from region effects. Three of four zones are each filled by exactly one administrative region (Table S3). For those three, "zone" and "region" are the same variable. Any difference attributed to ecology could equally be attributed to the region — its health system, its drug supply, its sampling protocol, its collection year. No statistical adjustment can separate variables that are collinear by construction.

Denominator for the batch numbers in this section. The study-composition and within-zone batch tests quoted below are computed on the 4,905 Pf8-QC-passing isolates — every one of them carries Pf8's own QC-pass flag, they are the set on which study membership and Fws are defined, and they sit before the Fws and clonality cuts that yield the 1,900 entering the population analyses (Guinea savanna 1,334, deciduous forest 337, coastal savanna 204, transitional zone 25). This single label, 4,905 Pf8-QC-passing (pre-Fws), is used throughout the document; "pre-QC" and "QC-passing" are not used as alternatives for it, because they invite a reader to think two different denominators are in play. So "1083-PF-GH-CONWAY is 100 per cent of the transitional zone, 50 of 50" and "transitional zone, n = 25" are both correct and describe different sets. Study nesting within zone is a property of the Pf8 sampling frame and is not altered by the later cuts, but the counts here are not the analysis-set counts, and the zone-pair overlap was not recomputed on the 1,900 set. The same denominator applies to the polyclonality figures in §7.3 and to limitation 2 in §9.1. Note separately that the marker panel in §7.5 carries a third set of denominators: it is computed on the 1,900 analysed isolates but reports a per-codon called count, 1,834–1,900 depending on the codon, because a mixed call leaves a codon uncalled rather than being resolved to a residue.

[Observed] Zone effects cannot be separated from sequencing-study effects either. This confounder was absent from the previous draft and is at least as serious as the collinearity above. Pf8 isolates arrive through named partner studies, and in Ghana those studies are almost perfectly nested within zones: four of six zone pairs share no sequencing study at all, including Coastal savanna vs Guinea savanna, the pair that produces the maximum FST and the polyclonality contrast. Three of the four zones are dominated by a single study — the transitional zone is one study outright (50 of 50 isolates, 1083-PF-GH-CONWAY), coastal savanna 89 per cent one study (387 of 435), Guinea savanna 74 per cent one study (2,678 of 3,605) — and deciduous forest is the only zone with no majority study, its largest contributing 391 of 815 (48 per cent). Study-associated heterogeneity in these data is demonstrable rather than hypothetical: holding zone constant, so that study varies together with collection year, finer geography and recruitment, call rate differs between the six Guinea savanna studies at p = 2.8 × 10⁻¹⁹⁷, and between four of deciduous forest's five contributing studies at p = 7.7 × 10⁻⁸⁸ (the fifth, 1114-PF-PDN-DBS-GH-GHANSAH, contributes 4 isolates and was too small to enter the test). The Coastal–Guinea contrast has no within-study replicate and cannot be separated from a batch contrast by any reanalysis of these data. This applies with full force to the marker clines in §7.5: the pfaat1 S258L contrast runs between the two zones that share no study. See §9.1, limitation 2.

[Observed] The transitional zone breaks nearly every ordering it appears in, and this is stated once here rather than re-argued later. It is the second-most-northern of the four zones, it contributes 25 of the 1,900 analysed isolates, and it is supplied entirely by one sequencing study. On the final run it is non-monotone with latitude in every variable listed here: minor-component ancestry (0.042, the lowest of the four, below both zones to its south and the zone to its north); pfdhfr S108N and C59R (both 1.000, the highest of the four) and N51I (0.880, above the deciduous forest's 0.802); the pfdhfr IRN triple-mutant haplotype (0.880, the highest of the four, against 0.750 in Guinea savanna); pfdhps G437 (0.800, the lowest of the four, below the Guinea savanna to its north at 0.875); and polyclonal fraction (0.500, below the deciduous forest's 0.539). The one clear exception is pfaat1 S258L, where the transitional zone's 0.640 sits in its correct place between the deciduous forest's 0.735 and the Guinea savanna's 0.544 (§7.5) — so the ordering is broken in most variables examined, not in all of them. Any statement of the form "higher in the south" is therefore a two-group contrast — coast and forest on one side, Guinea savanna on the other — and not a latitudinal cline. That is how it is worded throughout this discussion, and the rest of the document should cross-reference this paragraph rather than re-assert a gradient.

[Observed] Spatial effects cannot be separated from temporal ones. Median collection year differs by six years across zones, spanning 2012 to 2018 (Table S4), and for the transitional zone and coastal savanna the year ranges do not overlap at all: the transitional zone contributes no isolate after 2013 and the coastal savanna none before 2016, leaving a two-year gap between them. Over 2009–2021 Ghana changed first-line therapy and scaled up chemoprevention, so drug-related allele frequencies would be expected to shift with time regardless of geography. Every marker frequency in §7.5 is therefore a 2009–2021 average, not a current value.

[Interpreted] Barrier hypotheses cannot be tested as such. Testing whether a specific feature (the forest–savanna transition, the Volta Lake system) impedes parasite dispersal requires differentiation to be measurable across the feature and compared against differentiation at the same distance elsewhere. With four populations, six pairwise comparisons and FST values below 0.0024, there is no resolution to work with.

[Calculated] The outlier list is a ranking, and only part of it is a finding. Nineteen SNPs exceed the calibrated POD 0.999 quantile of 10.840; 3.9 false positives are expected at that threshold by construction, giving an expected-null-exceedance ratio of 20.6 per cent. Thirteen of the nineteen also survive Benjamini–Hochberg correction at q ≤ 0.10, and eight at q ≤ 0.01. Report the 13 as the finding and the 19 as a ranking. Seventeen of the nineteen fall in annotated genes — 13 genes in all, four of them carrying two SNPs each — and two are intergenic; one of those intergenic sites ranks fifth in the entire scan on XtX (20.79, q = 0.005), so the gene list is not simply the top of the ranking and should not be described as "19 outliers in 13 genes" — although that same site is now flagged lower confidence on variant-quality grounds (see the note below). Nothing about an elevated XtX value establishes that a locus is under selection rather than drifting unusually or sitting near an assembly or alignment artefact; a per-SNP variant-quality check has now closed the artefact reading for the set as a whole (next paragraph), but no quality check can convert a ranking into evidence of selection. The one partial exception is pfaat1 S258L: its XtX of 24.20 is the second-highest in the scan, behind PTP7 (Pf3D7_03_v3:96,476, XtX 26.44); it sits inside the FDR-10% set at q = 0.0049, which is now readable from the restored per-SNP table rather than only from the corrections record (see the note in §7.5); and it is the only outlier carrying a residue for which published gene-editing work exists. That functional support is real but modest and conditional, and the previous draft overstated it — see §7.5, where the experiment is described as its authors described it.

[Calculated] The outlier set is not a calling artefact, and the specific worry about exported proteins can be closed. MQ, VQSLOD, FS, SOR, QD, ReadPosRankSum and the culprit field were pulled from the Pf8 Zarr for all 19 outliers and compared against the other 3,897 scanned SNPs (results/outlier_quality_check.csv). Every significant difference runs in the outliers' favour: higher VQSLOD (p = 0.008), QD (p = 0.016) and QUAL (p = 0.007). No outlier has MQ < 50, strand bias or read-position bias, and all 19 are RegionType Core. The culprit field reads MQRankSum for all 19, but also for 99.6 per cent of the rest of the panel, so it carries no information here. The exported-protein concern raised against the gene list is not supported: the eight SNPs in PTP7, PIESP2, GEXP22, REX2, PF3D7_0831400 and PF3D7_0221200 are the cleanest subset in the panel — FS = 0.00 for all eight, MQ median 60.00, and VQSLOD significantly higher than the rest. Those gene families are repetitive, but these particular sites sit in uniquely mappable sequence. What this check licenses is narrow and should be stated narrowly: the ranking is not an artefact of variant quality. It remains a ranking.

Two outliers downgraded to lower confidence, for a different reason. Pf3D7_11_v3:1054078 (SNRPF, PF3D7_1126900, XtX 13.98, q = 0.031) and Pf3D7_11_v3:1054447 (intergenic, XtX 20.79, q = 0.005) lie 369 bp apart and are the two lowest-InbreedingCoeff sites in the panel (0.147 and 0.193, both below its first percentile). 1054078 carries ExcessHet = 69.59, the only nonzero value among all 3,916 SNPs and roughly 400 times the genome-wide maximum. In a near-haploid organism that points to collapsed paralogy or copy-number variation rather than to mismapping, since MQ and FS are clean at both sites. This matters because 1054447 is the fifth-ranked SNP in the whole scan. Both are therefore reported as lower confidence. The mechanism is [Hypothesized] and unverified: a read-level check — per-sample depth ratio against each isolate's genome-median depth at these two positions — would settle it, and it is item 1 in §8.2.

[Interpreted] It cannot establish that the retained panel is neutral, because no neutral mask was ever defined. The 3,916 SNPs are the sites that survived the FILTER_PASS, biallelic-SNP, missingness and minor-allele-frequency criteria, and not one of those is a neutrality criterion: no coding or non-coding partition, no synonymous/non-synonymous distinction, no gene-proximity exclusion and no recombination-rate weighting was applied at any stage. The panel therefore contains the resistance loci themselves — the XtX scan is run on this same panel and returns pfaat1 and PTP7 from it — so the set used to measure background differentiation also contains the sites under the strongest selection in the genome. That inclusion is very unlikely to be driving the differentiation result: 19 outliers among 3,916 sites cannot move a ratio-of-averages estimator away from 0.002, and the maximum FST is stable at 0.00204–0.00255 across six scenarios that change the panel composition substantially. The point is not that the number is wrong but that the word is unearned. Wherever “neutral” appears in this report it should be read as “genome-wide at the retained common variants”, never as “at sites shown to be free of selection”. Building a genuine neutral mask — excluding the known resistance loci, the antigenic and invasion gene families, and sites in high linkage with them, then recomputing differentiation on what remains — is a specified next step and is not done here.

7.3 Unexpected observations

[Observed] The filter artefact, and the second defect underneath it. The single most consequential finding of the audit is not a biological one. The ingest mask FILTER_PASS & is_snp & numalt == 1 silently removed most canonical antimalarial-resistance codons — among them pfcrt K76T, pfmdr1 N86Y, pfdhps K540E and most of the pfk13 domain — because in Pf8 these codons are overwhelmingly multiallelic or carry a spanning-deletion ALT. The previous draft's statement that no validated resistance locus appeared among the outliers was therefore not a biological result but a filtering artefact, and interpreting it biologically was a mistake.

The per-codon counts for this artefact are themselves superseded. The audit quantified the filter artefact as "28 of 44 codon-allele records removed; only 6 of the 21 polymorphic records ever visible to the original scan". Those figures are properties of the superseded 44-codon annotation-string panel, which has since been shown to be the wrong panel (§7.5). They are retained here only as a description of what the filter did to that panel, and should not be quoted as properties of the current one, which contains 57 codons across nine genes, 24 of them polymorphic, and which does not use numalt, is_snp or annotation strings at all.

[Interpreted] What the filter artefact turned out to be a symptom of. Removing the mask recovered the codons but did not fix the panel, because the panel's second defect was independent of the filter: markers were identified by matching SnpEff ANN_HGVS_p protein-change strings against a hand-written list. That fails for any resistance allele which is the reference residue, and for any residue reachable only by two changes within one codon. Both failure modes occurred, and one of them hid an allele at 0.897 (§7.5). The general lesson is now a double one: a filter written for one purpose (biallelic sites for a differentiation scan) silently redefined the question being asked of the data; and the repair, which addressed the filter alone, left the identification layer broken for a further round. Neither defect could be revealed by any downstream result, because both produced well-formed output. See §9.1, limitation 13.

[Observed] The common-variant ceiling — what was measured. The whole Ghanaian sample yields 3,916 SNPs at MAF ≥ 5 per cent after the corrected filter order, out of 1,807,331 sites that pass variant missingness — a reduction of 99.78 per cent at the MAF step alone. A diagnostic projection put the genome-wide ceiling at roughly 5,500 common sites, which would make the analysed panel about 71 per cent of what is available; that projection (and the related 4,832 figure) was computed under a depth-only mask on the superseded 3,359-SNP panel and should be read as a pre-re-run estimate, not a measured quantity. What is now measured is the MAF floor's effect downstream: lowering it to 1 per cent yields 10,876 SNPs on the same 1,900 isolates, and lowering it to 2 per cent yields 6,852 (Table S35).

[Interpreted] What follows from it. Fewer than four thousand common SNPs materially constrains every downstream analysis — the outlier scan most of all, where 6 of the 19 quantile outliers do not survive FDR correction. The shortage is most plausibly a property of P. falciparum population genomics within a single country, an abundance of rare variation with comparatively little common variation, rather than a defect of this pipeline; that attribution is an inference from the literature and not something these data establish. On either reading, a study of this design has considerably less power than a sample of 1,900 isolates would suggest.

[Observed] Polyclonality does not order cleanly along the gradient, and the previous draft's claim that it does was formed by quoting only the two endpoints. Ghana's zones run north to south as Guinea savanna, transitional, deciduous forest, coastal savanna. The polyclonal fractions in that geographic order are 0.597, 0.500, 0.539, 0.444 (§6.2, on the 4,905 Pf8-QC-passing isolates — see the note in §7.2) — not monotone: the transitional zone, which lies north of the deciduous forest, has the lower value, and its 25-of-50 figure carries essentially no information. The contrast is also between-study: the coastal and Guinea savanna samples share no sequencing study, and within deciduous forest alone the polyclonal fraction ranges 0.430–0.578 between the four of its five contributing studies large enough to test, while the underlying Fws distributions differ between those studies at Kruskal–Wallis H = 14.9, p = 1.9 × 10⁻³ (n = 811). No test on the polyclonal fraction itself exists in the outputs, and the p-value must not be attached to it. Reported in fairness, the two zone pairs that do share a study reproduce the overall direction (within 1083-PF-GH-CONWAY, Guinea savanna 0.532 against transitional 0.500), so the gradient is not purely an artefact — it is simply not separable from batch for the pairs that carry the largest differences. The honest statement is that mixed-infection frequency is highest in Guinea savanna and lowest on the coast, with no clean monotone ordering in between and no separation from sequencing study.

[Hypothesized] If polyclonality tracks transmission intensity and transmission intensity tracks ecological zone, then Fws is itself a candidate zone-level phenotype worth analysing in its own right rather than used only as a QC filter. That analysis has not been performed, and any version of it would have to be stratified by sequencing study and run on a stated denominator. The threshold-sensitivity analysis bears on it indirectly and in a direction worth recording: relaxing the Fws cut raises maximum pairwise FST slightly rather than lowering it — 0.00232 at Fws ≥ 0.95 against 0.00255 at Fws ≥ 0.80 and 0.00254 with no cut at all (Table S35) — so the polyclonal isolates the filter removes were not masking between-zone differentiation.

[Calculated] Likelihood monotonicity in TreeMix — why the likelihood curve cannot choose a model. The log-likelihood never falls as migration edges are added — 89.65 at m = 0, 91.21 at m = 1, 92.23 at m = 2, 92.26 at m = 3, and 92.2642 at both m = 4 and m = 5, identical to the recorded precision — so likelihood cannot select a model, and the previous draft left the choice between m = 2 and m = 3 open. Re-running the sweep with -se narrows it without settling it: the first two edges carry weights 0.152 (SE 0.048, p = 7.5 × 10⁻⁴) and 0.805 (SE 0.100, p = 3.3 × 10⁻¹⁶), while the third edge added at m = 3 has weight 0.065 (SE 0.147, p = 0.33) and is indistinguishable from zero. A two-edge graph is the model the edge tests select, which brings statistical support to what was previously argued only from theory — that four populations can support only about two edges — without settling it, since those tests are conditional on a topology, root and edge count all fixed beforehand and the variance-explained criterion across the full m = 0 to 5 sweep still prefers m = 3. The m = 2 vs m = 3 hedging in the previous draft is replaced by a stated model choice, not by a resolution.



Figure S19. TreeMix variance explained against the number of migration edges on the corrected 3,916-SNP matrix: 0.8970 at m = 0, 0.9405 at m = 1, 0.9969 at m = 2, 0.9997 at m = 3. The gain past m = 2 is not supported by the edge standard errors (third edge p = 0.33), and m ≥ 3 is not identifiable with four populations.

7.4 Practical significance

[Interpreted] What the neutral result licenses. Neutral common-variant differentiation between zones is negligible — maximum pairwise FST 0.00232, with one of six confidence intervals spanning zero (Guinea savanna vs transitional zone, [−0.00083, 0.00178]), and the maximum staying within 0.00204–0.00255 across six threshold scenarios (Table S35) — so a spatially sparse design is adequate for characterising neutral structure. Marker-frequency representativeness is a separate question, and the rebuilt resistance panel shows significant between-zone variation at six of the nine panel genes, including pfdhfr, pfdhps and pfaat1 (the others being pfcrt, pfmdr1 and pfmdr2). If the question is whether a handful of sites will give a representative picture of the neutral genomic background, these data answer yes; if the question is whether those sites will give a representative picture of resistance-marker frequencies, these data answer no.

[Interpreted] What it does not license, and what the previous draft got wrong. The previous draft went further and concluded that "sentinel surveillance at a small number of sites is an adequate design for detecting emergence — the samples do not need to be spatially comprehensive to be representative". That claim is withdrawn. It is unsupported by anything measured here, and it is contradicted by the mechanism this project's own literature review sets out: selection builds connectivity that neutral demography does not, so a selected haplotype can sweep across a region while the neutral background stays homogeneous — and, symmetrically, resistance-allele trajectories can remain site-specific even under neutral panmixia. Sentinel surveillance measures marker frequencies, not neutral structure. Low genome-wide neutral FST says nothing about whether marker frequencies at one site represent the country.

[Observed] The resistance panel settles the question empirically, and the answer is no. Marker frequencies do differ by zone. pfaat1 S258L spans about 28 percentage points from coast to far north (0.824 to 0.544, χ² p = 1.89 × 10⁻¹⁸, n = 1,896 called). pfdhps G437 — the allele that was missing from the panel altogether in the previous draft — runs 0.964 in deciduous forest and 0.941 on the coast against 0.875 in Guinea savanna and 0.800 in the transitional zone (χ² p = 1.2 × 10⁻⁶, n = 1,892). The pfdhfr codons are heterogeneous across the four zones (S108N χ² p = 9.4 × 10⁻⁷; C59R 4.8 × 10⁻⁵; N51I 2.4 × 10⁻⁴) and the IRN triple-mutant haplotype itself runs 0.750 in Guinea savanna, 0.788 in deciduous forest, 0.867 on the coast and 0.880 in the transitional zone on 25 isolates. These are all four-zone heterogeneity tests; no directional south-versus-Guinea contrast was run, and the transitional zone's values rest on 25 isolates (§7.2). A sentinel site on the coast and a sentinel site in Upper East would report materially different resistance-marker profiles from the same panmictic parasite population. The operational implication is the reverse of the previous draft's: neutral structure can be characterised sparsely; marker frequencies cannot. A surveillance design intended to track resistance needs to span the gradient, and this analysis is evidence for rather than against that requirement.

[Calculated] Robustness of the readings above to the corrected panel. The conclusions in this section were reached on a neutral panel that grew from 3,359 to 3,916 SNPs (+16.6 per cent) once the filter order was corrected, with stages 4 to 9 re-run on the larger matrix. The substantive readings held — near-panmixia, maximum pairwise FST of about 0.0023, and a near-flat cross-validation curve. One reading did not hold, though it failed a later check rather than this one: isolation by distance, described in earlier drafts as a weak, leverage-dependent but real distance effect, is significant in only 2 of 6 threshold scenarios and is now reported as not robust (§8, Table S35). Within the corrected run it had in fact improved — the Mantel result no longer depends on the undisclosed censoring, and significant jackknife replicates went 2 of 7 to 4 of 7 — which is a useful reminder that a result can be made more reproducible and still not be true. One result gained inferential support: TreeMix now reports m = 2 as a selected descriptive model on edge p-values instead of hedging, though the variance-explained criterion across the full sweep still prefers m = 3. The BayPass threshold, outlier count and gene list all moved (11.232 to 10.840; 15 to 19 outliers; 11 to 13 genes). The marker panel is a different matter: it was not re-run but rebuilt, by a new script that reconstructs codons rather than matching annotation strings, and several of its conclusions did change (§7.5, §8). This wording is used identically in §6 and in §8; details and the full comparison are in Table S34.

[Interpreted] The containment corollary, correctly scoped. Because there is no detectable neutral barrier, there is no demographic barrier for a geographically targeted containment intervention to exploit, and an allele that establishes anywhere in Ghana has an unobstructed demographic route to the rest of the country. That remains a reasonable reading, and it is now the better supported for the absence of a robust distance effect: there is not even a weak isolation-by-distance gradient left to qualify it. It does not follow that spread will be uniform in rate or that a single site will see it first; the pfaat1, pfdhps and pfdhfr zone differences show that selection and drug pressure impose their own geography on top of free gene flow.

Caveat on that inference. The argument from low FST to free gene flow runs from an absence of detectable structure to an assertion about process. Low FST is equally consistent with recent common ancestry and with a large effective population size holding drift down. Distinguishing "currently well connected" from "recently separated but not yet differentiated" requires demographic inference these analyses have not attempted (§8.2).

[Proposed] The unqualified surveillance claim must be removed wherever it is restated. There is no section titled "plain-language summary" in this manuscript; the nearest equivalents are the Abstract's concluding sentence, which reproduced the claim without even the caveat above, and the positive-findings list in §10.1. Both must carry the qualified form given in the first paragraph of this section — neutral structure sparsely characterisable, marker-frequency representativeness a separate and unanswered question — and not the unqualified one. This is the clearest case in the document of a conclusion that the corrected resistance panel reverses, so the three locations must not disagree. The same applies to isolation by distance: any statement of it in the Abstract or in §10.1 must now carry the 2-of-6 grading, not a "weak but real" one.

7.5 The rebuilt resistance panel

[Observed] The panel in the previous draft was the wrong panel, and this section is a replacement rather than a revision. An external critique raised five points about it; all five were upheld, and four were defects in code. The panel is now produced by step12_codon_panel.py, which maps each requested codon to its three genomic positions through the CDS, collects every variant record at those positions regardless of numalt or is_snp, applies each isolate's alleles to the reference codon and translates the result. It reports the full residue spectrum including the reference residue. The panel covers 57 codons across nine genes on the 1,900 analysed isolates, of which 24 are polymorphic and 33 monomorphic; a mixed call makes a codon uncalled rather than being resolved to a residue, so the denominator is per codon, 1,834–1,900 called, and is stated with every frequency below. Six of the nine genes sit on the plus strand (pfap2mu, pfcoronin, pfcrt, pfdhfr, pfdhps, pfmdr1) and three on the minus strand (pfaat1, pfk13, pfmdr2), which is the direction the reconstruction has to get right. Every number in this section comes from that run; nothing from the previous panel is carried forward.

[Observed] Why the old panel failed. It identified markers by matching SnpEff ANN_HGVS_p strings against a hand-written list. Two failure modes, both realised. (i) A resistance allele that is itself the reference residue can never appear as an ALT change. The official Pf8 methodology says so in terms: "3D7 sequence translates as a G at 437, this is thought to be the mutant allele, i.e. 3D7 does not have the wild type allele." At pfdhps 437 the only annotation is Gly437Ala, the revertant; a search for "Ala437Gly" matched nothing, and A437G was absent from the panel entirely at a true frequency of 0.897. (ii) A residue reachable only by two changes in one codon is never annotated. pfcrt codon 75 carries 403621 (A>G, annotated Asn75Asp) and 403623 (T>A, annotated Asn75Lys); Asn→Glu requires both, so neither annotation names N75E, which is the residue the CVIET haplotype actually carries. This, and not a "naming discrepancy", is the entire explanation of the MalariaGEN disagreement the previous draft reported at that codon. The phrase "naming discrepancy" is withdrawn.

[Observed] Antifolate resistance is high but not uniform, and "near fixation" was the wrong description. The pfdhfr marginal residue frequencies are N51I 0.786 (n = 1,891), C59R 0.886 (n = 1,888) and S108N 0.908 (n = 1,889), each heterogeneous across the four zones and lowest in Guinea savanna (χ² p = 2.4 × 10⁻⁴, 4.8 × 10⁻⁵ and 9.4 × 10⁻⁷). S108N at 0.908 is the most frequent antifolate residue in the panel. The previous draft quoted the range of those three marginals, 0.786–0.908, as though it were the triple-mutant frequency. It is not a haplotype frequency at all, and that range is withdrawn. Counted within isolates on the 1,876 called at all three codons, the IRN triple mutant sits at 0.771 (1,446 of 1,876; 95% CI 0.751–0.789) — below the minimum component frequency on the same complete-case set (51I = 0.788), as it must be. At roughly three-quarters it is not "close to fixed", and it is also not a single dominant haplotype: NRN sits at 0.116 and the fully wild-type NCS at 0.091, so roughly one isolate in eleven is wild type at all three pfdhfr codons. The quadruple IRNL, which would add I164L, is 0.000 (n = 1,874; 164 is monomorphic at the reference residue in all 1,898 called).

[Observed] pfdhps G437 is at 0.897 and was missing from the previous draft altogether. Overall 0.897 on 1,892 called, and by zone 0.964 deciduous forest, 0.941 coastal savanna, 0.875 Guinea savanna, 0.800 transitional zone (χ² p = 1.2 × 10⁻⁶) — the transitional zone again breaking the ordering (§7.2). It is a high frequency but not the highest antifolate residue in the panel: pfdhfr S108N is 0.908. The reason sulfadoxine–pyrimethamine retains utility in chemoprevention is therefore not that the pfdhps background is clean; it is that the downstream codons remain rare. K540E is 0.009 (n = 1,895) and A581G 0.014 (n = 1,890), and the 437G+540E double haplotype, which is what actually marks high-grade sulfadoxine resistance, is 0.010 on 1,888 complete cases. That is the standard West African pattern — 437G near-fixed, 540E rare — and it is a pattern the previous draft could not see, because it was reporting a pfdhps panel with its highest-frequency locus deleted.

[Observed] Chloroquine resistance has largely reverted, and CVIET is now demonstrated to be a unit rather than inferred to be one. The pfcrt CVIET codons travel together at marginal frequencies of 0.071 (M74I, N75E, K76T), 0.070 (A220S), 0.074 (Q271E) and 0.076 (R371I). Counted as a haplotype on the 1,873 isolates called across codons 72–76, CVIET is 0.071 (95% CI 0.060–0.084), and the only two haplotypes observed at all are CVMNK (0.929) and CVIET (0.071), with nothing intermediate. The previous draft inferred co-travel from six similar marginals; it is now measured. The residue at codon 75 is Glu, as MalariaGEN's published calls state, and codon reconstruction reproduces their call on every isolate. Note that codon 75 is called in 1,874 of the 1,900 isolates, so it loses 26 to mixed calls; the 52 figure circulating for this codon is the mixed-call record count and not an isolate count, and the two must not be conflated. Four pfcrt codons reach the four-zone heterogeneity threshold — 74, 75/76 (χ² p = 3.5–3.7 × 10⁻²), 220 (2.6 × 10⁻²) and 356 (I356T, χ² p = 1.6 × 10⁻³, the strongest of them) — so it is not the case that no pfcrt codon shows between-zone heterogeneity.

[Observed] Artemisinin resistance is absent, on a panel that is now the right panel — and the negative is historical, not current. The pfk13 list in the previous draft was written from memory. It held 13 distinct protein changes (a duplicated M476I record inflated the apparent count to 14), misclassified P441L and C469F as validated when WHO lists them as candidate, omitted P574L and G533S, and requested R622I without ever retrieving it, so that marker became a silent non-result. The panel now follows the WHO compendium v1.0 (December 2025) and covers all 14 validated markers (F446I, N458Y, C469Y, M476I, Y493H, G533S, R539T, I543T, P553L, R561H, P574L, C580Y, R622I, A675V) and all 6 candidates (E252Q, P441L, C469F, N537I, G538V, V568G). That is 20 variants at 19 distinct codons, because C469Y and C469F share codon 469. The reconstruction covers 23 pfk13 codons in all; the four beyond those 19 are potential-tier or regionally reported codons retained for completeness, and the validated and candidate tiers are kept distinct throughout — the candidates are not described as validated anywhere in this document. Twenty-two of the 23 codons are monomorphic at the reference residue, and the twenty-third, codon 458, is at 0.9995 (1,865 of 1,866 called; one isolate carries Asn458Asp, which is not the validated Asn458Tyr). So the WHO negative stands, but the codon is not strictly fixed and should not be described as such. Two qualifications the critique rightly demanded. The denominator is per codon, 1,845–1,876 of 1,900 called — not "all 1,900 at every codon", which the previous draft asserted. And the sampling period is 2009–2021, so this is a historical negative about the parasites sequenced, not a statement about Ghana today.

[Observed] The kelch13 corroboration is weaker than the previous draft claimed. Pf8 assigns a kelch13 status over the whole 349–726 domain and classifies any missing call in that range as undetermined, so only 36 of 1,900 isolates have a complete status. Within those 36 the commonest change is A578S, which is explicitly not a resistance marker. Thirty-six complete statuses are weak corroboration for per-codon calls on roughly 1,860 isolates, and the two should not be presented as equivalent evidence. The real independent check on this panel is the per-sample concordance in §8 — but it reaches none of the 23 pfk13 codons, so for the artemisinin negative these 36 domain-level statuses remain the only external check there is, rather than the kelch13 column.

[Observed] The pfaat1 S258L cline is the positive result. Pf3D7_06_v3:1215233 is Ser258Leu, with XtX 24.20 and q = 0.0049 — the second-highest XtX in the scan, behind PTP7 at 26.44. Its frequency is 0.609 overall on 1,896 called, falling from 0.824 in coastal savanna through 0.735 in deciduous forest to 0.544 in Guinea savanna, with the transitional zone at 0.640 on 25 isolates (χ² p = 1.89 × 10⁻¹⁸). This is the one marker in which the transitional zone sits in its correct latitudinal place (§7.2). The previous draft gave p = 2.03 × 10⁻¹⁸ on an assumed denominator of 1,900; the shift comes from counting only the isolates actually called, and the coastal frequency rounds to 0.824 on either denominator. The manuscript's XtX of 24.44 for this locus is the superseded 8-thread figure and should not be quoted; the final run gives 24.20. The variant-quality check places this SNP in the clean tier: MQ 60.00 at the 100th percentile, VQSLOD 12.09 at the 96.7th, FS 0.00 (§7.2).

Provenance of the pfaat1 q-values — now resolved. The XtX values quoted above are read from the final run's own output (results/baypass/outlier_snps.bed: 24.19825 at Pf3D7_06_v3:1215233 and 16.53525 at 1214350, with PTP7 at Pf3D7_03_v3:96476 = 26.44106 the only higher value). The q = 0.0049 attached to both was previously sourced only to the corrections document's narrative, because results/baypass/xtx_all_snps.csv for the final run was empty. That file has since been found to have been truncated to zero bytes by accident, and it has been regenerated from the intact BayPass outputs, with consistency checks rather than proof of row-for-row identity (§9.3). The per-SNP q-values are therefore on disk again: both AAT1 SNPs carry q = 0.00489 in results/outlier_quality_check.csv, so the value and the tie are now reproducible from a primary output rather than from the narrative, and the instruction in the previous draft to regenerate the table before this sentence ships is discharged. The superseded run's table, in which 1214350 carried q = 0.0240 and the two were not tied, remains superseded and should not be quoted.

[Observed] One correction to the previous draft's reporting of this locus carries over unchanged: it listed "two outlier SNPs" in AAT1, which read as two independent lines of evidence. The second outlier, Pf3D7_06_v3:1214350, is Ile552Ile — synonymous, low impact, 883 bp away, XtX 16.54 and carrying the same q-value, 0.0049, now read from the regenerated per-SNP table (see the note above). This is one signal plus a linked synonymous site, not two signals. The locus also did not appear in the pipeline's KNOWN_RESISTANCE_LOCI list, which is why it surfaced as an anonymous transporter in a table while the abstract simultaneously declared that no validated resistance locus had been found.

[Interpreted] What S258L actually does — the previous draft inverted the source paper's own conclusion, and the error was ours. The previous draft called S258L "the functionally validated chloroquine-resistance variant", then built the discussion on the claim that "nothing in it or in these data tests amodiaquine". Both statements are wrong, and checking them against the source reverses the argument. Amodiaquine was tested: the methods state that "Parasite IC50 values for CQ, amodiaquine, lumefantrine, mefloquine and quinine were measured". The shifts were sub-threshold: "these IC50 value shifts were well below the threshold associated with clinical resistance". And the authors explicitly reject the other-quinoline hypothesis — "is not supported" — concluding instead that chloroquine was "the primary selective force". Framing the Ghanaian cline as amodiaquine-maintained therefore did not extend the paper's finding; it contradicted it.

[Interpreted] And the effect itself is modest and conditional. S258L raised chloroquine IC50 1.5-fold, in a single chloroquine-resistant Southeast Asian line (NHP4026), with epistasis between S258L and F313S and an explicit caveat that the effect depends on pfcrt background. The much-cited rise from 0 per cent to 97 per cent is Gambian, 1984–2014; the paper says nothing about Ghana. The accurate description is therefore: pfaat1 S258L is a variant with a demonstrated but modest, epistasis- and background-dependent effect that potentiates chloroquine resistance — not a validated resistance determinant, and not a marker of any current drug. One detail of this sample sharpens the caveat: pfaat1 F313S, the epistatic partner, is absent — codon 313 is at the reference residue in all 1,900 isolates — so the interaction the experiment demonstrated cannot be operating in these parasites at all.

[Hypothesized] What might maintain the cline, with the candidates ranked by how well they are supported. Three non-exclusive possibilities, and the honest ordering is not the previous draft's. (i) Historical persistence. Chloroquine was first-line therapy in Ghana until 2005 and the paper's own conclusion is that chloroquine was the selective force; a chloroquine-era allele decaying unevenly is the explanation most consistent with the source. (ii) A documented fitness cost without drug, which would slow reversion rather than reverse it and is the reason a standing intermediate frequency is expected rather than either fixation or zero. (iii) Residual 4-aminoquinoline pressure from artesunate–amodiaquine, a current first-line ACT whose deployment is not uniform across the country. This third possibility is offered as an untested possible contemporary driver only. Nothing in these data tests it, no drug-pressure or deployment data were available to this analysis, and the experimental work that does exist found amodiaquine shifts below the clinical threshold and argued against the partner-drug hypothesis. It is a hypothesis worth stating and not a reading of the evidence.

[Hypothesized] If any version of the drug-pressure hypothesis holds, the cline should correlate with zonal AS-AQ deployment and adherence rather than with any ecological variable, and should be unstable over time as treatment policy changes. Neither prediction is tested here, and the alternative that S258L tracks some unmeasured ecological or demographic covariate cannot be excluded. Note also that the coast-versus-Guinea-savanna contrast that carries the cline is exactly the zone pair with no shared sequencing study (§7.2), so a batch contribution to its magnitude cannot be ruled out by any reanalysis of these data.

Temporal controls on the cline — computed on the superseded panel. The previous draft reported that the S258L gradient survives restriction to a single collection year and that there is no within-Guinea-savanna trend with year. Both checks were run on the superseded annotation-string panel and have not been recomputed on the reconstruction panel. S258L is a single-SNP ALT change that the old annotation handled correctly, and its frequencies are essentially unchanged between the two panels (coastal 0.824 either way, Guinea savanna 0.544 against 0.544), so the checks very likely carry over — but "very likely carries over" is not a result, and they are listed as outstanding in §8.2 item 2. [Unknown]

[Observed] WHO labels attach to variants, not genes, and the previous draft's gene-level labels must go. The compendium classifies individual variants. It contains no validated or candidate lumefantrine marker at all — verbatim: "The review did not identify any validated or candidate molecular markers associated with lumefantrine resistance." The label "pfmdr1, WHO-validated, lumefantrine" used in the previous draft is therefore a category error twice over. Further: pfaat1, pfap2mu and pfmdr2 appear nowhere in the compendium at any tier. They are retained in the panel because they are scientifically interesting — pfaat1 S258L in particular is this analysis's strongest marker signal — but they must never be described as WHO-classified. pfcoronin is in the compendium at the potential tier only, and all three of its panel codons are monomorphic at the reference residue here.

[Observed] What can and cannot be said about tiers in this panel. Every polymorphic variant here that does carry a WHO tier falls within pfcrt, pfdhfr, pfdhps or pfmdr1 — but the converse does not follow, and the previous draft's phrasing asserted it. Not every polymorphic codon in those four genes is WHO-classified. The polymorphic codons in them are pfcrt 74/75/76/220/271/326/356/371, pfdhfr 51/59/108, pfdhps 431/436/437/540/581/613 and pfmdr1 86/184/1246, and no source available to this analysis gives a per-variant tier for most of them: pfdhps I431V and pfcrt I356T are not established compendium variants in any material provided, and the pfmdr1 codons N86Y, Y184F and D1246Y carry no tier at all, since the only indication under which they would is lumefantrine and the compendium lists no lumefantrine marker. [Unknown] — per-variant tiers for the polymorphic pfcrt and pfdhps codons. Separately, every WHO-validated and WHO-candidate pfk13 variant is at the reference residue, but that is not the same as saying no pfk13 codon varies: codon 458 is polymorphic, Asn 0.9995 against Asp 0.0005 on 1,866 called, and the observed residue is Asp rather than the validated Tyr, so the WHO negative holds while the codon is a genuine non-WHO change. The three remaining polymorphic codons outside those four genes — pfaat1 258, pfap2mu 160 and pfmdr2 492 — are non-WHO by construction, since none of those genes appears in the compendium.

8. Robustness checks and alternative approaches

[Calculated] Correcting the pipeline did not overturn its population-genetic conclusions, and that is worth reporting precisely rather than as a slogan. The corrected filter order recomputes variant missingness on the 1,900 isolates that actually survive QC rather than on the 2,017 measured before 117 of them are dropped. At identical thresholds and with an identical sample set, this yields 3,916 SNPs instead of 3,359 — a 16.6 per cent larger analysis matrix for free — and stages 4 through 9 were re-run on it with a corrected FDR, seeded permutation tests, single-threaded BayPass and TreeMix standard errors in place. The substantive readings held — near-panmixia, maximum pairwise FST of about 0.0023, and a near-flat cross-validation curve. Two results were strengthened rather than unchanged: the Mantel result no longer depends on the undisclosed censoring, and TreeMix now reports m = 2 as a selected descriptive model on edge p-values instead of hedging, though the variance-explained criterion across the full sweep still prefers m = 3. The BayPass threshold, outlier count and gene list all moved (11.232 to 10.840; 15 to 19 outliers; 11 to 13 genes). No population-genetic conclusion changed under this correction, which is the claim being made — not that nothing changed, and not that nothing has changed since. A later check did overturn one, and it is reported two paragraphs below.

[Calculated] The marker panel is the exception, and the claim above must not be extended to it. The resistance panel was not re-run on a larger matrix; it was rebuilt by a different method, and conclusions did change (§7.5). An allele absent from the old panel turned out to be at 0.897; a "triple-mutant frequency" of 0.786–0.908 turned out to be the range of three marginals and the haplotype is 0.771; two pfk13 markers were misclassified as validated; and a reported "naming discrepancy" with MalariaGEN turned out to be a defect in our own identification layer. Anyone reading §8 as "the audit found nothing substantive" is reading the neutral half and missing the marker half.

[Calculated] The four outstanding checks have now been run. Three confirmed robustness; one overturned a conclusion. (i) Threshold sensitivity. scripts/step13_sensitivity.py rebuilds the analysis matrix under six combinations of the two thresholds that define the dataset — the Fws monoclonality cut, which discards 57 per cent of isolates and does so unevenly by zone, and the MAF floor, which discards 99.8 per cent of sites — and recomputes pairwise FST and the region-level Mantel test for each (Table S35). Near-panmixia is insensitive to both: maximum pairwise FST moves only between 0.00204 and 0.00255, a total spread of 0.0005, over sample sizes from 1,900 to 4,403 and SNP counts from 3,275 to 10,876. That is the strongest evidence for the headline conclusion in the whole analysis. Isolation by distance is not: the Mantel test is significant in 2 of 6 scenarios, failing at every relaxation of the Fws cut and at MAF ≥ 0.01, and holding only at the published thresholds and marginally at MAF ≥ 0.02. Taken with the leave-one-region-out jackknife, which already showed 4 of 7 and collapse when Upper East is dropped, the finding must now be reported as not robust. It was the manuscript's only positive spatial finding, and it did not survive the two checks the audit itself identified as most needed. That is the overturned conclusion, and it is a loss rather than a refinement. (ii) Outlier variant quality. The 19 XtX outliers are better-supported calls than the rest of the panel, not worse, and the exported-protein artefact worry is closed; two adjacent chromosome-11 SNPs are downgraded for an unrelated and still-unverified reason (§7.2 and its note). (iii) Stage 3 re-run on the 1,900-isolate analysis set. The ordination conclusion is unchanged, the silhouette becomes slightly more negative, and the figures now describe the same sample as every other result (Figure S20). (iv) GeoJSON provenance remains open and cannot be closed analytically; it requires a written enquiry to the likely custodian (§9.3). Two secondary observations from (i) are worth recording in their own right: relaxing Fws slightly raises FST (0.00232 to 0.00255), so the polyclonal isolates the filter removes were not masking differentiation; and tripling the SNP count via MAF ≥ 0.01 lowers it (0.00204), as expected if rarer variants are less differentiated between zones.

Table S34. The conclusions against the corrected pipeline. Original-run values are from the superseded manuscript and the corrections record and are given only as before/after comparisons; corrected-run values are read from the final run's own outputs. Where a quantity moved, the row says so. The Mantel region row records a conclusion that this correction strengthened and a later check overturned; the grading that governs is the one in Table S35. The last two rows concern the marker panel, which was rebuilt rather than re-run; the SNP-count columns do not apply to them.

Why marginal agreement was not a validation. The previous draft validated the panel by comparing marginal frequencies against MalariaGEN's published calls and reporting that 21 of 23 shared markers agreed within 0.03. That check cannot detect compensating per-sample errors — two pipelines can assign different residues to different isolates and still return the same frequency — and it is the check that let the pfcrt 75 defect be recorded as a "naming discrepancy at identical frequency" rather than as a bug. The replacement is call-by-call: on the isolates where both pipelines called, 47,012 of 47,012 calls agree, 100.0000 per cent across 25 markers, all nine pfcrt codons included. Codon 75 now agrees exactly, because reconstruction yields Glu where string-matching yielded Asp. Per-sample concordance is the claim to quote; the 0.03 marginal-agreement figure is superseded and should not appear anywhere in the document.

BayPass run labels — three thresholds circulate and must not be conflated. The manuscript run was 8-threaded: threshold 11.232, 15 outliers, 11 genes (results/baypass_8thread_archive/xtx_calibration.json). An 8-thread re-run of the same command with the same -seed 42 returned a different outlier count (16); that disagreement is how the non-determinism was found. An interim single-threaded run on the same 3,359-SNP panel gave threshold 11.047 and 17 outliers (corrected/xtx_calibration.json); this is the value quoted in §4 of the corrections document, and it is superseded. The final single-threaded run on the 3,916-SNP panel gives threshold 10.840, 19 outliers above threshold, 13 at q ≤ 0.10 and 13 outlier genes, and is the only run reported as a result. Quoting 11.232 to 11.047 and 11.232 to 10.840 in the same document is consistent only if these labels are used. [Unknown] whether the 8-thread re-run and the interim single-threaded run were the same job: the logs do not record it, and the two documented outlier counts (16 and 17) differ, so they are listed separately here.

8.1 Checks performed

[Observed] The first eight rows below were performed during the original analysis; the remainder were performed during or after the audit. Rows 15 to 18 concern the marker panel and were new to the previous revision. Rows 19 to 21 are new to this revision: the threshold-sensitivity analysis, the variant-quality profile of the outlier set and the stage 3 re-run on the analysis set. Three of the four checks carried as outstanding into this revision are now closed; the fourth, the provenance of the zone polygon layer, cannot be closed analytically (§9.3). Row 19 is the only check in this table that overturned a conclusion.

Table S36. Robustness checks performed. Bold rows are new since the original analysis. Four rows on the marker panel were new to the previous revision — the rebuild, the per-sample concordance, the within-isolate haplotypes and the pfk13/WHO check (rows 15 to 18) — with a fifth (the numalt re-fetch, row 14) carried forward but recorded as insufficient. Rows 19 to 21 are new to this revision; row 19 is the only check in the table that overturned a conclusion, and row 12 is graded by it.

Table S35. Threshold sensitivity. Both thresholds that define the analysis dataset were varied and pairwise FST and the region-level Mantel test recomputed from scratch under each scenario (scripts/step13_sensitivity.py; sensitivity_summary.csv, sensitivity_fst.csv). Maximum FST is the Coastal savanna–Guinea savanna pair in all six scenarios. Mantel statistics are the censored versions, with negative FST pairs floored to zero, on 9,999 seeded permutations; on the uncensored statistic only the baseline scenario clears p < 0.05 at all (baseline 0.041, MAF ≥ 0.02 0.050, the rest 0.064–0.200). The six scenarios are not independent analyses — they share data and a pipeline — but they test the analyst's threshold choices, which no other check in this document does. Near-panmixia survives; isolation by distance does not.



Figure S20. The three ordinations re-run on the 1,900-isolate analysis set and coloured by ecological zone, from MalariaGEN's published Pf8 genotype-distance matrix re-sliced to those isolates. Silhouette by zone is −0.020 on the raw distances, against −0.016 on the 4,905 Pf8-QC-passing isolates that the published figures describe: removing the polyclonal and clonally redundant isolates makes the zone separation slightly worse, not better. Trustworthiness is 0.499 (UMAP), 0.526 (t-SNE) and 0.510 (PCoA), each marginally higher than on the full set, while the PCoA distance correlation falls from 0.322 to 0.287. The conclusion of §6.3 is unchanged and the ordinations now describe the same sample as every other result in the document.

[Interpreted] What the pattern of these checks says about the verification, stated once. This is the fourth consecutive round in which a correction contained its own defect. The pattern is consistent across all four: each layer was checked for internal consistency against this project's own outputs, and what escaped was external correctness — whether the marker list matched an authoritative source, whether the annotation direction matched the reference genome, whether a co-occurrence claim had co-occurrence data behind it. The verification architecture was built to catch stale numbers, and it did catch them. It was not built to ask whether the right question was being asked of the data. The four checks added in the previous revision — the panel rebuild by codon reconstruction, the per-sample comparison against MalariaGEN call by call, the within-isolate haplotype counts in place of marginals, and the check against the WHO compendium — are all external by construction, which is the point of them. The three added in this revision are of a third kind again, and worth distinguishing: they vary the analyst's own choices rather than compare against anything outside. That is what caught the isolation-by-distance result, and nothing else could have. No external reference would have flagged it, because the number was correctly computed at the thresholds the pipeline specifies; it was the choice of thresholds, not the arithmetic, that was carrying the result. A number can be right, reproducible and externally consistent, and the conclusion drawn from it still not survive the decision that produced it.

8.2 Checks proposed but not performed

[Proposed] Three items listed here in the previous draft have since been run and have moved to Table S36 — the Fws sensitivity analysis, the MAF sensitivity analysis (now rows 19, jointly) and the stage 3 re-run on the analysis set (row 21). None of the following has been run. They are listed in order of expected value. The first two are the most important of what remains, and the second matters more than it did: a spatial result that holds at one threshold combination and almost nowhere else is exactly what an unresolved temporal confound can produce.

Read-level depth check on the two downgraded chromosome-11 SNPs — not done, and now the highest-value outstanding item on the outlier scan. Pf3D7_11_v3:1054078 (SNRPF) carries the only nonzero ExcessHet value in the panel (69.59, roughly 400× the genome-wide maximum), and it and Pf3D7_11_v3:1054447, 369 bp away, are the two lowest-InbreedingCoeff sites of the 3,916. Collapsed paralogy or copy-number variation would explain both, and MQ and FS are clean so mismapping would not; but that is an inference from summary annotations, not a demonstration. A per-sample depth ratio against each isolate's genome-median depth at these two positions would settle it. Until it is run the mechanism stays [Hypothesized] and both SNPs stay at lower confidence (§7.2). 1054447 is the fifth-ranked SNP in the whole scan, so this is not a cosmetic item.

Temporal control, and the marker-panel temporal checks re-run. Restrict to a single collection year or a narrow window, where sample sizes permit, and recompute FST. 2018 contributes 773 isolates across three zones and is the only year that could support this. Separately, the two temporal controls on the pfaat1 cline — survival of a 2018-only restriction, and absence of a within-Guinea-savanna trend with year — were computed on the superseded annotation-string panel and have not been recomputed on the reconstruction panel. S258L's frequencies are nearly identical between the two panels, so they are expected to carry over, but until they are re-run they are not results (§7.5 note). The same applies to any temporal statement about pfdhps G437, which has no temporal analysis at all because the locus was absent until now.

Region-level ADMIXTURE and TreeMix. Running these with populations defined by administrative region rather than zone would show whether the choice of grouping affects the conclusions, even though it cannot resolve the underlying collinearity.

Permutation test for the outlier set. Compare the functional composition of the 13 outlier genes against random gene sets matched for length and SNP density, to test whether the apparent enrichment for exported and host-interaction proteins exceeds chance. The variant-quality check (Table S36 row 20) establishes that those genes' SNPs are well-called, not that the enrichment is real; the two questions are separate. The re-run added exactly two genes, both exported proteins — REX2 (PF3D7_0936000, ring-exported protein 2) and PF3D7_0221200, a Plasmodium exported protein of unknown function (hyp15) — taking the list from 11 to 13, which strengthens the pattern but does not test it; the two intergenic outliers are outside any such test by construction.

Recompute the zone-pair study overlap and the polyclonality figures on the 1,900-isolate analysis set. They are currently computed on the 4,905 Pf8-QC-passing isolates (§7.2 note). The nesting of study within zone is a property of the sampling frame and will not reverse, but the quoted percentages would then match the denominators used everywhere else. The stage 3 re-run (Table S36 row 21) shows this kind of re-slicing is now cheap, which weakens the excuse for leaving it undone.

Stratify the marker clines by sequencing study. The pfaat1 S258L and pfdhps G437 contrasts both run between zone pairs that share no study (§7.2). Wherever a zone pair does share one, the within-study marker contrast should be reported separately from the between-study contrast, exactly as proposed for polyclonality below. No version of this has been run on the rebuilt panel.

Effective population size and divergence-time estimation, to distinguish ongoing gene flow from recent common ancestry (§7.4).

Extend the codon-reconstruction panel beyond the nine genes currently covered.
