The reconstruction method is general and the reference-state failure mode it fixed is not specific to pfdhps 437; any other canonical codon whose resistance allele matches 3D7 would have been invisible to the old panel and has not been looked for outside these nine genes.

Re-join the zone assignments against an authoritative six-zone Ghana layer. Because isolates join on administrative-level-1 centroids, the join can be repeated against a published classification. If zone assignments are stable, the provenance gap in §9.3 becomes a sensitivity result instead of a threat to reproducibility.

Manual review of the Brong Ahafo and Volta zone assignments against the polygon layer, as step 2 recommends.

Stratify the polyclonality and Fws comparisons by sequencing study wherever a zone pair shares one, and report the within-study contrasts separately from the between-study ones (§7.2, §9.1 limitation 2).

Written enquiry to the likely custodian of the zone polygon layer. This is not an analytical check and cannot be run as one, which is why it is still open: the provenance of data/ghanaAEZ.geojson can only be settled by asking. The addressee is the Centre for Remote Sensing and Geographic Information Services, University of Ghana (cersgis@ug.edu.gh), the most likely custodian of the pre-2005 national coverage that the file's ArcInfo fingerprint points to. Until a reply arrives the layer is cited as accessed, with its md5, and the custodianship remains an inference (§9.3).



Figure S21. ADMIXTURE cross-validation error from K = 2 to K = 8 on the corrected 3,916-SNP matrix (3,385 LD-pruned sites); the sweep that produced the plot was run over K = 2–8, so K = 1 is not a plotted point and is quoted from the log instead. The curve is near-flat: K = 2 is the minimum at 0.83925 against 0.84384 at K = 1 (from logs/admixture_K1.out, on the same 3,385-SNP panel), an improvement of 0.54 per cent, and the plotted K = 2–8 range is 0.0056 (0.67 per cent). The curve has essentially the same shape on a 16.6 per cent larger SNP panel, which is part of the robustness result reported in §8.

9. Limitations and potential sources of bias

9.1 Ranked by threat to the conclusions

Table S37. Limitations ranked by how much they constrain the conclusions. Bold entries are new; items 8 and 9 were new to the previous revision and concern the marker panel. Item 15 is new to this revision; items 3, 5, 6, 7, 10, 11 and 13 are updated by the four checks reported in §8, and item 13 is now resolved.

[Interpreted] What the threshold-sensitivity analysis does and does not change in this list. It strengthens the headline and removes a finding, and the two should be stated together. Near-panmixia no longer rests on a single pass through a single dataset: maximum pairwise FST stays within 0.00204–0.00255 across six combinations of the two thresholds that define the analysis set, over sample sizes from 1,900 to 4,403 and SNP counts from 3,275 to 10,876 (Table S35). Limitations 5, 6 and 7 — panel size, the transitional zone's 25 isolates and the Fws exclusion — therefore constrain the precision of that conclusion without threatening it, which is a stronger position than the previous draft could claim. Isolation by distance is in the opposite position. Significant in 2 of 6 threshold scenarios and 4 of 7 leave-one-region-out replicates, it is not a result this analysis can claim, and limitations 3 and 11 are its explanation rather than caveats on it: 21 pairs derived from 7 administrative centroids, with 70 per cent of the isolates at one of them, cannot support a distance effect that survives perturbation of the dataset that produced it. The manuscript's only positive spatial finding has been lost. No reanalysis of these data will recover it; recovering it would need isolates with recorded collection coordinates (§9.2), and that is a design change rather than an analytical one.

9.2 On the design limitations specifically

[Interpreted] Limitations 1, 2 and 3 are not analytical shortcomings that better methods would overcome. They are properties of the Pf8 metadata and of how Pf8 was assembled. No reanalysis of these data can produce a zone effect separable from a region effect, because for three of the four zones there is only one region in each; and no reanalysis can separate the Coastal–Guinea savanna contrast from a sequencing-study contrast, because no study contributed isolates to both. This should be stated plainly in any write-up rather than managed with hedging language, and it bears directly on how the thesis's objectives can be framed. Limitation 3 has now produced a concrete casualty rather than a theoretical one: the isolation-by-distance result it constrains did not survive threshold variation (§9.1, Table S35).

[Interpreted] Limitation 8 is of a different kind and deserves separating from the rest. It was not a property of the data but of the code, it was invisible to every check that looked only at this project's own outputs, and it was corrected only because an external reader asked whether the marker list was the right list. Unlike limitations 1 to 3 it is fixable, and it has been fixed; unlike them it was also, while it lasted, producing confidently stated numbers that were wrong. The appropriate response in a write-up is not a hedge but a statement of what the panel now does and how it was checked against something outside the pipeline (§8.1, rows 14 to 18).

[Proposed] The study confounder deserves particular emphasis because it was absent from the previous draft while the other three were disclosed, and because a reviewer will find it in a single crosstab. It should be reported with the study-by-zone table as a supplement, together with the within-zone, between-study tests and an explicit statement that both are computed within the 4,905 Pf8-QC-passing isolates — the crosstab on all 4,905, and the between-study tests on the 4,851 of them that fall in zones with at least two studies of n ≥ 20, which drops the transitional zone (one study, n = 50) and deciduous forest's 4-isolate study entirely. Readers can then see where batch heterogeneity is significant (deciduous forest Fws p = 0.002 across four of its five studies) and where it is not (coastal savanna p = 0.38; Guinea savanna Fws p = 0.10, though its call-rate heterogeneity is extreme at p = 2.8 × 10⁻¹⁹⁷).

[Proposed] If zone-level inference is essential to the thesis, the route is additional sampling with recorded collection coordinates — ideally within the Sudan savanna, and within regions that straddle a zone boundary, where zone and region could be decoupled — and, critically, with a single sequencing protocol applied across zones so that zone and batch are decoupled as well. That is also the only route to a defensible isolation-by-distance result, since the one obtained here rests on seven administrative centroids and does not survive varying the thresholds that define the dataset. Whether any of it is feasible within the project is a question for the author.

[Calculated] The pooled minor-allele-frequency filter imposes an ascertainment floor set by the largest zone. Allele counts are pooled across all 1,900 isolates before the 5% cut is applied, and Guinea savanna contributes 1,334 of them — 70.2% of the sample — against 337 deciduous forest, 204 coastal savanna and 25 transitional. With the variant-missingness ceiling of 10%, a retained site carries at least 1,710 called haploid alleles. A variant private to the transitional zone therefore cannot exceed 25/1,710 = 1.46% and is excluded even if every one of those 25 isolates carries it; a variant at 40% frequency in coastal savanna reaches only 82/1,710 = 4.80% and is excluded as well. The 3,916-SNP panel is accordingly a panel of variants common enough to clear a threshold dominated by one zone, and every differentiation statistic computed on it — the pairwise FST, the three ordinations, the ADMIXTURE fit, the population graph and the Mantel test — inherits that restriction. This does not bias the FST estimates themselves, which are computed on the sites that were retained and are unaffected by the existence of sites that were not. It bounds their scope. “Negligible differentiation” is a statement about commonly shared variation; it is not evidence against zone-restricted or locally common variants, which this design cannot see at all. The maf001 and maf002 scenarios probe the floor only partly: they raise the panel to 10,876 and 6,852 SNPs and leave maximum FST at 0.00204 and 0.00217, but both still pool allele counts across zones, so they lower the floor rather than remove it. Removing it would require either frequency filtering within zones or a design in which the zones contribute comparable numbers of isolates.

9.3 Reproducibility status

[Observed] What is reproducible. Most of the analysis's inputs are retrievable from public data sources, though not from a single one; that availability does not by itself establish that the pipeline as archived regenerates the reported results. All inputs except the zone polygon layer, the PlasmoDB 3D7 annotation and the Pf8 sample manifest are retrievable from https://pf8-release.cog.sanger.ac.uk — specifically the Zarr callset, the SNP-only VCFs, the reference FASTA and Pf8_drug_resistance_marker_genotypes.tsv. The annotation comes from PlasmoDB (config.PLASMODB_GFF = PlasmoDB-71_Pfalciparum3D7.gff), and it is the file the rebuilt marker panel depends on, since step12_codon_panel.py builds its CDS map from it; the manifest comes from malariagen.net (config.SAMPLE_MANIFEST = data/Pf8_samples.txt). Under Route A the core-regions BED (pf_core_regions.bed) must also be supplied by the user, as data/README.md states. Random seeds are fixed (config.RANDOM_SEED = 42; ADMIXTURE -s 42; TreeMix -seed 42; BayPass -seed 42), and the Mantel permutation test is now seeded as well (seed 42, 9,999 permutations), so both of its p-values are reproducible — previously neither was. The sensitivity analysis inherits those seeds and rebuilds each scenario's matrix from the same stored genotype array, so all six scenarios in Table S35 are reproducible from one script. Stage 3a is resumable and verifies its own output, and step03_umap.py now takes --samples and --suffix so an ordination can be recomputed on any subset without disturbing the published outputs. The marker panel is deterministic by construction: step12_codon_panel.py reads the CDS, the reference genome and the genotypes and involves no sampling. The stage sequence is driven by the following command, though the exact commands and script revisions used for the reported run are not archived:

bash scripts/run_all.sh --from 4 --parallel

[Observed] What has been fixed since the previous draft. Four defects that made the pipeline unreproducible or silently wrong have been corrected. (i) BayPass was non-deterministic across threads despite its fixed seed: the 8-thread manuscript run and an 8-thread re-run of the same command gave different thresholds and different outlier counts. It now runs single-threaded and is bitwise stable; the earlier multi-threaded outputs are archived rather than overwritten, and the three surviving threshold values are labelled in the note to Table S34. (ii) The Mantel test's silent flooring of negative FST values to zero is now disclosed, the affected pairs are named in the output JSON, and both censored and uncensored statistics are reported — which is how it became visible that the uncensored statistic clears p < 0.05 in only one of the six threshold scenarios (Table S35). (iii) The "skip if output exists" guards in stages 5b, 7b and 8b did not check whether the input had changed, so when the SNP panel grew from 3,359 to 3,916 the ADMIXTURE and TreeMix stages reused the previous day's results and reported success. Only stage 8 caught it, because it asserts that its position table and the BayPass output have matching row counts. All three guards now require the output to be newer than its input. (iv) The marker panel no longer identifies codons by annotation string. step12_codon_panel.py reconstructs each codon from the CDS and reference genome and emits the full residue spectrum including the reference residue, with a per-codon called count; it is validated call by call against MalariaGEN's published genotypes at the 25 of its 57 codons MalariaGEN publishes per sample (47,012 of 47,012 agree), leaving 32 codons, every pfk13 codon among them, with no independent per-sample check and its pfk13 list is taken from the WHO compendium v1.0 rather than written by hand. Defects (iii) and (iv) are the most dangerous kind: both produced no error and well-formed output, and in both cases wrong numbers would have been reported as though they were right.

Two incidents during this revision's runs, recorded because both bear on data integrity. (i) A results file was destroyed and restored. results/baypass/xtx_all_snps.csv was found at 0 bytes, truncated at 08:29:53 — hours after the 01:42 run that produced it, so almost certainly a stray shell redirect where a read was intended during a verification pass. It was the only zero-byte file under results/ or interim/. It was regenerated from the intact BayPass outputs, and the regeneration is consistent with the surviving outputs rather than proven row-for-row: outlier_snps.bed, xtx_calibration.json and xtx_threshold.txt are byte-identical by md5 before and after, and the restored file carries the same 3,916 rows and the same 19 outliers. The consequence is in fact positive — the per-SNP q-values are on disk again, so the pfaat1 q-value of 0.0049 and the tie between the two AAT1 SNPs are now reproducible from a primary output rather than from a narrative (§7.5 note). But the episode is also the clearest argument this project has produced for version control, which is gap (ii) in the paragraph below: nothing in the setup would have detected the truncation except someone opening the file. (ii) A log misreported its own output path. The --suffix patch to step03_umap.py suffixed the file writes but not the print statements, so the stage 3 re-run reported writing to the published dimred_comparison.csv when it had correctly written dimred_comparison_qcset.csv. No data were at risk — verified from the published file's mtime and contents — but a log that misstates where it wrote is exactly what causes a later misattribution, and this is one more instance of the pattern in §8.1: the defect was inside a correction, not inside the original code. Fixed.

[Observed] What still is not reproducible. Three gaps remain. (i) The provenance of data/ghanaAEZ.geojson is identified but not resolved, and cannot be resolved analytically. The file has been traced with certainty — it is byte-for-byte identical, md5 96b1a7f4da62d6f0153826f41af96152, to an ArcGIS Online item whose licenseInfo and accessInformation are both null and whose owner is an anonymous personal account created two days before the upload. It is an unattributed re-host, not an origin, and it is not citable as a published dataset; its only attributes are id, Name, AREA and PERIMETER, with no author, source or date. The uppercase AREA/PERIMETER pair is an ArcInfo-coverage fingerprint and points to a pre-2005 national coverage. The zone scheme it encodes is a hybrid of the MoFA/FAO savanna classification and a Hall & Swaine-lineage forest split, and matches no published Ghanaian classification. The layer should be cited as accessed rather than as authoritative, with the checksum given for identity, and the zone names reproduced verbatim — including the "Decidous forest" misspelling — because the spatial join keys on them, and because the rebuilt marker outputs carry that spelling in their column headers. Attributing the layer to any named institution would be a fabricated citation. A third party therefore still cannot reproduce the spatial join from public, licensed sources alone. The one unexhausted lead — the Centre for Remote Sensing and Geographic Information Services, University of Ghana, the most likely custodian of the pre-2005 national coverage the file's fingerprint points to — has not been contacted: closing this gap requires a written enquiry to that Centre (cersgis@ug.edu.gh), which is why it is the one of this revision's four outstanding checks that remains open (§8, §8.2). That custodianship is an inference and not an established link. (ii) The project is not under version control, so the exact script state at run time is not recoverable other than from the files currently on disk; the unmodified originals are in pf8_ghana_pipeline.zip. The accidental truncation recorded in the note above is a direct consequence of this gap. (iii) UMAP with a fixed random_state is single-threaded and deterministic, but t-SNE has not been verified to be bitwise reproducible across thread counts.

[Unknown] Whether ADMIXTURE returns identical results under a different thread count has not been tested. BayPass has now been tested and does not, which is a reason to expect the same of ADMIXTURE and to test it rather than assume it.



10. Conclusion and current state of the research

10.1 What has been established

This section has now been rewritten twice and revised a third time. The first rewrite followed an internal adversarial audit; this one follows an external critique of 10 October 2026 whose five points were all upheld, four of them identifying defects in the analysis code rather than in the prose. The defect that matters here is that the first corrected resistance panel was itself wrong: it found markers by matching SnpEff ANN_HGVS_p protein-change strings against a hand-written list, which cannot see a resistance allele that is the reference residue and cannot see a residue reached by two changes in one codon. A new script, scripts/step12_codon_panel.py, reconstructs each codon from the CDS and the reference genome and replaces the panel entirely. The following statements are therefore withdrawn from the previous version and should not be quoted from it: the pfdhfr triple mutant as "0.786–0.908"; the claim that pfk13 was tested at 14 validated codons "in all 1,900 isolates"; the classification of P441L and C469F as validated; the "marker-naming discrepancy" at pfcrt codon 75; the validation claim "21 of 23 markers agree within 0.03"; the description of pfaat1 S258L as "the functionally validated chloroquine-resistance variant"; and the statement that nothing in the functional work tested amodiaquine. The population-genetic results below are unchanged and were read from the corrected run of 10 October 2026 (3,916 SNPs x 1,900 isolates, mean missingness 0.0295); every resistance number below was read from step12's own outputs. A third round followed on the same date and is recorded in Appendix F: the four robustness checks that this section's own next-steps list carried as outstanding were run, and one of them overturned a conclusion instead of confirming it. Isolation by distance is withdrawn as a supported result and is reported below as not robust. Near-panmixia is strengthened by the same six scenarios. Nothing in the resistance panel is touched by these checks, and the withdrawals listed above stand exactly as they are.

[Calculated] Differentiation between agro-ecological zones is very low. The largest pairwise Hudson FST is 0.00232 between coastal savanna and Guinea savanna (block-jackknife SE 0.00053, 95% CI 0.00128–0.00336). Five of the six zone pairs have confidence intervals excluding zero; the exception is Guinea savanna against the transitional zone (0.00047, CI −0.00083 to 0.00178), where n = 25 on one side. This is a well-supported result, and it is now demonstrably the most robust one in the manuscript: the corrected run analyses 16.6% more SNPs than the original and the maximum estimate moved only from 0.0024 to 0.00232, and the paragraph below shows it moving by less than 0.0005 across six different settings of the two thresholds that define the dataset.

[Calculated] Near-panmixia survives both thresholds that define the dataset, and that is the strongest evidence the analysis holds for anything. scripts/step13_sensitivity.py loads the genotype matrix once, rebuilds the analysis matrix under six threshold scenarios and recomputes pairwise FST and the region-level Mantel test for each. The two thresholds varied are the ones that do the most work in the pipeline: the Fws monoclonality cut, which discards 57 per cent of isolates and does so unevenly by zone, and the MAF floor, which discards 99.8 per cent of sites. Across all six scenarios the maximum pairwise FST ranges only 0.00204 to 0.00255 — a total spread of 0.0005 — over sample sizes from 1,900 to 4,403 isolates and SNP counts from 3,275 to 10,876, and the maximum pair is coastal savanna against Guinea savanna in every one of them. Two secondary readings are worth recording. Relaxing Fws slightly raises FST (0.00232 at Fws >= 0.95, 0.00255 at Fws >= 0.80), so the polyclonal isolates the filter removes were not masking differentiation — the direction a reviewer would most likely suspect is the opposite of the one observed. And tripling the SNP count by lowering the MAF floor to 0.01 lowers it (0.00204), as expected if rarer variants are less differentiated between zones. A conclusion that holds at 1,900 isolates and 3,916 SNPs and also at 4,403 isolates and 10,876 SNPs is being tested rather than assumed.

Table S38. Threshold sensitivity of the two headline spatial results, from results/sensitivity_summary.csv (scripts/step13_sensitivity.py, 10 October 2026). The baseline row reproduces the published configuration exactly. Mantel r and p are the censored values, with negative FST pairs floored to zero, over 21 administrative-region pairs; the uncensored values are in the same file and change no conclusion (baseline p = 0.0413, and no relaxed scenario reaches 0.05 uncensored either). FST varies by less than 0.0005 across the six scenarios while the Mantel test crosses the 0.05 line four times, which is the whole of the difference between the two results' standing. This table is new in this revision; the research-questions table that carried the number 10.1 in previous versions is renumbered Table S39, and the one internal cross-reference to it, in Appendix F, is renumbered with it.

[Calculated] Isolation by distance is no longer a supported result. This is the one population-genetic conclusion that the threshold sensitivity overturned, and the loss is a real one: it was the manuscript's only positive spatial finding. The numbers themselves are unchanged. At the published thresholds the region-level Mantel correlation is r = 0.4269 (p = 0.0252) with negative FST pairs floored to zero, and r = 0.4138 (p = 0.0413) without flooring; three of the 21 region pairs were affected, so on the final matrix the censoring no longer determines significance, although both versions are reported and the disclosure stands. What has changed is what those numbers are worth. The test is significant in two of the six threshold scenarios — the published one and MAF >= 0.02 at p = 0.0467 — and fails at every relaxation of the Fws cut (p = 0.0874, 0.1687 and 0.1792) and at MAF >= 0.01 (p = 0.0617). The two thresholds fail it in different ways and the difference is informative. Relaxing Fws changes the correlation itself, which falls from 0.4269 to 0.2447 as the isolate count rises from 1,900 to 4,403; relaxing MAF leaves the correlation essentially untouched (0.4325 at MAF >= 0.01, 0.4341 at MAF >= 0.02) and moves only the permutation p-value across the 0.05 line. So the result is sensitive to which isolates are analysed in a way that cannot be written off as lost power, and it is marginal at the published MAF floor in a way that a different floor is enough to disturb. Taken with the leave-one-region-out jackknife, which already found it significant in only four of seven replicates — significance lost on dropping Upper East (n = 1,334; r = +0.260, p = 0.218), Greater Accra (n = 204; r = +0.250, p = 0.232) or Eastern (n = 34; r = +0.288, p = 0.136) — the honest grade is not robust: it holds at the published thresholds on the complete set of seven regions, and almost nowhere else. At zone level it was never demonstrable at all: r = 0.4709 with p = 0.1218 over six pairs, none of the four leave-one-out replicates significant, and only 24 distinct permutations available, which is a resolution limit rather than a negative finding. The result is not deleted and should not be. A correlation of 0.43 over 21 region pairs that survives neither a change of monoclonality cut nor a change of MAF floor is a statement about this dataset's resolution, not evidence of a distance gradient in Ghanaian Plasmodium falciparum, and it is reported here with its sensitivity so that a successor with more populations or finer spatial units can test it properly. Not robust.

One piece of the previous version's reasoning, kept for the record and no longer load-bearing. The previous version departed deliberately from the prescribed wording in the corrections document, which reads: "Dropping Upper East still removes significance, so the result remains substantially a single north–south contrast rather than a smooth distance gradient, and should be described that way." The departure was taken because dropping Eastern — neither northern nor heavily sampled, at n = 34 — also removes significance, which a single north–south contrast does not predict, and because the other two regions whose removal costs significance are the two largest of the seven (Upper East at 1,334 and Greater Accra at 204, against a median region of 125), so the fragility could not be laid at the door of small sample sizes. That observation stands and is unaffected by the sensitivity analysis. But it was an argument about how to describe a supported result, and the threshold sensitivity has removed the thing being described, so the two descriptions no longer need adjudicating: on the present evidence neither "a single north–south contrast" nor "fragile to the composition of a 21-pair test" is a finding. The flag is kept rather than deleted because the corrections document still prescribes its wording, and a successor should be able to see why the manuscript follows neither it nor the previous version's alternative.

[Calculated] Ancestry structure aligned to geography is detectable but negligible in magnitude. The original text asserted that no ancestry structure corresponds to geography. That is false as stated and was never tested. ADMIXTURE's preferred K = 2 improves on K = 1 by 0.54 per cent of cross-validation error (0.83925 against 0.84384, an absolute gain of 0.00459). The minor component's mean differs between zones — 0.085 coastal savanna, 0.092 deciduous forest, 0.053 Guinea savanna, 0.042 transitional zone — and a Kruskal–Wallis test over the four zones gives H = 105.4, p = 1.05 x 10-22. Zone nonetheless explains only 1.9 per cent of the variance in that component (η2 = 0.019, computed from the final Q matrix). The means are not monotone with latitude: deciduous forest exceeds coastal savanna, and the transitional zone, which is the second-most-northern zone, sits lowest of the four. The substantive conclusion survives in full; the wording does not. The correct statement is that zone-associated ancestry structure is statistically present, non-monotone in latitude, and biologically negligible.

[Calculated] A strictly bifurcating population tree is inadequate, and a two-edge graph is the model these data select. With jackknife standard errors enabled, variance explained rises from 0.897 at m = 0 to 0.941 at m = 1 and 0.997 at m = 2. Every edge in the m = 2 graph is significant (p = 7.5 x 10-4 and 3.3 x 10-16); the third edge added at m = 3 is not (weight 0.065, SE 0.147, p = 0.328), and m = 3 is in any case flagged non-identifiable with four populations. The manuscript previously recorded both m = 2 and m = 3 and left the choice open. m = 2 is now reported as the selected descriptive model — on the third edge’s own standard error and on parameter counting — rather than as a count of historical migration events that these data identify. The variance-explained criterion applied across the full sweep still prefers m = 3, so the two criteria do not agree, and choosing between them is a modelling decision taken here on identifiability grounds. Specific gene-flow corridors remain unidentifiable with four populations.

[Calculated] The ordinations now describe the analysis set, and removing the polyclonal and clonally redundant isolates does not sharpen the structure. Until this revision the ordination figures described the 4,905 isolates that pass sample-level QC while every other result described the 1,900 that survive the Fws and clonality cuts — a consistency defect rather than an open question, and it is now fixed. step03_umap.py takes --samples and --suffix and re-slices the distance submatrix rather than reusing the cached full-set one, with outputs carrying a _qcset suffix so the published figures are untouched. On the 1,900-isolate analysis set the silhouette by zone on raw distances is −0.020, against −0.016 on 4,905: slightly more negative, not less. Trustworthiness rises marginally for all three methods (UMAP 0.499 against 0.486, t-SNE 0.526 against 0.507, PCoA 0.510 against 0.495) and the PCoA distance correlation falls (0.287 against 0.322). No ordination recovers zone structure on either sample set, so the conclusion is unchanged; what has changed is that the figures can now be reported on the same isolates as everything else, and that the obvious objection — that mixed and clonally duplicated infections were blurring real structure — is answered in the negative.



Figure S22. UMAP, t-SNE and PCoA of the 1,900 × 1,900 analysis-set distance submatrix, coloured by agro-ecological zone: the same three ordinations as Figure S10, but on the 1,900 isolates analysed everywhere else rather than the 4,905 that pass sample-level QC. Silhouette by zone on the raw distances is −0.020, against −0.016 on the larger set. PCoA axes carry their variance proportions; UMAP and t-SNE axes are in arbitrary units and inter-cluster distances in those two panels carry no quantitative meaning. Generated by scripts/step03_umap.py with --samples and --suffix _qcset; the published Figure S10 and its underlying files are deliberately unchanged.

[Calculated] Nineteen SNPs exceed a calibrated differentiation threshold, of which 13 also survive false-discovery correction. The POD 0.999 quantile falls at XtX = 10.840. Nineteen SNPs clear it, 3.9 are expected by construction, and the expected-null-exceedance ratio at the threshold is 20.6 per cent; 13 SNPs also pass Benjamini–Hochberg q <= 0.10. Seventeen of the 19 intersect 13 annotated genes, headed by PTP7 (PF3D7_0301700, max XtX 26.44) with AAT1 (PF3D7_0629500, 24.20) second. The remaining two are intergenic, and one of them — Pf3D7_11_v3:1054447 at XtX 20.79, just outside SNRPF — is the fifth-highest XtX in the genome, so the gene list is not a complete account of the outlier set and should not be presented as one. AAT1 is the only entry carrying a direct and recent resistance-literature connection, and its missense SNP survives Benjamini–Hochberg correction at q = 0.0049. That q does not, however, single the site out: the linked synonymous site 883 bp away carries the identical q = 0.0049, both sitting at the Monte Carlo resolution floor of the POD null, so the q-value ranks the pair without discriminating between them. Report the 13 as the finding and the 19 as a ranking. Provisional as a candidate list; the FDR-passing subset is better than provisional. The 19 have now been checked against the callset's own quality annotations, SNP by SNP, against the other 3,897 scanned SNPs (results/outlier_quality_check.csv), and the outcome is favourable but not uniform. Every significant difference runs in the outliers' favour: higher VQSLOD (p = 0.008), QD (p = 0.016) and QUAL (p = 0.007). None has MQ < 50, none shows strand bias or read-position bias, and all 19 are RegionType Core. The culprit field reads MQRankSum for all 19, but also for 99.6 per cent of the rest of the panel, so it carries no information here. The exported-protein artefact concern is not supported: the eight outliers in PTP7, PIESP2, GEXP22, REX2, PF3D7_0831400 and PF3D7_0221200 are the cleanest subset in the panel — FS = 0.00 for all eight, MQ median 60.00, VQSLOD significantly higher than the rest. Those families are repetitive, but these particular sites lie in uniquely mappable sequence. Two SNPs are nonetheless downgraded, for an entirely different reason, and the note below gives it.

Two chromosome-11 outliers are downgraded to lower confidence. Pf3D7_11_v3:1054078 (SNRPF, XtX 13.98, q = 0.031) and Pf3D7_11_v3:1054447 (intergenic, XtX 20.79, q = 0.005) lie 369 bp apart and are the two lowest-InbreedingCoeff sites in the whole panel (0.147 and 0.193, at the 0.08th and 0.13th percentiles of the 3,916). 1054078 carries ExcessHet = 69.59, the only nonzero value among all 3,916 SNPs and roughly 400 times the genome-wide maximum. Mapping quality and strand bias are clean at both sites (MQ 59.99 and 59.95, FS 0.00 and 0.60), so whatever this is, it is not mismapping. It matters for two reasons: 1054447 is the fifth-ranked SNP in the entire scan, and both sites sit inside the 13 that pass Benjamini–Hochberg q <= 0.10 — so that subset should now be read as 11 SNPs of ordinary confidence plus these two, and any figure quoting "13" should carry the qualification with it.

[Hypothesized] What the chromosome-11 pair probably is. An excess of heterozygous calls of that magnitude in a near-haploid organism points to collapsed paralogy or copy-number variation — two or more diverged copies piling onto one reference locus, which produces apparent heterozygosity, a depressed InbreedingCoeff and inflated between-population variance without any degradation of mapping quality or strand balance. That is a hypothesis and not a result: nothing in the quality table distinguishes it from the alternatives, and no read-level evidence has been examined. A per-sample depth ratio against the genome median at the two positions would settle it in one pass, and it has not been run (Section 10.4, item 1). Until it is, both sites carry the downgrade.

[Calculated] Objective 3 is answered, not null — and this is the second attempt at answering it. The original abstract and Section 6.8 reported that no validated antimalarial resistance locus appeared among the outliers and interpreted that absence biologically. That absence was an ascertainment artefact of the genome-wide callset, which is built under FILTER_PASS & is_snp & numalt == 1; in Pf8 most canonical resistance codons are multiallelic or carry a spanning-deletion ALT, and the mask dropped 28 of the 44 annotation records the first panel assembled before any analysis ran. It did not drop all of them, and the manuscript should not say so: 16 records survived the mask, including six pfk13 alleles and pfmdr1 Ser1034Cys, Asn1042Asp and Asp1246Tyr. That diagnosis of the scan's silence stands unchanged. What did not stand was the replacement panel built to answer the objective, and the figures below come from its replacement in turn.

[Calculated] The panel as it now stands. step12_codon_panel.py maps each requested codon to its three genomic positions through the CDS, collects every variant record at those positions regardless of numalt or is_snp, applies each isolate's alleles to the reference codon read from the genome, and translates. It reports the full residue spectrum at every codon, the reference residue included, so a resistance allele cannot hide in the reference and a residue requiring two changes in one codon cannot be missed; a mixed call makes a codon uncalled rather than being assigned a residue. The panel covers 57 codons across all nine panel genes — 171 genomic positions, 125 variant records, every codon carrying at least one record — in the same 1,900 isolates. Of the 57 codons, 24 are polymorphic and 33 are fixed at the reference residue. Two statements from the previous version are retired by this: that pfcoronin "contributed no canonical codon" (it contributes three, G50, R100 and E107, all fixed at the reference residue), and the "44 canonical codon-allele records" count, which was a count of annotation records and not of codons. The scan-side ascertainment cascade is unaffected and is stated once here: of the panel's 171 genomic positions only four ever reached the MAF-filtered scan matrix — pfdhfr Cys59Arg (XtX 9.11, q = 0.43), pfdhfr Asn51Ile (XtX 8.15, q = 0.67), pfaat1 Ser258Leu (XtX 24.20, q = 0.0049) and, fourth, Pf3D7_07_v3:403623, the second of pfcrt codon 75's two variant positions (BayPass MRK 1406, M_XtX 4.18, XtXst 6.21 against the threshold 10.840; M_P 0.914, so MAF 0.086 cleared the 0.05 cut). The previous count of three was carried over from step10's annotation-matched panel, which held 403621 but never 403623 at all — a residual instance of the very two-SNP-codon failure mode the rebuild was written to fix. Three more passed the ingest mask and were then cut by MAF >= 0.05: pfcrt Ile356Thr at 0.016, pfmdr1 Asp1246Tyr at 0.011 and pfcrt Asn326Ser at 0.0005 — the last of which the previous version reported as 0.008, which was also wrong. The distinction worth drawing explicitly therefore survives, with its scope corrected: the scan's silence at pfdhfr 51 and 59 and at pfcrt 75 is a tested negative, because those were the codons in the matrix and all three came back firmly null, whereas its silence on pfmdr1, pfdhps and pfk13 is an ascertainment artefact, because it contributes no scanned codon position and never tested them at all. pfcrt does not belong on the artefact side: codon 75 was tested. Nor can "the antifolate codons" stand as a blanket term here — only two of the five pfdhfr codons reached the matrix, none of the six pfdhps codons did, and pfdhps is itself an antifolate target.

Why the previous panel missed markers, stated plainly. step10 identified markers by matching SnpEff protein-change strings. Two failure modes followed, and both occurred. (i) A resistance allele that is itself the reference residue can never appear as an ALT change. At pfdhps 437 the only annotation is Gly437Ala — the revertant — because, in the official Pf8 methodology's own words, "3D7 sequence translates as a G at 437, this is thought to be the mutant allele, i.e. 3D7 does not have the wild type allele." Searching for "Ala437Gly" matched nothing, so A437G was absent from the panel entirely, at a true frequency of 0.897. (ii) A residue reachable only by two changes in one codon is never annotated. pfcrt codon 75 carries two variants, 403621 (A>G, annotated Asn75Asp) and 403623 (T>A, annotated Asn75Lys); Asn→Glu requires both, so no annotation names N75E, the residue the CVIET haplotype actually carries. This, and not a "naming discrepancy", is the whole explanation of the apparent disagreement with MalariaGEN that the previous version reported.

[Calculated] Positive finding one: pfdhps Gly437 is at high frequency and is zone-structured — and was wholly absent from the previous panel. The resistance residue at this codon is the 3D7 reference residue, which is why string-matching never saw it. Reconstructed, G437 sits at 0.897 overall (n = 1,892): 0.964 in deciduous forest (95% CI 0.939–0.980), 0.941 in coastal savanna (0.900–0.966), 0.875 in Guinea savanna (0.856–0.892) and 0.800 in the transitional zone (0.609–0.911), with chi-square p = 1.2 x 10-6. The revertant residue A437 is correspondingly at 0.103. This is the single largest substantive addition to the resistance results, and the previous version of this section reported nothing at this locus at all.

[Calculated] Positive finding two: the pfdhfr triple mutant is at 0.771, not near fixation, and the previous figure was not a haplotype frequency. The previous version gave the triple mutant as "0.786–0.908", which is the range of its three marginal codon frequencies and is not a co-occurrence statement. Counted within isolates on the 1,876 called at all three codons, the IRN haplotype (N51I+C59R+S108N) is 0.771 (95% CI 0.751–0.789). As it must, it falls below the smallest of its components on the same complete-case set (51I 0.788; 59R 0.887; 108N 0.908). It is also not a single near-fixed haplotype: NRN sits at 0.116, the fully wild-type NCS at 0.091, ICN at 0.018 and NCN at 0.004. By zone, IRN is 0.867 in coastal savanna (n = 203), 0.788 in deciduous forest (n = 330), 0.750 in Guinea savanna (n = 1,318) and 0.880 in the transitional zone (n = 25). The quadruple IRNL is 0.000 (n = 1,874; CI 0.000–0.002), with I164L absent from every called isolate. The marginal codon frequencies are 0.786 for Asn51Ile (n = 1,891), 0.886 for Cys59Arg (n = 1,888) and 0.908 for Ser108Asn (n = 1,889); all three differ across the four zones (chi-square p = 2.4 x 10⁻⁴, 4.8 x 10⁻⁵ and 9.4 x 10⁻⁷) and all three are lowest in Guinea savanna. No directional contrast was tested and none should be claimed: those are four-zone heterogeneity tests, and for both Cys59Arg and Ser108Asn the highest frequency is the transitional zone at 1.000 on n = 25. pfdhps Lys540Glu (0.009) and Ala581Gly (0.014) remain rare, and the pfdhps 437+540 double GE is 0.010 (n = 1,888) against GK 0.887 and AK 0.103 — so the sulfadoxine-resistance picture is a high-frequency single substitution, not a high-frequency double.

[Calculated] Positive finding three: pfaat1 Ser258Leu forms a coastal-to-northern cline. The variant at Pf3D7_06_v3:1215233 is a missense change of moderate predicted impact. Its frequency runs 0.824 in coastal savanna, 0.735 in deciduous forest, 0.640 in the transitional zone and 0.544 in Guinea savanna, against 0.609 overall (n = 1,896; chi-square p = 1.9 x 10-18). It is independently the second-ranked outlier in the genome-wide scan at XtX 24.20, q = 0.0049. The original manuscript carried this locus as an anonymous amino acid transporter in a table while simultaneously reporting that no validated resistance locus had been found. One further reading from the new panel bears directly on how much weight the functional literature can carry here: the second pfaat1 codon in the panel, Phe313, is fixed at the reference residue in all 1,900 isolates — so F313S, the variant with which S258L shows epistasis in the published experiment, is absent from this sample entirely.

One signal, not two. The second AAT1 outlier, at Pf3D7_06_v3:1214350, is a synonymous variant 883 bp away (XtX 16.54, and the same q = 0.0049). The gene's "two outlier SNPs" are one functional signal plus a linked synonymous site, and must not be presented as two independent lines of evidence.

[Interpreted] The functional interpretation of S258L in the previous version was wrong, and the error was ours. It described S258L as "the functionally validated chloroquine-resistance variant" and asserted that the functional work did not test amodiaquine, then built a Ghanaian amodiaquine-pressure argument on that gap. Checked against the source, every step of that is unsafe. Amodiaquine was tested: "Parasite IC50 values for CQ, amodiaquine, lumefantrine, mefloquine and quinine were measured", and the shifts were "well below the threshold associated with clinical resistance". The authors explicitly reject the other-quinoline reading — the alternative hypothesis "is not supported" — and conclude that chloroquine was "the primary selective force". The effect itself is modest and conditional: a 1.5-fold rise in chloroquine IC50, in a single chloroquine-resistant Southeast Asian line (NHP4026), with epistasis between S258L and F313S and an explicit pfcrt-background caveat. The longitudinal 0% to 97% rise is Gambian, 1984–2014; the paper says nothing about Ghana. The defensible description is therefore that S258L is a variant with a demonstrated but modest, epistasis- and background-dependent effect that potentiates chloroquine resistance, and that it is a strong candidate for the Ghanaian cline rather than an explanation of it. On what maintains the cline, three possibilities are offered and none is tested by these data: historical chloroquine pressure persisting after the 2005 switch to artesunate–amodiaquine; the reported fitness cost of S258L, which would predict a cline rather than fixation or loss; and contemporary amodiaquine pressure, which must be labelled an untested possible driver, since the one experiment that measured amodiaquine found the shift sub-threshold and the one parasite line tested (NHP4026) does not carry a Ghanaian genetic background, and the effect it showed was conditional on epistasis with F313S — a partner residue absent from every one of the 1,900 isolates here. Nothing is claimed about NHP4026's own F313S genotype: no source available to us states it, and since NHP4026 is itself a Southeast Asian line and the hypothesis the authors reject concerns high F313S frequencies in Southeast Asia, the sources point the other way. Nothing in these data identifies the drug.

[Calculated] The chloroquine haplotype itself has largely reverted, and it is genuinely a haplotype. Reconstructed within isolates on the 1,873 called at all five codons, only two pfcrt 72–76 haplotypes occur: CVMNK at 0.929 and CVIET at 0.071 (95% CI 0.060–0.084), with nothing intermediate. By zone CVIET is 0.054 in coastal savanna (n = 204), 0.104 in deciduous forest (n = 335), 0.064 in Guinea savanna (n = 1,310) and 0.125 in the transitional zone (n = 24). The previous version inferred that the set travelled together from six similar marginal frequencies; it is now demonstrated. The motif's own variant positions carry the haplotype frequency exactly, as they must: Met74Ile, Asn75Glu and Lys76Thr all at 0.071. The flanking CVIET-associated residues, which are external to the 72–76 motif and so corroborate it rather than restate it, agree closely: Ala220Ser at 0.070, Gln271Glu at 0.074 and Arg371Ile at 0.076. Codon 75 is Glu, as MalariaGEN has it; the previous version's "naming discrepancy" was our annotation-matching failure, not a disagreement between pipelines, and the note in Appendix F has been rewritten accordingly.

[Observed] Clean negative: no artemisinin-resistance residue anywhere in the panel. The pfk13 panel was rebuilt from the WHO compendium of molecular markers, v1.0 (December 2025), after the previous list — written from memory — was found to contain 13 distinct changes rather than the claimed 14, to misclassify P441L and C469F as validated when both are candidate, and to omit P574L, G533S and R622I; R622I was requested but never retrieved, so it had become a silent non-result. The panel now covers all 14 WHO-validated markers (F446I, N458Y, C469Y, M476I, Y493H, G533S, R539T, I543T, P553L, R561H, P574L, C580Y, R622I, A675V), all 6 candidates (E252Q, P441L, C469F, N537I, G538V, V568G) — 20 variants which, because C469Y and C469F share codon 469, occupy 19 distinct codons — plus four further potential-tier codons in the same region (449, 481, 515, 527), giving 23 codons in all (19 + 4). The two WHO tiers are kept distinct throughout: the six candidates are candidates, not validated. Twenty-two of the 23 codons are fixed at the reference residue, and the twenty-third, codon 458, sits at 0.9995: of its 1,866 called isolates 1,865 carry Asn and one carries Asp — which is not N458Y, the validated residue. No validated, candidate or potential pfk13 resistance residue was observed in a single isolate. Two caveats belong in the same breath as the result. The denominator is per codon, 1,845 to 1,876 of the 1,900 isolates called, not "all 1,900 at every codon" as the previous version said, so between 24 and 55 isolates are uncalled at each codon. And the sampling period is 2009–2021, so this is a historical negative and not a statement about the present.

WHO tiers apply to variants, not to genes. The compendium classifies individual variants. Three consequences for this panel. First, it contains no validated or candidate lumefantrine marker at all — verbatim: "The review did not identify any validated or candidate molecular markers associated with lumefantrine resistance." A label such as "pfmdr1, WHO-validated, lumefantrine" is a category error twice over and does not appear in this revision. Second, pfaat1, pfap2mu and pfmdr2 appear nowhere in the compendium at any tier. They are retained in the panel because they are scientifically interesting, and S258L is the strongest candidate the scan produced, but none of the three may be described as a WHO-classified marker. Third, pfcoronin is in the compendium at the potential tier only; its three panel codons are fixed at the reference residue here. In this sample pfmdr2 Ile492Val runs at 0.549 and pfap2mu Ser160Asn at 0.096, and neither carries any WHO standing.

[Observed] Per-sample concordance replaces the previous validation, which did not establish what it claimed. The previous version validated the panel by saying that marginal frequencies agreed with MalariaGEN's published values "within 0.03" at 21 of 23 markers. Agreement of marginals does not show that two pipelines called the same isolates the same way, and it concealed the codon-75 problem rather than exposing it. Compared call by call on the isolates where both pipelines made a call, 47,012 of 47,012 calls agree — 100.0000 per cent across 25 markers (nine pfcrt codons, five pfmdr1, five pfdhfr, five pfdhps and one pfmdr2), with per-marker denominators from 1,834 to 1,900. Codon 75 now agrees exactly, because reconstruction yields Glu where string-matching yielded Asp. The limit of this check must be stated with it: 32 of the panel's 57 codons have no independent per-sample corroboration at all. Twenty-nine of those 32 are accounted for by the four panel genes MalariaGEN publishes no per-codon genotype for — every pfk13 codon (23), both pfaat1 codons, all three pfcoronin codons and pfap2mu 160. The remaining three sit in genes MalariaGEN does publish, but it publishes no column for them: pfcrt 73 (concordance covers nine of the panel's ten pfcrt codons), pfdhps 431 (five of six) and pfmdr2 492 (one of two). The log records no MalariaGEN column for these three and gives no reason. pfmdr2 492 in particular is therefore an unchecked panel result, which matters because its frequency (0.549) is quoted two paragraphs below.

[Interpreted] What the artemisinin negative is now worth, and what the kelch13 cross-check is worth. The result is a tested negative across the complete WHO-validated set rather than an untested absence, and that is the change that matters. Calling it the most policy-relevant result the analysis produces remains an assessment and not a finding, and three qualifications now attach to it. Surveillance for artemisinin resistance was not among the manuscript's stated objectives. The sampling period is 2009–2021. And the corroboration from MalariaGEN's own kelch13 calls is much weaker than the previous version implied: Pf8 assigns a status over the whole 349–726 domain and classifies any missing call in that range as undetermined, so only 36 isolates have a complete published status — under 2 per cent of the sample — which is weak corroboration for per-codon calls on roughly 1,860 isolates. The two must not be presented as equivalent evidence. Among those 36, the commonest observed change is Ala578Ser, which is explicitly non-resistance.

[Calculated] Mixed-infection frequency does not order monotonically along the north–south gradient. In geographic order from north to south the polyclonal fraction is 0.597 in Guinea savanna (2,153 of 3,605), 0.500 in the transitional zone (25 of 50), 0.539 in deciduous forest (439 of 815) and 0.444 in coastal savanna (193 of 435). The transitional zone is the second-most-northern zone, lies north of the deciduous forest, breaks the trend, and at 25 of 50 carries no information. The original text quoted only the two endpoints. The gradient is also confounded with sequencing study, and the demonstration is in deciduous forest, not Guinea savanna: of the five studies contributing to deciduous forest, the four large enough to test (the fifth has 4 isolates) differ in Fws — Kruskal–Wallis H = 14.9, p = 1.9 x 10-3, n = 811 — and their polyclonal fractions span 0.430 to 0.578. The p-value belongs to the Fws distributions; no test of the polyclonal fraction itself exists in the outputs, and the 7.7 x 10-88 figure printed beside deciduous forest in the corrections document is its call-rate test, not its polyclonality. Guinea savanna is the wrong zone to cite here: its six studies span 0.532 to 0.682 but do not differ significantly (p = 0.103), so it is one of the two testable zones in which this between-study heterogeneity is not detected — coastal savanna is the other (two studies, n = 435, p = 0.378) — and the transitional zone, with a single study, cannot be tested at all. Of the three testable zones the heterogeneity is detected in exactly one and is not detected in two.

[Interpreted] These fractions are a by-product of quality control rather than an analysed result. With one zone non-monotone, one zone uninformative at n = 25, and between-study variation inside a single zone of the same order as the between-zone variation, mixed-infection frequency is not safe to present as the clearest ecological signal in the data.

Which isolate set these numbers describe. The polyclonality fractions, the study-by-zone composition and the within-zone batch tests in this section are computed on the 4,905 isolates that pass sample-level QC — the set on which sequencing-study membership and Fws are defined — and not on the 1,900-isolate analysis set behind every FST, Mantel, ADMIXTURE, TreeMix, BayPass and codon-panel number above. That is why "the transitional zone, 25 of 50" appears here against "the transitional zone, n = 25" elsewhere. Within the codon panel there is a further and smaller denominator distinction that must be kept: per-codon frequencies are over the isolates called at that codon (1,834–1,900 depending on the codon), while a haplotype frequency is over the isolates called at every constituent codon (1,873–1,888), which is why 51I is 0.786 as a marginal and 0.788 on the triple-mutant complete-case set. Study nesting within zone is a property of the Pf8 sampling frame and is not created by the later cuts, but the 4,905-set counts are not the analysis-set counts, and the zone-pair study overlap was not recomputed on the 1,900 set. The denominator is stated in full at Section 2.6 and cross-referenced from Sections 4.2.2, 6.2, 7.2 and 7.3.

[Interpreted] What this implies for surveillance design, stated narrowly. Neutral common-variant differentiation between zones is negligible, so a spatially sparse design is adequate for characterising neutral structure. The withdrawal of the isolation-by-distance result reinforces that narrow claim rather than complicating it: on the present evidence there is no robust distance gradient for a denser neutral design to resolve either. Marker-frequency representativeness is a separate question, and it is the question sentinel surveillance actually answers; on that question this analysis finds significant between-zone variation at pfdhps 437 (p = 1.2 x 10-6), pfdhfr (p <= 2.43 x 10-4 at all three triple-mutant codons; the largest of the three is 2.425 x 10-4 at Asn51Ile) and pfaat1 (p = 1.9 x 10-18). The original Section 7.4 conclusion — that sentinel surveillance at a small number of sites is an adequate design for detecting emergence — is therefore withdrawn in its unqualified form, here and in Section 7.4. This is the clearest case in the manuscript of a conclusion that the resistance panel reverses, and the two sections must not disagree.

Robustness, where it stops, and the one conclusion it removed. The population-genetic conclusions did not change when the SNP panel grew by 16.6 per cent, the BayPass scan became reproducible and false-discovery accounting was added — and one of them has now been tested against the two thresholds that define the dataset and did change. What survived: maximum FST stayed within 0.00204–0.00255 across six threshold scenarios spanning 1,900 to 4,403 isolates and 3,275 to 10,876 SNPs; the ADMIXTURE cross-validation curve stayed near-flat with a negligible K = 2 improvement; re-running the ordinations on the analysis set left the silhouette slightly more negative rather than less; and the 19 outliers came through a quality check with every significant difference in their favour, though two chromosome-11 sites are downgraded on an ExcessHet and InbreedingCoeff signal the check itself surfaced. What did not survive: isolation by distance, significant in two of six threshold scenarios and four of seven region jackknife replicates, now reported as not robust. Near-panmixia and isolation by distance were graded together in the previous version, both under "robust to the largest correction the pipeline received"; they are not the same kind of result and the same six scenarios separate them cleanly. The resistance results are a third case and should not be described as robust in the same sentence as either: the first correction created the finding and the second changed its content. The triple mutant moved from an implied 0.786–0.908 to a measured 0.771, a locus at 0.897 appeared that had been absent altogether, two pfk13 variants were reclassified, a marker discrepancy dissolved into an annotation bug, and the central functional interpretation was reversed. Two successive corrections in the same place is itself a result about the process, recorded in Appendix F. The threshold sensitivity was not run on the codon panel, so nothing is claimed about the resistance frequencies' behaviour under other thresholds.

10.2 Answers to the research questions

Table S39. Research questions and the answers the current evidence supports. This table carried the number 10.1 in previous versions and is renumbered because the threshold-sensitivity table in Section 10.1 is now Table S38; the one internal cross-reference, in Appendix F, Table S44, is renumbered with it. The distance row is the only answer the robustness round of 10 October 2026 changed, and it changed downwards, from "Moderate" to not robust. The final row replaces the clause "No validated resistance locus among them", which the original table carried, and also replaces the first corrected version of that row, whose triple-mutant range, pfk13 denominator and pfk13 variant classification were all wrong.

10.3 What remains unresolved

Whether any apparent zone effect survives control for administrative region, collection year and sequencing study. Not resolvable with these data (Section 9.2). Study is a fourth confounder added since the original draft: four of the six zone pairs share no sequencing study at all, including coastal savanna against Guinea savanna, the pair behind every headline number. That overlap is computed on the 4,905 QC-passing isolates and was not recomputed on the 1,900-isolate analysis set (see the denominator note in Section 10.1).

Whether the conclusions are stable under alternative Fws and MAF thresholds is no longer unresolved. It was tested on 10 October 2026 (scripts/step13_sensitivity.py, Table S38) and answered in both directions: near-panmixia is stable, with max FST ranging only 0.00204–0.00255 over six scenarios, and isolation by distance is not, being significant in two of the six. The item is retained in this list only to record that it was the open question whose answer cost the manuscript a finding, and that the finding it cost was the spatial one.

Whether Ghanaian Plasmodium falciparum shows isolation by distance at all. This has moved out of the answered column and into this one. A Mantel correlation that appears at one setting of the two thresholds and vanishes at four others is not evidence either way, and 21 pairs from seven administrative regions is a thin test on which to rest a spatial claim. Settling it needs more populations, finer spatial units, or a method that does not require populations to be defined at all, and none of those is available from Pf8's Ghanaian metadata as it stands (Section 10.4, item 5).

Whether the outlier positions are alignment or assembly artefacts is now largely settled, and one part of it is not. Mapping quality and the rest of the callset's annotations have been checked at all 19 positions: none has MQ < 50, strand bias or read-position bias, all are RegionType Core, every significant difference from the other 3,897 scanned SNPs runs in the outliers' favour, and the exported-protein worry is specifically not supported — the eight exported-protein SNPs are the cleanest subset in the panel. What remains open is the mechanism at the two chromosome-11 sites 369 bp apart, Pf3D7_11_v3:1054078 and Pf3D7_11_v3:1054447, where ExcessHet = 69.59 at the first and the panel's two lowest InbreedingCoeff values at both point to collapsed paralogy or copy-number variation rather than to mismapping. A read-level depth check would settle it and has not been run; until it is, both sites are lower confidence and one of them is the fifth-ranked SNP in the scan.

Whether the outlier set is enriched for any functional category beyond chance. A length- and SNP-density-matched permutation test has not been run in this pipeline. Note that two of the 19 outliers are intergenic, so any gene-set test must state how it handles them.

Whether the pfaat1 S258L, pfdhps 437 and pfdhfr clines reflect spatially varying drug pressure, differing deployment histories, or the design confounders above. The frequencies are measured; their cause is not identified. The amodiaquine argument is now explicitly an untested possibility rather than the preferred explanation: the one experiment that measured amodiaquine found the IC50 shift sub-threshold, its authors conclude chloroquine was the primary selective force, and the F313S variant with which S258L shows epistasis is absent from all 1,900 isolates here.

Whether the pfk13 negative can be corroborated by anything other than our own pipeline. MalariaGEN publishes no per-codon kelch13 genotype, and its domain-level status (349-726) is complete for only 36 isolates, so 32 of the panel's 57 codons - every pfk13 codon and pfaat1 S258L among them - rest on a single pipeline. The 25 codons that can be checked agree on 47,012 of 47,012 calls, which is reassuring about the method but is not evidence about the other 32.

Whether the four potential-tier pfk13 codons (449, 481, 515, 527) and the three panel genes with no WHO tier at all (pfaat1, pfap2mu, pfmdr2) belong in a surveillance panel. They are retained here on scientific-interest grounds, which is a judgement of ours and not a WHO recommendation, and the manuscript must not blur the two.

Whether low differentiation reflects ongoing gene flow or recent common ancestry. Requires demographic inference not attempted here.

The provenance and licence of the zone polygon layer, which remains a publication blocker (Appendix E). It cannot be closed analytically — no further computation will produce a licence — and the remaining action is correspondence with the likely custodian (Section 10.4, item 2).

10.4 Most valuable next steps

[Proposed] The resistance-marker work that headed this list two revisions ago is done, and done twice: the first rebuild was itself defective and has been replaced by step12_codon_panel.py, whose outputs are the basis of every resistance number in this revision. Four further items the previous version carried are also now done, and they are struck from the list rather than restated. The Fws and MAF sensitivity analyses were run, and they overturned the isolation-by-distance result rather than defending it (Section 10.1, Table S38). Mapping quality and the other callset annotations were checked at all 19 outlier positions, clearing the exported-protein objection and flagging two chromosome-11 sites. Stage 3 was re-run on the 1,900-isolate analysis set (Figure S22). And results/baypass/xtx_all_snps.csv, which the previous version's item 6 asked to be restored, was restored and the restoration verified by md5 against three unchanged downstream outputs (Appendix F, Table S43, row 13). What follows is what is left, ordered by expected value per unit of effort. Only the first two are cheap, and the second of them is not an analysis at all.

Run a read-level depth check at `Pf3D7_11_v3:1054078` and `Pf3D7_11_v3:1054447`. A per-sample depth ratio against the genome median at two positions, from AD and DP fields the Zarr store already holds: one pass, and the cheapest item on this list. It decides the standing of the fifth-ranked SNP in the whole scan. Either the ExcessHet = 69.59 and the panel's two lowest InbreedingCoeff values are collapsed paralogy or copy-number variation, in which case both sites should be dropped from the outlier set and the FDR-passing count falls from 13 to 11, or they are not, in which case the downgrade can be lifted and the scan's fifth-ranked hit is restored to full standing. Leaving it undone leaves both sites permanently qualified in a list of only 19.

Establish the provenance of `data/ghanaAEZ.geojson`, or retire it. The layer has been identified with certainty as ArcGIS Online item 0776dae5c5314102bd03fdaab8df0356, byte-for-byte identical to the project's copy (md5 96b1a7f4da62d6f0153826f41af96152), but that item carries null licenseInfo and null accessInformation under an anonymous personal account, so it is an unattributed re-host and not a citable source. Two leads remain: whoever supplied the original Colab notebook, whose !wget cells are the most likely surviving record of a fetch URL, and the Centre for Remote Sensing and GIS at the University of Ghana, which the ArcInfo-coverage fingerprint points to as the likely custodian. In parallel, repeat the spatial join against an authoritative six-zone Ghana layer: if zone assignments are stable, the provenance gap becomes a sensitivity result rather than a threat. The practical next action is an email to cersgis@ug.edu.gh. This item cannot be closed analytically and no further computation will close it, which is why it has outlasted every other item this list has carried.

Check every externally sourced list against its source document before it is used again. This is the lesson of the round that produced this revision, and it is cheap. The pfk13 marker list was written from memory and was wrong in three ways; the reference-allele direction at pfdhps 437 was stated in the official Pf8 methodology and was not read; WHO tiers were applied to genes rather than to variants. The lists still carried on trust are the drug-policy dates, the fitness-cost claim for S258L and the gene-to-drug assignments in the panel definition. Each should be checked against its source and the source quoted in the text.

Corroborate the pfk13 and pfaat1 calls against something other than this pipeline. The per-sample concordance check covers 25 markers in five genes and is perfect on 47,012 calls, but it cannot reach the 32 codons MalariaGEN does not publish, and the kelch13 domain status gives a complete call for only 36 isolates. Options are a direct read of the Pf8 VCF at the 23 pfk13 codons by an independent route, or comparison against a published Ghanaian pfk13 survey over the same years. This matters because the artemisinin negative is the result most likely to be quoted.

Test isolation by distance properly, or stop reporting it as a finding. The region-level Mantel test on 21 pairs is at the limit of what Pf8's Ghanaian metadata supports, and it fails at four of the six threshold settings tried. Re-running it will not help. Two routes would: district- or site-level coordinates wherever the metadata carries them, which raises both the number of populations and the number of pairs; and a continuous-space method that does not require populations to be defined at all. Either is a new analysis rather than a robustness check, and neither is cheap. Until one is done the manuscript should carry near-panmixia as its spatial result and isolation by distance only as a non-robust observation, which is how Section 10.1 and Table S39 now report it.

Reframe the thesis objectives in light of the collinearity, and in light of Objective 3 now being answered. The question "do ecological zones structure the parasite population?" cannot be answered with Pf8 metadata alone. Two questions that can be, and have been, are "is Ghanaian P. falciparum spatially structured at all, and what does that imply for surveillance design?" and "do resistance-marker frequencies vary across the country?" The second was originally written off as unanswerable from these data and is in fact the strongest result the analysis holds — with the caveat that it took two attempts to measure correctly. The first of those two questions now has a narrower answer than it did a revision ago: the structure is negligible and the distance gradient is not robust, so what the analysis establishes about space is a magnitude rather than a pattern, and the surveillance-design implication rests on that measured magnitude alone.

Analyse Fws by zone and year as a transmission-intensity phenotype in its own right, with study as a covariate, since the between-study variation within a single zone is of the same order as the between-zone variation: the polyclonal fraction (Fws < 0.95) spans 0.430 to 0.578 across the four testable studies in deciduous forest. Those two numbers are fractions of isolates below the monoclonality cut, not Fws values, and Section 10.1 labels them that way; the two statements must agree.

[Interpreted] A closing assessment. The analysis has done what a baseline characterisation should do: established the scale of structure, found it to be small, identified precisely which design features limit what can be inferred, and produced a short candidate list for follow-up. What has changed across the three rounds is the standing of the negative results, the standing of our own corrections, and now the standing of a positive result. The absence of resistance loci from the outlier scan was not a result at all but a filter artefact, and correcting it converted the thesis's third objective from a null finding into its best-supported positive one — pfdhps G437 at 0.897 and zone-structured, the pfdhfr triple mutant measured as a haplotype at 0.771, and pfaat1 S258L as a strong candidate whose demonstrated functional effect is modest and background-dependent. The absence of artemisinin resistance is now genuinely tested, across the complete WHO-validated and candidate sets, on 1,845 to 1,876 called isolates per codon over 2009–2021, and genuinely negative. But the first correction of the panel was itself wrong in four ways, and only an external reader caught it. The honest conclusion is therefore two claims rather than one. A negative result is worth nothing until the thing it denies has been shown to be measurable. And internal consistency is not correctness: every defect in the first corrected panel came from a list, a direction or a label taken on trust, and the verification architecture built to catch stale numbers was never built to ask whether the inputs were the right ones. The third round adds a third claim, and it is the least comfortable of the three. Four checks this list carried as outstanding were run, and the one previously described here as addressing "the two most likely reviewer objections" removed a finding instead of answering them: isolation by distance survives neither a relaxation of the monoclonality cut nor a lower MAF floor, and is significant at two of six threshold settings. The gain and the loss come from the same six runs. The check that destroyed the spatial finding is the check that made near-panmixia the best-evidenced conclusion in the manuscript, because FST moved by less than 0.0005 while the Mantel p-value crossed 0.05 four times. A result that holds only at the thresholds that produced it was never a result; it was a setting. The manuscript is shorter by one finding and better by one method, and the third claim is simply that: a threshold is a hypothesis, and until it has been varied, every conclusion downstream of it is conditional on a number nobody tested.

12. Appendices

Section numbering — resolved. The previous version had no section 11: the headings ran 1 to 10, then 12, and the gap was flagged here because the reference list had not been written. Section 11 is now the reference list, carrying 42 entries, and the appendices remain section 12. Every entry was resolved against a primary source rather than a citation aggregator, and the eight corrections and two editorial decisions that process produced are itemised at the head of that section and in Appendix D. The numbering defect and the missing-bibliography defect are both closed.

Appendix A — Software environment

Table S40. Software versions, re-confirmed from the live environment on 10 October 2026. No version changed between the original run, the corrected run or the codon-panel run; step12_codon_panel.py adds no dependency the pipeline did not already use. Its only import outside the rows above is requests, which step03a_fetch_callset_zarr.py and step10_resistance_panel.py already required and which is not itself listed here. The same holds for step13_sensitivity.py and for the --samples/--suffix re-run of step03_umap.py: both recompute with the packages already listed — scikit-allel for Hudson FST and the block jackknife, scikit-bio for the Mantel test, scikit-learn for the ordination metrics — and neither adds a dependency.

The environment lives at /home/gyadu/.conda/envs/spatial and was built with micromamba 2.9.0 against conda-forge and bioconda; the shared conda installation's classic solver was unusable at this dependency scale. Activation:

source /home/tools/anaconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate spatial

[Observed] BayPass is run single-threaded, and this is a correctness requirement rather than a performance choice. Two 8-thread runs with the same -seed 42 produced different XtX values and therefore different outlier sets; the superseded outputs are retained under results/baypass_8thread_archive/ so the non-reproducibility can be re-demonstrated. NTHREADS=1 is now the default in step08b_baypass.sh and run_all.sh --parallel exports 1 where it previously exported 8.

Appendix B — Key configuration values

Table S41. Configuration values in force for the corrected run and the codon-panel run. Three rows are marked "Correction after audit" and three "Correction after the external critique"; MAX_MISSING kept its value but moved in the cascade; three rows had the wrong file in the "Set in" column and are corrected here. RANDOM_SEED, MIN_SAMPLE_CALL_RATE and MIN_GENOTYPE_DP were dropped from the previous version of this table in error and are restored, as is CLONAL_THRESHOLD's correct value and MIN_FWS's citation. A twenty-eighth row is added in this revision for the grid over which step13_sensitivity.py varied MIN_FWS and MIN_MAF; it records a range rather than a value in force and is marked as such.

PI_MAF is not a MAF filter. The original text described the POD as "100,000 MAF-matched simulated SNPs". That is not what pi.maf does. In baypass_utils.R it truncates the simulated ancestral frequency to [0.05, 0.95] and binomial sampling then freely produces observed minor-allele frequencies below 0.05, so the POD is not matched to the observed MAF >= 0.05 spectrum. 0.05 is also simulate.baypass's own default, so setting it changed nothing. The "MAF-matched" wording has been removed from Sections 5.6, 6.8 and 8.1 as well as from this table. Section 6.8 had carried it in different words — "with the same MAF filter as the observed data" — which survived two earlier correction rounds precisely because those rounds searched for the phrase rather than for the claim; it has now been removed as well. The pseudo-observed set is not MAF-matched.

[Observed] Session decisions versus inherited defaults. Of the 28 rows above, 27 are values in force and the twenty-eighth records the sensitivity grid rather than a setting. Of the 27, five are session decisions taken during this analysis and carry no external justification — CLONAL_THRESHOLD, MIN_GENOTYPE_GQ, TREEMIX_BLOCK_SIZE, BayPass NVAL and the codon panel's mixed-call policy — and two more, TSNE_PERPLEXITY and PCOA_N_AXES, are additions rather than judgements. Three are corrections forced by the internal audit (TreeMix SE, BayPass NTHREADS, the XtX q-value thresholds) and three by the external critique (the GENES list, the HAPLOTYPES definitions and the codon-panel ALT restriction), and all six are marked as such — six in total, not the seven stated in the previous version, whose count did not match the marks actually carried in the table; MAX_MISSING is unchanged in value but changed position in the filtering cascade, which is why the SNP count moved. The remainder are pipeline defaults or thesis-workflow specifications. The rows that most affect reported numbers are MIN_GENOTYPE_GQ, which governs the size of the SNP panel; BayPass NTHREADS, which governs whether the outlier scan is reproducible at all; and the codon panel's GENES list, which governs whether a marker exists in the manuscript or not — a point made the hard way, since pfdhps 437 was missing from the previous version because of how markers were identified rather than because of any threshold. Two of those rows have since been varied rather than merely justified: MIN_FWS and MIN_MAF were both relaxed in the sensitivity round, and the outcome is in Table S38 — near-panmixia unaffected, isolation by distance not surviving. MIN_GENOTYPE_GQ, which the paragraph above names as the largest discretionary choice in the pipeline, has still not been varied, and it governs the size of the SNP panel that every one of those six scenarios was drawn from.

[Observed] Dead constants. config.BAYPASS_THREADS (= 4), config.ADMIXTURE_CV_FOLDS, config.LD_PRUNE_ARGS and config.MANTEL_JSON are referenced by no script. Editing them changes nothing and produces no warning. They should be deleted or the consuming scripts should be made to read them, as step07b_treemix.sh already does for the TreeMix parameters.

Table S42. Principal outputs, relative to /media/biodataA/pf8/gyadu.
