Appendix D — Reference verification status

[Observed] Unchanged from the previous version except for the entry counts, which the audit found to be internally inconsistent (26 claimed in the opening sentence, 30 entries listed, subtotals summing to 27). The counts should be recomputed against the final reference list before circulation; no total is asserted here. Three entries change in this revision. Amambua-Ngwa et al. (2023, Nature Microbiology) is cited in Sections 6.8 and 10.1 for the pfaat1 S258L functional work, now read correctly: a 1.5-fold chloroquine IC50 rise in a single chloroquine-resistant Southeast Asian line (NHP4026), with epistasis between S258L and F313S and an explicit pfcrt-background caveat; amodiaquine, lumefantrine, mefloquine and quinine were all measured and the shifts were sub-threshold; the authors conclude chloroquine was the primary selective force and reject the other-quinoline hypothesis; and the 0% to 97% longitudinal rise is Gambian, 1984–2014, with nothing said about Ghana. The previous version's claim that amodiaquine was untested is withdrawn. The WHO compendium of molecular markers of antimalarial drug resistance, v1.0 (December 2025), is added as the authority for the pfk13 panel and for every tier label in it; it classifies variants and not genes, contains no validated or candidate lumefantrine marker, and contains no entry at any tier for pfaat1, pfap2mu or pfmdr2. The official Pf8 methodology document is added as the authority for the reference-allele direction at pfdhps 437, quoted in Section 10.1 and in Appendix F.

Appendix E — Reproducing the analysis

From the project directory as it currently stands on disk, on a machine with the environment of Appendix A — the project is not under version control, so the script state behind the reported run is recoverable only from those files, the exact commands and script revisions of that run are not archived, and pf8_ghana_pipeline.zip holds only the unmodified originals, which predate this revision's corrections and will not reproduce the reported numbers:

source /home/tools/anaconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate spatial
cd /media/biodataA/pf8/gyadu/scripts
python check_env.py              # verifies packages and external binaries
bash run_all.sh --list           # lists the stages
bash run_all.sh                  # full pipeline, stages 1-9
bash run_all.sh --from 4 --parallel   # stages 5-8 concurrently after stage 4

Three analyses are not stages of run_all.sh and are run directly, and one stage is re-runnable on a sample subset. All of them read Pf8 or the Zarr store together with the canonical sample table, and all write only new files under results/ and figures/: none overwrites a published output, and the Stage 3 re-run writes _qcset variants beside the originals rather than replacing them. They can be run at any time after stage 4:

python step12_codon_panel.py          # resistance markers by reconstructed codon
python step11_batch_confounding.py    # study x zone confounding
python step13_sensitivity.py          # Fws and MAF threshold sensitivity, 6 scenarios

# Stage 3 on the 1,900-isolate analysis set; outputs take the suffix, originals are untouched:
python step03_umap.py --samples <analysis sample list> --suffix _qcset

# superseded, retained only to re-demonstrate the annotation-matching defect:
# python step10_resistance_panel.py

[Observed] step12_codon_panel.py supersedes step10_resistance_panel.py entirely, and no frequency in the manuscript comes from step10 any longer. It maps each of 57 requested codons to its three genomic positions through the CDS (171 positions in total), fetches every variant record at those positions with no numalt or is_snp restriction (125 records; every codon has at least one), applies each isolate's alleles to the reference codon read from the genome, translates, and reports the full residue spectrum including the reference residue. Reference residues are read rather than assumed, so a wrong expectation in the panel definition cannot bias the output; a mixed call makes a codon uncalled. On the 1,900-isolate analysis set the run fetches about 2.53 GB of GT data in 9 x 76 chunks and completes in minutes. Note that step11_batch_confounding.py operates on the 4,905 isolates that pass sample-level QC, not on the 1,900-isolate analysis set, because sequencing-study membership and Fws are defined on that larger set; step12_codon_panel.py operates on the 1,900. Any comparison of their outputs must respect the difference (see the denominator note in Section 10.1).

[Observed] step13_sensitivity.py loads the 2.19 M × 4,682 genotype matrix once and then rebuilds the analysis matrix six times, varying one threshold at a time from the published pair, and recomputes pairwise Hudson FST with block-jackknife standard errors and the region-level Mantel test under each. It writes results/sensitivity_summary.csv and results/sensitivity_fst.csv and touches nothing else; the baseline scenario reproduces config.py exactly, which is the check that makes the other five comparable. step03_umap.py --samples --suffix re-slices the distance submatrix for the requested isolates instead of reusing the cached full-set matrix — the bug that would otherwise make a subset run silently return the full-set answer — and suffixes every output it writes. Both are re-runnable at any time and neither has a skip-if-output-exists guard.

Stage 3a is resumable: completed variant chunks are recorded and skipped, and the store is built under a .partial name and renamed only on completion, so an interrupted run cannot be mistaken for a finished one. It also self-verifies:

python step03a_fetch_callset_zarr.py --validate-only \
--validate-vcf ../data/Pf8_Ghana_Pf3D7_10_v3.vcf.gz \
--validate-region Pf3D7_10_v3:400001-440000

[Observed] In --parallel mode the thread budget is divided as: ADMIXTURE 7 concurrent K values x 4 threads; TreeMix 6 concurrent m values, single-threaded; BayPass single-threaded (NTHREADS=1, where --parallel previously exported 8 — see Appendix A); NumPy/BLAS and Numba capped at 8 threads each. Stages 5-8 write disjoint outputs and depend only on stage 4, so concurrency does not affect results. Fixed seeds make each stage deterministic given its inputs, and the Mantel permutation test is now among them: it previously set no seed, so both reported p-values were irreproducible Monte Carlo estimates.

Cache invalidation — read this before re-running. The "skip if the output exists" guards in step05b_admixture.sh, step07b_treemix.sh and step08b_baypass.sh originally tested only for the presence of an output file, not for whether its input had changed. When the SNP panel grew from 3,359 to 3,916, every stage regenerated its inputs, and then ADMIXTURE and TreeMix found their previous outputs in place, skipped the work entirely, and reported "stage finished". Only stage 8 caught the problem, because step08c asserts that its position table and the BayPass output have matching row counts and so failed loudly with the right diagnosis. All three guards now require the output to be newer than its input ([ output -nt input ]). A successor re-running after any change to stage 4 should confirm this behaviour before trusting a "skipping" message. step12_codon_panel.py has no such guard and always recomputes.

[Observed] Reproducibility caveat. The zone polygon layer (data/ghanaAEZ.geojson) still has no citable provenance, so Stage 2 cannot be reproduced from public sources alone. The file has been identified with certainty — it is byte-for-byte identical to ArcGIS Online item 0776dae5c5314102bd03fdaab8df0356, md5 96b1a7f4da62d6f0153826f41af96152 on both sides — but that item's licenseInfo and accessInformation are both null and its owner is an anonymous personal account, so it is an unattributed re-host rather than an origin. It must not be attributed to any named institution. Cite it as accessed, record the checksum for identity, cite the underlying classification separately (the scheme is a hybrid: MoFA/FAO savanna and coastal names with a Hall & Swaine-lineage evergreen split), and state the provenance gap as a limitation. This remains a publication blocker. Three further gaps are unchanged or newly noted: the project is not under version control, so the exact script state at run time is recoverable only from the files on disk; t-SNE and the ADMIXTURE runs have not been verified to be bitwise reproducible across thread counts, although BayPass now is; and the results/baypass/xtx_all_snps.csv gap is narrowed — the file was found truncated to zero bytes, has been regenerated from the raw BayPass summaries, and the regeneration is consistent with three unchanged downstream outputs by md5, which does not establish row-for-row identity with the lost table (Appendix F, Table S43, row 13) — but the incident itself belongs in this list, because nothing under results/ is write-protected and the project is not under version control, so a stray redirect into an output file is undetectable except by looking for it. A fifth, added in this revision: the codon panel's pfk13 codon list is transcribed from the WHO compendium into step12_codon_panel.py by hand, which is exactly the kind of step that produced defect 10 below, so a successor should re-check the list against the compendium rather than against the previous script.

Appendix F — Audit and correction record

[Observed] This appendix exists for traceability, and it now records three rounds. Round one, 9–10 October 2026: an adversarial multi-agent audit. Eight reviewers working across separate dimensions raised 110 findings, each of which was then put to three independent verifiers prompted to refute it and defaulting to refuted where they could not substantiate the claim. A finding counts as confirmed only where at least two of three verifiers failed to refute it after checking the code, data or outputs themselves. Sixty-one findings were confirmed — 14 high severity, 31 medium, 16 low — with 19 disputed and 21 refuted. Thirty verifications in the reproducibility dimension were lost to a rate limit and are recorded as unverified rather than confirmed. Seven confirmed defects required code changes; stages 4 to 9 were re-run on 10 October 2026 with all seven in place. Round two, 10 October 2026: an external critique of the corrected manuscript. It raised five points. All five were upheld and four were defects in the analysis code, not in the prose — in the resistance panel that round one had introduced to fix its own largest finding. The panel was rebuilt as scripts/step12_codon_panel.py and every resistance number in this revision was read from that script's outputs. This is the fourth consecutive round in which a correction contained its own defect, and the pattern is consistent: each layer was checked for internal consistency against our own outputs, and what escaped was external correctness — whether the marker list matched an authoritative source, whether the annotation direction matched the reference genome, whether a co-occurrence claim had co-occurrence data behind it. The verification architecture was built to catch stale numbers, and it did. It was not built to ask whether the inputs were the right ones. Round three, 10 October 2026: the four outstanding robustness checks. This round was not an audit of the manuscript but the execution of four items that Section 10.4 carried as outstanding, and it is recorded here because one of them overturned a conclusion instead of confirming it. The Fws and MAF sensitivity analysis (scripts/step13_sensitivity.py) withdrew isolation by distance as a supported result — significant in two of six threshold scenarios — and, from the same six runs, made near-panmixia the best-evidenced conclusion in the manuscript. The outlier quality check cleared the exported-protein artefact concern and downgraded two chromosome-11 SNPs on an ExcessHet and InbreedingCoeff signal. Stage 3 was re-run on the analysis set. The GeoJSON provenance cannot be closed analytically and remains open. Round three found no defect in the prose and none in the analysis code; what it found was that a published result depended on the thresholds that produced it. Two integrity incidents occurred during the round and are registered as rows 13 and 14 of Table S43 rather than left in the logs.

Table S43. Confirmed defects that required code changes: eight from the internal audit (the eighth found by the re-run itself), four from the external critique, and two integrity incidents from the robustness round of 10 October 2026. All fourteen are fixed. Defects 1-8 were re-run on 10 October 2026; defects 9-12 are fixed in step12_codon_panel.py, whose outputs supersede step10's. Rows 13 and 14 are operational rather than analytical: neither changed a reported number, and they are registered here rather than left in the logs because a defect register that records only the defects that changed a number is not a defect register.

Table S44. Confirmed defects corrected in the text rather than in code, from both rounds. No re-run was needed for any of them with one exception, which is noted rather than hidden: the pfcrt codon 75 entry's correction comes from the panel rebuild recorded as defect 9 of Table S43 and did require a re-run; it is repeated here because the wording it replaces was prose. The four entries shown in bold were raised by the external critique, whose five points are otherwise recorded as defects 9-12 of Table S43; on the critique's own four-code/one-prose split only the pfaat1 interpretation and the gene-level WHO tier labels are text-only, so the bold marks here should not be read as four further text-only defects.

[Observed] Confirmed and not yet acted on. Three items have come off this list since the previous version, all in the robustness round of 10 October 2026: the Fws and MAF sensitivity analyses, now reported in Section 10.1 and Table S38; the mapping-quality check at the outlier positions, now in Section 10.1 and results/outlier_quality_check.csv; and the re-run of Stage 3 on the analysis set, now Figure S22. What remains: the functional-enrichment permutation test for the outlier gene set; the temporal control at a single collection year; the robustness join against an authoritative six-zone Ghana layer; the recomputation of the zone-pair study overlap on the 1,900-isolate set; independent corroboration of the 32 panel codons MalariaGEN does not publish per sample; and, added by the robustness round, a read-level depth check at Pf3D7_11_v3:1054078 and Pf3D7_11_v3:1054447, without which two of the 19 outliers stay qualified. These are carried in Sections 8.2, 10.3 and 10.4 rather than here. Four items that the corrections document listed as outstanding — the filter order, the TreeMix run without -se, the study/batch confounding and the absence of FDR accounting — were subsequently fixed and are reported as results in Sections 4.2.6, 6.1, 6.7, 2.6, 6.2, 7.2 and 6.8; any defect register that still lists them as open is out of date. Two high-severity findings — that the canonical markers were excluded at ingest, and that the top AAT1 outlier is S258L — were confirmed by the audit but not independently re-verified before the server went off-LAN; both have since been re-derived directly from Pf8 by the codon panel. The previous version's cross-validation claim for that panel, "21 of 23 shared markers agree within 0.03", is withdrawn and replaced: per-sample comparison against MalariaGEN's published genotypes now gives 47,012 of 47,012 calls in agreement across 25 markers, which is a stronger result obtained by a stricter test, and which also exposed the codon-75 error that the weaker test had hidden.

On pfcrt codon 75 — not a naming discrepancy. The previous version reported that Pf8's SnpEff annotation labels the ALT at pfcrt codon 75 as Asp while MalariaGEN calls it Glu, described this as the one marker-naming discrepancy among 23, and credited MalariaGEN with being right on the biology. The diagnosis was wrong. Codon 75 carries two variants — 403621 (A>G, annotated Asn75Asp) and 403623 (T>A, annotated Asn75Lys) — and Asn→Glu requires both, so no single-variant annotation can name N75E at all. The appearance of a discrepancy was created entirely by identifying markers from annotation strings. Reconstructed from the codon, residue 75 is Glu at 0.071 (n = 1,874), exactly matching the other CVIET component residues, and it agrees with MalariaGEN on every one of the 1,874 jointly called isolates. There is no discrepancy to follow a convention about: there is one residue, Glu, and it is ours and MalariaGEN's alike.

— End of manuscript —
